# Improved Keybindings — Decision Briefs (adversarial)

**Date:** 2026-06-13
**Status:** Decision briefs for the 38 non-code (`user-product-decision` + `cross-team-decision`) design-readiness questions
**Companion to:** [`...design-readiness-questions.md`](./2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md) (the questions) and [`...code-investigation-answers.md`](./2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-code-investigation-answers.md) (the verified facts these briefs are grounded in)

## Method

Each decision got a brief (named options, each with a steelman and an adversarial *what-breaks*, plus effort/reversibility), grounded in the verified code facts. Each brief was then **red-teamed** by an independent agent that re-checked the grounding, hunted for a missing option, priced the effort/dependencies honestly, and either upheld, hardened, **flipped**, or downgraded-to-toss-up the recommendation. A final pass sequenced all decisions into waves and surfaced the linchpins.

Verdicts: **33** HARDENED · **5** FLIPPED

> These are **recommendations to decide against**, not decisions. Each names its owner and what would settle it. Read the sequencing section first.

---

## Decision sequencing

Treat A1-Q1 as the gate to everything: rule NOW that R3b (the context/platform record schema) is deferred to Phase 2, which resolves the end-goals-vs-report contradiction and collapses the whole schema-dependent question chain out of Phase 1. In the same Wave-0 sitting, lock the cross-cutting contracts that have ~0 code but contaminate everything downstream - X2 (harden the loader, stay flat now, migrate to records once in Phase 2 behind a duplex deserializer + absent-vs-parse-error split), X3/A1-Q11 (import/export is same-platform-only, flat reuse, no encoding commitment), X11 then X4 (ratify the canonical-name authority before freezing the versioned catalog key), X8 (decoupled reset-all-reapply watcher, route file-format forward-compat to X2, reject any Phase-1 user-layer object), and X10/A5-Q10 (the conflict honesty floor and current-OS-only doc declaration). Then build Phase 1 over the flat map (Wave 1): the flagged hot-reload watcher + ledger trait, the hardened write path, the warn banner, search index, and the honest-floor conflict relabel - and crucially TAKE X13's resolution-snapshot capture before any of Phase 2 reorders bindings(), because that dump is irreversible. Phase 2 then splits into a foundations wave (A2-Q2/A2-Q3/A2-Q5 schema-and-mechanism, A3-Q2/A3-Q5/A3-Q13 layer+unbind engine which ships first to unblock R3b's inject path, plus X13's harness gate) and a consumers wave (grammar/serializer, scoped settings UI, conflict truthfulness tiers, and the platform read-only editor). Phase 3 modal editing (A4-Q1/Q2/Q3) comes strictly last and is where X11's alias registry is finally built and seeded at the first deliberate rename. The two live decisions a lead must force before writing Phase-1 code are: publish the A1-Q1 defer ruling so X11/X2/X8 read consistently, and pick ONE Phase-1 portability story - same-OS-only (X3/A1-Q11) versus a cmd<->ctrl read-rule slice (A6-Q10) - because those two cannot both be the promise."

### Linchpin decisions (resolve these first)

- **A1-Q1** — The single highest-fan-out ruling. Deciding to DEFER R3b (rich context/platform schema) to Phase 2 resolves the documented end-goals-vs-report contradiction and nullifies the entire schema-dependent question chain for Phase 1 (round-trip, cross-platform import, unknown-name migration, schema back-compat). It directly gates A1-Q2, A1-Q10, A1-Q11, A1-Q15, frames X2/X11/A6-Q10, and determines whether Axis 1 is a one-week quick win or a multi-week schema project. Until this is settled, no Phase-1 file-writer can be scoped.
- **X2** — The canonical file-schema authority. Its 'harden the loader now, stay flat in Phase 1, migrate to records once in Phase 2 with a duplex/untagged deserializer + absent-vs-parse-error split' is the seam every file-touching decision inherits. Gates X3 (export is just another legacy flat file), X8 (file-format forward-compat is routed here), A1-Q10 (alias write shares the chokepoint), A1-Q11 (origin-platform stamp rides the envelope), A2-Q3 (the record shape it reserves), and A6-Q10 (full {mac,other} pair needs this schema). It is the data-loss firewall the whole installed base depends on.
- **X13** — The regression net the entire Phase-2/3 refactor program is gated on. Its irreversible CAPTURE of the (context,keystroke,OS)->winning-action baseline must land in Phase 1 before the first bindings() reorder (R2); after that the golden cannot be re-derived cleanly. Every axis's 'no cross-axis regression' acceptance claim (Axis 2/3/4/5/6) is gated on this diff existing, and it supplies Axis 6 its target-OS injection seam. It unblocks confidence rather than features, but nearly every engine decision depends on it.
- **A2-Q3** — The Phase-2 persistence linchpin. The record-list [{name,trigger,context?,platform?}] shape co-decides A2-Q4 (override targeting), A2-Q6 (grammar/serializer), A2-Q8 (interning), A2-Q15 (write path) and feeds A2-Q18 (settings UI), A5-Q14 (conflict scope field), the catalog platform column (X4), and A6's platform field. Almost every Phase-2 engine deliverable that persists or reads scope funnels through this single schema choice.
- **A2-Q5** — The mechanism-and-dependency selector that sets Phase-2 scope. Choosing mutate-vs-inject and which use-case class (rebind disjoint-context fork vs add net-new scope/suppress) determines whether Axis 2 can ship standalone or hard-requires the full Axis-3 (layer,recency) precedence store + Unbound. It hard-couples A2-Q3/A2-Q4 and decides whether A3-Q1/A3-Q3/R2 get pulled into Phase 2 - i.e. it shapes the size of the entire engine wave.
- **X11** — The cheap-but-high-fan-out name-authority contract. Naming a canonical-name DRI and ratifying the unknown-name policy must happen BEFORE the R9 catalog v1 (X4) turns action name into a committed public id. It gates X4's stable key, feeds A5 catalog and the agent skill, owns the A1-Q10 alias facet, and is the just-in-time build trigger for Axis-4 Step-D renames. ~0 code to decide, but freezing the wrong key contaminates two consumers and Phase-3 migration.

### Recommended waves

- **Wave 0 - Cross-cutting contracts & scope rulings (settle before ANY code; near-zero code):** A1-Q1, X2, X3, X11, X4, X8, X10, A5-Q10, A6-Q10
  - These are rulings, not builds, and every Phase-1 file-writer/contract is downstream of them. A1-Q1 must first resolve the R3b scope contradiction (defer to Phase 2). X2 then fixes the loader-hardening + flat-now/records-later + duplex-deserializer strategy that all file work inherits, and X3 sets the import/export portability promise (same-platform-only, flat reuse, no encoding commitment). X11 ratifies the canonical-name authority before X4 freezes the versioned catalog manifest and turns action name into a public id. X8 settles that the hot-reload watcher ships decoupled (reset-all reapply, no persistent applied-set) and routes file-format forward-compat to X2 while rejecting any Phase-1 user-layer object. X10 fixes the four-tier conflict-truthfulness contract and the Phase-1 honesty floor, A5-Q10 mandates the doc scope declaration (current-OS-only), and A6-Q10 makes the R5 split call (whether any Phase-1 cmd<->ctrl portability read-rule slice exists vs full pair deferred to Phase 2). Locking these prevents the Phase-1 quick wins from being built forward-incompatible and discarded.
- **Wave 1 - Phase-1 build (quick wins over today's flat HashMap; no engine/schema change):** A1-Q2, A1-Q14, A1-Q15, A1-Q10, A1-Q11, A3-Q14, A3-Q18, A5-Q1, A5-Q11, A5-Q14, A5-Q15, X13
  - All buildable over the flat map once Wave 0 rulings hold. A1-Q2 gates hot-reload behind a dedicated FeatureFlag with a clean disabled path; A3-Q14 ships the applied-names ledger behind a 3-method trait that must honor that flag in lockstep. A1-Q14 (malformed-reload error surface) and A1-Q15 (IndexMap stable ordering + the atomic temp-rename write that fixes the only runtime data-loss bug) harden the write path. A1-Q10 ships the warn-on-unknown-name banner (alias CODE deferred to Step-D), A1-Q11 labels and guards same-platform-only import. On Axis 5, A5-Q1 lands the resolvability CI test + shared source builder, A5-Q11 does the mandatory honest relabel + ConflictMap rekey floor, A5-Q14 ships warn-only-advisory save, A5-Q15 adds the hidden name-search index, and A3-Q18 adds the apply-time prefix-shadow guard riding chord capture (R8). Critically, X13's CAPTURE - the frozen pre-refactor resolution baseline - must be taken during this wave, because all of Phase 1 sits safely before the first bindings() reorder and the dump is irreversible once R2 lands.
- **Wave 2 - Phase-2 foundations (record schema + layer/precedence engine + harness gate):** A2-Q2, A2-Q3, A2-Q5, A2-Q1, A3-Q2, A3-Q5, A3-Q13, X13
  - The engine core. A2-Q2 fixes the context-only engine key with platform as a reserved load-filter field, which co-decides A2-Q3's record-list schema; A2-Q5 then selects the mechanism (mutate-vs-inject) and the use-case class, which sets whether full R2 is pulled in. A2-Q1 does the public-alias vocabulary RFC in parallel (design now, publish incrementally). On Axis 3, A3-Q2 proves the N-layer resolver against a synthetic mode-pack fixture (policy deferred), A3-Q5 ships the additive name-scoped unbind + match_custom leak fix, and A3-Q13 ships the named-editable layer + keystroke-addressable Unbound tombstone - this layer ships FIRST and is the actual unblocker for R3b's inject path. X13's harness+gate is built just-in-time at this R2 boundary so every refactor in this and later waves diffs against the Wave-1 golden. (X13 appears here as build, distinct from its Wave-1 capture.)
- **Wave 3 - Phase-2 consumers (grammar, conflict truthfulness, platform editor, scoped settings UI):** A2-Q6, A2-Q10, A2-Q18, A3-Q19, A6-Q2, A6-Q1, A6-Q11
  - These consume the Wave-2 schema and engine. A2-Q6 ships the Display serializer now (read-only catalog/export) and defers the FromStr parser; A2-Q10 symmetrizes unknown-atom eval so a bad atom degrades to inert-within-scope. A2-Q18 un-collapses same-name rows by registered context and makes existing scoped rows editable in place (serialize-and-match, no parser). A3-Q19 adds the user-unbound badge + universal file-wipe reset-all safety net atop the new unbind model. On Axis 6, A6-Q2 builds the keymap-only editor with the serde-additive constraint, A6-Q1 shows read-only inactive-platform display with portable current-platform edits, and A6-Q11 reserves R6's target_os parameter at design time while staging the inactive-conflict build. The deeper conflict tiers (X10 v1/v2/v3) and the A5-Q14 gate/swap half also build here once the scope field and platform pair exist.
- **Wave 4 - Phase-3 pluggable modal editing (Axis 4):** A4-Q1, A4-Q2, A4-Q3
  - Strictly after Phase 2: per-mode keymaps are unusable without context-scoped overrides (R3b) and layer precedence (R2). A4-Q1 cuts user-editable per-mode keymaps to post-Phase-2 and ships the engine + Helix proof + dropdown with a forward-compatible Modal:{engine}:{submode} seam confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit. A4-Q2 scopes modality to the code editor only and migrates the vim_mode:bool setting to an editing_mode enum (deferring flag-multiplication). A4-Q3 sets the tiered Helix acceptance bar as a Phase-3-entry spike decision, not a commitment made now. This is also where X11's alias registry is finally BUILT and seeded (Step-D is the first deliberate rename) and A1-Q10's alias CODE ships.

### Dependency edges (FROM must be settled before TO)

- A1-Q1 → X2 — Scope ruling that records defer to Phase 2 is what lets X2 keep the file flat in Phase 1 and migrate once; the two are co-determined (A1-Q1 settles scope, X2 settles file mechanics + duplex deserializer).
- A1-Q1 → A1-Q2 — If the watcher is deferred (R1 not in Phase 1) the feature-flag question is moot; A1-Q1 confirms the watcher ships in Phase 1, enabling A1-Q2.
- A1-Q1 → A1-Q11 — Schema ruling decides feasibility: defer R3b => same-platform-only import (full {mac,other} pair impossible without the record schema).
- A1-Q1 → A1-Q15 — IndexMap stable-ordering guarantee holds only if R3b is deferred; if records land in Phase 1 the serializer is rebuilt and the guarantee re-opens.
- A1-Q1 → A1-Q10 — Warn-banner works over the flat map regardless, but the R3b placement sets whether the loader chokepoint is open in Phase 1 and when the alias contract's consumer (Step-D) exists.
- A1-Q1 → X11 — Loader chokepoint is open in Phase 1 only if R3b lands then; defer => registry decided now but built just-in-time at Step-D.
- X2 → X3 — A Phase-1 flat export is just another legacy flat file that X2's mandatory duplex/migrating deserializer must already handle; X3 adds no new schema seam under A'.
- X2 → A1-Q11 — The recommended origin-platform stamp must ride the X2 file envelope; bolting it on independently trips the read-returns-None silent-drop hazard.
- X2 → X8 — X8 explicitly routes the only migration-bearing artifact (file-format forward-compat for the file-writers import/export and chord capture) to X2's duplex deserializer + absent-vs-parse-error split.
- X2 → A2-Q3 — X2 commits that the record schema lands once in Phase 2; A2-Q3 defines that record shape, inheriting X2's back-compat/version-detection obligations.
- X2 → A6-Q10 — The full {mac,other} platform pair cannot live in the flat file; it needs X2's Phase-2 record schema, so A6-Q10's full-pair half is gated on X2.
- X11 → X4 — The frozen editable-name catalog key is stable across renames only if X11's alias map is consulted at catalog generation; X4's agent contract binds to that key.
- X11 → A1-Q10 — The alias contract is the Axis-1 facet of X11 and must be ratified with it; A1-Q10 reserves the contract now and ships alias code at Step-D.
- X11 → A4-Q1 — Axis-4 Step-D must not rename any editable vim name until the X11 registry exists and is seeded; the registry's just-in-time build is triggered by Step-D.
- A3-Q14 → A1-Q2 — The applied-names ledger owner must honor the hot-reload feature flag in lockstep so the disabled path never seeds a partial ledger - a hard, not soft, coupling.
- A1-Q2 → A1-Q14 — The malformed-reload error surface is gated on hot-reload existing in Phase 1; at launch-only, silence is benign and the question defers with the watcher.
- X13 → A3-Q2 — The irreversible resolution-snapshot capture must precede the first bindings() reorder (R2) that A3-Q2's layer proof performs.
- X13 → A3-Q13 — The precedence rewrite needs the snapshot regression net in place before merge to prove no unrelated binding changed winner.
- X13 → A2-Q5 — The scoped-override engine change must diff against the frozen baseline; X13 supplies the before/after gate for Axis-2 resolution changes.
- X13 → A6-Q11 — Asserting inactive-platform conflicts on single-OS CI requires X13's target-OS injection seam (OperatingSystem::get() has no native override).
- A2-Q2 → A2-Q3 — The context-only engine key and the reserved platform? load-filter field are co-decided before the record-list schema shape can be fixed.
- A2-Q5 → A2-Q3 — All non-trivial mechanism options require the record list; A2-Q5 favoring inject/recency precedence is the condition under which A2-Q3's list-ordering advantage holds.
- A2-Q1 → A2-Q10 — The curated public-alias vocabulary IS the allowlist that A2-Q10's unknown-atom validation checks against.
- A2-Q1 → A2-Q6 — The documented vocabulary defines the legal atoms the grammar and serializer/parser are bound to.
- A2-Q3 → A2-Q18 — The settings-UI scoped-row editor needs the record schema's context field to un-collapse and persist same-name rows.
- A2-Q3 → A2-Q6 — The persisted schema must gain a context field for FromStr/Display to read and round-trip.
- A2-Q3 → A5-Q14 — The conflict save gate/swap half needs the per-(name,context) scope field that the record schema introduces.
- A3-Q13 → A2-Q5 — Axis-3's binding-layer model ships first and is the unblocker for R3b's inject path; A2-Q5's inject option couples to the layer store A3-Q13 builds.
- A2-Q3 → A4-Q1 — Per-mode keymap editability is deferred until R3b's record schema; A4-Q1 confirms the modal seam shape against it at Phase-2 exit.
- A3-Q13 → A4-Q1 — Axis-3 layers/precedence/Unbound (R2) gate the modal editability sub-goal per the dependency spine.
- A4-Q1 → A4-Q2 — A4-Q1's surface scope and v1 acceptance bar frame A4-Q2's modality-surface and bool->enum decision.
- A4-Q1 → A4-Q3 — The Helix acceptance bar sits inside the engine deliverable A4-Q1 keeps in scope; A4-Q3 sets that bar as a Phase-3-entry spike.
- A6-Q10 → A6-Q2 — Under read-rule-first, the canonical-representation choice is deferred to a Phase-2 spike that A6-Q2 resolves.
- A6-Q10 → A6-Q1 — Full-pair persistence (the inactive-platform display/edit) is gated behind the record schema A6-Q10 routes to Phase 2.
- A6-Q2 → A6-Q11 — The canonical representation must carry both platform sides or there is no inactive-OS keystroke for R6 to conflict-check.
- X4 → A5-Q1 — The versioned catalog contract shapes the shared source builder and resolvability test A5-Q1 converges onto.
- X10 → A5-Q14 — The four-tier conflict-truthfulness contract gates what the named save-warning may claim and when the hard-conflict save-gate (B) may exist.
- A5-Q11 → A5-Q14 — The Phase-1/Phase-2 conflict scope line sequences A5-Q14: warn-only is the Phase-1 floor, the gate/swap is Phase 2.

### Contradictions / tensions to resolve

- **A1-Q1 ↔ X2 ↔ X8 ↔ X11** — Documented scope contradiction the whole sequence inherits: the end-goals doc (L52-53) lists R3b as an Axis-1/Phase-1 leaf, while the report roadmap (section 7) and dependency spine (L117) place R3b in Phase 2. A1-Q1, X2, and X8 all resolve it in favor of the Phase-2 placement (defer records, harden the flat loader now). X11's brief flags that its own 'build now' wording silently assumed R3b-in-Phase-1; once A1-Q1's defer ruling holds, X11 must be read as build-just-in-time (Step-D). The lead must publish the A1-Q1 ruling FIRST and propagate it, or X11/X2/X8 briefs can be read inconsistently.
- **X3 ↔ A1-Q11 ↔ A6-Q10** — Phase-1 cross-OS portability is contested. X3 (REC=A', literal flat reuse, same-platform-only, warn on cross-OS) and A1-Q11 (same-platform-only v1, guard+label) both decline to pull any of R5 into Phase 1 and make NO cross-OS portability promise. A6-Q10 (read-rule-first) instead wants to ship the A6-Q12 back-compat READ/resolution rule in Phase 1 so the common cmd<->ctrl case ports cross-OS with no new on-disk representation - which X3 explicitly enumerates as its rejected Option B. The lead must pick one Phase-1 portability story: same-OS-only (X3/A1-Q11) OR a cmd<->ctrl read-rule slice (A6-Q10). They cannot both be the Phase-1 promise.
- **A2-Q3 ↔ X2** — Schema-firmness mismatch. X2 commits, as a settled Phase-2 deliverable, that 'records land once in Phase 2' with the {name,trigger,context?,platform?} shape reserved. A2-Q3 keeps that very record list CONDITIONAL on (1) product confirming Phase 2 needs NEW per-context scopes and (2) A2-Q5 favoring inject/recency precedence - otherwise it recommends deferring the schema change further. If A2-Q3's conditions fail, X2's reserved record shape may never materialize (or stay flatter), so X2's 'migrate once' planning must treat the record shape as provisional until A2-Q5/A2-Q3 are jointly decided in Wave 2.
- **A2-Q5 ↔ A3-Q2 ↔ A3-Q13** — Fixed-fork migration timing. A2-Q5's recommended added option D migrates the ~19 flagship Vim Fixed forks to named EditableBindings in Phase 2 (assigning names, pulling a narrow slice of A3-Q1 forward). A3-Q2 explicitly KEEPS A3-Q1 (Fixed migration) and A3-Q3 (stable identity) in Phase 3 and does NOT migrate real vim in Phase 2; A3-Q13 defers context-aware fixed REMAP behind R3b. The tension is whether ANY Fixed-fork migration happens in Phase 2 (A2-Q5 option D) or none (A3-Q2/A3-Q13 keep it in Phase 3). Resolve by confirming whether the disjoint-context vim rebind use-case is in Phase-2 scope; if yes, A3-Q2's 'no real-vim migration in Phase 2' boundary must move.
- **X4 ↔ X11 ↔ A1-Q10** — Resolved-but-load-bearing tension on the stable-name key. X4 freezes the editable-name as the agent's committed public id and binds the agent contract to it; X11 and A1-Q10 acknowledge action names can drift (Step-D renames). X4's own correction notes Step-D is Phase 3, so the key is stable through Phase 1 - but only if the X11 alias registry is consulted at catalog generation once Step-D lands. The contract (X11) must be ratified before X4 ships, or the catalog freezes a key that later renames silently break for the agent.

---

## Axis 1 — Config as portable, live data

### A1-Q1 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product DRI for the keybindings initiative (owner of the end-goals/roadmap docs), who makes the scope call with the keybindings tech lead for sizing. The question is tagged user-product-decision, so product holds the ruling; engineering supplies the effort estimate. Immediate, owner-level actionable independent of the ruling: correct end-goals L52-53 to match report section 7 / dependency spine L117 (R3b = Phase 2) — a one-line doc fix that removes the documented contradiction. The tech lead additionally owns recording the Axis-2 duplex-reader (A1-Q12) acceptance criterion so deferral carries no migration debt.

**Decision.** Whether the rich context/platform-scoped persisted schema (R3b: flat HashMap<name, PersistedTrigger> becoming records of {name, trigger, optional context, optional platform}) is in scope for Axis 1, or whether Axis 1 ships ONLY the hot-reload watcher + reset/last-applied bookkeeping over today's flat map and defers R3b to Axis 2. Re-grounding sharpens the ruling: the schema-dependent question chain (per-context round-trip, cross-platform import, unknown-name migration, schema back-compat) is genuinely Axis-2 work, AND R3b alone cannot deliver per-context capability without a separate keymap engine change, AND the flat back-compat that deferral allegedly risks is already owed to every existing customizer regardless. So the call is not a close scope judgment hedged by serde tricks — it is a clear defer, with the only residual product input being whether cross-OS shared-config import is a first-release promise (a narrow R5 slice, not R3b).

**Stakes.** Sizes the whole axis. Watcher-only over the flat map is a low-effort win delivering the "config as portable, live data" outcome (instant apply of a shareable file with correct revert/unbind). R3b-in-scope turns it into a multi-week schema + parser + migration + back-compat project AND, because per-context overrides also require a keymap engine change (update_custom_trigger is name-keyed, keymap.rs:422-433), would ship a rich format with no engine consumer. Mis-scoping over-builds (slips the live-data win on a speculative, currently-unusable schema) far more than it under-builds: the under-build risk the brief feared (a breaking migration of interim hand-authored files) is largely illusory because the in-app UI already writes the same flat format to disk today (keyboard.rs:64-75), so Axis 2 owes a flat back-compat reader to the entire existing user base whether or not the watcher ships. The decision is surfaced now because the docs contradict each other: end-goals lists R3b as an Axis-1 leaf (L52-53) while the report roadmap (section 7) and dependency spine (L117, "no schema/engine change") put R3b in Phase 2. That contradiction needs a one-line doc correction now; the engineering can proceed against the flat map immediately either way.

_Constrained by:_
- Today's persisted schema is a flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>; PersistedTrigger is a newtype around a single normalized string. No context or platform fields. R3b is precisely a change to this record shape. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:169,174`
- The flat file is already richer than the in-app UI: TryFrom parses multi-keystroke chords via split_whitespace into Vec1<Keystroke>, and 'none' deserializes to an explicit unbind (Removed). So no user-facing capability gap forces R3b now; the usual capability-gap argument is inverted. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15,22,192,196-211`
- No hot-reload exists: handle_warp_managed_paths_event has branches for themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings.toml but NO keybindings.yaml branch, and load_custom_keybindings runs exactly once at launch. Axis 1's real work (watcher branch + last_applied ledger) is greenfield and orthogonal to schema shape. — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134; app/src/lib.rs:2557`
- DECISIVE, MISSED BY BRIEF: existing users ALREADY have flat keybindings.yaml files on disk — the in-app UI writes the flat format today via write_custom_keybinding, called from in-app set/reset/unbind. Therefore Axis 2's R3b MUST provide flat-file back-compat for the entire existing customizer base regardless of whether Axis 1 ships a watcher. The 'breaking migration on early hand-authors' downside of deferral is not a new cost. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:64-75; app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506,513; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:600`
- DECISIVE, MISSED BY BRIEF: per-context overrides require BOTH R3b schema AND a keymap engine change. update_custom_trigger is name-keyed and rewrites the custom_trigger on ALL same-name bindings, taking no context/binding-id. So R3b-now (Option B) would ship a rich file the engine cannot consume — a format with no consumer, not merely a speculative one. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433; answers doc A1-Q9 L246,L257`
- A1-Q7 late-registration is settled BENIGN, so it does NOT inflate the watcher effort: all 109 editable-binding registrations run once in initialize_app, before the launch-time load runs; zero registrations sit in render/new/constructor/paint/layout paths. A re-apply-on-registration hook is defensive-only, not a correctness fix. — `answers doc A1-Q7 L148,L170; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:405-419; app/src/lib.rs:1648-1703,2557`
- The CONFIRMED watcher mechanics — DIFF reload, a persistent last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind{Keystrokes|Empty|absent}> ledger seeded from the launch load, and tri-state reconciliation (keys=set, 'none'=set Empty, absent=remove/revert) — are all defined over the flat name-keyed map and need zero context/platform fields. Warp's own writers already round-trip this tri-state (in-app unbind persists 'none'; in-app reset deletes the entry). — `answers doc A1-Q3/A1-Q4 L32-44,L102; app/src/keyboard.rs:43-44; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:600`
- A hold-last-good-on-parse-error reload precedent exists for settings (load-without-write-back to avoid watcher feedback loops) and is copyable over the flat map independent of any schema change. NOTE the precedent does NOT cover keybindings' novel hazard: the in-app writer mutates the same file the watcher watches (echo-suppression, A1-Q6), a feedback path settings never had. — `crates/settings/src/manager.rs:327; app/src/keyboard.rs:64-75`
- A flat-vs-rich back-compat duplex deserializer is structurally feasible and belongs in Axis 2 (when R3b's shape is KNOWN), not now: legacy is a top-level YAML map of name->scalar, distinguishable from a record list/mapping-valued schema via serde untagged. The None-on-parse-failure path (keyboard.rs:132-138) is why this reader is mandatory — without it a struct change silently drops every existing user's bindings on upgrade. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:132-138; readiness doc A1-Q12 L216-221`

**Options:**

- **Defer R3b to Axis 2 — naive watcher-only over the flat map** _(low effort · easy)_ — Axis 1 ships only the hot-reload watcher + last_applied bookkeeping + tri-state reconciliation + echo-suppression over today's flat HashMap. R3b and its dependents (per-context round-trip, unknown-name migration, cross-platform import, schema back-compat) defer to Axis 2, with the future flat-file migration accepted as an open cost rather than an explicit Axis-2 commitment.
  - 👍 Every CONFIRMED Axis-1 mechanic (DIFF, tri-state, hold-last-good) is fully specified over the flat name-keyed map and needs no schema change; the file is already richer than the UI, so R3b buys no near-term user capability. Delivers the live-data outcome as a low-effort, reversible win and lets the rich schema be designed against Axis-2's real requirements. Matches report section 7 and the L117 dependency spine.
  - 👎 Without an explicit Axis-2 back-compat acceptance criterion, the flat-file duplex reader could be forgotten — and the None-on-parse path (keyboard.rs:132-138) would then silently nuke every flat file on the R3b upgrade. Leaves the end-goals L52-53 leaf list factually wrong, so a doc correction is mandatory. Effort framed as '~1 week' understates two schema-independent unknowns: A1-Q6 echo-suppression (a write-back feedback loop settings never faced) and A1-Q13 test infrastructure (keyboard.rs IO is cfg(not(test)) no-ops). The 'breaking migration taxes early hand-authors' fear is mostly moot — existing customizers already hold flat files (keyboard.rs:64-75), so that back-compat is owed regardless.
- **R3b in scope for Axis 1 — full schema redesign now** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Redesign the persisted format to records {name, trigger, optional context, optional platform}, build the parser, the legacy-flat + unknown-name migration, cross-platform import, and context round-trip, then wire the watcher on top.
  - 👍 Design the schema once, up front, so you never ship a format you must later evolve; Axis-1 adopters get the final on-disk format immediately, and it honors the end-goals leaf list.
  - 👎 Converts a low-effort win into a multi-week schema+parser+migration+back-compat project and SLIPS the instant-apply outcome. Decisively: R3b ALONE delivers no per-context capability, because update_custom_trigger is name-keyed and rewrites all same-name bindings (keymap.rs:422-433) — so you would ship a rich format the engine cannot consume until a separate keymap engine change (Axis 2/3) lands. Commits to a context/platform model before Axis-2 modal-modes requirements are known. Migrating users' on-disk files to the rich format is effectively one-way.
- **Watcher now + forward-compatible serde headroom (no context behavior yet)** _(med effort · easy)_ — Ship the watcher over the flat map but change the serde value type now to an untagged form accepting either today's bare 'keys' string or a future record, reserving R3b's shape so Axis 2 is purely additive. Implement no context/platform behavior.
  - 👍 Buys the watcher win and pre-empts a breaking Axis-2 migration for a few hours of serde work, so early file-sharers are already on a forward-compatible format.
  - 👎 Solves a problem Axis 2 is already obligated to solve regardless (every existing customizer already has a flat file on disk, keyboard.rs:64-75), so the headroom is redundant. Reserving the shape requires GUESSING enough of R3b to be correct; guess wrong and you ship a half-baked variant that itself needs migration. Adds parser-ambiguity and test surface (untagged enums are order-sensitive in serde_yaml) to an otherwise trivial flat TryFrom reader, and invites 'might as well implement it' scope-creep toward Option B. The correct time to write a duplex reader is Axis 2, when R3b's shape is known — not now, on a guess.
- **Defer R3b + bind Axis 2 to the flat back-compat (duplex) reader it already owes (RECOMMENDED)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Identical engineering to Option A — watcher + last_applied + tri-state + hold-last-good + echo-suppression over the flat map — but make two things explicit: (1) correct the end-goals doc so R3b is Phase 2; (2) record as a tracked Axis-2 acceptance criterion that R3b MUST ship a duplex deserializer reading the legacy flat map losslessly (A1-Q12). No serde change now. Optionally, IF and only IF cross-OS shared-config import is a first-release promise, pull in the narrow cmdorctrl/platform-modifier slice (R5/A1-Q11) — which is NOT full R3b.
  - 👍 Delivers the same low-effort, reversible watcher win as Option A while ELIMINATING its one real risk (a forgotten Axis-2 reader silently nuking flat files) without paying Option C's now-cost on a guessed shape. The duplex reader is not extra work invented to hedge deferral — it is work Axis 2 already owes the entire existing customer base, since the in-app UI writes flat files today (keyboard.rs:64-75). So deferral carries zero net migration risk once the acceptance criterion is tracked. Lets the rich schema be designed against Axis-2's real modal/context requirements, and against the engine change per-context overrides also require (keymap.rs:422-433).
  - 👎 Relies on Axis 2 honoring the duplex-reader acceptance criterion; if it slips, the keyboard.rs:132-138 None-on-parse path drops flat files on upgrade — so the criterion must be a tracked gate, not a verbal promise. The optional cross-platform slice is a separate product call (A1-Q11) and, if taken, adds a small amount of R5 work to Axis 1. Effort risk still lives in A1-Q6 echo-suppression and A1-Q13 test infra (both schema-independent).

**→ Recommendation: Defer R3b + bind Axis 2 to the flat back-compat (duplex) reader it already owes (RECOMMENDED).** Re-grounding settles the substrate and strengthens the original call. The entire Axis-1 value (live reload of a shareable file with correct revert/unbind) is achievable over today's flat HashMap with ZERO schema change: A1-Q3 (DIFF), A1-Q4 (tri-state), and A1-Q5 (hold-last-good) are CONFIRMED over the flat name-keyed map; the file is already richer than the UI (chords + 'none'); and the writers already round-trip the tri-state. Two facts the brief omitted make the defer ruling near-decisive rather than a hedged close call. First, R3b buys no near-term capability NOT merely because the file is rich enough, but because per-context overrides also require a keymap engine change (update_custom_trigger is name-keyed and rewrites all same-name bindings, keymap.rs:422-433) — so Option B would ship a format with no engine consumer. Second, the lone conceded downside of deferral — a breaking migration of interim hand-authored files — is largely illusory: the in-app UI already writes the flat format to disk today (keyboard.rs:64-75), so Axis 2 owes a flat back-compat reader to the entire existing user base regardless of the watcher. That dissolves Option C's whole reason to exist (it solves a problem Axis 2 must solve anyway, by guessing R3b's shape early) and removes Option B's 'final format now' steelman. The recommended option is therefore Option A's engineering plus two explicit moves: correct the doc (R3b -> Phase 2) and make the duplex flat reader a tracked Axis-2 acceptance criterion (A1-Q12). This converts deferral's residual risk to near-zero without any premature serde commitment. Effort is low, with the honest risk relocated to A1-Q6 echo-suppression and A1-Q13 test infrastructure — both schema-independent, so neither changes the ruling. The decision can even be deferred operationally: the watcher work can start now and the ruling only needs to land before Axis 1 ships, since A1-Q7 confirms no late-registration correctness gap (answers L148/L170).

- _Residual risk:_ Cross-platform (cross-OS) shared-config import is the strongest surviving counter to shipping pure flat. normalized() bakes a literal cmd-/ctrl- modifier and never emits cmdorctrl, with cmdorctrl resolved only at load via OperatingSystem::get() (per readiness doc A1-Q11, keymap.rs:970-993,914-919). So a macOS export of cmd-k imported on Linux is a literal cmd binding Linux rarely emits — a shared file is silently platform-locked. If 'drop in a teammate's config across OSes' is a first-release Axis-1 promise, a NARROW platform-modifier slice (cmdorctrl persistence, a slice of R5/L7) must enter Axis 1 — but that is still far short of the full context/platform record schema R3b, and it is a separate product call. Secondary residual: the no-migration guarantee is only as good as Axis 2 actually shipping the duplex reader; if that acceptance criterion is dropped, the keyboard.rs:132-138 None-on-parse path silently discards every existing flat file on the R3b upgrade. Both are bounded and tracked, not reasons to pull R3b into Axis 1.
- _Dependencies:_ GATES the existence of the schema-dependent questions: per-context round-trip (A1-Q9/Q7), unknown-name migration (A1-Q10), cross-platform import (A1-Q11/Q14), schema back-compat (A1-Q12/Q17) — choosing defer nullifies them for Axis 1. INDEPENDENT of the watcher mechanics, which proceed over the flat map regardless: A1-Q2 (feature-flag gate), A1-Q3 (DIFF reload), A1-Q4 (tri-state), A1-Q5 (hold-last-good), A1-Q6 (echo-suppression), A1-Q8 (event plumbing). A1-Q7 (late registration) is settled benign (answers L148/L170), so it is NOT a hidden effort dependency. The one conditional cross-axis dependency: IF cross-OS shared-config import is a day-one promise, a narrow R5 slice (cmdorctrl persistence, A1-Q11) enters Axis 1 — but full R3b should NOT be resolved 'in scope' until Axis 2's modal/context model is at least sketched, since R3b's record shape and the per-binding engine override it needs (keymap.rs:422-433) are both downstream of that. Hidden assumption made explicit: the recommendation assumes Axis 2 will honor the duplex-reader acceptance criterion (A1-Q12); without that tracked gate the deferral's no-migration claim does not hold.
- _What would settle it:_ Three concrete inputs, in order. (1) DOC FIX (do now, no spike): the DRI corrects end-goals L52-53 to match report section 7 / L117, pinning R3b to Phase 2 — this dissolves the forcing contradiction. (2) ONE PRODUCT CALL: is cross-OS shared-config import a first-release promise? The flat map already handles chords and unbind, so the only real day-one gaps are (a) per-context bindings — which are gated on BOTH R3b AND a keymap engine change (keymap.rs:422-433), so genuinely Axis-2+, and (b) platform-scoped variants in a shared file. If (b) is promised, scope the narrow cmdorctrl/platform-modifier slice (A1-Q11/R5) into Axis 1; if not, ship flat. (3) TRACK THE COVENANT: record 'Axis 2 ships a duplex deserializer that losslessly reads the legacy flat map (A1-Q12)' as a hard acceptance criterion — feasible because legacy is a top-level YAML map distinguishable from a record schema via serde untagged, and built in Axis 2 against the KNOWN shape rather than a guess. No engineering spike on Option C is needed: the duplex reader is owed to existing customers regardless (keyboard.rs:64-75), so it is Axis-2 work to schedule, not Axis-1 work to pre-pay.
- _Red-team added option:_ Defer R3b + bind Axis 2 to the flat back-compat (duplex) reader it already owes the existing user base — no special Axis-1 migration
- _Red-team:_ Re-grounded every cited fact against keyboard.rs, native.rs, lib.rs, keymap.rs, manager.rs, and the settled answers doc. The brief's six code_grounding facts all hold. The recommendation (defer R3b) is correct and survives — but the reasoning is materially rebuilt, so I mark it HARDENED rather than upheld.

ATTACKS THAT LANDED:
(1) The brief's single conceded downside of deferral — "a breaking migration taxes the early adopters who hand-author files during Axis 1" — is OVERSTATED to near-moot. Existing users ALREADY have flat keybindings.yaml files on disk now: the in-app UI writes the flat format today (keyboard.rs:64-75, called from bindings.rs:491-506 and keybindings.rs:600). So Axis 2's R3b must ship flat-file back-compat for the entire existing customizer base whether or not the watcher ever ships. The watcher does not create the back-compat obligation; it adds more (and some hand-authored) files of the IDENTICAL format. Incremental migration risk from deferring is ~zero.
(2) This collapses Option C. Option C buys forward-compat serde headroom now to avoid a migration Axis 2 is already obligated to absorb, paying parser-ambiguity + test cost NOW on a GUESSED record shape, to solve a problem A1-Q12 shows must be solved in Axis 2 anyway with the REAL shape. Dominated.
(3) Option B is weaker than stated. Per-context overrides are gated on BOTH R3b schema AND a keymap engine change: update_custom_trigger is name-keyed and rewrites ALL same-name bindings with no context/binding-id (keymap.rs:422-433; answers L246/L257). R3b-now would ship a rich file the engine cannot consume — a format with no consumer.
(4) I tested an effort-optimism angle (A1-Q7 late registration forcing a re-apply hook) and the answers doc REFUTES it: all 109 editable-binding registrations run once in initialize_app before the launch-time load (lib.rs:1648-1703 vs 2557), zero in render/constructor paths (answers L148/L170). So it does NOT inflate effort; I do not raise it as a cost. I correct my own would-be over-attack here.
(5) Effort risk is real but RELOCATED, not eliminated. The watcher's genuine unknowns are A1-Q6 echo-suppression (in-app writer mutates the same file the watcher watches — a feedback loop settings explicitly never had; manager.rs:327 solved it by load-without-writeback, which keybindings cannot copy) and A1-Q13 test infrastructure (keyboard.rs load/read/write are cfg(not(test)) no-ops). Both schema-independent, so the ruling is unaffected, but "~1-week quick win" should read "low effort, risk concentrated in echo-suppression + test seam."
(6) DEFERABILITY: the brief calls the decision "forced now." Engineering-wise it is not — the watcher is schema-independent and can start immediately. The scope ruling only needs to land before Axis 1 SHIPS, and even then the cost of wrong is bounded by the flat back-compat Axis 2 already owes. Only the doc contradiction needs an immediate fix, a one-line edit (R3b -> Phase 2), not a code blocker.

MINOR CITATION TIGHTENING: the brief's fact #5 cites keybindings.rs:592 for in-app unbind persisting 'none'; :592 sets Trigger::Empty in memory, the 'none' is written at :600. Substance holds.

### A1-Q2 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: A user-product / rollout decision ('Answerable-by: user-product-decision'). The keybindings-initiative product owner sets the policy (defer watcher vs flag: yes; default rollout stage) AFTER A1-Q1 rules on whether the watcher is in Phase 1. The initiative eng-lead, with the settings-platform owner who owns FeatureFlag::SettingsFile and the WarpConfig watcher (native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event), co-own the flag name and the written disabled-path contract (watcher branch + event emission + applied-names seeding all no-op together; launch load unchanged). The applied-names ledger owner (A3-Q14/X8) must commit to honoring the flag in lockstep.

**Decision.** Whether keybindings hot-reload, IF it ships in Phase 1, is gated behind its own runtime FeatureFlag with a clean disabled path that falls back to today's launch-only load (mirroring settings reload gated on FeatureFlag::SettingsFile at native.rs:129) — or ships ungated, or reuses an existing flag — OR whether the live watcher is deferred out of Phase 1 entirely so the question is moot. This decision is downstream of A1-Q1 / the Axis-1 killer question: if R1 (watcher + reset bookkeeping) is not committed to Phase 1, A1-Q2 should not be settled now.

**Stakes.** Hot-reload introduces a brand-new live-mutation path that can rebind or wipe keybindings on every external file save, including a reset path with genuinely different live outcomes (Empty=unbind via set_custom_trigger vs revert-to-default via remove_custom_trigger) and an applied-names ledger that does not exist in the codebase today (grep = 0). If a reset/diff bug or the writer-watcher feedback race (A1-Q6) ships ungated, recovery requires a hotfix client release; a flag instead lets the team dial it down via a prod warp-server experiment / channel stage WITHOUT a client release, and stage the rollout Dogfood->Preview->Stable. The precedent this work copies (settings reload, native.rs:129) deliberately kept that gate. Because the entire watcher is greenfield (no keybindings branch in native.rs:74-134), the cheapest risk reduction of all is to not ship the live watcher in Phase 1 at all. This must be settled before the watcher branch is written because it dictates where the guard goes and how the disabled path is specified — but only after A1-Q1 confirms the watcher is in Phase 1 scope.

_Constrained by:_
- Settings hot-reload is flag-gated AT RUNTIME ONLY: the branch is `if FeatureFlag::SettingsFile.is_enabled() && update_touches_path(update, &user_preferences_toml_file_path())` with NO #[cfg] wrapper, so it always compiles and gates purely at runtime. There is NO keybindings branch anywhere in handle_warp_managed_paths_event. — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:129-133 (handler 74-134)`
- The disabled-path fallback is free and is byte-for-byte today's behavior: keybindings load once at launch via keyboard::load_custom_keybindings(ctx), the sole non-test caller, currently ungated. — `app/src/lib.rs:2557`
- The risky live surface is real and the kill-switch's target does not exist yet: load_custom_keybindings only ever calls set_custom_trigger(Empty)/(Keystrokes) (keyboard.rs:44,47) and NEVER remove_custom_trigger; the distinct revert primitive lives at app.rs:1656 -> update_custom_trigger(None); grep for an applied-names ledger across app/ and crates/ = 0 hits. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57; crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1656; A1-Q4 (code-investigation-answers.md:63-90)`
- CORRECTION to the brief's compile-time claim: a FeatureFlag's cfg(feature) half only controls inclusion in SUPPORTED_FEATURES (features.rs:64); the enum variant is defined unconditionally (warp_features lib.rs:114) and the precedent branch (native.rs:129) carries no #[cfg]. So 'disabled builds don't compile the watcher branch' is false for the pattern copied — cfg-vs-runtime drift risk is low. — `app/src/features.rs:64-65; crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs:114; app/src/user_config/native.rs:129`
- A FeatureFlag is a server-dial / channel kill-switch, not an instant in-process toggle: is_enabled() resolves via FLAG_STATES seeded by per-channel lists (DOGFOOD/PREVIEW/RELEASE_FLAGS) plus prod warp-server experiments ('enabled via 100% experiment on prod warp-server'); only RuntimeFeatureFlags-gated flags (just LocalClaudeCodexChildHarnesses) are live in-session toggles. So a flag avoids a client release for incident recovery — the decisive property — but is not an in-process off-switch. — `crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs:981-995, 933, 983`
- DECISIVE against Option C: SettingsFile is in the `default` Cargo feature bundle, so it is effectively already at Stable / on everywhere — reusing it yields ZERO Dogfood soak and cannot be dialed down for keybindings without killing settings reload for all users. Separately, read_local_setting_value branches the cloud-sync source on this flag, entangling keybindings reload with settings cloud-sync. — `app/Cargo.toml:667 (default bundle starts :499); crates/settings/src/manager.rs:233`
- The disabled-path contract ('confirm the disabled path does not leave a partially-seeded applied-names set') is a direct spec statement in the A1-Q2 how-to-answer, not merely an inference; it ties the flag to the ledger owner (A3-Q14/X8). The brief's fact #4 cited code-inv:227, which is actually the A1-Q8 residual. — `design-readiness A1-Q2 how-to-answer (2026-06-13-...-design-readiness-questions.md:147); A1-Q8 residual (code-investigation-answers.md:227)`

**Options:**

- **Dedicated FeatureFlag::KeybindingsHotReload, default Dogfood, disabled = launch-only (conditional on the watcher shipping in Phase 1)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Mint a new feature flag that gates the new native.rs keybindings watcher branch, its WarpConfigUpdateEvent emission, and the applied-names ledger seeding as one unit. When disabled, behavior is identical to today: launch-only load via lib.rs:2557, no watcher, no ledger. Place the flag in DOGFOOD_FLAGS (not the default bundle) so it is on for WarpDev/dogfood only, then promote Preview->Stable.
  - 👍 Directly mirrors the proven precedent (native.rs:129); since SettingsFile gates the analogous settings reload, omitting a flag is a strict regression in safety. The disabled path costs nothing because 'off' is literally the current shipped behavior (lib.rs:2557 unchanged) and — corrected — needs no #[cfg] on the branch, just one is_enabled() guard mirroring native.rs:129. It buys a server-dial / channel kill-switch that recovers a reset/feedback-race incident WITHOUT a client release (lib.rs:933), and lets the flicker window (A1-Q3), none-vs-absent reconciliation (A1-Q4), and the writer-watcher race (A1-Q6) soak in Dogfood before any user is exposed. add-feature-flag automates the boilerplate.
  - 👎 Carries flag-lifecycle cost: it must later be promoted/removed or it rots. The sharpest failure mode is mis-specifying the disabled path: if the flag gates the watcher emit but not the applied-names seeding (or vice-versa) you get exactly the 'partially-seeded applied-names set' the spec warns against — so the ledger owner (A3-Q14/X8) must honor the flag in lockstep, which is the real (modest) cost, not the flag itself. The flag does not decide reset semantics; it only defers blast radius, so A1-Q3/Q4/Q6 still must be answered before promotion. And the kill-switch is a server-dial/channel lever, not an instant in-process toggle — incident recovery is fast (no client release) but not literally one click in-session unless wired into RuntimeFeatureFlags.
- **Defer the live watcher out of Phase 1 — launch-only load, optional user-initiated reload command (MISSING OPTION)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Do not build the native.rs keybindings watcher branch in Phase 1 at all. Keep the launch-only load (lib.rs:2557) as the only apply path. Optionally add a user-initiated 'Reload keybindings from file' command so users get no-restart reloads on demand without an on-every-save watcher.
  - 👍 The whole stakes case is that the net-new live-mutation path is dangerous (live unbind/revert on every external save, brand-new reset bookkeeping, a ledger that doesn't exist, the A1-Q6 feedback race). The lowest-risk Phase-1 answer is to not ship that path: the disabled path == launch-only == today is already free and correct (lib.rs:2557), so keeping it ships ZERO new risk and defers A1-Q2/Q3/Q4/Q6 wholesale while still delivering the 'hand-editable, shareable file' half of Axis 1. A user-initiated reload command recovers most of the convenience while killing the on-every-save feedback race (reload fires only when the user asks). Reversible and cheap; build the watcher (and then this flag) later with reset semantics fully designed.
  - 👎 Does NOT deliver the 'applies instantly' half of Axis 1's stated end state; a manual command is a UX compromise. The Axis-1 killer question frames the watcher (R1) as THE Phase-1 quick win, so deferring it may empty or re-scope Axis 1's Phase 1 — a product call above A1-Q2, owned by A1-Q1. The moment ANY reload ships (even manual), the tri-state reset semantics (A1-Q4) still must be solved, so a manual-reload variant only partially defers risk; a pure launch-only variant defers it fully but ships the least user value. Pushes, not eliminates, the work.
- **Ship ungated (no flag)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Add the keybindings watcher branch to native.rs and the reset/apply path with no FeatureFlag guard. Hot-reload is always on once merged; the launch load stays as-is and the watcher path is additive.
  - 👍 Cheapest to WRITE: nothing to mint or bridge. Consistency argument: the launch load itself is ungated today (lib.rs:2557), so the watcher that re-runs the same apply logic arguably needn't be gated. Fewest moving parts.
  - 👎 Drops the kill-switch the analogous settings path explicitly keeps (native.rs:129) — the exact adversarial angle of A1-Q2. The ungated surface is the most dangerous part: live Empty-unbind vs remove-to-default on every external save, driven by brand-new reset bookkeeping and a ledger that doesn't exist yet. An ungated reset/feedback-race bug reaches 100% of users with NO server-dial to disable it without a client release — recovery requires a hotfix release. Forecloses staged Dogfood/Preview soak for A1-Q3/Q4/Q6. Reverting an always-on behavior users rely on is costly. NOTE: the brief mis-rated this very-high effort; it is in fact the cheapest to write — the cost is entirely in reversibility and blast radius, not effort.
- **Reuse FeatureFlag::SettingsFile** _(low effort · costly)_ — Guard the keybindings watcher branch with the existing FeatureFlag::SettingsFile, treating 'live config files' as one gated capability covering settings.toml and keybindings.yaml.
  - 👍 No new flag to create, bridge, or clean up; both reloads share the same WarpConfig watcher (native.rs:74-134), so one flag for 'watch live config files' keeps that surface coherent.
  - 👎 DECISIVE: SettingsFile is in the `default` Cargo bundle (app/Cargo.toml:667) — already effectively at Stable / on everywhere — so reusing it gives ZERO Dogfood soak (defeating the flag's only purpose) and forces keybindings reload on for 100% of users immediately. You cannot dial keybindings reload down during an incident without also killing settings reload for everyone, and read_local_setting_value branches cloud-sync source on this flag (manager.rs:233), entangling keybindings reload with settings persistence/cloud-sync. Disentangling later means minting the dedicated flag anyway plus migrating state. Strictly dominated by Option A.

**→ Recommendation: Dedicated FeatureFlag::KeybindingsHotReload, default Dogfood, disabled = launch-only (conditional on the watcher shipping in Phase 1).** Two-step. FIRST settle A1-Q1 / the Axis-1 killer question: if the live watcher (R1) is NOT committed to Phase 1, take the missing 'Defer the live watcher' option — A1-Q2 is then moot and should not be settled now, since the disabled path already exists for free (lib.rs:2557) and deferring sheds A1-Q2/Q3/Q4/Q6 wholesale. IF the watcher IS in Phase 1, gate it behind its own flag. The asymmetry is decisive and survived attack: the downside of the flag is small and bounded (one is_enabled() guard mirroring native.rs:129 — no #[cfg] on the branch, corrected — plus making the ledger owner honor the flag in lockstep), while shipping ungated is unbounded (a reset/feedback-race bug clobbering every user's bindings, recoverable only by a client release because there is no server-dial). 'Off' is byte-for-byte today's behavior, so the disabled-path contract is unusually cheap and safe as long as the watcher branch AND the applied-names seeding no-op together. Reusing SettingsFile (Option C) is now strictly dominated: it is already in the default Cargo bundle, so it offers zero soak and no independent kill-switch, and it welds keybindings reload to settings cloud-sync. Default the new flag to Dogfood (place in DOGFOOD_FLAGS, not the default bundle) so the flicker window (A1-Q3), none-vs-absent reconciliation (A1-Q4), and the writer-watcher race (A1-Q6) bake before Stable.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument is that a feature flag may be safety theater for THIS feature if the team won't actually exercise the kill-switch the way it would for settings. The flag's incident value depends on (a) it being wired into the prod warp-server experiment system (only then is it dial-down-able without a client release — not automatic; add-feature-flag/promote-feature must do this), and (b) the flag genuinely gating the ledger seeding in lockstep, which depends on a SEPARATE decision (A3-Q14/X8, who owns the applied-names ledger) going a compatible way. If the ledger owner doesn't honor the flag, the disabled path leaves a partially-seeded ledger — the precise failure the flag was meant to prevent. And the flag does nothing to make the reset semantics (A1-Q3/Q4) or the feedback race (A1-Q6) correct; it only defers their blast radius. So a reviewer could fairly argue the cheaper, more honest Phase-1 move is the missing option — defer the live watcher — and only introduce the flag when the watcher and its reset bookkeeping are actually designed, rather than minting a flag to babysit a not-yet-correct feature.
- _Dependencies:_ UPSTREAM (newly explicit): gated by A1-Q1 / the Axis-1 killer question — if R1 (watcher + reset bookkeeping) is deferred to Phase 2, A1-Q2 is moot and the missing 'defer the watcher' option is taken by default. Also gated by A1-Q1's schema ruling: the disabled path must still cleanly load today's flat files (A1-Q12 back-compat). WRAPS and is informed by A1-Q3 (diff vs nuke-and-reapply) and A1-Q4 (none-stays-unbind vs absent-reverts): the riskier the reset semantics, the stronger the kill-switch case — but the flag does not answer them. COUPLED to A3-Q14/X8 (who owns the applied-names ledger): the disabled path must not seed a partial ledger, so the ledger owner must honor the flag in lockstep — a hard dependency, not soft. Also wraps A1-Q14 (malformed-reload error banner) and depends on the writer-watcher feedback-race resolution (A1-Q6). Independent of X2's schema-commit decision except via A1-Q1.
- _What would settle it:_ (1) A1-Q1 / killer-question ruling on whether the live watcher (R1) is in Phase 1 — if not, defer the watcher and this question closes. (2) If yes: the settings-platform owner's input on mint-new vs reuse-SettingsFile, plus confirmation that SettingsFile is in the default bundle (it is — app/Cargo.toml:667), which already rules out reuse. (3) Running add-feature-flag to fix the flag name and place it in DOGFOOD_FLAGS, and confirming it is wired into the prod warp-server experiment system so the kill-switch actually avoids a client release. (4) A3-Q14/X8 confirming the ledger owner will gate seeding on the same flag. (5) A Dogfood bake of the enabled path measuring reload-induced binding loss, intra-reload flicker (A1-Q3), and writer-watcher feedback races (A1-Q6) — clean metrics justify promoting toward Stable.
- _Red-team added option:_ Defer the live watcher out of Phase 1 (launch-only load; optional user-initiated reload command); revisit the flag question when the watcher is built
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all five grounding facts against code; the load-bearing ones hold (native.rs:129 settings reload is flag-gated, lib.rs:2557 launch load is the sole ungated path, keyboard.rs:37-57 never calls remove_custom_trigger, applied-names grep = 0). Three corrections and one added option:

1) MISSING OPTION (the big one): the brief never considers NOT building the live watcher in Phase 1. The entire 'stakes' section argues the net-new live-mutation path is dangerous; the lowest-risk Phase-1 answer is to keep launch-only load (which already == the proposed disabled path, lib.rs:2557) and defer the watcher. That makes A1-Q2 moot. Added as 'Defer the live watcher out of Phase 1'. A1-Q2 is downstream of A1-Q1 / the Axis-1 killer question (does R1/the watcher ship in Phase 1 at all?) — so this decision should only be SETTLED once A1-Q1 commits the watcher to Phase 1. Made that precondition explicit.

2) OPTIMISM/dishonesty correction on effort axis: the brief rates Option B (ungated) effort 'very-high'. That is wrong — shipping ungated is the LEAST implementation effort (you simply omit the flag). The brief inflated effort to make B look bad; the real danger lives in reversibility (costly) and blast radius, not effort. Corrected B to effort=low, reversibility=costly.

3) HAND-WAVING correction on the compile-time half of fact #5: the precedent's branch (native.rs:129) has NO #[cfg] guard — it compiles unconditionally and gates purely at runtime via is_enabled(). The cfg(feature='settings_file') at features.rs:64 only controls inclusion in SUPPORTED_FEATURES; the enum variant is defined unconditionally (warp_features lib.rs:114). So 'disabled builds don't compile the watcher branch' is false for the pattern being copied, and the cfg-vs-runtime drift risk in Option A's what_breaks is overstated. This STRENGTHENS Option A: the disabled path is a single is_enabled() guard mirroring native.rs:129, no cfg gymnastics on the branch.

4) Sharpened the 'kill-switch' claim with the actual rollout machinery (lib.rs:981-995, 933, 983): flags resolve via FLAG_STATES seeded by per-channel lists + prod warp-server experiments, so a flag IS a server-dial/channel kill-switch that avoids a CLIENT RELEASE — the decisive incident property the brief asserts holds. But only RuntimeFeatureFlags-gated flags (just LocalClaudeCodexChildHarnesses) are instant in-session toggles, so 'runtime kill-switch' should read 'server-dial / channel kill-switch (no client release)'.

5) Killed Option C harder with a concrete finding: SettingsFile is in the `default` Cargo bundle (app/Cargo.toml:667) = effectively already at Stable / on everywhere. Reusing it gives ZERO Dogfood soak and can't be dialed down for keybindings without killing settings reload for 100% of users; read_local_setting_value (manager.rs:233) branches cloud-sync source on it — confirmed coupling. Option C is now strictly dominated by A.

6) Citation fix: brief's fact #4 cites 'A1-Q2 residual (code-inv:227)'; line 227 is actually the A1-Q8 residual. The 'don't seed a partial applied-names set' constraint is a direct spec statement in the A1-Q2 how-to-answer of the design-readiness doc, re-cited there.

### A1-Q10 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: The warn-vs-silent UX and the import-trust promise are a product call owned by the keybindings product owner (PM): 'must we warn' and 'is import-just-works a committed leaf'. The decision that actually determines whether an alias/registry is needed at all — does Axis-4 Step-D HARD-RENAME existing action names or ADD new ones while retaining old names as aliases — is owned by the Axis-4/modal lead (the first/only renamer). IF hard-rename is chosen, the X11 alias contract (format, single-registry-vs-per-PR-convention, who maintains it) is a cross-team eng decision jointly owned by the Settings-UI eng-lead (file loader + catalog), the Axis-4/modal lead, and the agent-skill owner (name is the catalog/agent key, X4).

**Decision.** When a keybindings.yaml entry names an action the running build does not recognize (typo, older/newer config, or a renamed action), should Warp surface feedback instead of silently no-op'ing, and should it ship a name-alias/migration map so renamed actions keep working? Decompose into two separable sub-decisions: (a) the warn (do we tell the user when an entry was dropped) — cheap, reversible, Phase-1; and (b) the alias (do renamed actions keep working) — which is entirely contingent on whether Axis-4 Step-D hard-renames action names or adds new ones while retaining old names as live aliases.

**Stakes.** Credibility of the two headline Axis-1 promises: 'drop in a teammate's config and it just works' (import) and back-compat across upgrades. Today an unrecognized name silently no-ops with ZERO feedback (keymap.rs:422-433 + keyboard.rs:37-57), so a colleague's config, an older config, or a config written after a rename loses exactly those bindings invisibly, and the failure is blame-shifted onto the user ('my shortcut doesn't work'). The warn is also the minimal CONSISTENCY fix: the loader already warns on a whole-file parse error (keyboard.rs:135) and on an unparsable trigger (keyboard.rs:49-53) but is totally silent on an unknown NAME — closing that third gap is the cheapest credibility win. The alias only matters if action names actually drift: Axis-4 Step-D plans to rename the Vim-prefixed batch (X11/Axis 4-Q15), which would orphan every persisted custom trigger keyed on the old names — UNLESS either an alias ships in Step-D's own build or Step-D is constrained to additive naming. It matters NOW because Axis 1 is the first shippable slice and the import/back-compat leaves are being committed.

_Constrained by:_
- Unknown action names silently no-op: update_custom_trigger(name, trigger) only iterates editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings filtering by b.name == name; if nothing matches, nothing happens — no error, no log, no return value. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433`
- The loader's ONLY warning paths are a whole-file deserialize error (keyboard.rs:135) and an unparsable TRIGGER (Err from UserDefinedKeybinding::try_from); the NAME is passed straight to app.set_custom_trigger with no validation — so an unknown name is the one silent gap. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57 (warn at 49-53; deserialize warn at 135)`
- set_custom_trigger applies an override only to bindings ALREADY registered: there is no persistent by-name override store and register_editable_bindings never re-applies a stored override (custom_trigger defaults None). So whether the override and any validation work at load depends entirely on registration preceding load. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:300,405-419,422-433,661`
- DECISIVE: all editable bindings register inside initialize_app (call lib.rs:1065; keybinding block lib.rs:1648 'Register initial keybindings prior to creating menus') BEFORE launch (lib.rs:1077) runs load_custom_keybindings (lib.rs:2557). Therefore the live-keymap name set is COMPLETE at the single load point — validating an unknown name there is reliable today; the A1-Q7 late-registration false-positive caveat is future/plugin-only, not a live risk. — `app/src/lib.rs:1065,1077,1648,2557; A1-Q7 (answers L148,163,170)`
- A validation index already exists: editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>> (the Vec proves names are deliberately registered more than once). But the public enumerators get_binding_by_name and editable_bindings() both filter by is_enabled(), so they would false-positive on registered-but-DISABLED bindings; correct validation must use the raw keyset (one small new accessor). — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30,378-386,441-447`
- There is no action catalog today (R9 is an Axis-5 deliverable), but none is needed for validation: the live keymap keyset is authoritative AND complete at load (see above). ~400+ inline static-str names exist (451 raw EditableBinding::new call sites incl. some helper/test sites); exact count isn't decisive. — `X4 (questions L51-55); grep EditableBinding::new=451; keymap.rs:30`
- keybindings.yaml is loaded exactly once at startup with NO watcher branch (native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event covers themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings only), so the Phase-1 warn is startup-only — which still covers the headline import/upgrade cases. The surfacing mechanism is proven twice: settings.toml SettingsErrors→banner (init.rs:239/251 → workspace/view.rs:2672) and the tab_config_errors load+collect+emit-error pattern (native.rs:114-127). — `app/src/lib.rs:2557; app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134 (tab errors 114-127); app/src/settings/init.rs:239,251; app/src/workspace/view.rs:2672`
- Option-D data-loss path confirmed: create_file → fs::File::create TRUNCATES (util/file.rs:132-145); the existing write path (read-then-unwrap_or_default, keyboard.rs:71,89,103-122) already clobbers the file to one binding on a parse-failure None. An auto-rewrite-on-load (D) compounds this existing data-loss hazard on hand-edited, version-controlled files. — `app/src/util/file.rs:132-145; app/src/keyboard.rs:71,89,103-122; A1-Q12 (answers L285,293)`

**Options:**

- **A. Silent no-op (status quo / defer)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship Axis 1 unchanged: an unrecognized name continues to do nothing, no warning, no alias. Revisit if complaints arrive.
  - 👍 Zero engineering cost, zero new failure surface in the Phase-1 quick win. The flat-map round-trip is already faithful for the per-name model (A1-Q9), so for users who never rename or import, nothing is broken. A warn path is non-breaking to add later, so deferring forecloses nothing technically.
  - 👎 Directly falsifies 'import just works' and back-compat: a teammate's or older config silently loses exactly the drifted-name bindings, blame shifted onto the user. It is also INCONSISTENT — the loader already warns on file-parse and trigger-parse errors but not unknown names (keyboard.rs:135 vs 49-53 vs silence). And it becomes a guaranteed silent breakage the moment Axis-4 Step-D hard-renames the Vim batch.
- **B0. Log-only unknown-name warning (no banner)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Add an else-branch in the existing load loop: when a file name is not in the keymap's registered-name keyset, emit a log::warn! (mirroring the existing unparsable-trigger warn). No new UI.
  - 👍 The genuinely cheapest honest fix — ~3 lines plus one small keyset accessor. Makes the loader fully symmetric (it already log-warns on the other two failure classes), gives support/debugging a signal immediately, ships zero new UI surface, and is the safe floor if banner plumbing turns out non-trivial. Reliable today because the name set is complete at load (registration precedes load).
  - 👎 Users do not tail logs, so it does NOT satisfy the user-facing 'import just works' promise — a silently-dropped binding stays invisible to the person who dropped in the config. It is observability, not trust. Insufficient alone if import-trust is a committed Axis-1 leaf.
- **B. Warn banner (surface unknown names, no alias map)** _(low effort · easy)_ — At startup load, diff file names against the keymap's raw editable_bindings_by_name keyset; for any unknown name, surface a user-facing banner via the proven SettingsErrors→banner / tab_config_errors event path. No automatic rename remapping.
  - 👍 Converts a silent partial failure into an actionable, trustworthy signal — the single biggest credibility win for import — for low cost: the surfacing mechanism is proven twice (settings + tab configs), the validation source already exists and is COMPLETE at the single load point (A1-Q7: registration precedes load, so NO false-positive risk today, correcting the brief's caveat), and it needs no Axis-5 catalog. Honest about drift without pretending to auto-heal it.
  - 👎 Warns but does not fix: a renamed action still loses its binding and the user must know the new name to repair it. For Axis-4 Step-D's batch rename this produces a banner full of orphaned names instead of a seamless migration. Validation must use the raw registered-name keyset, not get_binding_by_name/editable_bindings() (both filter is_enabled and would nag about valid-but-disabled bindings). Startup-only: warning on a LIVE edit would need a new watcher branch keybindings.yaml does not have today (deferrable).
- **C. Warn + name-alias/migration registry (the X11 shared contract)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Option B plus a single old-name→new-name alias table consulted at load so known renames auto-resolve; unknown-and-unaliased names warn. The table is the X11 shared registry consulted by the file loader, catalog builder, and Axis-4 modal migration. RECOMMENDED VARIANT: ship B now; ratify the X11 contract now; ship the alias CODE with the first rename (Step-D), not as empty Phase-1 code.
  - 👍 The only option that delivers 'a renamed action keeps working,' the real user promise. Because the override path is purely name-keyed and rewrites all N registrations (A1-Q9), an alias is an unambiguous single-name→single-name map — cheap to apply correctly. Agreeing the contract (format/owner) BEFORE Step-D is what stops one team unilaterally orphaning every persisted trigger; the cost of retrofitting cross-team agreement AFTER names have shipped renamed is far higher than reserving the contract now.
  - 👎 Introduces a permanent ownership/lifecycle tax: someone must maintain the registry on every rename forever, and the format becomes a published contract (X11) costly to change — drop an alias later and you re-break the configs it healed. Cross-team ownership can stall on coordination. The brief's 'seed it lazily, empty' bundles the SEAM into Phase 1: an empty alias table is dead code plus a prematurely-frozen format with nothing to test it against — a skeptical reviewer rejects that. The alias code only needs to exist in the build that renames; Phase 1 should ship B, not an empty registry.
- **D. Catalog-backed validation + alias + auto-rewrite-on-load** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Make the R9 catalog (Axis 5) the authoritative validation source and, on an aliased rename, rewrite keybindings.yaml in place to the new name so the file self-heals.
  - 👍 Most complete and durable: the file converges to current names, future loads need no alias lookup, exports stay clean, and catalog-backed validation also flags names that exist but are unreachable — strongest 'truthful' guarantee with a single source shared with the agent skill.
  - 👎 Heaviest dependency and collision load, and the catalog buys little here because the live keymap keyset already validates correctly and completely at load. Writing the file on load collides with the confirmed truncating-write data-loss path (create_file→File::create truncates, util/file.rs:132-145; existing write already clobbers to one binding on a None-parse, A1-Q12) and the back-compat deserialize hazard; rewriting hand-edited, version-controlled files (comments/ordering) is one-way and trust-sensitive. Pulls the not-yet-built Axis-5 catalog onto the Axis-1 critical path. Over-scoped for Phase 1.

**→ Recommendation: B (warn banner) ships in Phase 1; ratify the X11 alias contract now; ship alias CODE with Axis-4 Step-D (deferred-C). B0 is the guaranteed cheap floor if banner plumbing slips..** Ship the warn banner (B) in Phase 1: it is essentially free given two proven precedents (settings.toml SettingsErrors→banner and the tab_config_errors load-collect-emit pattern, native.rs:114-127), it is the minimal CONSISTENCY fix for an already-asymmetric loader, and — correcting the brief — it carries NO false-positive risk today because all editable bindings register in initialize_app (lib.rs:1648) before load_custom_keybindings runs in launch (lib.rs:2557), so the live keymap keyset is complete and reliable at the load point (A1-Q7). Validate against the raw editable_bindings_by_name keyset, not the is_enabled-filtered enumerators. DECOUPLE the alias from Phase-1 code: the brief is right that an alias is non-optional IF Axis-4 Step-D hard-renames, and that the cross-team CONTRACT (X11 format/ownership) should be agreed before Step-D — but it overstates that the alias SEAM must physically ship in Phase 1. The alias code only has to exist in the build that performs the first rename; an empty 'lazily seeded' registry in Phase 1 is dead code plus a prematurely-frozen published format with nothing to test against. So: ratify the contract now, ship the code with Step-D. Silent (A) is disqualified the moment import/back-compat is a committed leaf — it is invisible data loss blamed on the user. B0 is the safe floor but does not meet the user-facing import-trust promise (users don't read logs). Auto-rewrite (D) is the right long-term shape but is deferred: the catalog buys nothing for validation here (live keymap already validates completely), and rewriting the file collides head-on with the confirmed truncating-write clobber (util/file.rs:132-145 + A1-Q12). Net: B now, alias-contract-now / alias-code-at-Step-D — cheap user-facing win locked, costly/irreversible commitment deferred to the moment it is actually forced.

- _Residual risk:_ If Axis-4 Step-D can be designed ADDITIVELY — introduce new modal action names while keeping the old names registered as live aliases declared in the SAME PR — then no central cross-team registry is ever needed: each rename self-heals locally in its own PR, and the X11 'shared registry' becomes over-engineering that imposes a permanent cross-team maintenance tax to solve a problem a one-line naming convention ('never delete a shipped action name; keep it as an alias') already solves. In that world the complete answer is just B plus that convention, and even ratifying the X11 contract now is premature ceremony. A skeptical staff engineer would want the rename-vs-additive question answered before funding any registry. Secondary residual: B is startup-only because keybindings.yaml has no watcher (native.rs:74-134); if users expect live re-validation on edit, that is a separate increment.
- _Dependencies:_ B reuses the SettingsErrors→banner path (A1-Q14: init.rs:239/251 → workspace/view.rs:2672) and the tab_config_errors load-collect-emit pattern (native.rs:114-127); it needs the A1-Q8 plumbing, which already exists (load_custom_keybindings takes &mut AppContext at lib.rs:2557). Validation source is the raw editable_bindings_by_name keyset (keymap.rs:30), reliable at load because registration precedes load (A1-Q7: lib.rs:1065 before 1077). The alias is the Axis-1 facet of X11 and must be decided with it; its first and only consumer is Axis-4 Step-D — so the alias CODE ships with Step-D, only the CONTRACT is reserved now. D is gated on Axis-5 R9 (X4) AND A1-Q6 (write-back loop) AND A1-Q12 (truncating clobber, confirmed via create_file→File::create, util/file.rs:132-145). Note: keybindings.yaml has NO watcher branch today (native.rs:74-134), so the Phase-1 warn is startup-only; hot-reload warn is a separable, deferrable increment that would add a watcher branch. Loosely gated by A1-Q1 (R3b scope) — warn works over today's flat map regardless.
- _What would settle it:_ (1) DECISIVE: does Axis-4 Step-D HARD-RENAME action names or ADD new names while keeping old ones as live aliases? Confirm by reading the Step-D plan (actions.rs:50-294, the Vim-prefixed batch). Hard-rename → an alias must ship in Step-D's build and the X11 contract should be agreed first (the recommendation). Additive → B plus a 'never delete a name' convention is the complete answer; no registry. (2) Is 'drop in a teammate's config and it just works' / back-compat a committed Axis-1 leaf? If yes, silent (A) is disqualified and B is the floor; if names are declared a frozen public API, B0 may even suffice. (3) Measurement: walk git history of EditableBinding::new literal names over the last N releases to quantify actual drift — near-zero drift weakens even the warn case toward B0/A; meaningful drift confirms B and (if hard-rename) the alias.
- _Red-team added option:_ B0. Log-only unknown-name warning (symmetric with the loader's existing unparsable-trigger log warn; no new UI)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code anchor; the brief's core grounding holds but three things needed correction and one decisive fact was missing. (1) MISSING OPTION: the brief jumps from silent (A) straight to a user-facing banner (B), skipping the genuinely cheapest honest fix — a ~3-line log::warn! that makes the loader symmetric with its EXISTING unparsable-trigger log warn (keyboard.rs:49-53). Added as B0. (2) OPTIMISM, in B's favor: the brief's B/C 'false-positive from late registration (A1-Q7)' caveat is OVERSTATED for today's code. I confirmed all editable bindings register inside initialize_app (lib.rs:1065, keybinding block lib.rs:1648) BEFORE launch's load_custom_keybindings (lib.rs:1077→2557), so the live-keymap name set is COMPLETE at the single load point — validating there is reliable today; the caveat is future/plugin-only. This makes B cheaper and safer than the brief states. (3) The validation index already EXISTS — editable_bindings_by_name (keymap.rs:30) — but the public enumerators (get_binding_by_name keymap.rs:378-386, editable_bindings() keymap.rs:441-447) filter by is_enabled() and would false-positive on registered-but-disabled bindings; validate against the raw keyset (one tiny new accessor).
