If Axis 3 is not in Phase 2, publish only the editor/terminal/AI cross-view subset and defer the Vim-mode vocabulary (or defer the whole vocabulary per option D). 2) Confirm the per-binding context-aware override path (the keymap.rs change beyond name-only update_custom_trigger, A2-Q11) is in scope; the vocabulary is inert without it. 3) Decide alias-indirection vs bare-allowlist, and if alias, design the compile-time check binding aliases to live flag constants so the table cannot silently skew. 4) Finish the partition (note: under minimal-first you need NOT exhaustively classify all ~454 ui_name flags; bless a handful, reject the rest by default). 5) Naming-convention RFC resolving duplicates (PaneGroup_PaneDragging vs Workspace_PaneDragging), casing/prefix, and mode-vs-flag tagging, reviewed by the keymap owner. 6) Dogfood/user-research signal on which scopes users actually request, to size the first published set and prioritize promotions. 7) A throwaway validator prototype confirming intern+reject is cheap given &'static str storage (leak on accept, reject otherwise), and that the back-compat deserializer (A1-Q12) preserves a legacy flat keybindings file.
- _Red-team added option:_ Curated public alias layer (indirection table) decoupling published names from internal Rust names
- _Red-team:_ Direction upheld and strengthened; recommendation hardened with a better option and an exposed hidden dependency. (1) ui_name() coupling is LARGER than stated: `grep fn ui_name` returns 456 (incl. trait decl view/mod.rs:60 + doc example :52, so ~454 real impls), not 383; each auto-leaks via ctx.set.insert(Self::ui_name()) at view/mod.rs:116. Understatement strengthens the anti-raw-soup case. (2) Overstatement corrected: the a/b/c 'test junk' literals live only in context_tests.rs and never enter the runtime namespace; the ~60 inconsistent PRODUCT literals are the real hygiene problem. (3) Grounding fact #5 is under-claimed in the brief's favor: there ARE 12 real ne! negatives in product (terminal/view/init.rs), every one ANDed with id!(\"Terminal\") — the positive-gate discipline is real and even covers negation, the single strongest argument against exposing raw `!`/`!=` to config authors. (4) DECISIVE MISSED FACT: the entire override write path is NAME-ONLY. Confirmed set_custom_trigger(name, trigger) (app.rs:1637) and update_custom_trigger(name,...) (keymap.rs:422-433) carry no context arg and rewrite ALL same-name bindings; there is no path to create a context-scoped binding or target one same-name binding. The flagship per-context forks (Vim enter/backspace/tab) are FixedBindings (19 sites per A2-Q13) the override path never touches. So the vocabulary this decision defines is INERT until two siblings land: a per-binding context-aware override path (A2-Q11) and Axis 3 Fixed-remappability (A2-Q13). The brief sells A2-Q1 as foundational (true) but hides that it ships zero user value alone. (5) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY exposed: the recommendation's 'Vim modes in the day-one set' silently assumes Axis 3 lands; without it a published Vim-mode vocabulary is a frozen API users cannot exercise. (6) MISSING OPTION added: a curated public ALIAS layer (indirection table) decouples published names from internal Rust names, defusing the one-way trap/costly-reversibility the bare-allowlist admits it still carries, and collapses the duplicate-concept problem (PaneGroup_PaneDragging vs Workspace_PaneDragging) to one public alias. (7) DEFERABILITY re-priced: option D loses far less than 'kills the headline value prop' because that value is already blocked on Axis 3 + the missing override path. Synthesis adopted: separate DESIGN (partition/RFC now — cheap, prevents the trap) from PUBLISH/FREEZE (incur the one-way commitment only when a consuming override path exists). Also surfaced the A1-Q12 silent-reset back-compat hazard the brief omitted.

### A2-Q2 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team. The engine tech lead (owner of crates/warpui_core/keymap) decides the engine answer — (name x context). The config-schema owner (app/src/keyboard.rs load/write path + user_config) co-signs and OWNS the new pieces: the platform? load-filter and the write-preservation of non-host records. The R5 / cross-platform-foundation owner is explicitly OFF the critical path for file portability — R5 owns only the in-app cross-platform EDITOR (showing/editing the other OS's binding). Product owns the gating input: whether a single per-OS-divergent keybindings.yaml synced across machines is in scope for release 1.

**Decision.** Split the question into the two layers the brief merged. (1) At the ENGINE layer, a context-scoped override is identified by (name x context); platform can never be an in-process disambiguator because every per-platform path — FixedBinding::new_per_platform AND the override-relevant editable builders — collapses to the host keystroke at registration, and update_custom_trigger filters by name alone over host bindings. Decide this now. (2) At the FILE layer, is platform? (a) absent/fully deferred to R5, (b) part of an engine-honored (name x context x platform) identity, or (c) a RESERVED optional load-FILTER selector (host-match-or-skip) defined now but wired only on a product greenlight for synced cross-OS files? Co-committed with the A2-Q3 schema shape (record list with optional fields).

**Stakes.** A2-Q3 forces a one-time fork from today's flat name->trigger map to a scoped-override record; platform rides the same record and the same migration, so its treatment must be co-committed. Both error directions are costly: omit any platform provision and a later need re-opens the migration; make platform an engine-honored identity dimension and you ship config the host-only engine cannot exercise. The brief's framing hid a conflation that changes the cost calculus: making a synced keybindings.yaml portable across OSes does NOT require the R5 engine refactor (only an in-app cross-platform EDITOR does, per L7/report:132,146,231); a file-level load filter suffices because each build already applies only host-resolved keystrokes. So the real lever is the FILE/loader, not the engine, and the decision pivots on a product call about whether synced cross-OS files are in scope for release 1.

_Constrained by:_
- Both the fixed AND the override-relevant editable per-platform paths collapse to host-only at REGISTRATION. FixedBinding::new_per_platform picks the host keystroke via OperatingSystem::get().is_mac(); the EditableBinding builders with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding no-op on the non-matching OS. update_custom_trigger only mutates editable bindings, so the override path is host-only too — platform can never be an in-process disambiguator. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:502-507; :688-713; :422-433`
- Per-platform divergences are NOT uniform modifier swaps: genuinely different keys (cmd-left vs home; cmd-g vs f3; cmd-shift-G vs shift-f3), modifier swaps (alt-left vs ctrl-left), AND mac-only bindings with no linux/windows counterpart (ctrl-a, commented 'Mac-only to not conflict with SelectAll on Linux'). An unresolved CmdOrCtrl modifier cannot represent the key-level or the absence cases; only R5's full {mac, other} pair can. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:304-386 (esp. :362-363, :370-371); app/src/code/editor/find/view.rs:115-126`
- User file is a flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>; PersistedTrigger is a bare host-resolved keystroke string with no platform field and no context field; TryFrom yields only Keystrokes/Removed. There is nowhere to put platform or context today. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:169, :174, :189-213`
- update_custom_trigger filters solely by b.name == name (reads neither context_predicate nor platform) and clobbers ALL same-name host bindings across editable_bindings and editable_custom_action_bindings (the loops have no break). The cross-platform clobber the question fears cannot occur in-process; it is only a hazard for a file shared across machines — a hazard that already exists today with host-resolved strings. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433; app/src/keyboard.rs:169`
- ContextPredicate is an enum with no FromStr/Display/serde (derives only Debug/Clone/Eq/PartialEq). R5 (platform-in-data-model) is listed as OPTIONAL in Phase 2 and deferred behind cheaper prerequisites, and the report scopes it explicitly to 'a cross-platform settings UI / binding editor' (L7) — an in-app editor, NOT cross-machine file portability. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18; report .understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:132,146,231,258,270`
- DECISIVE, brief missed: a synced keybindings.yaml can be made portable across macOS/Linux WITHOUT R5 — by adding an optional platform field to the FILE and applying a host-match-or-skip filter at LOAD, since the engine already only ever applies host-resolved keystrokes (the non-host records are dropped at the serde boundary and never reach update_custom_trigger). R5 is needed only to SHOW/EDIT the other platform's binding inside one build (the editor). The brief's 'portability must be solved via R5' conflates file portability with the in-app cross-platform editor. — `derived: crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:502-507,:688-713 (host-only apply) + app/src/keyboard.rs:189-213 (load path) + report L7 (:132)`

**Options:**

- **Context-only key; platform fully deferred to R5 (record list, generic optional fields) [brief's original recommendation]** _(low effort · easy)_ — Engine override identity = (name x context). File is a record list with generic optional fields; platform is neither a key nor a defined field now, declared 'owned by R5' and addable later as a generic additive optional field.
  - 👍 Smallest surface. The engine is host-only (keymap.rs:502-507,688-713) so platform-in-key buys zero engine behavior, and the only foot-gun (host-resolved keystroke in a synced file) already exists today (keyboard.rs:169), so it is no regression. A record-list-with-optional-fields keeps a later additive platform? serde-compatible without a version bump.
  - 👎 Falsely couples portability to R5 ('platform owned by R5') when a load-filter solves a portable synced file without R5. Risks a second migration if platform's filter semantics are not at least reserved now. The brief's proposed round-trip test (old-file / new-reader) checks the SAFE direction; the dangerous direction (new-file with platform tags / old reader that ignores them, then applies non-host entries) is untested.
- **Full (name x context x platform) as engine-honored identity [Option B]** _(med effort · costly)_ — Platform is part of the engine override identity; a synced file holds platform: mac and platform: linux entries for the same name x context and the ENGINE is expected to honor/store both.
  - 👍 Aligns the file with the eventual R5 shape, one migration, directly answers 'decide the full tuple up front.'
  - 👎 The engine cannot honor it: only the host binding exists in a build (keymap.rs:502-507,688-713), so a non-host entry has no target binding and would be dead config with no catalog to validate against (the A2-Q1 unversioned-coupling failure mode). Note its failure is the load-everything-then-stash IMPLEMENTATION, not the schema itself — the load-FILTER variant (Option D) is viable.
- **Platform-as-value: pull R5's unresolved CmdOrCtrl modifier into Axis 2 [Option C]** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Keep (name x context); make the persisted keystroke value platform-neutral via an unresolved CmdOrCtrl modifier, resolved at match/display via OperatingSystem::get(). No platform field.
  - 👍 Attacks the foot-gun at the value layer, matches R5's recommended value shape (report:146), and a single override {name, context, cmd_or_ctrl-shift-a} works on every machine with no platform field.
  - 👎 Incomplete: sampled divergences include genuinely different keys (cmd-left vs home, cmd-g vs f3) and mac-only-absent bindings (ctrl-a) that a CmdOrCtrl modifier cannot represent — you still need the full {mac,other} pair. Pulls a broad-blast-radius R5 refactor (Keystroke::parse/normalized/displayed + every display surface) into Axis 2, coupling its ship date to a deferred refactor.
- **Context-only ENGINE key + platform as a reserved, DEFINED optional LOAD-FILTER field in the file (host-match-or-skip, write-preserving); R5 off the portability path [RECOMMENDED, added]** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Engine override identity = (name x context), decided now. Schema = record list co-committed with A2-Q3. platform? is DEFINED now as an optional file field with load-filter semantics: at load, keep records where platform is absent or == host, skip the rest; on write, PRESERVE non-host records. Filter wired only when product greenlights synced cross-OS files; until then platform? is reserved/unemitted. R5 stays deferred and is needed only for an in-app cross-platform EDITOR, not for file portability.
  - 👍 Same near-zero cost as Option A today (reserve a field) but removes A's false coupling of portability to R5 and forecloses any future migration by defining the field's semantics now. Cleanly answers the literal question: engine identity is (name x context); the FILE may carry platform as a load-time selector, never an engine disambiguator — so it is neither dead config (it is deterministically filtered at the serde boundary, not stashed) nor an engine change. Solves portability (the 'yes' branch of the product question) at LOW cost without R5. The platform vocabulary is tiny (mac vs linux_and_windows, matching the engine's actual binary granularity at keymap.rs:488-492), unlike the context vocabulary.
  - 👎 Forward-compat hazard: a newer client that emits platform tags read by an older client that ignores platform makes the old client apply non-host entries (the cross-platform clobber, realized). So the ignore-non-host filter must ship in all supported clients BEFORE any client writes platform tags — a rollout-ordering constraint. Needs write-preservation so a single-OS settings-UI save does not drop the other OS's entries (interacts with G5 import/export and hot-reload write-back). If product never wants synced cross-OS files, the reserved field is unused surface (but costs nothing).

**→ Recommendation: Context-only ENGINE key + platform as a reserved, DEFINED optional LOAD-FILTER field in the file (host-match-or-skip, write-preserving); R5 off the portability path [RECOMMENDED, added].** The literal question — is the override (name x context) or (name x context x platform)? — splits into two layers the brief merged. (1) ENGINE/in-process identity is unambiguously (name x context): every per-platform path, including the override-relevant editable builders (keymap.rs:688-713) and FixedBinding (502-507), collapses to the host keystroke at registration, and update_custom_trigger (422-433) only ever sees host bindings, so platform can never be an in-process disambiguator and the clobber the question fears cannot occur in a single build. Decide this now; it is bulletproof. (2) The FILE record MAY carry platform as an OPTIONAL load-filter selector (host-match-or-skip), the cheap and correct way to make a synced keybindings.yaml portable across machines — and crucially this does NOT require R5. The brief's one claim that does not survive: 'if product wants synced cross-OS files, portability must be solved via R5.' R5 (unresolved CmdOrCtrl or {mac,other} Trigger) is required only to SHOW/EDIT the other platform's binding inside one build (an in-app cross-platform editor, L7/report:132,146,231), not to make the file portable. Option B fails only because it assumes the engine must honor non-host entries (load-everything-then-stash); the load-FILTER drops them deterministically at the serde boundary, so it is neither dead config nor an engine change. Option C is incomplete (cannot represent cmd-left-vs-home or mac-only bindings) and expensive. So decide (name x context) for the engine and the record-list schema now (co-commit A2-Q3); DEFINE platform? as a reserved optional load-filter field now so there is no second migration; wire the filter and write-preservation only when product greenlights synced cross-OS files, shipping the ignore-non-host behavior before any client emits tags. This decision blocks nothing in Phase 1 (all of Axis 2 is Phase 2) and must be ratified at the A2-Q3 schema kickoff.

- _Residual risk:_ Defining platform? now, even unwired, creates a forward-compat trap: if any client ships that WRITES platform-tagged entries before every supported client understands the host-match-or-skip filter, the older client silently applies non-host bindings — the exact cross-platform clobber, now realized via the file. A disciplined team avoids this by shipping the ignore-non-host load behavior first and gating tag emission behind a version/capability check, but if that ordering slips, the recommended option is strictly worse than the do-nothing-A stance. Equivalently: if product firmly rules synced cross-OS files out of scope forever, the reserved field and its filter spec are wasted surface and plain Option A (defer entirely) is simpler. The decision therefore still pivots on the product call, which remains the gating unknown.
- _Dependencies:_ Co-decide with A2-Q3 (record list with optional fields) — the 'no second migration' guarantee and the platform? field both ride that shape. NEW dependency the brief missed: the file WRITE path (settings-UI save / hot-reload write-back; interacts with G5 import/export) must PRESERVE non-host records, or a single-OS save clobbers the other OS's entries. Needs a small stable platform vocabulary (mac vs linux_and_windows, matching the engine's binary granularity at keymap.rs:488-492) — interacts with A2-Q1's vocabulary work but is far smaller than the context vocabulary. Feeds A2-Q4 (target relation; platform never enters the engine key tuple). R5 is deferred and is NOT a dependency of file portability — only of an in-app cross-platform editor. Cross-refs the A1 hot-reload applied-names ledger, which keys on the same override identity (name x context). Blocks NOTHING in Phase 1; must be settled before A2-Q3 implementation begins in Phase 2.
- _What would settle it:_ (1) Product call — is a single keybindings.yaml synced across macOS+Linux, binding differently per OS, in scope for release 1? Yes -> wire the load-filter (NOT R5). No -> reserve the field per the recommendation but leave it unwired, behaving as Option A. (2) Forward-compat spike (the test the brief's round-trip omitted, and the dangerous direction): write a platform-tagged file with a NEW client, load it with an OLD client that ignores platform, and confirm whether the old client wrongly applies the non-host entry — this proves the rollout-ordering constraint (ship the ignore-non-host filter before emitting tags). (3) Write-preservation spike: on a linux build, load a file containing mac entries, change one linux binding via the settings UI, save, and confirm the mac entries survive the round-trip. (4) Divergence tally (mostly run): the sample shows key-level (cmd-left vs home, cmd-g vs f3) and mac-only-absent (ctrl-a) cases, proving a value-only CmdOrCtrl is insufficient and confirming R5's full {mac,other} pair is the only complete value representation — and that it is needed only for the in-app editor, not for file portability.
- _Red-team added option:_ Platform as a reserved, defined optional LOAD-FILTER field in the file (host-match-or-skip, write-preserving); engine stays host-only; R5 off the portability critical path
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all five cited facts in the live code — all hold. Strengthened fact 1: the brief cited only the FixedBinding host-collapse (keymap.rs:502-507), but the override path runs through editable bindings; confirmed the EDITABLE per-platform builders with_mac_key_binding/with_linux_or_windows_key_binding ALSO collapse to host-only at registration (keymap.rs:688-713, no-op on the non-matching OS), so the override-relevant path is host-only too — the engine answer (name x context) is bulletproof. Strengthened fact 2: divergences include not just modifier swaps and different keys but mac-only bindings with NO linux counterpart (actions.rs:362-363 ctrl-a 'Mac-only to not conflict with SelectAll on Linux'), which a flat host-resolved string genuinely cannot represent. CAUGHT THE LOAD-BEARING ERROR: the brief conflates two requirements — an in-app cross-platform EDITOR (which L7/R5 is scoped to, report:132,146,231) versus a synced keybindings.yaml that is portable across machines. The latter does NOT need R5: since every build already applies only host-resolved keystrokes, a synced file is made portable by adding an optional platform field to the FILE plus a host-match-or-skip filter at LOAD — the non-host records never reach the engine, so they are not 'dead config' (Option B's stated failure is an implementation choice — load-everything-then-stash — not a property of the schema). Added that as the missing option and as the recommendation. Also corrected: R5 is listed as optional/deferred (report:258,270), not strictly Phase 3. Established deferability: A2-Q2 blocks nothing in Phase 1 (all of Axis 2 is Phase 2); it must be ratified at A2-Q3 schema kickoff. Surfaced two dependencies the brief missed: the file WRITE path must preserve non-host records (else a single-OS save clobbers the other OS), and the dangerous serde direction is new-file/old-reader (forward-compat), not the old-file/new-reader round-trip the brief proposed to test.

### A2-Q3 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: The keybindings/settings Product owner (the file format is a user-facing contract; A2-Q3 is tagged user-product-decision), co-signed by the warpui_core keymap engine tech lead (schema is coupled to the ContextPredicate parser + the interning a/b/c choice). Because the record's key tuple includes platform (A2-Q2), the owner must align with whoever owns the cross-platform foundation before freezing it. The settings PM is a required co-signer on whether the file or the in-app editor (A2-Q18) is the primary authoring surface, since that determines how much the name-as-unique-key ergonomic loss (and the composite-key option's authoring ugliness) actually matters.

**Decision.** Whether the on-disk keybindings.yaml schema keeps the bare action NAME as its key (today's HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>, which de-dupes two overrides of one action) or moves to a representation whose key is (name x context x platform) so multiple scoped overrides of the same action coexist. The realistic shapes are: keep name-as-key and defer scoping; a Vec of {name, trigger, context?, platform?} records; a flat map with composite/encoded keys (name+context in the key string, value still String); a name-keyed nested map HashMap<String, Vec<ScopedTrigger>>; or a hybrid (legacy flat map + a separate scoped section). The container being a HashMap is not itself the blocker — using the bare action name as the key is.

**Stakes.** Foundational, persisted, near-one-way: the format lives in every user's keybindings.yaml. With the bare action name as key, two overrides of the same action in two contexts (e.g. accept_suggestion in VimNormalMode vs default) collide at the serde layer before any engine logic runs (the second clobbers the first). Picking name-as-key now forecloses scoping and forces a second breaking file-format migration when scoping ships (and a third if platform, A2-Q2, is bolted on later). Any context-bearing shape commits to net-new FromStr+Display for ContextPredicate and an interning DECISION that do not exist in the crate today. Crucially this decision does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis-1 hot-reload rides on last_applied bookkeeping and needs no schema fork), so its true deadline is 'before Phase 2 ships,' leaving room for a prototype. The migration AND forward-compat contracts (old client reading a newer file) for existing flat files must both be settled before Phase 2.

_Constrained by:_
- Today's schema keys on the bare action name and the value is a bare String: CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>) with PersistedTrigger(String) — no context/platform field; the string is only space-joined keystrokes or the 'none' removal sentinel. Two entries with the same name key collide; serde keeps the last. The blocker is name-as-key, not the HashMap container. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:167-187`
- update_custom_trigger(name, trigger) filters editable_custom_action_bindings AND editable_bindings purely by `b.name == name`, writes the SAME custom_trigger to every match, takes no context/binding-id, and never reads context_predicate — so the name-keyed override path cannot target or hold one scope of a multi-context name. One name already fans out to N bindings via editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30,422-433`
- The engine ALREADY stores scoping per-binding: each EditableBinding carries its own context_predicate (keymap.rs:297) and custom_trigger (keymap.rs:300), and as_lens lets the custom trigger override the default (keymap.rs:741-747). So per-binding scoped storage exists today; what is missing is only (a) a schema field and (b) a context-aware targeting API — the engine refactor is smaller than a green-field framing implies. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:297,300,741-747; answers doc:483-504`
- ContextPredicate derive is exactly #[derive(Debug, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)] — NO Serialize/Deserialize, NO Display/ToString, NO FromStr (repo-wide grep empty); atoms are &'static str (:11-13); eval keys on &'static str (:100-120). A net-new parser + serializer + an interning decision are mandatory for ANY context-bearing shape. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18,100-120`
- The '~965 macro sites' interning cost is OVERSTATED as a blanket cost. Per the answers doc the 965 total (id!946+eq!5+ne!12+always!2) is an explicit UPPER BOUND that 'may include unrelated id! macros,' and only interning option (b) Cow/IDs touches them; option (a) leak/intern is 'near-zero macro change' with in-repo precedent (settings/ai.rs:183-194 Box::leak, warp_server_auth user_uid.rs:26 interner). Interning is a required DECISION shared by every context-bearing shape, not a mandatory 965-site refactor. — `docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-code-investigation-answers.md:421,430,434`
- The whole file loads in one serde_yaml::from_reader that returns None on ANY error, silently dropping ALL bindings; load then loops over the map calling set_custom_trigger per entry. Consequence the brief missed (FORWARD-COMPAT): a list/nested-map file read by an OLDER client (which expects a top-level map) fails the whole deserialize and drops every binding; a composite-key flat map stays a top-level map, so an old client simply no-ops on unknown keys (update_custom_trigger matches no binding) — graceful per-key degradation, not a file-wide drop. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:128-139,37-57,169`
- Same-name multi-context is a genuine, reachable runtime state (contexts are merged per-view via context_chain at app.rs:1846-1864 / core/mod.rs:252) but NO test exercises it: test_custom_triggers uses two distinct names ('first','second'). — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap_tests.rs:265-269; answers doc:614,629`

**Options:**

- **Keep name-as-key flat map, defer scoping** _(low effort · easy)_ — Leave HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> as-is; no context field in the file. Per-context scoping is dropped from Phase 2 (or limited to what the engine exposes without a schema change). Equivalent to deferring the A2-Q3 fork itself, since it does not block Phase 1.
  - 👍 Zero migration risk, zero new code, perfect forward/backward compat: the format already ships, hot-reloads, round-trips. Dodges the FromStr/Display work and the interning decision entirely. If the actual Phase-2 leaf stories (A2-Q5) are 'rebind an existing global action' rather than 'rebind per context,' the map is genuinely sufficient and the whole fork is premature. Because A2-Q3 does not block Phase 1, deferring the decision to Phase-2 planning is free and buys time for the confirming prototype — this is the correct default until scope is proven.
  - 👎 Forecloses Axis 2's end-state ('override hits only that scope'): name-as-key de-dupes the very thing the axis exists for. The flagship Vim forks (enter/backspace in Vim vs not) are unreachable. If scoping is later confirmed needed, forces a second breaking migration on the same users (third if platform is added separately). Hurts the power-user target persona most.
- **Record list [{name, trigger, context?, platform?}]** _(high effort · costly)_ — Replace the top-level map with a Vec of records (report R3). context?/platform? optional; a bare record reproduces today's clobber-all-by-name default. Duplicate-name records with different contexts are first-class. Legacy bare-string mapping accepted via an untagged-enum deserializer.
  - 👍 Only shape whose cardinality matches reality (one name already -> N bindings, keymap.rs:30) and whose ORDER is well-defined — which the inject-new / recency-precedence model (A2-Q5) and the already-flat matcher bindings() list want, and which a HashMap-keyed shape physically cannot express. Carries platform in the same record so the (name x context x platform) key tuple is decided once (closes A2-Q2's second-migration risk). One record type = one parser, one serializer, one migration, no permanent dual path. The interning decision and serde shim are needed under every non-defer option anyway, so the list pays the shared cost once and gets the most for it. Its hard cost is also smaller than the brief claimed: interning option (a) leak/intern is near-zero macro churn, so 'high' here is dominated by FromStr/Display + the bullet-proof shim + settings-UI row-model change, not a 965-site rewrite.
  - 👎 Highest net-new surface: FromStr + Display for ContextPredicate (don't exist) and an interning decision (mandatory; cheapest realization is leak/intern, NOT the 965-site Cow refactor the brief implied). The untagged-enum migration must be bullet-proof or one bad record drops the user's whole file (keyboard.rs:128-139). BREAKS FORWARD-COMPAT not flagged by the brief: an older client reading a newer list-shaped file expects a map, fails the whole deserialize, and drops every binding — a real hazard for power users who sync dotfiles across channels/machines. Loses name-as-unique-key, so editors/dedup and the settings-UI row model (A2-Q18) change. Costly to walk back once on disk. Its claimed edge over the cheaper composite-key/nested shapes is CONDITIONAL on A2-Q5 picking inject/recency ordering.
- **Composite/encoded-key flat map (name+context in the key, value stays String)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Keep HashMap<String, _> but make the key encode (name [+ context [+ platform]]), e.g. \"accept_suggestion@vim_normal\". Value stays PersistedTrigger(String). Legacy bare-name keys are just the context-less case; same-name-different-context are distinct keys, so no clobber and no value-type change.
  - 👍 The cheapest 80% the brief omitted. NO untagged-enum shim and NO value-type change — it sidesteps the single biggest 'silently drops the whole file' risk (the value is still a bare String, so a malformed value is no more likely than today). Best compat story of any scoping option: existing files unchanged, and crucially FORWARD-compatible — an old client reading a new file stays a top-level map and just no-ops on unknown composite keys (update_custom_trigger matches nothing) instead of dropping everything. (name x context) stays a unique key, so the settings-UI row model and dedup logic change far less than under the list. If platform stays out of the file and A2-Q5 picks mutate-in-place (order irrelevant), this is competitive with the list at materially lower risk.
  - 👎 Still needs FromStr/Display + the interning decision (to parse/emit the context segment) — no escape from the shared hard cost. Encoding is fragile: action names already contain ':' (e.g. 'editor:delete_all_left'), so the delimiter must be one that cannot appear in a name or a context atom, and platform as a third encoded segment is hacky. FATAL where ordering matters: a HashMap has no defined iteration order, so it CANNOT express the recency/inject precedence A2-Q5 may require — making its viability itself gated on A2-Q5 = mutate-in-place. Ugly to author by hand. Still a persisted contract, so still costly to walk back, though composite-key -> records is a mechanical later migration.
- **Name-keyed nested map HashMap<String, Vec<ScopedTrigger>>** _(high effort · costly)_ — Keep name as top-level key but make its value a list of {trigger, context?, platform?} scoped entries.
  - 👍 Preserves name-as-unique-key ergonomics and the editable_bindings_by_name mental model (name groups its bindings, mirroring keymap.rs:30); everything about one action is colocated and greppable. Inner Vec solves the clobber problem and DOES carry a defined order within a name.
  - 👎 Fully breaking: value type changes scalar String -> sequence, so the newtype-over-String deserializer fails and the single from_reader drops the whole file (keyboard.rs:173-174,128-139) — same untagged-enum shim burden as the list, AND the same forward-compat hazard (old client sees a sequence value, fails). No back-compat saving over the list. Order is defined only WITHIN a name, not across names, so cross-name recency/inject precedence (A2-Q5) is awkward. Platform x context inside the inner Vec re-creates the key-tuple question with extra nesting. More structure, less precedence clarity than the list.
- **Hybrid: legacy flat map + separate scoped section** _(high effort · costly)_ — Keep the legacy name:trigger map untouched as the default/global scope; add a separate 'contextual:' list of {name, trigger, context, platform?} only for scoped overrides (A2-Q14 option a).
  - 👍 Strongest back-compat: existing files literally unchanged, keep clobber-all by construction, zero migration and zero silent behavior change on upgrade. New scoping is additive/opt-in; risk isolated to users who write the new section. Axis-1 hot-reload ships on the untouched map while the scoped section matures behind a flag. Also forward-compatible for users who never adopt the new section.
  - 👎 Two schemas forever: two deserialize paths, two mental models, and an UNDEFINED interaction when an action appears in both the flat map and the scoped section (precedence unspecified, re-opens A2-Q4/A2-Q5). The scoped section STILL needs the full FromStr/Display/interning stack, so it pays the list's hard costs without the list's uniformity. Settings UI and conflict detection must reconcile two sources. Tech debt the eventual clean list migration still has to unwind.

**→ Recommendation: Record list [{name, trigger, context?, platform?}] — conditional on (1) product confirming Phase-2 needs NEW per-context scopes and (2) A2-Q5 favoring inject/recency precedence; otherwise defer..** The recommendation survives the attack but is now explicitly conditional and cheaper than the brief priced it. If per-context scoping is in Phase-2 scope, name-as-key is disqualified by a hard structural fact (it de-dupes two overrides of one action) — and update_custom_trigger already proves one name fans out to N bindings (keymap.rs:30,422-433). Among scoping shapes, the FromStr/Display work and the interning DECISION are unavoidable in all of them; I corrected the brief's worst-case framing — interning's cheapest realization is leak/intern with near-zero macro churn (precedent in-repo), not the ~965-site Cow refactor, so the list's true cost is FromStr/Display + a bullet-proof shim + the settings-UI row-model change. The list earns its 'high' cost with the one thing no HashMap-keyed shape can give: defined ORDER, which the inject/recency model (A2-Q5) and the flat matcher bindings() list want, plus a home for platform so A2-Q2 doesn't force a re-migration. TWO honesties the brief soft-pedaled, now explicit: (a) the list breaks FORWARD-compat (old client + new file = whole-file drop, keyboard.rs:128-139) — accept this only because Warp can gate the new format behind a min-version, otherwise the cheaper composite-key option's graceful degradation wins; (b) the list's edge over the new composite-key option and the nested-map is CONDITIONAL on A2-Q5 choosing inject/recency — if A2-Q5 picks mutate-in-place, ordering is moot and the composite-key flat map (no shim, no value-type change, forward-compatible) becomes the better risk-adjusted choice. DEFERABILITY: A2-Q3 does not block Phase 1 at all, so the disciplined sequence is ship Axis-1 hot-reload on the untouched map, run the confirming prototype, and only then freeze the schema before Phase 2. Recommend committing to the list ONLY after the scope and A2-Q5 calls land; until then, defer (the easy/low-risk default).

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is that the list is being chosen for an ordering property (A2-Q5 inject/recency) and a platform field (A2-Q2) that are BOTH still undecided — so it can pay full price for capabilities Phase 2 may not use. If product later confirms the leaf stories are 'rebind a global action' plus at most a handful of static mode scopes with mutate-in-place semantics, then the composite-key flat map delivers the same user value with no untagged-enum shim, no value-type change, and forward-compat preserved, and the list's order/platform advantages are dead weight that still cost the breaking migration and the forward-compat break. The list only clearly wins if BOTH A2-Q5 = inject/recency AND platform belongs in-file; otherwise the recommendation is a tossup with the cheaper composite-key option, and the brief should not let the list's elegance pre-empt those two upstream calls.
- _Dependencies:_ Does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis-1 hot-reload rides on last_applied bookkeeping, A1-Q3/A1-Q16, and needs no schema fork) — true deadline is before Phase 2 ships. Gated by A2-Q2 (the platform? field / full key tuple, cross-team). The list's recommendation rests on TWO conditions that must be made explicit: A2-Q5 must favor inject/recency precedence (else a HashMap-keyed composite-key shape becomes competitive — the list's order advantage evaporates under mutate-in-place), and A2-Q14 must define a context-less record as clobber-all-by-name default (else existing files silently change semantics on upgrade). Also co-decides A2-Q4 (which same-name binding an override targets), A2-Q6 (predicate grammar), A2-Q8 (interning a/b/c — choose leak/intern to avoid the 965-site refactor), A2-Q15 (Display/serializer write path). Feeds the settings-UI per-context editor and catalog (A2-Q18, Axis 5). New explicit dependency: a min-version / forward-compat policy must accompany any non-composite-key shape, because old clients reading a new sequence-shaped file drop the whole file.
- _What would settle it:_ First, a product call confirming Phase-2 leaf stories (A2-Q5) actually require NEW per-context scopes, not just global rebinds — if not, defer, this fork is premature. If they do, get the A2-Q5 precedence model (inject/recency vs mutate-in-place) decided IN THE SAME ROUND, because it is what separates the list from the cheaper composite-key flat map. Then a throwaway prototype of BOTH the record list AND the composite-key flat map against real same-name multi-context cases (an action bound differently in VimNormalMode vs default, plus a mac/linux split), proving for each: (a) every existing flat keybindings.yaml round-trips byte-for-byte unchanged; (b) two scoped overrides of one name coexist without clobbering; (c) one malformed record/key does not cause from_reader to silently drop the whole file (keyboard.rs:128-139); and the fact the brief missed — (d) an OLDER client reading the new file does not lose all bindings (the composite-key shape passes this for free; the list/nested-map need a min-version gate). Settings PM input on file-vs-in-app primary authoring surface (A2-Q18) decides how heavily to weight the name-as-unique-key ergonomic loss and the composite-key authoring ugliness.
- _Red-team added option:_ Composite/encoded-key flat map (HashMap key encodes name+context, value stays String)
- _Red-team:_ Re-opened every cited anchor; all five structural facts hold (keyboard.rs:169,174,128-139; keymap.rs:30,422-433; context.rs:9-18,100-120; keymap_tests.rs:265-269). Four substantive changes. (1) CORRECTED OVERSTATEMENT: the list's what_breaks pins ~965 macro sites of interning churn on the option. The answers doc (lines 430,434) calls 965 an explicit upper-bound that 'may include unrelated id! macros,' and it only applies to interning option (b) Cow/IDs; option (a) leak/intern is 'near-zero macro change' with in-repo precedent (ai.rs:183-194, user_uid.rs:26). Interning is a required DECISION shared by every context-bearing shape, not a mandatory 965-site refactor — so the list's hard cost is smaller than billed AND the defer option's steelman ('dodges the 965-site refactor') is equally overstated; the cost gap between defer and list narrows. (2) REFRAMED THE STRUCTURAL BLOCKER: the brief says 'a name-keyed map structurally cannot' hold two overrides. Verified the real blocker is the KEY being the bare action name, not the HashMap container — a HashMap whose key encodes (name,context) holds N overrides fine. This wedge surfaces a missing cheaper option. (3) ADDED MISSING OPTION: composite/encoded-key flat map keeps value type String, needs NO untagged-enum shim and NO value-type change (the single biggest 'silently drops whole file' risk), and DEGRADES GRACEFULLY on old clients. (4) ADDED MISSED FACT: forward-compat. List/nested-map break old-client-reads-new-file (from_reader expects map, gets a sequence -> whole-file drop, keyboard.rs:128-139); brief never raised this. Also surfaced two hidden dependencies the recommendation silently rests on: A2-Q14 must resolve context-less record = clobber-all (else every file's semantics silently change on upgrade), and the list's edge over both the nested-map and the composite-key option is CONDITIONAL on A2-Q5 picking inject/recency-precedence (a HashMap has no iteration order, so composite-key cannot express recency; if A2-Q5 picks mutate-in-place, ordering is moot and the cheaper shapes become competitive). Recommendation (record list, gated on scope confirmation) survives but is now explicitly conditional, cheaper-than-billed, and contested by a documented 80% option.

### A2-Q5 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team, but RE-SEQUENCED: the initiative PRODUCT/roadmap owner moves FIRST and makes the gating scope call — are net-new scopes, true suppression, and modal modes (Axis 4) firm Phase-2 commitments, yes/no? That answer selects the option. Then the engine/architecture lead (owner of keymap.rs and the resolved-binding/precedence model) ratifies the mechanism: Option D's context-targeted mutate + Fixed->Editable migration (no matcher-loop rewrite) if scope is narrow, or Option C's inject+R2 store (rewrites the resolution loop) if broad. The Axis-2 and Axis-3 feature leads jointly own the targeting (A2-Q4)/schema (A2-Q3) and the migration/R2 workstreams respectively. All ratify that Axis 2 is not a standalone-new-scopes Phase-2 deliverable.

**Decision.** Decide the mechanism for a context-scoped keybinding override AND the dependency it creates. Two real sub-decisions, not one: (a) MECHANISM — mutate `custom_trigger` in place via the `as_lens` path, vs inject a new higher-precedence `{name,trigger,predicate}` record; (b) what that mechanism depends on. The brief framed (b) as a binary "is the full Axis-3 R2 (layer/recency precedence + Unbound) a hard prerequisite." The corrected framing splits the dependency by use-case class: (i) REBIND an existing per-context fork that is DISJOINT-context (enter/backspace in Vim vs !Vim) needs Fixed-remappability + name+context targeting + a record schema, but NOT the full (layer,recency) precedence store, because disjoint contexts never compete at match time; (ii) ADD a net-new scope, SUPPRESS an underlying default, or resolve OVERLAPPING-context forks needs the full R2 inject store + Unbound. So the decision is really: which use-case class is in Phase-2 scope, and therefore which of {do nothing/global-rebind-only, migrate-to-editable (D), full inject+R2 (B/C)} you build.

**Stakes.** A roadmap-sequencing and team-allocation call for Phase 2 — but NOT a Phase-1 blocker (Phase 1 = Axis 1 hot-reload + Phase-1 slice of Axis 5; A2-Q5's only Phase-1 reach is the on-disk schema shape A2-Q3, which is already independently forced to a record list because one name maps to N bindings). Getting it wrong in EITHER direction is expensive: (1) shipping pure mutate-on-today's-editable-set (Option A) as the Phase-2 Axis-2 deliverable rebinds nothing users asked for — A2-Q13 proves zero reachable flagship targets; a hollow increment. (2) Conversely, over-committing to the full inject+R2 resolved-binding store (B/C) before any flagship value ships over-builds if product only wants to rebind existing disjoint-context forks — the matcher's core resolution-loop rewrite (keymap.rs:454-464) and the re-basing of ~265 registration sites' relationship to precedence is a one-way, very-high-effort commitment that stalls all Axis-2 user-visible value behind the core. The genuinely costly mistake is making this call WITHOUT the product scope decision (net-new scopes / modal modes / suppression in Phase 2, yes/no), because that single input flips which option is correct. Discovering the answer mid-flight forces rework of the schema (A2-Q3) and — only for the inject path — the matcher's resolution loop; the migrate path reworks the schema and get_binding_by_name's context-blindness but leaves the matcher loop intact.

_Constrained by:_
- The only precedence model today is bindings(): editable bindings in reverse/LIFO registration order, then fixed bindings in reverse. No layer field, no priority, no explicit ordering key. A true inject (out-precede a competing default) has no insertion point here. VERIFIED. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:454-464`
- update_custom_trigger is name-only and writes custom_trigger to editable_custom_action_bindings + editable_bindings ONLY, never fixed_bindings; it filters purely on b.name==name and never reads context_predicate. as_lens swaps custom_trigger in (keeping original_trigger as fallback). So mutate-in-place can only rewrite triggers on bindings that already exist as editable, and does so clobber-all across same-name siblings. VERIFIED. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433, :741-759`
- FixedBinding has no custom_trigger field; only EditableBinding does. Every flagship per-context fork (enter/numpadenter/shift-enter/backspace/delete/tab/shift-tab Vim-vs-not; ctrl-r Redo) is a FixedBinding, and there are ZERO same-name multi-context EditableBindings (19 Fixed Vim-context sites vs 6 editable, the 6 use distinct names). So mutate-in-place on TODAY's editable set reaches none of the flagship examples. Independently reproduced (19/6/409/437). VERIFIED. — `A2-Q13 (answers doc 548-597) + crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-304`
- DECISIVE OMISSION CORRECTED: the flagship forks are DISJOINT-context — enter/numpadenter/shift-enter/backspace register a non-Vim variant scoped `& !id!("Vim")` and a Vim variant scoped `& id!("Vim")`. Mutually exclusive predicates mean only one ever evaluates true in any given context, so REBINDING them needs no precedence resolution at match time. The brief's premise that reaching the flagships 'inherently needs a binding that out-precedes a default' is false for disjoint rebinds; it holds only for net-new-scope-add, overlapping forks, and suppression. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:51-90`
- EXCEPTION the migrate path must handle: shift-backspace's non-Vim variant is scoped just `text_entry` (no `!Vim`), so it OVERLAPS Vim mode and competes with VimBackspace on the same key. A few such sloppy forks need predicate-tightening to `& !Vim` during migration (cheap) OR genuine precedence (R2). Most flagship forks do not. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:91-95 + A2-Q13 (answers doc 563)`
- Registration order is the only tiebreaker (recency = source-ordered single init chain); deterministic per build but NOT invariant across releases (reorders), platforms (cfg gates, lib.rs:1652), or feature-flag states (lib.rs:1691). A config-time inject has no stable LIFO insertion point — it needs R2's explicit stored ordering. ALSO: get_binding_by_name (the settings-UI lookup) is name-only FORWARD (first-enabled), opposite to the matcher's reverse order and context-BLIND — so migrating forks to same-name editable bindings exposes a display-vs-dispatch asymmetry that any non-trivial option must fix. VERIFIED. — `A3-Q6 (answers doc 866-913) + crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:378-385`
- Trigger::Empty is shadow-by-non-participation ('cannot actually be matched'), not suppression; there is no Unbound variant. So suppressing an underlying default / Fixed fork needs R2 + a real Unbound. The flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> schema cannot hold an inject record {name,trigger,predicate} and ContextPredicate has no FromStr/Display/serde, so a parseable context schema is mandatory for ANY scoped option. VERIFIED. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:44-48 + app/src/keyboard.rs:169 + A2-Q11`
- FACTUAL CORRECTION: a GLOBAL removal path already exists — UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed -> set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty), serialized as the sentinel 'none'. Only SCOPED removal (remove in context X, keep in Y) is missing, and only TRUE suppression of an underlying default needs Unbound. 'No removal exists' would be wrong. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15, :43-44, :182-184`

**Options:**

- **Mutate-in-place only on today's editable set (ship Axis 2 standalone)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Extend update_custom_trigger with Option<ContextPredicate> and rewrite custom_trigger only on matched existing EDITABLE bindings via as_lens. Declare new scopes and Fixed forks out of scope. No R2 dependency.
  - 👍 Reuses the proven as_lens/custom_trigger/original_trigger live-apply machinery and the diff-apply reload path; adds no precedence engine; smallest blast radius; preserves the roadmap promise that Axis 2 is independent of Axis 3.
  - 👎 A2-Q13 proves ZERO same-name multi-context EditableBindings, so without migrating anything this reaches no flagship example (all enter/backspace/tab Vim forks are FixedBinding). Yet it still pays for the record schema (A2-Q3), targeting (A2-Q4), and predicate interning (A2-Q8). Near-zero flagship value for most of the cost — a hollow increment. Dead unless paired with migration (Option D).
- **Migrate flagship Fixed forks to named EditableBindings + context-targeted mutate (MISSING OPTION, added)** _(high effort · costly)_ ✅ **recommended** — Promote the ~19 Vim-context Fixed forks to EditableBindings (giving them names + a custom_trigger path), extend update_custom_trigger with context targeting (A2-Q4), adopt the record-list schema (A2-Q3), make get_binding_by_name context-aware, and tighten the few overlapping predicates (e.g. shift-backspace) to disjoint. Delivers the flagship per-context REBINDS WITHOUT building the full R2 (layer,recency) precedence store or Unbound, because disjoint forks never compete at match time. Defers net-new-scope/suppression to a later R2.
  - 👍 Matches the verified code: the flagship forks are disjoint-context (actions.rs:51-90), so a context-targeted mutate on migrated editable versions reaches the entire headline set (enter/backspace/tab Vim-vs-not) with no precedence engine and no one-way matcher-loop rewrite. Converts Option A's empty target set into the FULL flagship set. Reuses the existing editable override path instead of inventing a Fixed-override path — strictly less new machinery than B/C. The migration surface is bounded (~19 sites across ~3 files, not all 397 Fixed). Ships flagship value in Phase 2 while leaving R2 to be built later only if/when net-new scopes are demanded; the migrated, now-named bindings are exactly what an eventual R2 store would consume, so little is thrown away. Lower-regret: delivers sooner and defers the one-way commitment.
  - 👎 Creates the FIRST same-name multi-context editable bindings, exposing the get_binding_by_name forward/context-blind asymmetry (A3-Q6) — settings display must become context-aware (work B/C also need). Migrating Fixed->Editable touches ~19 registration sites and needs an audit of which forks are user-facing (A3-Q1) and predicate-tightening for the overlapping ones (shift-backspace text_entry-only). Does NOT reach: net-new scopes (add a binding only in a new mode where none exists), true suppression of an underlying default (needs Unbound), or overlapping-context precedence — so if product wants modal modes (Axis 4) or 'add a binding only in this mode' in Phase 2, this is insufficient and you build R2 anyway (partial detour, though the naming work carries forward).
- **Inject + full R2 as hard prerequisite (build the resolved-binding store first)** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Overrides inject {name,trigger,predicate} into a new resolved-binding store with explicit (layer,recency) precedence and a real Unbound (R2). Axis 2 writes through it and does NOT ship standalone in Phase 2.
  - 👍 The only path that expresses genuinely NEW scopes, true SUPPRESSION, and OVERLAPPING-context resolution; combined with Fixed-remappability it also reaches the flagship forks. Replaces release/platform/flag-unstable registration-order recency (A3-Q6) with explicit user-visible precedence that Axis 5's truthful-conflicts work also needs. Builds the single primitive the meta-question says every axis must read/write through, avoiding throwaway scaffolding over the flat map.
  - 👎 Highest effort and a one-way commitment: rewrites the matcher's core resolution loop (keymap.rs:454-464) and re-bases ~265 registration sites' relationship to precedence. Stalls ALL Axis-2 user-visible value behind the R2 core — if R2 slips, Axis 2 slips. Over-builds if product ultimately only wants disjoint-context rebinds (which Option D delivers far cheaper).
- **Hybrid: declare the dependency edge, build override on the resolved-binding store once, slice delivery (brief's pick)** _(high effort · costly)_ — Commit inject+R2 as the target mechanism, formally mark the Axis-2 -> Axis-3(R2) edge, do not promise Axis 2 standalone for new scopes, build the single resolved-binding store once, optionally ship a labeled thin slice.
  - 👍 If net-new scopes / modal modes / suppression are firm Phase-2 commitments, this is correct: it commits to the right end-state (one resolved-binding store) once, draws the dependency edge honestly, and avoids both the hollow increment (A) and a second migration later. The single store is the meta-question's 'one primitive' that Axes 1/2/4/5 all read/write through.
  - 👎 Over-commits to the full layer/precedence/Unbound store before delivering ANY flagship value, when Option D ships the headline disjoint-context REBINDS without it. The brief's own 'thin slice' is described as hitting 'the same near-empty target set as Option A' — that is the flaw: without migration the slice has no targets; with migration it becomes Option D, which is the cheaper standalone path. Only justified once product confirms net-new scopes/suppression are in Phase-2 scope; otherwise it buys a one-way commitment the requirements don't yet demand.

**→ Recommendation: Migrate flagship Fixed forks to named EditableBindings + context-targeted mutate (MISSING OPTION, added).** Make the product scope call FIRST — it is the actual fork in the road and the brief buried it. Absent that call, Option D (migrate-editable) is the lower-regret default and is what a skeptical staff engineer should sign off on, for three code-verified reasons. (1) The flagship forks are DISJOINT-context (actions.rs:51-90: `& !id!(\"Vim\")` vs `& id!(\"Vim\")`), so rebinding them needs no match-time precedence — the brief's central claim that reaching them 'inherently needs a binding that out-precedes a default' is false for the disjoint rebind class, which is the headline use case. (2) Migration converts the dead Option A into a working flagship vehicle by populating the target set (the move the brief never considered), reusing the EXISTING editable override path rather than building a new Fixed-override path or the full (layer,recency)/Unbound store — strictly less new machinery, and crucially it does NOT rewrite the matcher's core resolution loop, so it is not the one-way commitment B/C are. (3) It delivers Phase-2 flagship value without gating on the R2 core, and the migrated/named bindings are exactly what an eventual R2 store would consume, so the carry-forward loss is small. ESCALATE to Option C (build the resolved-binding store once) ONLY if product confirms that net-new scopes ('add a binding only in this mode where none exists'), true suppression of underlying defaults, modal modes (Axis 4), or overlapping-context forks are firm Phase-2 commitments — those genuinely need inject+R2+Unbound, which D cannot express. Option A is dead either way (zero standalone targets). Option B is C without the slicing/edge discipline. The brief's core diagnosis is UPHELD and strengthened — mutate-on-today's-set is hollow, the flagships are Fixed, and Axis 2 is not a clean standalone Phase-2 win — but the required dependency is Fixed-remappability + targeting + a record schema, NOT the full R2 precedence/Unbound store for the disjoint rebind class. Finally, this decision does not block Phase 1; only the schema shape (A2-Q3) has Phase-1 reach and is already independently forced to a record list, so A2-Q5's mechanism/sequencing call can be made at Phase-2 planning once the product scope input lands.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument: if product wants modal modes (Axis 4 — custom Vim/Helix/Kakoune-style modes), 'add a binding only in this mode', or true suppression of defaults in Phase 2 — which the keybindings initiative's own modal-modes ambition makes plausible — then Option D is a detour: you build R2's resolved-binding store anyway, and the migration's value collapses to just the naming work. In that world Option C's 'build the one primitive once' is correct and D's incremental migration + get_binding_by_name fix is partly throwaway. D also bets that the few overlapping-context forks (shift-backspace text_entry-only) can be cleanly tightened to disjoint without behavior regressions; if any flagship fork turns out to genuinely need overlapping-context precedence, that one case alone pulls in R2. So D is the right call ONLY conditional on a narrow, rebind-existing-forks Phase-2 scope; that conditionality is real and the decision should not be ratified until the product scope input is on record. There is also a secondary risk that migrating ~19 Fixed sites to Editable subtly changes dispatch (e.g. an editable binding can now be globally cleared by a name-only clobber, A2-Q14) in ways the Fixed sites were relied upon to prevent.
- _Dependencies:_ Does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis 1 + Phase-1 Axis 5); its only Phase-1 reach is the schema shape A2-Q3, already independently forced to a record list (one name -> N bindings, keymap.rs:30). Gated by the product scope call (net-new scopes / modal modes / suppression in Phase 2?) which selects D vs C, and by the Cross-axis meta-question (one primitive vs three separable). Hard-couples to A2-Q3 (record list, not flat map — required by ALL non-trivial options) and A2-Q4 (name+context targeting — required by D and C). Option D additionally couples to a NARROW slice of A3-Q1 (which ~19 Fixed forks to migrate) but NOT to the full A3-Q3 stable-Fixed-identity work (migration assigns names, sidestepping it) and NOT to the full R2 (layer,recency) precedence store. Option C/B couple to the full A3-Q1/A3-Q3/R2 and to A2-Q11's Unbound. The get_binding_by_name context-blindness (A3-Q6, keymap.rs:378-385) must be fixed by D and C alike once same-name multi-context editable bindings exist. Net-new-scope-add and scoped removal/suppression (A2-Q11) require R2 + Unbound regardless. Downstream feeds Axis-5 truthful-conflicts (R9 catalog) and the X-axis phase-sequencing call.
- _What would settle it:_ Two inputs, in order. (1) PRODUCT SCOPE (the gating input the brief under-weighted): get an explicit Phase-2 yes/no on each of — 'add a binding only in this mode where none exists', true 'disable/suppress an underlying default', modal modes (Axis 4), and rebinding OVERLAPPING-context forks. All-no => Option D. Any-yes => Option C. (2) ENGINEERING SPIKE to de-risk D: build a synthetic keymap of the real flagship cases, MIGRATE the enter/backspace Vim-vs-!Vim forks from Fixed to named EditableBindings, extend update_custom_trigger to take Option<ContextPredicate>, and assert via Matcher::test_keystroke that (a) a Vim-scoped rebind changes only the Vim fork and (b) the !Vim fork is untouched in both dispatch and the get_binding_by_name display path — proving disjoint rebinds need no precedence store. Tighten shift-backspace's text_entry-only predicate to `& !Vim` and confirm no regression. Produce the Axis-2 leaf-user-story inventory with TWO columns: 'reachable by migrate+context-mutate (no R2)?' and 'requires inject+R2+Unbound?'. If the inventory's must-have column for Phase 2 is entirely the first, ratify D, draw the Axis-2 -> narrow-Fixed-remappability edge, and keep R2 deferred; if any must-have falls in the second, ratify C, draw the Axis-2 -> full-R2 edge, and remove Axis 2 from the standalone-new-scopes Phase-2 list.
- _Red-team added option:_ Migrate flagship Fixed forks to named EditableBindings + context-targeted mutate (narrow Fixed-remappability, no full R2 store)
- _Red-team:_ I independently re-opened every cited file and the answers doc; all five of the brief's grounding facts are real and not overstated (bindings() LIFO with no layer field at keymap.rs:454-464; update_custom_trigger name-only + editable-only at :422-433; as_lens custom_trigger swap at :741-759; FixedBinding has no custom_trigger at :277-286; A2-Q13's "zero same-name multi-context EditableBindings / 19 Fixed vs 6 distinct-named editable" reproduced; A3-Q6 registration-order instability; Trigger::Empty is shadow-not-suppress and there is no Unbound). So the core diagnosis stands: mutate-on-today's-editable-set is hollow (zero flagship targets) and Axis 2 is not a clean standalone Phase-2 win. THREE attacks landed and changed the brief: (1) MISSING OPTION — the brief never considered MIGRATING the ~19 Vim-context Fixed forks to named EditableBindings, which converts Option A's "near-empty target set" into the FULL flagship set and reaches the headline per-context REBINDS WITHOUT building the full R2 resolved-binding/precedence/Unbound store. (2) OVER-CLAIM — I verified at actions.rs:51-90 that the flagship forks (enter/numpadenter/shift-enter/backspace) are cleanly DISJOINT-context (`& !id!("Vim")` vs `& id!("Vim")`), so at match time only one ever evaluates true; the brief's claim that reaching them "inherently needs a binding that out-precedes a default" is true only for net-new-scope-add, overlapping-context forks (e.g. shift-backspace's text_entry-only non-Vim variant at :91-95), and suppression — NOT for disjoint rebinds. Full R2 precedence is therefore required for a SUBSET, not for the flagship rebinds. (3) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY + DEFERABILITY — the recommendation silently assumes net-new scopes / modal modes (Axis 4) / true suppression are firm Phase-2 commitments; if Phase-2 scope is limited to rebinding existing forks, R2 is NOT a hard prereq and the cheaper migrate-path wins. And A2-Q5 does NOT block Phase 1 at all; its only Phase-1 reach is the schema shape (A2-Q3), which is already independently forced to a record list. I also corrected a factual slip: a GLOBAL removal path already exists (Removed -> Trigger::Empty, keyboard.rs:43-44); it is only SCOPED removal that is missing. Net: grounding upheld, recommended vehicle changed from the build-the-core-first Hybrid (C) to the lower-regret Migrate-editable (D), conditional on a product scope call.

### A2-Q6 — FLIPPED · _product_ · owner: Joint, with one addition. Surface syntax + familiarity target: Keybindings product/design owner (A2-Q6 is labeled user-product-decision). Round-trip contract + canonical form + Display impl: warpui_core keymap engine owner (owns the AST and the FromStr/Display guarantee against context.rs). ADDED co-signer: the Axis-2/Axis-3 owner of the per-binding override path (A2-Q4/A2-Q5) must sign off on SEQUENCING, because the FromStr half is inert until update_custom_trigger gains a context-aware consumer — they own the gate that makes building the parser worthwhile.

**Decision.** Decide the text grammar for ContextPredicate (operator set, precedence, associativity, parenthesization, atom rules, canonical Just(false)/Just(true) tokens) plus the Display serializer / FromStr parser that round-trips losslessly with the macro/operator-built predicates. Corrected scope SPLITS the deliverable: (a) the canonical TEXT FORM + Display serializer (read-only catalog/export of existing predicates) is decidable and shippable now; (b) the FromStr parser + user-editable config context field is a downstream Axis-2 deliverable gated on a per-binding override path and the atom-storage decision — decide-on-paper-now, build-later.

**Stakes.** Correctness, not cosmetics: under different precedence the same string `a & b | c` selects a different scope, and the negation operators (!, !=) additionally fail OPEN (context.rs:109-113), leaking bindings into views that never set the key. BUT the one-way lock-in only bites once users hand-author predicate strings in keybindings.yaml, which cannot happen until the FromStr parser, a schema context field (A2-Q3), an interning strategy (A2-Q8), AND a per-binding override consumer (A2-Q4/A2-Q5) all ship together. None exist today. So the expensive/irreversible risk is deferrable; the cheap/safe slice (decide canonical form on paper + ship Display for the catalog) is what is actually near-term. Mis-stating this as 'Phase-1 critical path, build the full parser now' over-invests against a capability the engine cannot yet apply.

_Constrained by:_
- AST is fixed; Equal/NotEqual are LEAF atoms holding two &'static str (not boolean ops over predicates), so the only real precedence/associativity decision is among the connectives ! (Not) / & (And) / | (Or); ==/!= are atom-internal token rules. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18,11-13`
- Repo-wide there is NO FromStr, NO Display/ToString, NO serde for ContextPredicate (grep returns zero); construction is only compile-time macros over literals (id!/eq!/ne!/always!) plus operator overloads Not :53, BitAnd :61, BitOr :69. So FromStr(Display(p))==p is currently 0% — both halves absent. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:53-87; A2-Q15 answers lines 622,647`
- always!() yields Just(true); there is NO Just(false) macro/token and zero Just(false) occurrences in the repo — the serializer must invent a canonical false token AND a canonical minimal/unambiguous parenthesization for the And/Or/Not Box tree. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:79-83; A2-Q15 hazards (b),(c)`
- All atom-bearing variants hold &'static str; a runtime FromStr over file text yields String, which cannot inhabit the variants without interning/leaking (Box::leak precedent at settings/ai.rs:183-194) or a closed static vocabulary; ~965 macro sites depend on the &'static str contract. So the PARSE direction is blocked on the A2-Q8 storage decision; the PRINT direction is NOT (printing existing &'static str needs no interning). — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:11-13; A2-Q8 answers line 423`
- Real engine predicates are AND-chains of a positive scope gate plus negatives: id!("EditorView") & !id!("IMEOpen") & !id!("Vim") and exactly 12 ne!("TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality","None") chains each ANDed with id!("Terminal"). Any grammar lacking boolean operators cannot express these. Persisted schema is flat name->PersistedTrigger(String) with no context field. — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:200; app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:385+ (count=12); app/src/keyboard.rs:169-174`
- DECISIVE OMISSION: a parsed predicate has NO consumer today. update_custom_trigger(name: &str, trigger: Option<Trigger>) filters editable bindings by name ONLY and rewrites ALL same-name registrations — it takes no context/predicate. Per-context override is a NEW engine capability (A2-Q4/A2-Q5), so even a perfect FromStr cannot apply a predicate to a single scope until that path exists. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433; A2-Q15 answers lines 234,257`
- SEMANTIC FOOTGUN for the grammar's negation operators: NotEqual/Not fail OPEN — NotEqual matches when the key is absent (unwrap_or(true)) and Not(Equal) over-matches identically. Exposing !/!= to config authors without a negation policy (A2-Q9) silently leaks bindings; engine-internal predicates are safe only because they AND a positive id gate. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:109-114; A2-Q9 answers lines 446,473`

**Options:**

- **Zed-style infix, build Display+FromStr now (original recommendation)** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Infix grammar: bare-identifier or key==value / key!=value atoms; connectives !, &&, ||, parens; precedence ! > && > ||, left-assoc; Display emits minimal-parenthesized form; canonical false token. Build both halves now.
  - 👍 The fixed AST maps 1:1 onto a standard boolean grammar, so any infix dialect reproduces the macro-built tree; the differentiator is familiarity and Warp's id/eq/ne/And/Or/Not model descends from Zed's KeyBindingContextPredicate, so migrating devs already know !/&&/||/==/!= and its precedence. A fixed conventional precedence yields a deterministic, re-parseable Display, satisfying round-trip against real predicates.
  - 👎 Builds the EXPENSIVE half (FromStr) before it has a consumer: update_custom_trigger is name-only (keymap.rs:422-433), so a parsed predicate can't be applied until A2-Q4/A2-Q5 ship; can't be CONSTRUCTED until A2-Q8 interning lands; no schema context field (A2-Q3) to read it from. 'Medium' is the grammar slice only — as a shippable capability it is HIGH and gated on 3 other decisions. Once user files contain predicate strings, precedence is one-way. Internal spelling is single &/|, so &&/|| creates a second dialect. Negation fail-open (A2-Q9) unaddressed.
- **Rust-operator mirror (single &/|, Rust precedence), build both now** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Tokens !, &, | with Rust's own precedence (! tightest, & tighter than |), left-assoc, parens, key==value atoms; Display literally prints the operator-overload structure.
  - 👍 Exactly reproduces the operator overloads (context.rs:53-75) the engine already builds, so the serializer is a near-mechanical print and reparse is provably structurally identical with the least translation for the ~965 macro authors; uses well-defined Rust precedence so nothing novel is invented.
  - 👎 Single &/| reads oddly to end users who expect logical &&/||; optimizes for engine authors who never edit the YAML over the hand-edited config surface. Same downstream block (no override consumer), same A2-Q8/A2-Q3 prerequisites, same one-way precedence lock-in, same unaddressed negation fail-open.
- **Structured (non-infix) encoding in YAML** _(med effort · costly)_ — No infix string grammar: nested data, e.g. {all_of:[...]} / {not:...} / {eq:[k,v]} / {const:false}, (de)serialized via serde directly off the AST shape.
  - 👍 Eliminates precedence/associativity/parenthesization entirely (a & b | c ambiguity cannot arise), makes round-trip derive-trivial, makes Just(false) a literal, and is MORE compatible with deferral because you needn't decide precedence to start. Sidesteps minimal-paren canonicalization and the structural-vs-eval equality trap.
  - 👎 Verbose and hostile to hand-authoring, the point of a config file; diverges from Zed/VS Code familiarity and the compact EditorView & !IMEOpen & !Vim form. Still needs the A2-Q8 String->&'static str bridge, still needs the A2-Q5 override consumer, and the settings UI (A2-Q18) must render the structured form. Migrating to/from infix later is a file migration.
- **Defer: single-atom-only, no boolean operators in v1** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship the context field restricted to a single identifier or single key==value atom — no !/&/|, no compound parser/serializer.
  - 👍 Dodges precedence, parenthesization, Just(false), and canonicalization; covers the common scope (often a single id like Vim or Terminal); a single atom stays valid under any richer grammar later, so it's additively extensible. Cheapest path to SOME context scoping.
  - 👎 Cannot express the real engine AND-chains (EditorView & !IMEOpen & !Vim; gated ne! Terminal chains), so the catalog still can't be serialized back to text and users can't scope combinations. Still adds a config context field and a mini-parser that share the A2-Q8 interning and A2-Q5 override blockers — pays setup cost without delivering the catalog/export the question cites. Mostly defers rather than decides.
- **Serializer-first: decide Zed-style on paper, ship Display-only for the catalog, defer the parser (ADDED — recommended)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Write the canonical grammar now (Zed-style: ! > && > ||, left-assoc, parens, key==value atoms, explicit false token, canonically parenthesized so Display is precedence-unambiguous). Implement Display ONLY — over the existing &'static str engine predicates for the read-only bindings catalog/export. Do NOT add a user-editable context field or FromStr until A2-Q5 (override path), A2-Q8 (interning), A2-Q3 (schema field) are scheduled.
  - 👍 Display over existing predicates needs NO interning (you print &'static str, not parse into it), NO override consumer (read-only), NO schema field — genuinely low-effort and dependency-light, yet IS the 'serialize a predicate back to text' deliverable the question cites and the catalog (Axis 5 R9) wants. Deciding precedence on paper costs nothing and locks the canonical form. Because Display emits unambiguous parens, catalog text users copy is precedence-safe even under later precedence refinement — so the one-way risk is deferred to when the parser actually ships, by which time A2-Q8/A2-Q5/A2-Q3 are settled and a round-trip proptest can validate against the real predicates. Keeps the Zed-style familiarity win without front-loading the blocked, irreversible half.
  - 👎 Does not deliver user-authored compound predicates this phase — if Axis 2 context-scoped USER bindings are committed for the immediate next phase, this defers work that will be picked up shortly (a context switch). A paper grammar untested by a round-trip proptest could be subtly wrong (left-assoc / structural-equality trap), so the Display slice must include the fixture test over the real predicates. Shipping catalog text in Zed-style is a soft commitment to that syntax (mitigated by unambiguous parenthesization).

**→ Recommendation: Serializer-first: decide Zed-style on paper, ship Display-only for the catalog, defer the parser (ADDED — recommended).** The brief is right that Zed-style is the correct SYNTAX (the AST maps 1:1 to a boolean grammar and target users descend from Zed's predicate language), and that's upheld. What it gets wrong is treating the FULL Display+FromStr parser as Phase-1 critical path. Three verified facts flip the action: (1) a parsed predicate has no consumer — update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) is name-only, so per-context application is a NEW engine capability (A2-Q4/A2-Q5) that doesn't exist; (2) FromStr can't even construct the &'static str atoms until A2-Q8 picks interning; (3) there is no schema context field (A2-Q3) to parse from, and Axis 1 ships independently per-name without any of this (A2-Q15 lines 234,257). So the expensive, one-way half is blocked on three other decisions and should be DECIDED ON PAPER (free) but BUILT LATER. Meanwhile the cheap, dependency-light half — Display over the existing &'static str predicates for the read-only catalog/export — is exactly the 'serialize back to text' deliverable the question names, needs none of those blockers, and de-risks the catalog now. Crucially, if Display emits canonically parenthesized output, the precedence choice is NOT load-bearing for any text users copy, so one-way lock-in is genuinely deferred to when the parser ships and a round-trip proptest can validate it against EditorView & !IMEOpen & !Vim and the 12 ne! Terminal chains. Options 1/2 over-invest; structured YAML (3) trades author ergonomics for a problem this approach already neutralizes; single-atom defer (4) pays parser/schema setup cost without delivering the catalog. When the parser is eventually built, !/!= must ship with a presence-required or guarded-negation policy because NotEqual/Not fail open (context.rs:109-113).

- _Residual risk:_ If Axis 2 context-scoped USER bindings (and the A2-Q5 per-binding override path) are firmly committed for the very next phase, deferring the parser saves little and the team eats a context switch; front-loading the spec+parser then avoids re-paging the grammar. And shipping a Zed-style Display for the catalog is a soft public commitment to that syntax — if users copy catalog strings before the parser exists, you have de-facto committed precedence even without a parser (mitigated, not eliminated, by unambiguous parentheses). Finally, 'decide on paper now' is only as good as its validation: a paper precedence spec never run through the round-trip fixture test (left-assoc / structural-equality trap) can be quietly wrong — so the cheap option still carries the brief's recommended spike, just scoped to the print direction.
- _Dependencies:_ HARD upstream / co-decide before building the parser: A2-Q5 + A2-Q4 (per-binding/context-aware override path — the parsed predicate's only possible consumer; update_custom_trigger is name-only today, keymap.rs:422-433); A2-Q8 (atom storage: intern/leak vs Cow vs side table — whether runtime String atoms can inhabit the &'static str variants); A2-Q3 (the persisted schema must gain a context field for FromStr to read). PARSER must be designed jointly with A2-Q15 (Display must emit text this grammar re-parses — same precedence, false token, canonicalization). Bounded by A2-Q1 (documented vocabulary = legal atoms) and A2-Q10 (validate unknown atoms at parse time). MEANING (not syntax) constrained by A2-Q7 (per-view, non-merged eval — cross-level ANDs never match) and A2-Q9 (negation fail-open — !/!= need a presence/guard policy before exposure). Feeds A2-Q18 (settings-UI predicate editor). NOTE: the Display-only slice depends on NONE of A2-Q3/Q5/Q8 — it prints existing &'static str predicates read-only — which is why it can ship first.
- _What would settle it:_ 1) Sequencing (settles defer-vs-build): is Axis 2 context-scoped USER customization, and its A2-Q5 per-binding override path, committed for the immediate next shippable phase? If NOT (A2-Q15 says Axis 1 ships without it), defer the parser. 2) Cheap spike buildable NOW with zero dependencies: implement Display over the REAL predicates (EditorView & !IMEOpen & !Vim + the 12 ne!
