TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality chains) and assert it produces unambiguous, stable text — de-risks the catalog and pins the canonical form without touching the blocked parser. 3) Before committing FromStr: resolve A2-Q8 (interning) and A2-Q5/A2-Q4 (override consumer), then run the full round-trip proptest parse(display(p))==p using eval-equivalence or canonicalization (avoid the structural-equality / left-assoc trap) plus the real-predicate fixture test. 4) One design input from the keybindings owner: confirm Zed-style &&/|| over Rust &/|. 5) Decide the A2-Q9 negation policy (presence-required or guarded !/!=) as a precondition of exposing those operators to config authors.
- _Red-team added option:_ Serializer-first: decide Zed-style grammar on paper, implement Display-only for the read-only bindings catalog now, defer FromStr + the config context field until A2-Q5 (override path), A2-Q8 (interning) and A2-Q3 (schema field) are scheduled
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all five original grounding facts against code — every one holds (context.rs:9-18,11-13 AST; zero repo-wide FromStr/Display/serde; no Just(false) anywhere; &'static str atoms; mod.rs:200 + 12 confirmed ne! Terminal chains; flat keyboard.rs:169 schema). The brief's facts are clean. What I attacked is the FRAMING, not the syntax pick.

1) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY (decisive): the brief omits the dependency that makes the read path inert today — update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) takes (name, trigger) ONLY and rewrites ALL same-name bindings; there is no per-binding/context-aware override path. A parsed ContextPredicate has NOWHERE to be applied until A2-Q4/A2-Q5 land. FromStr is blocked downstream, not just upstream on A2-Q8.

2) DEFERABILITY: A2-Q15 answers (lines 234,257) settle that Axis 1 ships independently with the flat schema and no file-vs-UI gap. The grammar is Axis-2 critical-path, NOT Phase-1. With (1), the A2-Q8 interning blocker, and no schema context field (A2-Q3), a user-facing FromStr cannot be USEFULLY shipped now even if specified.

3) MISSING OPTION + decomposition: the brief fuses Display and FromStr. Display over EXISTING predicates needs no interning (you print &'static str, you don't parse into it), no override path, no schema field — it is read-only catalog/export and is the actual 'serialize back to text' deliverable the question cites. It is cheap and dependency-light. FromStr is the blocked, one-way, expensive half.

4) ONE-WAY claim refined: precedence is one-way only once USERS hand-author predicate strings. If Display emits unambiguous (canonically parenthesized) text, copied catalog text is precedence-safe even under later precedence changes; only un-parenthesized hand-authored strings are at risk — which don't exist until the parser ships. So 'cheap to fix now' argues for deciding the grammar ON PAPER now (free), not BUILDING the parser now.

5) MISSED SEMANTIC FOOTGUN: the operators this grammar exposes (! and !=) fail OPEN — NotEqual/Not match when the key is absent (context.rs:109-113 unwrap_or(true)). A2-Q9 is undersold: shipping !/!= to config authors without a negation policy silently leaks bindings into views that never set the key.

6) STRUCTURAL round-trip nuance: ContextPredicate derives structural PartialEq, so (a&b)&c != a&(b&c) though eval-equal; the proptest must canonicalize or test eval-equivalence, and Display must match Rust's left-assoc & to reproduce real predicates.

Syntax sub-verdict UPHELD: if/when a parser is built, Zed-style remains right. Actionable recommendation FLIPPED: do not build FromStr now; decide it on paper, ship Display-only.

### A2-Q10 — FLIPPED · _product_ · owner: Joint: Product owner / PM for the keybindings initiative as decider (it is a user-facing trust-vs-strictness UX policy), with mandatory co-sign from the warpui_core/keymap engineering lead — who is also the A2-Q9 negation-policy owner and the R9-catalog owner. Consolidating these is deliberate: the recommendation's key feasibility claim (that the safe-by-construction anchoring rule is registry-free and lifts the engine's existing 100%-gated discipline) is an engineering fact that changes the product tradeoff, so the eng lead must confirm it before the PM commits.

**Decision.** When a config keybinding predicate references a context atom that is not a known flag (typo, or a flag a refactor renamed/removed), do we hard-error and reject the binding, warn-and-keep with a load-time diagnostic, accept it silently with today's eval semantics, or first remove the eval asymmetry so that 'unknown atom' can only ever fail one (safe) direction? The reframe is load-bearing: the dangerous outcome the question names — an unknown negative atom silently firing EVERYWHERE — is produced by the absent-key asymmetry (NotEqual unwrap_or(true) / Not(absent)=true) governed by A2-Q9, NOT by the act of accepting silently. Fix the asymmetry and 'silent' degrades to 'binding inert within its scope', which is bounded and user-discoverable.

**Stakes.** This policy is the trust contract for the entire new scoped-keybinding config format, and the default the engine gives for free (silent) is genuinely unsafe ONLY while the eval asymmetry remains: a misspelled positive atom (Identifier/Equal) fails-closed (binding never fires), while a misspelled negative atom (NotEqual/Not) fails-OPEN (unwrap_or(true) / !absent=true) and seizes the key in every view lacking the key, with no feedback. The catastrophic direction is therefore owned by the A2-Q9 negation policy, not by this validation decision per se. Getting it wrong means exported colleague configs and power-user dotfiles either no-op or hijack keys after an upgrade, undetectably. The expensive trap to avoid is over-committing Phase 1 to a known-flag registry that does not exist (A5-Q2/R9 is an engine refactor) and whose only Phase-1 output would be an invisible log line (no in-app surface, answers:141).

_Constrained by:_
- Eval is asymmetric on a missing key: Identifier=>set.contains (false when absent), Equal=>unwrap_or(false) (false when absent), NotEqual=>unwrap_or(true) (TRUE when absent), Not(p)=>!p.eval (so Not of an absent positive is true). Unknown positive atoms fail-closed; unknown negative atoms fail-open — the DIRECTION of failure depends on the operator. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:103-114`
- DECISIVE / brief under-weighted: the engine ships ZERO bare negative atoms — every negative is AND-gated by a positive scope identifier. All 12 ne!("TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality","None") are ANDed with id!("Terminal"); every sampled !id! site is anchored by a positive id! (e.g. id!("EditorView")&!id!("IMEOpen")&!id!("Vim")). Because the positive anchor fail-closes outside its view, the fail-open negative is masked. This safety discipline is a structural/AST rule that needs NO registry, and can be required of config authors directly. — `A2-Q9 (answers:441,452,454) + app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:385-765 + app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:200-210`
- Greenfield: ContextPredicate has no FromStr/Display/serde anywhere, and the on-disk schema is a flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> over String with no context/when field. There is no existing config-predicate validation behavior to preserve, and a config-predicate PARSER (A2-Q8) is a shared prerequisite for EVERY option here. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:169 + A2-Q8 (answers:421-423) + A2-Q14 (answers:622)`
- There is NO known-flag registry / enumerable scope id today: ContextPredicate is an arbitrary 7-variant boolean tree, scope is private (EditableBindingLens.context, keymap.rs:311), and CommandBinding has no scope field. Building one is an engine refactor (R9). Therefore hard-error and warn-against-a-vocabulary both carry a net-new registry build that the symmetrize option avoids entirely. — `A5-Q2 (answers:1748,1759)`
- Predicates eval against a SINGLE per-view Context (no responder-chain merge), so 'unknown' is context-relative: a real atom like Workspace_SingleTab is valid in the Workspace view but absent/unknown in the code-editor view. A flat global allow-list cannot catch 'valid flag, wrong scope', so a truthful validating registry must be PER-VIEW — i.e. most of R9, not a small subset. — `A2-Q7 (answers:377,389)`
- keybindings has NO in-app parse-error surface: load_custom_keybindings only log::warn! and skips on an unparseable trigger, while settings emits SettingsFileError::FileParseFailed and has no keybindings analogue. So any warn-based policy delivers only an invisible log in Phase 1 — near-zero user-facing value until that surface is built. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:49-53 + answers:141 (A1 residual item 2)`

**Options:**

- **Symmetrize then defer (positive-anchor + key-presence; accept-silently; defer registry/warn to Phase 2)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — In the config grammar, (1) require every predicate to be positively anchored by >=1 scope identifier — mirroring the engine's own 100%-gated discipline (A2-Q9) — and (2) require key-presence for map negations (!=). Then accept unknown atoms silently with NO registry: an unknown positive atom fail-closes (binding inert); an unknown negative atom can only over-match WITHIN its anchored positive scope (bounded, never global). Defer the known-flag registry + warn/error + in-app surface to Phase 2 (or to whenever R9 lands). If Phase 1 ships no `when` field, this collapses to 'defer entirely'.
  - 👍 Removes the ACTUAL footgun (global hijack) at the lowest cost and with NO dependency on the nonexistent R9 catalog (A5-Q2). It reuses a grammar/AST rule you can compute without any vocabulary list, plus the A2-Q9 negation decision you must make regardless. Matches the VS Code 'unknown when-key => expression inert' contract the brief already blesses as acceptable. Fully reversible: a registry + warn + in-app diagnostic layer on later as pure addition, with zero behavior change to already-safe bindings. Honest about the engine we actually have and ships Axis 2 without blocking on an engine refactor.
  - 👎 Does NOT signal a renamed positively-anchored atom going inert — a shared/exported colleague config silently stops working within its scope after a refactor, with no diagnostic until the Phase-2 warn exists. The anchoring rule bans bare negatives, narrowing config expressiveness (though the engine itself never uses bare negatives, so this is consistent with how the product is built). Still depends on A2-Q6/Q7 to define and enforce 'positive anchor' in the grammar, on A2-Q9 for the negation policy, and on the A2-Q8 parser (all shared, none R9). Requires writing the AST anchoring/negation lint and tests.
- **Accept silently (naive status-quo eval, no symmetrization)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship the parser with NO atom validation and NO negation fix. A predicate parses if syntactically valid; unknown atoms flow into eval under today's asymmetric set.contains / map.get / unwrap_or(true) semantics.
  - 👍 Zero new infrastructure, zero dependency on R9, ships fastest, and matches VS Code's treatment of unknown when-keys for the positive case.
  - 👎 This is the worst case the question names, but ONLY because it keeps the asymmetry: a typo'd !Flag or `mode != insert` fails-open (context.rs:109-114) and hijacks the key in every view lacking the key, silently. Note the brief disqualifies this option for a footgun that its own phased recommendation also escapes only via the paired A2-Q9 fix — so the honest distinction is the negation policy, not the silence. Without symmetrization this is genuinely unsafe; with it, it becomes the recommended option. As-is it burns trust in the format on day one and forecloses any claim the config is safe to share.
- **Hard error at load (reject the binding against a known-flag registry)** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Validate every atom against a known-flag registry at parse/load; if an atom is unknown, reject that binding and surface an error, ideally in-app.
  - 👍 Eliminates BOTH silent-misfire directions and gives the clearest feedback ('line 7: unknown context Vmm'). Forces the catalog (R9) to exist, which Axis 5 needs anyway. Correct END state once the tooling exists.
  - 👎 Over-committed for v1. Requires the R9 catalog as a HARD prerequisite (A5-Q2/A5-Q3: no enumerable scope id, fixed bindings have no stable id) PLUS a per-view vocabulary (a flat global list gives false confidence per A2-Q7) PLUS an in-app error surface keybindings lacks (answers:141). It breaks exactly in the scenario named: when a refactor renames/removes a flag, every existing user file and exported colleague config hard-fails on upgrade (collides with A2-Q14 back-compat). Rejecting bindings is closer to one-way than costly: you must keep the registry exhaustive and stable forever, or you break users on every internal rename.
- **Warn-and-keep against a curated vocabulary, escalate to error later (phased)** _(high effort · easy)_ — Ship a small curated allow-list of supported atoms that doubles as the A2-Q7 vocabulary; parse succeeds; atoms outside it produce a load-time diagnostic (mirroring keyboard.rs:49-53) but the binding is still applied. Reserve hard-error for a later phase once R9 and an in-app surface exist.
  - 👍 Gives a real diagnostic the moment the in-app surface exists and leaves a clean, reversible path to strictness. The right Phase-2 DESTINATION once tooling matures.
  - 👎 Re-priced as over-built for Phase 1. Its Phase-1 user-facing value is an INVISIBLE log (no in-app surface, answers:141) — so in Phase 1 it is the symmetrize option plus a half-built registry whose only output nobody sees. The 'small curated subset' must be a runtime-checkable PER-VIEW artifact to avoid the A2-Q7 false-confidence trap — that is most of R9, so effort is HIGH not medium. And the warn's only actual Phase-1 SAFETY still comes from the paired A2-Q9 fix, not the validation: warn-and-keep must not mean warn-and-misfire. Needs the curated registry, the Q7 vocabulary, and the A2-Q8 parser up front.

**→ Recommendation: Symmetrize then defer (positive-anchor + key-presence; accept-silently; defer registry/warn to Phase 2).** The brief mislabeled WHICH phase does the work. The catastrophic outcome it raises (a typo'd negative atom hijacking keys in every view) is owned by the eval ASYMMETRY (A2-Q9), not by silent acceptance — and the brief's own recommended phased option only avoids that outcome by pairing with the A2-Q9 fix. Once you grant that fix, the decisive question is no longer 'warn vs error vs silent' but 'do we need a registry in Phase 1 at all?' The answer is no: A2-Q9 establishes the engine ships ZERO bare negatives — every negative is AND-gated by a positive id! scope (all 12 ne! gated by id!(\"Terminal\"); every sampled !id! anchored). Requiring config authors to follow that same discipline (positive anchor + key-presence for !=) is a registry-free AST rule that makes unknown atoms fail-CLOSED-only: an unknown positive atom renders the binding inert; an unknown negative can only over-match WITHIN its anchored scope, never globally. This removes the footgun at the lowest cost and with NO dependency on the nonexistent R9 catalog (A5-Q2), the missing in-app surface (answers:141), or a per-view vocabulary (A2-Q7) — all of which the hard-error and phased-warn options require. The phased option's Phase-1 deliverable is, in practice, this same symmetrization plus a half-built registry whose only output is an invisible log (keyboard.rs:49-53 / answers:141) that delivers ~no user value. So pay for the symmetrization (which you do anyway under A2-Q9) and DEFER the registry + warn + escalation to Phase 2, when R9 and the in-app surface actually exist. Crucially, this decision is also deferrable wholesale: it only bites once a user-authored `when` field ships (greenfield today, A2-Q8/A2-Q14), and that field should not ship before A2-Q7 + the parser land — so if Phase 1 omits `when`, A2-Q10 is not a Phase-1 decision at all. The phased curated-vocabulary option remains the correct END state and the natural Phase-2 step; it is simply over-committed for v1.

- _Residual risk:_ The symmetrize+defer option gives a shared/exported config NO signal when a positively-anchored atom is renamed by a refactor — the binding silently goes inert within its scope, undetectably, until the Phase-2 registry+warn lands. If config SHARING/EXPORT is a marquee Phase-1 trust feature, 'silently inert after upgrade' may be judged as unacceptable as the hijack case, which would force at least the warn (and therefore the R9 registry + in-app surface) onto the Phase-1 critical path after all — partially vindicating the brief's phased option. Secondary: the anchoring rule bans bare negatives, a real expressiveness narrowing of the config grammar (the engine accepts this internally, but power-user authors might chafe), and it presumes A2-Q7/Q6 land an enforceable 'positive anchor' grammar in Phase-1 time.
- _Dependencies:_ Whole-decision gate: A2-Q10 only applies once a user-authored `when` field exists, which requires A2-Q8 (the FromStr parser) AND a Phase-1 decision to ship `when`. If `when` is not in Phase 1, A2-Q10 defers entirely. Recommended (symmetrize) option depends ONLY on decisions you make regardless — A2-Q9 (negation/anchoring policy), A2-Q6/Q7 (grammar must express and enforce a positive scope anchor), and the A2-Q8 parser — and explicitly does NOT depend on A5-Q2/R9 (the registry), the in-app diagnostic surface, or a per-view vocabulary. The registry options (hard-error, phased-warn) ADDITIONALLY hard-depend on A5-Q2/A5-Q3/R9 (catalog + stable atom ids, an engine refactor that does not exist), an in-app keybindings analogue of SettingsFileError::FileParseFailed (answers:141), and the A2-Q7 per-view vocabulary — these are the Phase-2 costs the recommendation defers. Interacts with A2-Q14: the hard-error variant breaks back-compat for refactor-removed atoms in future shared files.
- _What would settle it:_ 1) Decide whether the user-authored `when`/context field is in Phase 1 scope at all — if no, A2-Q10 is deferred wholesale and this is settled (the decisive question). 2) Decide whether config SHARING/EXPORT is a Phase-1 headline — if yes, the 'renamed-positive-atom silently goes inert' residual may force the registry+warn into Phase 1 and undercut pure-defer; if no, symmetrize+defer is clearly correct. 3) Confirm with the keymap eng lead that the anchoring rule (require >=1 positive scope atom — which the engine already satisfies 100% of the time per A2-Q9 — plus key-presence for !=) is a cheap AST/grammar check; if yes, the symmetrize option is low-cost and registry-free as claimed. 4) Confirm whether the in-app keybindings diagnostic surface (parity with SettingsFileError::FileParseFailed) is budgeted — if not, do NOT pay for any registry-backed warn in Phase 1, since its only output is an invisible log. 5) Once `when` exists, run a dogfood scan of real/exported predicate files to measure how often atoms fall outside the (eventual) curated set; a low rate makes escalation to hard-error safe, a high rate kills it as too brittle.
- _Red-team added option:_ Symmetrize the eval asymmetry (mandatory positive scope-anchor + key-presence for negatives), accept unknown atoms silently with safe-by-construction fail-closed semantics, and defer the known-flag registry + warn/error to Phase 2
- _Red-team:_ Verified all 5 grounding citations against source (context.rs:103-114; keyboard.rs:49-53,169; A5-Q2 answers:1748-1759; A2-Q7 answers:377-389; A2-Q8 answers:421-423; answers:141) — all hold. Six challenges landed: (1) MISSING OPTION — the brief's worst case ("a typo'd negative hijacks keys in EVERY view") is a property of the eval ASYMMETRY (the A2-Q9 negation policy), not of silent acceptance. A2-Q9 establishes (answers:441,452,454) that the engine itself ships ZERO bare negatives — all 12 ne! and every sampled !id! are AND-gated by a positive id!("Terminal")/id!("EditorView") scope. Lifting that same discipline into the config grammar (require ≥1 positive scope anchor + key-presence for !=) makes unknown atoms fail-CLOSED-only with NO registry, killing the global-hijack footgun structurally. I added this as the recommended option. (2) DEFERABILITY — the decision only bites once a user-authored `when` field exists, which is greenfield today (A2-Q8/A2-Q14); if Phase 1 doesn't ship `when`, A2-Q10 is fully deferrable past Phase 1, and it shouldn't ship `when` before A2-Q7+the parser land. (3) INCONSISTENCY — the brief disqualifies accept-silently for the fail-open footgun, yet its own recommended phased option is only safe BECAUSE of the same paired A2-Q9 fix; once both get that credit, accept-silently's residual collapses to the milder fail-closed no-op. (4) RE-PRICED the phased option: its Phase-1 user-facing value is an invisible log (keyboard.rs:49-53 / answers:141, no in-app surface), and its "small curated subset" must be a runtime-checkable PER-VIEW artifact (A2-Q7 false-confidence problem) = most of R9, so effort is HIGH not medium; in Phase 1 it is Option A plus a half-built registry whose output nobody sees. (5) Promoted the A2-Q9 gating discipline from a footnote "pairing condition" to the decisive enabling fact. (6) Hard-error's reversibility is closer to one-way than "costly": rejecting bindings commits you to keeping the registry exhaustive+stable forever or you break shared files on every refactor-rename.

### A2-Q18 — FLIPPED · _product_ · owner: Product owner for the Settings/Keybindings surface (PM) — A2-Q18 is tagged user-product-decision — deciding jointly with the Axis 2 engineering lead and a designer. The PM owns the scope cut (defer editor vs ship D vs B/C); the eng lead sizes the substrate (dedup/lossless-save/R6/context exposure) plus D's Display serializer and BindingId-keyed in-session override path, and explicitly owns the call that BindingId is NOT a persistence key; the designer owns per-row context LABELING (humanizing composite predicates) rather than a preset taxonomy.

**Decision.** Once Axis 2's engine/file gain context scoping, what does the keybindings settings UI do about it in Phase 1? Options span: (A) make the existing UI scope-SAFE but the scoped rows read-only (defer any editor); (B) add a curated global preset context dropdown; (C) add a full free-form predicate editor at file parity; or (D) un-collapse same-name rows by their already-registered context and make each EXISTING scoped row editable in place, with no predicate authoring. The sub-question — curated picker vs free-form vs registration-derived — turns on whether the UI authors NEW predicates (needs parser + interning + a permanent grammar contract) or only surfaces and edits the predicates that ALREADY exist in code (needs only a deterministic serializer).

**Stakes.** This sets the Axis 2 UI scope boundary and decides whether the settings UI stays truthful to the North Star ("what the UI shows is the truth"). Today the editor shows one row per name and saves by name (keybindings.rs:759-782, keymap.rs:422-433), so the instant the engine/file gain context scoping the GUI both HIDES file-authored scoped overrides and RE-CLOBBERS them on save — re-introducing the exact bug Axis 2 exists to kill. The choice also decides how much of a net-new serialization/parser/interning stack (none exists today) gets built, and whether a predicate grammar gets locked into a permanent user-file contract. The under-appreciated stake: BindingId cannot be a persistence key (it is a per-run counter), so a serialized context in the file format is unavoidable for ANY restart-surviving scoped override; the only real lever is whether that serialization covers a CLOSED set of existing predicates (cheap, no parser) or arbitrary user-authored ones (expensive, permanent grammar).

_Constrained by:_
- CommandBinding has NO context field (name/description/trigger/action/group/id) but carries a BindingId; from_editable_lens never reads the predicate because EditableBindingLens.context is a private cross-crate field the app crate cannot read. A UI row today cannot represent or target a single scoped binding by predicate; it can only hold the in-session BindingId. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541,762-771; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:307-311`
- DECISIVE CORRECTION: BindingId is a process-global AtomicUsize counter (NEXT_BINDING_ID.fetch_add at construction); its doc says it is stable only so a trigger edit doesn't change identity WITHIN a run. It is renumbered every launch and whenever registration order shifts (feature flags, view init order, version bumps). It is therefore valid as an IN-SESSION override target but NOT a persistence key. The brief's 'target the BindingId' framing fails for the file: any restart-surviving scoped override MUST serialize the context, so BindingId does not let any option avoid a context serializer. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:262-272`
- The override/write path is name-only end to end: AppContext::set_custom_trigger(name,trigger) -> Matcher::set_custom_trigger(name,trigger) -> Keymap::update_custom_trigger(name,...) which filters solely by b.name==name across BOTH editable vectors and never reads context_predicate. Targeting a single scoped binding requires a NEW override path keyed by BindingId (in-session) or name+context. — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1637; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433`
- DECISIVE NEW FACT: the context vocabulary is NOT a small closed set. ~94 distinct context-predicate expressions appear in real with_context_predicate registrations, and most are composite, mixing view atoms with IME state, ~7 feature flags, drag state, and vim sub-modes — e.g. id!("Terminal")&!id!("IMEOpen")&id!("LongRunningCommand"), id!("Workspace")&id!(flags::IS_ANY_AI_ENABLED), id!("PaneGroup")&!id!("PaneGroup_PaneDragging"), text_entry&(id!("VimNormalMode")|id!("VimVisualMode")). The contexts available for a binding are per-registration, not a global Terminal/Editor/Vim list, so a curated global preset dropdown (B) is semantically mismatched and a designer cannot enumerate a faithful small set. — `grep with_context_predicate across app/src + crates/ (94 distinct expressions)`
- The '9 names' figure is the WRONG sizing denominator for an editor. 9 is the count of names whose DESCRIPTIONS differ so they survive name+description dedup (answers doc corrects this from 7 to 9; e.g. terminal:find 'find in block' vs 'find in terminal', plus two capitalization-only pairs). The actual scoping surface is the larger set of same-name+same-description bindings registered with DIFFERENT predicates across views, which today collapse into ONE row and are exactly the bindings a user would want to scope. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:759-782; docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-code-investigation-answers.md:234-269`
- ContextPredicate stores all atoms as &'static str with NO FromStr/Display/Serialize/Deserialize anywhere (repo-wide grep returns zero). Just(false) IS a representable enum variant (Just(bool)); only an authoring macro token for it is absent. A serialize-and-MATCH design (Display-serialize each existing predicate, string-compare on load) needs only the Display half and NO parser and NO interning, because it never builds a new 'static predicate from file text. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18,100-120`
- The persisted schema is a flat name-keyed map with no context field: CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger(String)>), the string being space-joined keystrokes or the 'none' removal sentinel. Any scoped persistence must add a serialized context to the schema (a BindingId column would not survive restart per the AtomicUsize fact). — `app/src/keyboard.rs:169,174,176-187`
- ConflictMap is keyed by Keystroke only (HashMap<Keystroke,usize>) and is context-blind. Any option that un-collapses rows by context (A, B, and D) will mis-report two same-keystroke/different-context bindings as a conflict unless R6 (context-aware conflict detection) lands in lockstep. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-145`

**Options:**

- **A. File-only; UI made scope-safe but scoped rows read-only (defer the editor)** _(med effort · easy)_ — Engine + file get context scoping (Axis 2 core). No predicate editor in the UI. Two mandatory UI changes: change row dedup from name+description to name+context so each scoped registration gets its own visible row, and make the save path lossless so it preserves file-authored scoped entries instead of clobbering them. Scoped rows render read-only with an 'edit in config file' affordance. Requires exposing EditableBindingLens.context to the app crate (private today, keymap.rs:311) and a schema that carries context for lossless round-trip, plus R6.
  - 👍 Smallest cut that does not regress and is the non-negotiable substrate under every other option. It needs NO write-side serializer (the UI never authors context, only preserves file-authored entries) and NO parser, so it de-risks the release and lets Axis 2's engine land now. Power users who scope already hand-edit YAML, so a truthful read-only-with-link row serves them. Fully reversible: the editor can be added later.
  - 👎 A read-only row a GUI user cannot edit in place is only a half-truth versus the North Star: the 80% case (rebind X only in the terminal, where X already has scoped registrations) stays visible but uneditable for GUI-only users, hurting discoverability and risking calcification as permanent debt. It is NOT a do-nothing option: leaving today's name+description dedup and name-only save in place would actively clobber scoped overrides, so the dedup change, lossless save, context exposure, and R6 are all unavoidable even here.
- **B. Curated global preset context dropdown** _(high effort · costly)_ — Add a per-row dropdown exposing a small curated set of named contexts (Global/Terminal/Code editor/Vim/...) that map to compile-time predicates; row dedup becomes name+context. Unlisted/arbitrary predicates authored in the file fall back to A's read-only row.
  - 👍 If a faithful small vocabulary existed, this would give GUI users a real in-UI editor while sidestepping a free-form grammar and arbitrary-string interning, and align with the curated-vocabulary direction A2-Q7 points toward.
  - 👎 The premise fails against the data: ~94 distinct, mostly-composite real predicates (IME/flags/drag/vim-submodes) mean there is no faithful small global set, and the contexts available for an action are per-registration, not global, so a label like 'Terminal' cannot deterministically pick among composite terminal registrations. The '9 names' sizing is the wrong denominator. Two costs the brief omits: (1) BindingId is not persistable (keymap.rs:264-272), so B still needs a file-side context serializer; (2) letting a GUI user create a scoping that does NOT already exist requires the engine to INJECT a new (name,context) binding (the A2-Q4 path), a substantial engine change. Plus taxonomy is a hard product commitment and R6 is required. Net: less viable and more expensive than stated.
- **C. Full free-form predicate editor (UI parity with the file)** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — A predicate-expression editor per row (&&/||/!/==/!=) round-tripping to/from the file. Requires the full A2-Q15 Display serializer + A2-Q19 FromStr parser, &'static interning/Box::leak, Just(false) canonicalization, and context-aware conflict detection.
  - 👍 True parity: the UI is exactly as expressive as the file, fully satisfying 'the UI is the truth' with no second-class rows and one coherent model.
  - 👎 Largest, riskiest surface and worst cost/benefit. Builds the entire serializer+parser+interning+grammar stack from scratch (none exists, context.rs:9-18) plus a boolean-expression authoring UX that is a usability minefield. Once users author arbitrary predicates that serialize to file, the grammar becomes a permanent public contract you must parse forever (one-way). &'static storage forces Box::leak/interning of unbounded user input, a slow memory-leak vector. Massive over-build for a capability almost no GUI user needs.
- **D. Editable scoped rows derived from existing registrations (serialize-and-match; no parser, no preset taxonomy, no inject path)** _(high effort · costly)_ ✅ **recommended** — Build on A's substrate, but make each un-collapsed scoped row EDITABLE in place rather than read-only. The context is shown as a derived, humanized read-only LABEL (no predicate authoring). In-session, the edit applies via a new override path keyed by the row's BindingId (resolving A2-Q3 as: BindingId in-memory). On disk, persist by (name + Display-serialized predicate); on load, Display-serialize each registered binding's predicate and string-MATCH to locate the target binding, then apply. This needs only the write-side Display serializer (A2-Q15): NO read-side parser (A2-Q19 avoided), NO interning/Box::leak (a new 'static predicate is never built from file text), and NO inject path (only contexts that already exist are editable).
  - 👍 Best cost/faithfulness point for Phase 1. It serves the real 80% case (rebind X only in the terminal) wherever X already has scoped registrations, which the data shows is the common shape: same name registered across views with different predicates, collapsed today. It offers EXACTLY the contexts that exist, eliminating B's taxonomy guesswork, the preset->registration matching ambiguity, the power-user cliff, and the inject engine path. Strictly cheaper than B (no parser, no interning, no taxonomy, no inject) yet more faithful to the North Star (every existing scoped binding gets its own truthful, editable row). The serialize-and-match trick dissolves the parser/interning problem that dominates C and partially burdens B.
  - 👎 Does not let a GUI user CREATE a scoping that does not already exist in code (e.g., fork a globally-registered action to terminal-only): that still routes to YAML or a later inject capability (A2-Q4). The Display serialization format becomes a contract for orphan-avoidance: if a binding's predicate changes in code, previously-persisted overrides for it are silently orphaned until re-saved (acceptable, same inert-override semantics as an unregistered action today, and migratable on load while the binding still exists). Still requires A's substrate + R6 + exposing the context field cross-crate. Humanizing composite predicates into readable labels (e.g. Terminal&!IMEOpen&LongRunningCommand) needs a small design pass.

**→ Recommendation: D. Editable scoped rows derived from existing registrations (serialize-and-match; no parser, no preset taxonomy, no inject path).** Ship A's scope-safe substrate (dedup by context, lossless save, expose context cross-crate, R6) as the non-negotiable floor — that part is unavoidable the moment Axis 2's engine lands or the clobber bug returns (keybindings.rs:759-782, keymap.rs:422-433). On top of it, ship D, not B. The brief recommended B on two premises the code refutes: that BindingId is the persistence target (it is a per-run AtomicUsize counter, keymap.rs:264-272, so the file MUST serialize context regardless), and that a curated set of ~3-6 contexts covers the need (the actual data is ~94 distinct, mostly-composite predicates over IME/flags/drag/vim-submodes, with contexts that are per-registration, not global). B is therefore both less faithful (a global label cannot deterministically target a composite per-binding predicate) and more expensive than stated (it needs the same serializer AS WELL AS a taxonomy and, to author new scopings, the A2-Q4 inject engine path the brief omits). D gives GUI-only users a genuine in-place editor for exactly the scoped bindings that already exist — the realistic 80% — while needing only the write-side Display serializer: the serialize-and-MATCH design (serialize existing predicates, string-compare on load) eliminates A2-Q19's parser and the Box::leak/interning that C demands and B partially needs. C remains over-build and a one-way grammar commitment. D's one real gap vs B/C — creating a NEW scoping in the GUI — is a separate, larger engine capability (A2-Q4 inject) that should be deferred; until then YAML serves it, consistent with D's read-only fallback for unrepresentable predicates. Deferability: A2-Q18 is alive only if Axis 2's engine/file scoping lands in Phase 1; if Axis 2 itself slips, this whole decision defers with it (Phase 1 ships today's name-only model with no regression because there are no scoped overrides to clobber). Even if Axis 2 lands, only A's substrate is strictly non-deferrable — D's editor can ship in Phase 1 or as a fast-follow once the Display serializer (A2-Q15) lands.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter: D cannot author a NEW scoping in the GUI (fork a globally-registered binding to a narrower context), and a meaningful slice of the real 80% case may be exactly that — a user who wants 'X only in terminal' when X is registered ONLY globally. For those users D is no better than A (route to YAML), and if that slice is large, B's ability to inject a scope (despite its cost) or a later A2-Q4 inject path becomes necessary, making D an interim rather than the endpoint. The coverage measurement settles it: if most desired overrides correspond to scopes that ALREADY exist as separate registrations, D is sufficient; if many require inventing new scopes, prioritize the inject path over the preset dropdown either way. Secondary risk: humanizing ~94 composite predicates into trustworthy per-row labels is non-trivial; a poor labeling pass could make scoped rows confusing even though editable.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated on Axis 2's engine/file context scoping landing at all — if it slips to Phase 2, A2-Q18 defers entirely. Resolves A2-Q3 concretely: override key = BindingId in-session (keymap.rs needs a new BindingId-keyed override path; current update_custom_trigger is name-only, keymap.rs:422-433) and name+Display-serialized-predicate on disk, bridged by serialize-and-match on load. Needs A2-Q15 (write-side Display serializer for ContextPredicate) but explicitly does NOT need A2-Q19 (read parser) or an interning/Box::leak strategy — the decisive cost reduction over B and C. Shaped by A2-Q4 (mutate-vs-inject): D deliberately scopes OUT inject (authoring new scopes), leaving it to YAML/a later phase. Shaped by A2-Q14 (context-less = all-scopes vs default-scope-only): defines dedup-migration and load-matching semantics for legacy name-only entries. Co-requires R6 (context-aware conflict detection) because ConflictMap is Keystroke-keyed and context-blind (keybindings.rs:104-145). Requires exposing EditableBindingLens.context (private cross-crate, keymap.rs:311) to the app crate. Shaped by A2-Q7 (curated vocabulary) for LABEL humanization only, not for an authoring taxonomy. Gates Axis 5 (settings UI discoverability): under A the editor is an Axis-5 follow-up; under D the editable scoped surface lands here.
- _What would settle it:_ 1) Coverage measurement, re-scoped to the right denominator: enumerate distinct (name, predicate) pairs across the default keymap AND a sample of real keybindings.yaml files, and split desired overrides into 'scope already exists as a separate registration' (D covers) vs 'requires inventing a new scope' (needs inject/A2-Q4). If the former dominates, D is sufficient and B's preset+inject machinery is over-build; if the latter is large, prioritize the inject path over a preset dropdown. 2) Eng spike on the A2-Q15 Display serializer + serialize-and-match load: confirm round-trip of the ~94 existing composite predicates is deterministic and stable enough that code-side predicate-edit orphaning is acceptable, and that match-on-load is O(n) cheap. A clean result confirms D; a hard result drops to shipping A as the interim. 3) Design spike: usability-test per-row editable scoped rows with humanized context LABELS (D) vs a free-form predicate field (C) with the power users who actually scope bindings — verify composite-predicate labels are legible. 4) Confirm Axis 2's engine/file scoping is in Phase 1 at all; if not, defer A2-Q18 with it.
- _Red-team added option:_ D. Editable scoped rows derived from existing registrations (serialize-and-match; no parser, no preset taxonomy, no inject path)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every cited anchor against source. All five original code_grounding facts hold (CommandBinding has no context field but carries BindingId, bindings.rs:531-541; EditableBindingLens.context is private cross-crate, keymap.rs:311; dedup by name+description, keybindings.rs:759-782; override path name-only end-to-end, app.rs:1637 -> matcher.rs:137 -> keymap.rs:422-433; flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger(String)> schema, keyboard.rs:169-174; ContextPredicate has zero FromStr/Display/serde, context.rs:9-18). Minor overstatement corrected: Just(false) IS a representable enum variant (ContextPredicate::Just(bool)); only the macro/authoring token is absent. The "50 names duplicated" figure was not independently re-verified; the load-bearing, answers-doc-confirmed figure is 9 names with >1 distinct description.

Two decisive facts the brief got wrong or missed, both of which break Option B's case:

(1) BindingId is NOT persistable. keymap.rs:264-272 shows BindingId is a process-global AtomicUsize counter (NEXT_BINDING_ID.fetch_add at construction). It is unique only WITHIN one run and is renumbered every launch / whenever registration order changes (feature flags, view init order, version bumps). The brief repeatedly calls BindingId "the natural target" the override uses. That is true only for the IN-SESSION override; the keybindings.yaml file CANNOT key by BindingId. Therefore EVERY option that makes scoped overrides survive restart still needs a serialized context in the file. BindingId does not let B dodge the serializer.

(2) The preset taxonomy B is built on does not exist in the data. I enumerated the actual predicates: ~94 distinct context-predicate expressions are used in real registrations, and most are COMPOSITE — e.g. id!("Terminal")&!id!("IMEOpen")&id!("LongRunningCommand"), id!("Workspace")&id!(flags::IS_ANY_AI_ENABLED), id!("PaneGroup")&!id!("PaneGroup_PaneDragging"), text_entry&(id!("VimNormalMode")|id!("VimVisualMode")). Atoms include IME state, ~7 feature flags, drag state, vim sub-modes, runtime flags like LongRunningCommand. There is no clean global set of 3-6 user-namable contexts. The "9 names" the brief uses to size the dropdown is the wrong denominator: 9 is the count of names that survive UI dedup because their DESCRIPTIONS differ; the real scoping surface is the larger set of same-name+same-description bindings registered across views with DIFFERENT composite predicates (collapsed to one row today).

Consequence: a GLOBAL preset dropdown (B) is semantically mismatched. The contexts available for a given action are per-binding, not a global Terminal/Editor/Vim list, and a preset label like "terminal" cannot deterministically pick among multiple terminal-ish composite registrations. Worse, letting a GUI user create a scoping that does NOT already exist requires the engine to INJECT a brand-new (name,context) binding — the A2-Q4 mutate-vs-inject path — which the brief omits from B's what_breaks. So B is both less viable and more expensive than stated.

This opens a cheaper AND more faithful option the brief missed (added as Option D): un-collapse same-name rows by their already-registered context, render a humanized context label per row, and make each row EDITABLE in place. In-session the override targets BindingId. On disk, persist by (name + Display-serialized predicate); on load, serialize each registered binding's predicate and string-match to find the target — so you need only the write-side Display serializer (A2-Q15) and NO read-side parser (A2-Q19 avoided) and NO Box::leak/interning (you never construct a 'static predicate from user text). D covers the 80% case wherever X already has scoped registrations, needs no taxonomy, no parser, no interning, no inject path. Recommendation flips from B to D.

---

## Axis 3 — Layering, precedence & unbinding

### A3-Q2 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team, with a sharper split than the brief stated. The ENGINEERING done-boundary (prove N-layer mechanism + real Unbound + assert the {default,user} truth table) is owned by the Axis 3 keymap/engine lead (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs + matcher.rs) and can proceed as a single-team spike WITHOUT a binding cross-team contract. The mode-pack-vs-Unbound POLICY (A3-Q5) is the cross-team/product piece, co-owned with the Axis 4 / modal-modes lead, and is explicitly DEFERRED to Phase-3 planning. The keybindings-initiative product owner adjudicates the phase boundary AND owns the prerequisite X5 meta-decision (is the unified resolver shared infrastructure built once before Phase-2 axis work) on which this depends.

**Decision.** Define the Phase-2 'done' boundary for Axis 3 layering by SEPARATING two things the brief bundled: (a) the engineering mechanism — prove the layer enum + generic resolution hosts N>=3 layers and that a real Unbound primitive suppresses correctly across all three match paths for the {default, user} case that is real today; versus (b) the product policy — the contentious A3-Q5 row (does a user Unbind block a key a higher mode-pack then re-binds). Ship Phase 2 when (a) is proven end-to-end against a synthetic third (mode-pack) layer fixture AND the {default,user} truth table is asserted, while (b) is encoded as a RESOLVER-EXPRESSIBLE parameter (both candidate policies shown to resolve) and the choice is deferred to Phase-3 planning when real vim requirements exist. Do NOT migrate real vim (the ~19 Vim-Fixed sites / ~168 user-reachable Fixed surface) and do NOT do the A3-Q3 stable-identity work in Phase 2.

**Stakes.** The end-goal names a three-layer order (default < user < mode-pack) but mode-packs are Phase 3 / Axis 4 and do not exist. The failure mode the question flags is symmetric: a 2-layer resolver that cannot host a 3rd forces a second precedence/matcher refactor; demanding real vim now drags the A3-Q1 Fixed migration and A3-Q3 identity work onto the Phase-2 critical path. A subtler trap the brief under-weighted: over-committing on the mode-pack-vs-Unbound POLICY (A3-Q5, a user-product-decision) before vim requirements exist freezes a guess that Axis 4 may have to re-decide. The genuinely unavoidable cost in Phase 2 is the real Unbound suppression primitive itself (there is none today — Trigger has only Keystrokes/Standard/Custom/Empty, and Empty shadows-not-suppresses), because the whole axis exists to let a user 'truly disable a default.' This decision gates when A3-Q4 (data structure) and A3-Q5 (truth table) can be called settled, but it does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis 1 hot-reload) — only the user-LAYER shape (A3-Q4), via Axis-1 reset bookkeeping, touches Phase 1; the mode-pack third-layer boundary does not.

_Constrained by:_
- No layer concept and no suppression primitive exist today: bindings() composes precedence as editable-first then fixed, each in reverse registration order (recency-only); Trigger has exactly four variants {Keystrokes, Standard, Custom, Empty}, Empty 'cannot actually be matched.' Both a 'user layer' and a real Unbound must be invented from scratch. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:454-464 (bindings order), :43-49 (Trigger variants)`
- Empty is SHADOW, not SUPPRESS: push_keystroke matches only `if let Trigger::Keystrokes` and keeps scanning, so an Empty override does not disable an underlying default/Fixed of the same key; match_custom additionally falls back to original_trigger, so Empty does not even suppress the menu path. Therefore a real Unbound primitive across all three match paths is unavoidable for the disable-a-default goal — it is NOT a differentiator between the layering options. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-337, :363-377; answers doc 'OVERLOADED Trigger::Empty IS SHADOW, NOT SUPPRESS'`
- The A3-Q5 mode-pack row ('can a higher mode-pack re-bind an unbound key') is explicitly a user-product-decision, and the brief itself calls mode-pack semantics 'genuinely undefined.' A stub that 'asserts the A3-Q5 truth table' can therefore only encode a GUESS — the same false-confidence flaw the brief pins on the Reserved-slot option. — `docs/specs/...readiness-questions.md:472 (A3-Q5 _Answerable by:_ user-product-decision)`
- A meta/X5 decision exists about whether the unified resolver (single bindings() walk applying layer + context + Unbound across all trigger kinds) is SHARED infrastructure that lands ONCE before Phase-2 axis work. If it resolves 'yes,' the N-layer proof belongs to that shared milestone, not to Axis 3 — a hidden dependency the brief's owner section omits. — `docs/specs/...readiness-questions.md:22 (meta question), :69 (X5 unified resolver as shared infra)`
- This decision does not block Phase 1: Axis 1 (hot-reload watcher + reset bookkeeping) is Phase 1; mode-packs/Axis 4 are 'gated behind Phase-2.' Only the user-LAYER data shape (A3-Q4), via Axis-1 reset bookkeeping, plausibly touches Phase 1 (X8); the mode-pack third-layer boundary (A3-Q2) is genuinely deferrable to Phase-2 planning. — `docs/specs/...readiness-questions.md:615 (Axis 4 gated behind Phase 2), :82-83 (X8 Axis-1 reset vs layer shape), :24`
- Migrating real vim into a layer is large and entangled: vim's forks are ordinary FixedBindings gated by id!("Vim") (enter→Enter under !Vim vs enter→VimEnter under Vim), 19 Fixed + 6 Editable Vim-context sites; FixedBinding has no name, as_lens hardcodes name=Default, BindingId is a non-deterministic atomic counter — so a real vim mode-pack needs A3-Q3 identity work, whereas a synthetic stub uses its own names and sidesteps it. Fixed surface = 397 ::new (409 all-constructor), 168 user-reachable. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:51-60; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286/:264-272/:633; answers doc A3-Q1 (19/6/409/168)`

**Options:**

- **Defer-2-layer (cheap/defer)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Ship only {default, user} precedence in Phase 2. Define no Layer enum and no mode-pack contract; let Axis 4 introduce layering when mode-packs land.
  - 👍 Lowest layering cost and fastest to the one user-visible win (a user truly disabling a default). Honest about speculative generality: the user 'layer' itself must be invented from in-place custom_trigger mutation. It also genuinely does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis 1 hot-reload), so it can be deferred to Phase-2 planning. And A3-Q5's mode-pack semantics are a user-product-decision that may be cheaper to settle once real vim requirements exist than to guess now.
  - 👎 Directly invites the second refactor the question warns about. Because there is no layer abstraction (keymap.rs:454-464), a 2-layer resolver hard-codes 'user beats default' first-match logic; inserting a third precedence band later means re-touching the matcher's three scan loops (matcher.rs:324/351/363). It saves little: the expensive, unavoidable part — the real Unbound primitive across all three match paths — is built either way; the only thing skipped is the cheap layer-enum + synthetic-3rd-layer proof, which is precisely the cheap insurance. Strands A3-Q4 and A3-Q5 as 'half-decided.' Hurts the Axis 4 owner most.
- **Reserved-slot (enum + documented contract, unwired)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Define a Layer enum {Default, User, ModePack} and make resolution generic over it, wire only Default+User, write the mode-pack contract as a doc/trait, but ship NO validation that a third layer actually resolves.
  - 👍 Cheap insurance against the data-structure rewrite: resolving over a Layer enum (A3-Q4 tag+stable-sort) costs little more than the 2-layer version, so Axis 4 slots in without re-architecting the matcher, while deferring identity (A3-Q3) and vim migration (A3-Q1).
  - 👎 An unexercised code path is an untested code path. The contract doc can silently disagree with the resolver, and the genuinely hard part — Unbound interacting with a higher layer — is proven by nothing. 'We left room for it' tends to mean 'we discovered at Axis 4 the room was the wrong shape.' Provides false confidence at medium cost.
- **Stub-validated N-layer (brief's original recommendation)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Define the Layer enum + generic resolution and prove it end-to-end against a synthetic stub mode-pack layer, asserting THE A3-Q5 truth table (default-bind, user-unbind, mode-pack-rebind) across the matcher. No real vim, no A3-Q3 identity work.
  - 👍 Validates the hard mechanism (three-layer precedence + Unbound suppression interacting with a higher layer) without paying the ~168-site Fixed surface or stable-identity migration, because the stub uses synthetic names. It forces A3-Q4 concrete and tested, and yields the deterministic harness Axis 4 reuses. It is the right instinct: prove the mechanism, don't migrate vim.
  - 👎 It over-claims. It says it 'settles A3-Q5,' but A3-Q5's mode-pack row is explicitly a user-product-decision (questions doc :472) the brief itself calls 'genuinely undefined' — so the stub asserts a GUESSED policy and returns false confidence, the very flaw it pins on Reserved-slot. It also requires a BINDING cross-team contract NOW (Axis 4 owner's time) for a Phase-3 consumer, and silently assumes the resolver is an Axis-3 deliverable rather than shared pre-Phase-2 infra (meta/X5, questions doc :22/:69). The unavoidable real-Unbound-across-3-paths core is the bulk of its cost; the incremental 3rd-layer proof is small.
- **Resolver-proven, policy-deferred (RECOMMENDED — added by critic)** _(med effort · costly)_ ✅ **recommended** — Prove the layer enum + generic resolution hosts N>=3 layers against a synthetic third-layer fixture, build the real Unbound primitive and prove it across all three match paths (push_keystroke/match_standard/match_custom + original_trigger fallback) for the {default, user} truth table that is real today. For the contentious mode-pack-vs-Unbound row (A3-Q5), encode it as a resolver PARAMETER, prove BOTH candidate policies resolve as specified, and DEFER the canonical choice to Phase-3 planning. No real vim, no A3-Q3.
  - 👍 Strictly dominates Stub-validated on cost and honesty. It buys the same data-structure insurance (the resolver provably hosts a 3rd layer, killing the matcher-refactor risk A3-Q2 flags) and delivers the actual Phase-2 user win (real Unbound = disable a default), but it does NOT freeze a guessed product policy: by proving the resolver can express EITHER mode-pack-vs-Unbound rule, changing the rule at Phase 3 is a parameter flip, not a refactor. It needs only the single-team Axis-3 engine spike (no binding cross-team contract negotiation now — just an interface seam), it respects that A3-Q5 is a product decision that may need real vim, and it surfaces the X5 dependency instead of hiding it.
  - 👎 Same residual as the brief's stub risk, relocated: a parametrization shaped only on a friendly synthetic layer might miss a dimension real vim needs (e.g., per-key block vs per-context block granularity), forcing a small resolver change at Phase 3. Mitigation: shape both the synthetic layer and the parameter on the real vim fork pattern (actions.rs:51-60). Also slightly more discipline than Reserved-slot, and it commits A3-Q4 now.
- **Real-vim-now** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Declare Axis 3 done only when the actual vim forks (19 Fixed + 6 Editable id!("Vim") sites) are migrated into a real ModePack layer and precedence is proven against real vim.
  - 👍 Maximum confidence: proves the contract against a real consumer, eliminating any 'the stub lied' risk, and if vim must become a layer eventually, doing it now avoids a context-switch and tests the hardest real case (same key forking under opposing predicates).
  - 👎 Over-scopes Phase 2 into Phase 3. It hard-pulls A3-Q3 (FixedBinding has no name, BindingId non-deterministic, keymap.rs:264-272/277-286) and A3-Q1 (the 19 Vim forks plus potentially the 168 user-reachable Fixed sites) onto the Phase-2 critical path, and forces layered resolution across all three match paths for real menu/Standard vim actions. Highest cost, latest delivery, couples two teams' timelines into one gate. Justified only if product confirms mode-packs co-ship with Phase 2.

**→ Recommendation: Resolver-proven, policy-deferred (RECOMMENDED — added by critic).** duplicate

- _Residual risk:_ The brief's original thesis survives as the strongest counter: deferring the mode-pack-vs-Unbound POLICY means you might discover at Phase 3 that your parametrization missed a dimension real vim needs — e.g., per-key 'block' vs per-context 'remove' granularity, or a mode-pack needing to re-bind across a user's Unbind in a way the seam can't express — forcing a small resolver change. This is the same 'the stub was too friendly' risk, relocated from the mechanism to the policy seam. Two things bound it: (1) shaping the synthetic layer and the policy parameter on the real vim fork pattern (id!(\"Vim\") at actions.rs:51-60) makes the miss unlikely; (2) even if it happens, you flip a parameter/branch on an already-generic resolver rather than re-architecting the matcher — strictly less bad than the data-structure refactor the cheap options invite. A second residual: if the X5 meta-decision rules the unified resolver is shared pre-Phase-2 infrastructure, this entire done-boundary narrows to 'Axis 3 consumes the shared resolver,' and the proof work moves out of Axis 3 — so this recommendation is contingent on X5 landing 'resolver built inside Phase-2 axis work,' which must be confirmed first.
- _Dependencies:_ PREREQUISITE: X5 / meta-question (questions doc :22, :69) — whether the unified resolver is shared infra built once up front; if 'yes,' the N-layer proof leaves Axis 3. CO-DECIDED with A3-Q4 (layer data structure: tag+sort vs per-layer collections) — must be chosen before the stub can assert anything. ASSERTION TARGET A3-Q5 — but only the {default,user} subset is asserted now; the mode-pack row is parametrized and deferred. SHARED (not a differentiator) the real Unbound primitive (R2) is a prerequisite of ALL options, since Empty shadows-not-suppresses (keymap.rs:43-49; matcher.rs:324-337/363-377). KEPT IN PHASE 3 under this option: A3-Q1 (Fixed migration) and A3-Q3 (stable identity). MUST EXERCISE A3-Q11 (uniform precedence across all three match paths matcher.rs:324/351/363) and A3-Q9 (Unbound vs original_trigger fallback :370-373) beyond keystroke-only. FEEDS Axis 4 (the contract it consumes) and should connect to A3-Q17's full-keymap resolution snapshot harness. NOTE: does not block Phase 1 (Axis 1).
- _What would settle it:_ First resolve X5: confirm whether the unified resolver is built inside Phase-2 Axis-3 work or as pre-Phase-2 shared groundwork (this decides where the proof lives). Then a ~1-day single-team Axis-3 engine spike: against the A3-Q4-chosen data structure, (1) build the real Unbound primitive and make all three match paths honor it (push_keystroke/match_standard/match_custom + original_trigger fallback); (2) register a synthetic third (mode-pack) layer fixture and assert the {default,user} truth table for the same key+context across keystroke + at least one of Standard/Custom; (3) prove the resolver can express BOTH candidate mode-pack-vs-Unbound policies (block-wins vs mode-pack-rebinds-wins) without choosing between them. Decision rule: if (1)-(3) pass WITHOUT touching FixedBinding identity (A3-Q3) or migrating real vim, ship the policy-deferred boundary and Phase 2 ships there. If the synthetic third layer cannot express vim-style id!(\"Vim\") contextual forks without the identity work, that escalates A3-Q3 into Phase 2 and shifts toward Real-vim-now. Product inputs needed: (a) confirm mode-packs are firmly Phase 3, not co-shipping with Phase 2 (questions doc :615) — co-shipping would justify Real-vim-now; (b) agree the canonical A3-Q5 mode-pack-rebind policy is deferred to Phase-3 planning when real vim requirements exist.
- _Red-team added option:_ Resolver-proven, policy-deferred (prove N-layer mechanism + real Unbound across all 3 match paths; defer/parametrize the mode-pack-vs-unbound policy)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every cited anchor by opening the files; all of the brief's grounding holds (bindings() order keymap.rs:454-464; Trigger has no Unbound, Empty 'cannot be matched' :43-49; FixedBinding has no name :277-286; as_lens name=Default :633; atomic BindingId :264-272; three independent match paths matcher.rs:324/351/363 + original_trigger fallback :370-373; 397 new sites / 409 all-constructor / 168 user-reachable / 19 Vim-Fixed + 6 Editable per answers doc). I did NOT find inflated grounding. The brief is directionally right but optimistic in three places, which I corrected: (1) FALSE-CONFIDENCE: the recommended Stub-validated option claims it 'settles A3-Q5,' but A3-Q5's mode-pack row ('can a higher mode-pack re-bind an unbound key') is explicitly tagged user-product-decision (questions doc :472) and the brief itself admits mode-pack semantics are 'genuinely undefined.' Asserting a truth table for an undefined policy hands back a GUESS as a test — the exact flaw the brief pins on Reserved-slot. (2) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY: the recommendation silently assumes the N-layer resolver is built inside Axis 3, but the spec carries a meta/X5 decision (questions doc :22, :69) about whether the unified resolver is shared infrastructure built ONCE before any Phase-2 axis work — if that lands 'yes,' the N-layer proof leaves Axis 3 and this done-boundary narrows. (3) EFFORT/SCOPE framing: real Unbound suppression is unavoidable for Phase 2's disable-a-default goal regardless of the layering boundary (answers doc 'Empty IS SHADOW, NOT SUPPRESS'), so the marginal thing A3-Q2 actually decides is just 'also prove the 3rd layer' (the cheap enum + synthetic-layer test) — and A3-Q2 does NOT block Phase 1 (Axis 1 hot-reload), since mode-packs are firmly Phase 3 (questions doc :615). I added one option that strictly dominates the brief's recommendation on cross-team cost and honesty, and shifted the recommendation to it.

### A3-Q5 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Keybindings product DRI, co-signed by (1) the warpui_core/keymap engine lead — the leak gate and the tombstone-ready resolver seam are resolver semantics, not UI; (2) the Axis 1 hot-reload/file-writer owner (A3-Q14) — because the additive on-disk entry-kind decision and 'keep name-scoped none' both touch the file writer and the applied-custom-names bookkeeping; and (3) the Axis 4 mode-pack/vim owner — the deferred 'mode-pack MAY re-bind an unbound key' clause defines the mode-pack contract and cannot be set unilaterally by Axis 3.

**Decision.** Define the precedence truth table for a user-layer Unbind, separating three concerns the brief bundled: (1) is Unbind name-scoped (remove the same-name binding) or key-scoped (block all bindings on key+context); (2) may a higher mode-pack layer re-bind a key the user unbound; and (3) the orthogonal, ship-now correctness bug that a Custom/menu unbind is a silent no-op (match_custom still fires the original_trigger). The eventual semantic for disabling the 168 nameless Fixed editor keys is a key-scoped block; the near-term Phase-2 shipment is name-scoped unbind plus the leak fix, with the key-scoped tombstone designed (not yet persisted) as an ADDITIVE on-disk entry-kind so named unbinds never need re-migration.

**Stakes.** This is the semantic the axis exists to deliver — "a user can truly disable a default" (design doc line 432). Get it wrong and either (a) the matcher's first-match-wins (matcher.rs:324-338) yields ad-hoc results when default<user<mode-pack disagree, or (b) the headline "disable a default" promise silently fails for the 168 user-reachable nameless Fixed editor keys (A3-Q1). A live, shippable correctness leak also rides here: unbinding a menu/Custom action today is a silent no-op (match_custom matches original_trigger, matcher.rs:370-373) — but this is ORTHOGONAL to the precedence question and is fixable on its own now. The brief's stakes claim (c) — "the wrong data model forces a second refactor when Axis 4 lands" — is materially weaker than stated: the on-disk format is additive (no version field; name-scoped and key-scoped entries coexist, A3-Q15), and the in-memory tombstone is never persisted, so deferring the contested half forces an engineering refactor at most, never a data migration. The genuine cost of getting it wrong is a name-scoped-only IN-MEMORY resolver that has to be reshaped when fixed-key disable lands, not a file migration.

_Constrained by:_
- match_standard has NO original_trigger fallback (it only matches Trigger::Standard); the fallback exists ONLY in match_custom (the single live dispatch leak) plus two non-dispatch lookup helpers (default_binding_for_custom_action, binding_for_custom_action_in_context). So the brief's 'gate original_trigger across all three match paths' is wrong: match_standard is not one of them, and only match_custom fires a disabled action. — `matcher.rs:350-359 (match_standard, no original_trigger); matcher.rs:363-377 (match_custom :370-373); matcher.rs:239,257 (lookup helpers)`
- The Custom/menu-unbind leak is name-scoped and orthogonal to any tombstone: gating the match_custom fallback on !trigger.is_empty() closes it, because Rebound (trigger=Keystrokes) and Unbound (trigger=Empty) are discriminable via Trigger::is_empty. This fix ships in EVERY option and does not require the key-scoped block. — `A3-Q9 (matcher.rs:363-377; keymap.rs:52-54)`
- A key-scoped tombstone is keystroke-scoped, so it only affects push_keystroke; match_standard/match_custom resolve by action identity (Standard/Custom triggers are not keystrokes), so the tombstone needs no wiring there. The brief's 'apply suppression once so all three match paths inherit it' conflates the keystroke tombstone with the Custom-resolver leak. — `matcher.rs:324 (push_keystroke keystroke scan), :350-377 (standard/custom resolve by action)`
- The on-disk file is a HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> keyed by binding NAME with NO version/schema field; name-scoped 'none' is platform-portable and costs nothing, while a key-scoped tombstone is a NEW entry-kind that stores a resolved per-OS keystroke (cmdorctrl baked at parse). The two representations are ORTHOGONAL and additively composable, so shipping name-scoped now does NOT force re-migrating named unbinds when tombstones are added — defeating the brief's stated reason to persist tombstones immediately. — `A3-Q15 (keyboard.rs:15,167-169; keymap.rs:914-919)`
- The enter fork is TWO nameless Fixed bindings under opposing predicates — enter->Enter under (editable_state & !Vim) vs enter->VimEnter under (text_entry & Vim). Both are FixedBinding (no name) today, so a name-scoped unbind can reach NEITHER fork, and disabling one fork without the other requires Axis 2's context-scoped override (hard dependency A3-Q13). — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:47-60 (verified verbatim)`
- 168 of 397 Fixed sites are user-reachable nameless editor nav/edit keys; only a key-scoped block can reach them. Caveat the brief omits: a handful have editable aliases (terminal input binds bare 'tab' editable; Delete has an editable ctrl-d alias), so 'name-scoped disables ZERO of 168' is ~accurate for the bare nav keys but not literally all. — `A3-Q1 (keymap.rs:277-286); A3-Q15 verification note (terminal input bare 'tab'; ctrl-d Delete alias)`

**Options:**

- **A. Name-scoped remove (formalize today's behavior)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Unbind = remove the same-name binding's trigger only (today's 'none'/Trigger::Empty). It does not block a lower-layer binding on the same key; a mode-pack re-binds freely because the higher layer wins.
  - 👍 Zero migration: name-keyed 'none' keeps meaning with no schema bump and no break to existing keybindings.yaml (A3-Q15). Matches the existing matcher and the name-keyed override path and composes with the per-binding Tracked invalidation that makes live-apply work (A3-Q7). Mode-pack-wins falls out of the stated ordering for free.
  - 👎 Does not deliver the axis's reason to exist: nameless Fixed bindings (keymap.rs:277-286) mean a name-scoped remove can disable ZERO of the bare nav keys among the 168 user-reachable Fixed editor keys (A3-Q1). On a forked key, even if both forks WERE named, a name-only override clobbers both (actions.rs:47-60). Note: the match_custom leak (menu unbind no-op) is a SEPARATE bug fixed by the !is_empty gate, not by choosing A — A is not what leaves the leak open.
- **B. Key-scoped block / tombstone for EVERYTHING (mode-pack MAY re-bind)** _(high effort · costly)_ — Make every Unbind a key-scoped tombstone in the user layer for (key,context), including named bindings. Resolution walks high->low and stops at the first layer with an entry: a tombstone kills+blocks the default, a mode-pack that explicitly binds wins.
  - 👍 One predictable mental model that reaches all 168 nameless keys, keeps default<user<mode-pack intact, and is the eventual mechanism fixed-key disable needs.
  - 👎 HARDENED: persisting NAMED unbinds as tombstones NOW (the brief's 'on-disk form now') is a regression, not a head-start — it discards the free, platform-portable, back-compat name-scoped 'none' (A3-Q15) and forces resolving a name to a non-portable per-OS keystroke at unbind time. It is also unnecessary: the on-disk formats are additive, so you can add tombstones later without touching named unbinds. Plus it requires Axis 2 context-scope (A3-Q13) and a real mode-pack (A3-Q2) to even validate — neither exists. High build cost, one-way-ish on-disk commitment, for capability that cannot ship in Phase 2 anyway.
- **C. User-supremacy block (mode-pack CANNOT re-bind)** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Like B but user Unbind sits ABOVE mode-packs: once a user disables a key no mode-pack may re-bind it. Precedence becomes default < mode-pack < user-unbind.
  - 👍 Strongest user-intent guarantee ('off means off, even vim cannot steal ctrl-w'); attractive for power users carving keys for the terminal/OS.
  - 👎 Contradicts the axis's own stated ordering default<user<mode-pack (design doc line 432) and breaks the mode-pack contract Axis 4 depends on. Creates an incoherent asymmetry (user-rebind overridable by a pack, user-unbind not). Wrong as a GLOBAL default; defensible only as an opt-in per-key 'lock' affordance.
- **D. Defer (name-scoped now, design tombstone, gate fixed+mode-pack to Axis 4)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Phase 2 = name-scoped remove PLUS close the match_custom leak (!is_empty gate). Declare key-scoped disable of nameless fixed defaults and the mode-pack-rebind rule OUT of scope until A3-Q1's fixed->editable migration and a real mode-pack exist.
  - 👍 Matches what the code can do today; fixes the one real shipping bug cheaply with zero file migration; avoids designing mode-pack precedence for a layer that does not exist.
  - 👎 Leaves fixed keys undisable-able for now and punts the contested mode-pack half. Its ONE stated trap — 'name-only on-disk forces a later schema migration' — is largely illusory (the format is additive, A3-Q15); D under-specifies HOW to avoid the trap, which is exactly what Option E pins down.
- **E. Additive split (RECOMMENDED)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Phase 2 ships D's delivery (name-scoped unbind in the UI + the match_custom leak fix), but pins the on-disk and resolver strategy: keep name-scoped 'none' as the on-disk form for named unbinds (back-compat, portable, free), and RESERVE — design in the spec, do NOT yet persist — an ADDITIVE key-scoped tombstone entry-kind for nameless fixed keys. The in-memory resolver is shaped to admit tombstones from day one (cheap: a no-op code path), but no tombstone is written until A3-Q1 scopes fixed->editable and Axis 2 lands context-scope. Mode-pack-MAY-rebind is the declared eventual ordering, validated later against a stub vim pack (A3-Q2).
  - 👍 Delivers the headline goal's eventual mechanism (key-scoped block for fixed keys) without B's regression: named unbinds stay name-keyed/portable, existing 'none' files keep working with no schema bump, and the additive HashMap (A3-Q15) means the tombstone is added later as a new entry-kind with zero re-migration of named data. Fixes the only live bug (match_custom leak) now. Captures D's low risk while closing D's one trap by NAMING the additive split, and avoids B's cost of building/persisting a tombstone for a capability that cannot ship until Axis 2/Axis 4.
  - 👎 Still pays an IN-MEMORY resolver refactor (not a data migration) if the eventual tombstone shape diverges from the Phase-2 name-scoped resolver — mitigated by shaping the resolver tombstone-ready from day one, which reintroduces a little of B's up-front design. The contested mode-pack-vs-user-unbind half stays unvalidated until a stub vim pack exists (A3-Q2), so A3-Q16 golden fixtures can encode only the {default,user} rows now.

**→ Recommendation: E. Additive split (ship name-scoped unbind + match_custom leak fix now; keep name-scoped 'none' on disk; reserve an additive key-scoped tombstone for nameless fixed keys; defer fixed-key disable UI to A3-Q1/Axis 2 and mode-pack precedence to Axis 4).** E delivers B's eventual semantic (key-scoped block is the only thing that reaches the 168 nameless Fixed editor keys — A3-Q1, actions.rs:47-60) while removing B's and the original brief's central error. Re-checking A3-Q15 against the code shows the on-disk file is a name-keyed HashMap with no version field, so name-scoped 'none' and a key-scoped tombstone are ORTHOGONAL, additively composable entry-kinds. That single fact dissolves the brief's load-bearing claim that you must 'adopt the tombstone as the on-disk form NOW to avoid a re-migration': you don't, and doing so actively regresses named unbinds from a free, portable, back-compat 'none' to a non-portable per-OS keystroke. Named unbinds should STAY name-scoped on disk; the tombstone is only needed for nameless fixed keys, which aren't expressible today and are gated on A3-Q1's fixed->editable scope decision anyway.

Two grounding corrections also lower the Phase-2 cost below the brief's estimate. First, the live correctness leak (menu/Custom unbind is a no-op) is fixed by gating the original_trigger fallback on !trigger.is_empty() in match_custom ALONE — match_standard has no such fallback (matcher.rs:350-359), and the two sibling resolvers (:239,:257) are non-dispatch lookups where the original_trigger arguably should stay (they report the overridden default to the UI). Second, the tombstone is keystroke-scoped, so it only touches push_keystroke, not all three paths. So Phase 2 is genuinely low effort: a one-resolver leak gate, formalizing name-scoped 'none', and a no-op tombstone-ready resolver seam.

A is rejected because it never reaches the fixed keys; B because it over-builds and regresses portability for capability that cannot ship until Axis 2/Axis 4; C because it inverts the stated ordering and breaks the mode-pack contract (keep it only as an opt-in per-key lock, never the default). The contested half — 'may a vim pack I later enable reclaim a key I disabled?' — is genuinely undecidable until a mode-pack exists, and E correctly defers it without blocking Phase 2. Net: the eventual key-scoped truth table is upheld, but the decision that must be ratified NOW is much smaller than the brief implied — keep 'none' name-scoped, fix the match_custom leak, and reserve (do not persist) an additive tombstone.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the in-memory / A3-Q4 angle: even though the on-disk format is additive, the IN-MEMORY layered resolver must eventually support tombstones, and if the Phase-2 name-scoped resolver is shaped differently from the eventual tombstone resolver, fixed-key disable still triggers an engineering refactor (not a data migration). The mitigation — design the resolver tombstone-ready from day one — quietly reintroduces part of B's up-front cost, so E's 'low effort' holds only if that seam stays a genuine no-op rather than a half-built block path. Secondarily, deferring the mode-pack-vs-user-unbind clause means A3-Q16 golden fixtures cannot encode the full 8-row truth table until a stub vim pack (A3-Q2) exists, so the contested half stays unproven and genuinely re-opens at Axis 4 — E narrows, but does not eliminate, that re-litigation.
- _Dependencies:_ Ship-now (Phase 2) has only ONE hard dependency, already settled: A3-Q9 (gate the match_custom original_trigger fallback on !is_empty). Everything else is deferred or co-decided: A3-Q4 (resolver layer store — must admit a tombstone seam without yet exercising it), A3-Q12 (unify the four off-switches so enabled/context-false render 'unavailable' not 'user-unbound'), and A3-Q15 (commit to additive name-scoped + future key-scoped entry-kinds; explicitly keep 'none' name-scoped). DEFERRED, not blocking Phase 2: A3-Q1 (whether nameless fixed defaults are in-scope — the 168-257-site fixed->editable migration that makes key-scoped disable reach them); A3-Q13 / Axis 2 R3b (context-scoped override, required to disable forked keys like enter->Enter vs enter->VimEnter, actions.rs:47-60); A3-Q2 (a real/stub vim mode-pack to validate the mode-pack-override half). Corrected: the leak fix is NOT 'all three match paths' — match_standard has no original_trigger fallback; the gate is match_custom (dispatch leak) with the two lookup helpers (:239,:257) a separate UI-correctness judgment. This decision still gates A3-Q16 (golden fixtures encode the {default,user} rows now, {mode-pack} rows when A3-Q2 lands) and A3-Q19 (settings UI needs a 'user-unbound' state distinct from 'never bound').
- _What would settle it:_ Two cheap ratifications, not one big spike. (1) Phase-2 sign-off needs only the file-writer owner (Axis 1) to confirm the additive split: 'none' stays name-scoped on disk; a key-scoped tombstone is reserved as a future additive entry-kind; the match_custom leak gate ships. That can be ratified in one short review since A3-Q9/A3-Q15 are already code-confirmed. (2) The deferred half needs the explicit 8-row matrix ({default-bind} x {user bind / user unbind} x {mode-pack bind / none}, in same-name vs same-key form), de-risked by the A3-Q4 prototype spike: a layered resolver with a STUB vim mode-pack and golden fixtures (A3-Q16) for all 8 combos plus the enter-fork (actions.rs:47-60), proving a user tombstone blocks the default but a mode-pack bind wins. The only genuinely user-facing unknown — 'when I disable a key, may a vim mode-pack I later enable use it?' — is settled by a power-user/dogfood poll plus the Axis 4 owner's contract call, and does not block Phase 2. The key-vs-name half is settled the moment A3-Q1 commits to (or declines) disabling nameless fixed editor keys.
- _Red-team added option:_ E. Additive split — ship name-scoped unbind + the match_custom leak fix now, keep name-scoped 'none' on disk, RESERVE an additive key-scoped tombstone entry-kind for nameless fixed keys (no pre-migration)
- _Red-team:_ Three substantive corrections, one new option, one changed recommendation.

(1) RECOMMENDED OPTION CHANGED B -> E (additive split). The brief's literal instruction "adopt the KEY-SCOPED TOMBSTONE as the on-disk form NOW" is self-contradictory and a regression: A3-Q15 (re-verified) shows the file is a HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> keyed by binding NAME with NO version field, and name-scoped 'none' is platform-portable and free, whereas a tombstone must store a resolved per-OS keystroke (keymap.rs:914-919).
