Persisting NAMED unbinds as tombstones therefore (a) breaks back-compat with existing 'none' entries, (b) loses portability, and (c) is unnecessary, because the two representations are ORTHOGONAL and additively composable. You can add a key-scoped tombstone entry-kind later WITHOUT re-migrating named unbinds. So D's only stated trap ("name-only on-disk forces re-migration") is largely illusory, and B-now over-builds. The correct synthesis keeps name-scoped 'none' on disk and RESERVES (designs, does not persist) the tombstone -> that is the new Option E.

(2) GROUNDING FIX on the original_trigger leak. The brief says "gate original_trigger on !is_empty across all three match paths (A3-Q9/Q11)." Verified false: match_standard (matcher.rs:350-359) has no original_trigger fallback. The fallback lives in match_custom (the only live DISPATCH leak, :370-373) plus two NON-dispatch lookup helpers (:239, :257) whose original_trigger may legitimately stay (they report the overridden default to the UI). The leak that "fires a disabled menu action" is match_custom alone, is name-scoped, is ORTHOGONAL to any tombstone, and ships in every option. The brief conflates A3-Q9's "three Custom resolvers" with A3-Q11's "three match paths."

(3) HAND-WAVING FIX on tombstone scope. B's summary ("suppression applied once at the resolver so all three match paths inherit it") is imprecise: a key-scoped tombstone is keystroke-scoped, so it only matters for push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324). match_standard/match_custom resolve by action identity, not keystroke, so a tombstone needs no wiring there. The brief muddles the keystroke tombstone with the Custom-resolver leak.

(4) DEFERABILITY raised. Because the on-disk format is additive and the in-memory tombstone is never persisted, deferring the full precedence truth table does NOT force a data migration later, weakening stakes (c) and the A3-Q4 "second refactor" urgency.

The decision's EVENTUAL semantic (key-scoped block is what disables nameless fixed keys; mode-pack-MAY-rebind as the eventual ordering; C only as opt-in lock) is UPHELD and sharpened. What changed is the near-term implementation/sequencing claim, hence 'hardened'.

### A3-Q13 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team: the keybindings tech lead arbitrating between the Axis 2 owner (R3b / context-scoped override) and the Axis 3 owner (layering, precedence, Unbound), with Product owning the load-bearing ruling — is per-MODE remap of Enter/Vim forks required UX, and which of the 168 editor-core keys are 'remappable'/'disable-able' in Phase 1. Must also loop in whoever owns the A1-Q1 ruling (whether R3b lives in Axis 1 or Axis 2), since that determines which axis the remap gate sits on and its delivery date.

**Decision.** Decide the Phase-1 shippable slice and sequencing. Confirmed: ship Axis 3's binding-layer store + Unbound + precedence rewrite for the NAMED editable set now, and gate context-AWARE fixed-key REMAP behind Axis 2's R3b (name+context override targeting). Corrected/added: (a) require the Unbound tombstone to be keystroke/identity-addressable (NOT name-only) from day one so fixed-key DISABLE can follow without rework; (b) recognize that fixed-key DISABLE (suppression) is separable from REMAP (re-assignment) — disable is gated on stable fixed identity (A3-Q3) and platform-keystroke handling (A3-Q15) but NOT on R3b, so it can be pulled off the cross-axis critical path; (c) settle A3-Q9 (does Unbound suppress original_trigger for Custom triggers) before shipping even the named-set Unbound, or the named slice itself leaks dispatch for menu/Custom bindings.

**Stakes.** Sets the shippable slice and sequencing for the whole keybindings overhaul. Get it wrong three ways: (a) ship a context-aware 'remap Enter' with only the name-only path (keymap.rs:422-433) and you cannot scope by mode — name-only override reassigns both the Vim and non-Vim forks; this is NOT state corruption (mutually-exclusive predicates, only one fires per context) but IS a loss of per-mode remap granularity, so if Product requires per-mode remap it is unshippable, and if Product does not, gating it behind R3b needlessly delays the most-requested feature; (b) block ALL user-visible layering/Unbound behind a 160-257-site fixed→editable identity+migration project that does not need to ship first (A3-Q1); or (c) the subtler trap the brief missed — over-couple fixed-key DISABLE to R3b, delaying the highest-SAFETY capability (kill a dangerous default key) behind a cross-axis dependency it does not need. The named-editable layer leaf is independently shippable, but its value is the precedence/Unbound resolver rewrite, which is HIGH effort with zero characterization tests today (A3-Q16/Q17) and must be designed to not foreclose key-scoped disable.

_Constrained by:_
- 168 of 397 FixedBinding sites sit under persistent user-reachable editor contexts (CodeEditorView/EditorView/RichTextEditorView/Terminal) registering the exact core nav/edit/selection keys users want to rebind, with Vim forks. Making them remappable is a 160-257-site fixed→editable identity+migration project, not a contained Unbound+precedence refactor. — `A3-Q1`
- The same key is duplicate-registered to opposing-predicate forks: enter→Enter under editable_state&!id!("Vim") (actions.rs:54) and enter→VimEnter under text_entry&id!("Vim") (actions.rs:59); backspace/delete/tab/shift-enter follow the same pattern. Enter and VimEnter are variants of one enum sharing a single TypeId, so context (not key or action) is the only disambiguator. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:51-110`
- The sole override write path update_custom_trigger is name-only: it filters editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings by b.name==name and assigns custom_trigger to EVERY match, with no context parameter. There is no way to express 'remap enter only when not in Vim' through it. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433`
- Fixed bindings have no stable string identity (no name field, as_lens hardcodes name="", BindingId is a per-run atomic counter never serialized) and a derived action+context id is non-viable (single enum TypeId collapses Move/Select variants; ContextPredicate is not Serialize/Hash). Any fixed remap OR disable first requires authored &'static str names or promotion. This identity blocker is shared by remap and disable; R3b is not. — `A3-Q3`
- CORRECTION to brief Fact 5: the named editable set needs zero new IDENTITY model (live-apply keys on name, A3-Q7), but the Unbound SEMANTICS are not zero-work. Trigger::Empty is documented 'cannot actually be matched' (keymap.rs:48), yet match_custom still matches a Custom-trigger binding via original_trigger — so Empty does NOT unbind the Custom-trigger class that menu/fixed bindings use. Rebound-vs-Unbound (A3-Q9) and unification with the other three suppression mechanisms (A3-Q12) must be designed even for the named slice. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:363-374`
- The fork predicates are mutually exclusive (!id!("Vim") vs id!("Vim")), so a name-only override that reassigns both forks is internally consistent — only one fork is ever active in a given context, so there is no double-dispatch. The deficiency of name-only remap is loss of per-MODE granularity, not state corruption; and DISABLE-all-forks is harmless (only one fork is active to disable). This makes disable separable from remap and softens the 'dangerous correctness bug' framing to 'cannot scope by mode'. — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:54,59`
- The precedence rewrite touches bindings() feeding all three independent match loops (push_keystroke matcher.rs:324, match_standard :351, match_custom :363) with ZERO characterization/snapshot tests today (A3-Q16/Q17), and R3b's inject-vs-mutate override path may itself hard-depend on Axis 3's layer model (A2-Q5). So Axis 3's layer is the UNBLOCKER for R3b, not a parallel track racing it — and the resolver rewrite is the high-effort cost driver shared by all options. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324,351,363`

**Options:**

- **Defer fixed-REMAP behind R3b; ship named-editable layer + Unbound now (with a keystroke-addressable tombstone)** _(high effort · easy)_ — Ship Axis 3's layer store + Unbound/tombstone + precedence rewrite for the NAMED editable set now. Gate context-aware fixed-key REMAP behind Axis 2 R3b. Hardening over the brief's version: (1) require the Unbound tombstone to be keystroke/identity-addressable from day one (not name-only) so fixed disable can follow without rework; (2) settle A3-Q9 (Unbound vs original_trigger) before shipping the named-set Unbound; (3) do NOT couple fixed-key DISABLE to R3b.
  - 👍 Cleanest correct slice. The live-apply machinery already keys on name (A3-Q7) so the editable slice ships with no new identity model and no 160-257-site migration (A3-Q1). For context-aware remap there is genuinely no code-correct path until (name,context) targeting exists (keymap.rs:422-433 + the mutually-exclusive forks), so R3b is a real prerequisite. And because R3b itself likely depends on the Axis-3 layer model (A2-Q5), shipping this layer first is the literal unblocker for R3b rather than a track that races it. Users get layering, Unbound, and conflict detection on the editable set in Phase 1; fixed remap arrives correct.
  - 👎 Phase 1 cannot promise 'rebind Enter/arrows' — the keys users most want (A3-Q1) stay fixed, a real product/messaging cost. The value delivered is the precedence/Unbound resolver rewrite, which is HIGH effort (not medium): it touches bindings() across all three match paths with zero characterization tests (A3-Q16/Q17) and must disentangle Empty/original_trigger (A3-Q9) and unify four suppression mechanisms (A3-Q12). If the tombstone is built name-only it forecloses fixed disable and needs rework (A3-Q15/Q19). And if Product rules per-mode remap is NOT required UX, gating remap behind R3b is over-conservative.
- **Decouple fixed-key DISABLE from fixed-key REMAP (ship disable gated on identity, not R3b) [ADDED]** _(high effort · costly)_ — Within the Option-A slice, add fixed-key DISABLE as a near-term leaf gated only on stable fixed identity (A3-Q3) and platform-keystroke handling (A3-Q15) — NOT on Axis 2 R3b. The tombstone suppresses a fixed binding's keystroke in its context; remap (re-assignment) stays gated on R3b.
  - 👍 Delivers the highest-SAFETY capability users want (turn off a dangerous default key) without waiting for a cross-axis dependency that might slip. The clobber danger that justifies gating REMAP behind R3b does NOT apply to DISABLE: because the forks are mutually-exclusive predicates (actions.rs:54/59), disabling all forks of a key in a surface is harmless — only one fork is ever active. Disable needs identity (A3-Q3, shared with remap) but not the (name,context) OVERRIDE write path (R3b) and not a new trigger field, so it is a strict subset of the remap migration. Corrects the brief's overclaim that fixed keys 'become unbindable only after R3b plus identity work'.
  - 👎 Still needs A3-Q3 identity (authored names or context-tag handles) for the in-scope fixed set and the platform-baked-keystroke problem (A3-Q15) — not free; effort scales with how many of the 168 you make disable-able. Commits a disable/tombstone file schema early (reversibility costly). Requires the A3-Q19 fixed-row UI surface so a disabled default is visible and restorable, or users disable a default and get stuck. If mis-scoped to keystroke-without-context it disables a key everywhere, which is too crude to be useful.
- **Bundle (ship R3b + fixed→editable migration + remap as one coupled release)** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Do Axis 2 R3b and Axis 3's fixed→editable identity migration + remap as a single coupled deliverable; no partial layer slice first.
  - 👍 Avoids a half-feature where layering exists but 'remap any key' is missing; forces the identity model (A3-Q3) and the (name,context) override (R3b) to be co-designed so the 168 newly-named fixed bindings and the override API are consistent from day one — no second migration.
  - 👎 Very large blast radius: couples a 160-257-site migration to a context-override API and a precedence rewrite on one cut, with zero characterization tests (A3-Q16/Q17). The precedence rewrite touches bindings() feeding every real binding across 109 files. Delays ALL user-visible value behind the slowest piece; high schedule-slip and big-bang-merge regression risk. Also internally circular if R3b depends on the layer model (A2-Q5) — you would build the layer first anyway, so the bundle gains nothing but risk.
- **Name-only fixed remap now (mint per-fork names, skip context scope)** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Give every remappable fixed binding an authored &'static str name — distinct per fork (code_editor:enter vs code_editor:vim_enter) — and let the existing name-only override target them, baking context into the name instead of using R3b.
  - 👍 Unblocks remap without waiting for Axis 2. Per-fork names mean the Vim and non-Vim Enter are independently rebindable through the existing name-only path (keymap.rs:422-433), arguably correct UX since they are genuinely different actions. Reuses the entire existing name-keyed override/persistence/Tracked stack (A3-Q7). And because the forks are mutually-exclusive, even shared-name remap is internally consistent (no double-fire) — so the 'clobber' risk is smaller than the brief implies.
  - 👎 Leaks internal fork identities into the user-facing config and file schema; 'remap Enter everywhere' forces the user to find and rebind multiple internal names. Once shipped, those names and the on-disk schema are load-bearing and one-way — hard to change without breaking user configs (A3-Q15). Commits to name-as-context-encoding, making a later clean (name,context) model a migration. Does not solve key-scoped disable of nameless defaults and re-introduces the platform-baked-keystroke problem (A3-Q15).

**→ Recommendation: Defer fixed-REMAP behind R3b; ship named-editable layer + Unbound now (with a keystroke-addressable tombstone) — combined with the added option's decoupling of fixed-key DISABLE from R3b.** Hold the brief's core call but harden it. R3b is a genuine prerequisite for context-AWARE remap: the editor-core forks are duplicate-registered under opposing predicates (actions.rs:54/59) and the only override path is name-only (keymap.rs:422-433), so there is no code-correct way to scope a remap by mode until (name,context) targeting exists. Meanwhile the named editable set needs zero new identity model (A3-Q7) versus the 160-257-site migration fixed remap requires (A3-Q1/Q3). So the cheapest correct slice is: ship layers + Unbound + precedence for the named editable set now and gate fixed REMAP behind R3b. Three corrections make this a recommendation a skeptical staff engineer signs off on. First, price the effort honestly: the value here is the resolver rewrite, which is HIGH (all three match paths, no characterization tests — A3-Q16/Q17) and must disentangle the Empty/original_trigger subtlety (matcher.rs:370-373, A3-Q9) and unify four suppression mechanisms (A3-Q12) even for the named slice — so 'medium/zero-work' was wrong; build the A3-Q17 startup snapshot net before merging. Second, fix the dependency direction: R3b is not a parallel track that races this; R3b's inject path likely depends on the Axis-3 layer (A2-Q5) and R3b's home axis is contested (A1-Q1), so this layer is the unblocker FOR R3b — which makes deferring fixed remap behind R3b low-regret, since you are building R3b's foundation regardless. Third, decouple disable from remap (the added option): fixed-key DISABLE is gated on identity (A3-Q3) and platform (A3-Q15) but NOT on R3b, and the clobber danger is remap-specific because the forks are mutually exclusive (actions.rs:54/59) — so require the Phase-1 Unbound tombstone to be keystroke/identity-addressable and pull fixed-key disable off the R3b critical path as the next leaf. Reject Bundle (very-high, couples the migration to the precedence rewrite with no test net, and is circular if R3b needs the layer first) and reject Name-only-now (one-way schema lock-in and leaks internal fork names — A3-Q15), while conceding Name-only is less dangerous than the brief claimed.

- _Residual risk:_ The decision hinges on one un-taken product ruling. If Product rules that per-MODE remap of Enter (different binding in Vim vs editing) is NOT required UX — i.e. users overwhelmingly want 'remap Enter everywhere' — then R3b is NOT a hard prerequisite for the marquee remap capability, because the forks are mutually-exclusive predicates so a name-only (even shared-name) remap is internally consistent. In that world, gating fixed REMAP behind R3b is over-conservative and needlessly delays the single most-requested feature ('rebind Enter/arrows') for months behind a cross-axis dependency whose home axis is itself unsettled (A1-Q1); a narrow name-only or per-fork-name remap could ship in Phase 1 at the cost of a file-schema commitment. The recommendation is robust only if Product confirms per-mode remap is required (or if the schema-lock-in of Name-only is judged unacceptable regardless). Secondary surviving risk: even with this slice, Phase 1 cannot advertise 'rebind any key', which may read as an incomplete feature.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated BY Axis 2 R3b for context-aware REMAP only — but R3b is itself likely gated on Axis 3's layer model (A2-Q5) and its home axis is contested (Axis 1 vs Axis 2, A1-Q1), so this option's layer ships FIRST and is the unblocker for R3b, not a parallel track. Fixed-key DISABLE is gated BY A3-Q3 (stable identity) and A3-Q15 (platform keystroke), NOT by R3b. Tightly coupled to A3-Q3 (identity for nameless fixed bindings — required before ANY fixed remap or disable) and A3-Q1 (which of the 168/257 sites are in scope — a product call). Must resolve A3-Q9 (does Unbound suppress original_trigger for Custom triggers — matcher.rs:370-373) and A3-Q10/Q12 (tombstone model and unifying the four suppression mechanisms) BEFORE shipping even the named-set Unbound, or the named slice leaks dispatch for menu/Custom bindings. The Unbound tombstone must be designed keystroke/identity-addressable (not name-only) so fixed disable can be added without rework. Requires A3-Q16/Q17 (a startup full-keymap resolution snapshot) as the regression net before merging the precedence rewrite. Interacts with A3-Q14 (user layer is also Axis 1 hot-reload's write target) and A3-Q19 (fixed rows must surface so a disabled default is visible/restorable).
- _What would settle it:_ One product ruling plus three cheap spikes. (1) Product ruling: must 'remap Enter' be context-aware (different binding in Vim vs editing)? If YES, R3b is hard-required for remap and this option stands as-is. If NO, fixed remap can come off the R3b critical path (name-only or per-fork-name) at a file-schema cost — re-weigh against Name-only. (2) Blast-radius spike: across the 168 editor-core fixed sites, count how many keys have opposing-predicate forks sharing a key — sizes the per-mode-remap demand and whether deferring is mandatory or merely prudent. (3) Tombstone spike (1 day): prove the Unbound tombstone can be keystroke/identity-addressable so fixed-key DISABLE follows without rework, and confirm A3-Q9 — that a real Unbound suppresses original_trigger (matcher.rs:370-373) so the named-set Unbound does not leak Custom dispatch. (4) Schedule check: confirm with the Axis 2 owner AND the A1-Q1 ruling where R3b lives and its delivery date, so 'gated by R3b' maps to a real schedule rather than an open scope contradiction.
- _Red-team added option:_ Decouple fixed-key DISABLE from fixed-key REMAP (ship disable gated on identity, not R3b)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all 5 grounding facts at the cited lines: keymap.rs:422-433 (update_custom_trigger is name-only, assigns to every same-name match, no context param — confirmed), actions.rs:51-110 (enter→Enter under editable_state&!Vim vs enter→VimEnter under text_entry&Vim, plus backspace/delete/tab forks — confirmed verbatim), and A3-Q1/Q3/Q7 in the answers doc (168 user-reachable fixed sites, no stable fixed identity, live-apply keys on name — all CONFIRMED, high confidence). The core recommendation survives: for context-AWARE remap there is genuinely no code-correct path until (name,context) targeting exists, so R3b is a real prerequisite, not an arbitrary gate. But the attack found four corrections: (1) Fact 5 overstates readiness — I read matcher.rs:363-374 and Trigger::Empty 'cannot actually be matched' yet match_custom STILL matches a Custom-trigger binding via original_trigger (matcher.rs:370-373), so Empty does NOT unbind the Custom-trigger class menu/fixed bindings use; the named-set Unbound needs real Rebound-vs-Unbound design (A3-Q9) and unification with the other suppression mechanisms (A3-Q12); 'zero work' is only zero IDENTITY work. (2) Option A effort is understated as 'medium': the layer+tombstone+precedence rewrite touches bindings() across all three match paths (matcher.rs:324/351/363) with ZERO characterization tests (A3-Q16/Q17) — that resolver rewrite is HIGH and is the real cost driver shared by every option. (3) Hidden dependency the brief inverts: R3b is itself potentially gated on Axis 3's layer model via the inject path (A2-Q5), and R3b's home axis is contested (Axis 1 vs Axis 2, A1-Q1) — so Option A's layer is the UNBLOCKER for R3b, not a track that races it. (4) The 'clobber = dangerous correctness bug' framing is overstated: the forks use mutually-exclusive predicates (!id!('Vim') vs id!('Vim'), actions.rs:54/59), so name-only remap hitting both forks is internally consistent (only one fork active per context, no double-dispatch); the real loss is per-MODE remap granularity, not corruption — which makes DISABLE separable from REMAP (added as a missing option). Verdict hardened, not flipped: the sequencing call is right, but effort, grounding, dependency direction, and the over-coupling of disable-to-R3b all needed correction.

### A3-Q14 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Axis-1 owner (config watcher / hot-reload: native.rs WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Keybindings + settings/init.rs subscriber pattern, A1-Q8) ships the ledger behind the trait in Phase 1, with the keybindings-initiative tech lead signing off on the 3-method trait SIGNATURE. The warpui_core keymap-engine owner (Matcher/Keymap) does NOT need to sign off before Axis 1 ships — their sign-off is required at Axis-3 kickoff, on (a) whether the layer impl backs the trait and (b) whether that layer must be Tracked per A3-Q7 (the engine owner's concern, not the ledger's). This removes the up-front blocking dependency the Shared-store option imposed.

**Decision.** Decide how the new applied-names ledger (the launch-seeded last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind> that hot-reload diffs to revert removed entries to default) relates to the future Axis-3 user-override layer: is it the SAME object (the layer's membership), a SEPARATE app-side store, or — the option the brief omitted — a SWAPPABLE store behind a thin set/remove/snapshot-diff interface that Axis 1 ships now and Axis 3 later re-implements with a layer backing. This is deferrable past Phase 1 (Axis 1 needs the ledger for R1 regardless; the layer is Phase 2); the only thing Phase 1 must lock is the API boundary, not the eventual owner.

**Stakes.** No applied-names ledger exists today (grep = 0 hits; override state smeared across per-binding custom_trigger with no file-vs-UI provenance, A1-Q8), so a new owned store must be introduced no matter which axis builds it. The report's R1 hot-reload (revert removed entries to default) — explicitly the biggest, lowest-risk papercut fix (report:138 R1, report:245 Phase 1) — is blocked on that store. The real exposure is NOT the catastrophe the original brief painted: the corrected analysis shows (a) distributed keystroke hints stay live in EVERY option because the reload handler mutates the per-binding Tracked override store via set/remove_custom_trigger, independent of the ledger's own storage (A3-Q7 binds the layer, not the ledger); and (b) there is no persisted ledger data, so a later unification is an in-memory rewire, not a data migration. The genuine cost of getting this wrong is narrower: two independently-owned stores can briefly disagree about 'what the user set' during the release where Axis 3 lands but the diff still reads Axis 1's last_applied, and an informal (un-typed) agreement degrades into duplicated bookkeeping. Both are cheaply prevented by a compiler-enforced interface boundary, which is why the decision can be deferred but the API cannot.

_Constrained by:_
- No applied-names ledger exists anywhere today (grep applied_names/active_custom/custom_names = 0 hits across app/ and crates/); override state lives distributed in per-binding EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger> with no provenance (a file-applied override and an in-app UI edit are indistinguishable). A new owned store keyed by name must be introduced regardless of which axis owns it. — `A1-Q8; keymap.rs:300; grep app/ crates/ = 0 hits`
- Both diff and nuke-and-reapply reload shapes require the same new last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind> (Keystrokes | Empty | absent), seeded from the one-shot launch load; DIFF is recommended. The seed point is the only load site, lib.rs:2557. last_applied is in-memory launch-seeded state, NOT persisted on disk (the on-disk format stays the flat CustomKeybindings map, keyboard.rs:169), so unifying it into a layer later is an in-memory code rewire with no user-data migration. — `A1-Q3; app/src/lib.rs:2557; app/src/keyboard.rs:169`
- The reset path that reverts a removed entry to default is remove_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger(name, None), name-keyed, mutating only editable_bindings/editable_custom_action_bindings filtered by b.name==name — it NEVER touches fixed_bindings, which have no name field. So a name-keyed ledger covers the editable surface but cannot express disabling a nameless fixed default; the key-scoped path (A3-Q3/A3-Q15) must be RESERVED, not built, by whatever store ships. — `A1-Q4 / A3-Q3; matcher.rs:146; keymap.rs:422-433; FixedBinding keymap.rs:277-286`
- CORRECTED APPLICATION OF A3-Q7: the Tracked requirement binds the OVERRIDE STORE (the per-binding Tracked custom_trigger today, or a future layer that overrides MOVE INTO and that render-time hints read via get_binding_by_name, keymap.rs:378-385), NOT Axis 1's diff ledger. The reload handler changes the live keymap only by calling set/remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-152), which route through DerefMut→track_update (tracked.rs:50-55). The ledger appears in neither the render path nor the dispatch path — it is only the diff-source. Therefore distributed keystroke hints stay live in every option whether or not the ledger is Tracked; the brief's 'non-Tracked app-side ledger silently breaks hints' claim is a conflation of two different stores. — `A3-Q7; tracked.rs:41-55; keymap.rs:378-385; matcher.rs:137-152`
- All editable bindings register exactly once at startup before load_custom_keybindings runs, and the in-app writers already emit the tri-state (in-app unbind persists 'none'; in-app reset DELETES the file entry => name absent). Only the reload READER plus its ledger are missing; a re-apply-on-registration hook is defensive, not required for correctness now. This means Axis 1's R1 watcher is self-contained — it reuses the existing Tracked mutators and adds a side ledger, touching none of the engine layer/precedence/Unbound work. — `A1-Q7 / A1-Q4; keyboard.rs:37-57; util/bindings.rs:511-513`
- A3-Q4 (whether a layer is a tag-field on the flat Vec or physically separate per-layer collections — i.e. whether the ledger can physically BE the layer) is an UNSETTLED readiness question, not a decided fact. Any option that commits 'the ledger IS the layer' up front is implicitly betting on an A3-Q4 outcome; an interface boundary does not require A3-Q4 to be resolved. — `A3-Q4 (design-readiness doc:461, open); A3-Q14 dependencies`

**Options:**

- **Unify-Axis3-owns (layer IS the ledger, Axis 1 depends on it)** _(high effort · costly)_ — The Axis-3 user-layer becomes the single source of truth: it owns the applied-names set + per-name kind, exposes set/remove/snapshot-diff, and Axis 1's watcher does nothing but call layer.reload(parsed_file). The 'currently-applied names' set is just the layer's membership.
  - 👍 Architecturally cleanest: exactly ONE notion of 'what the user has set,' so desync is impossible by construction, and the layer's diff-against-prior-snapshot is the natural producer of the revert-to-default set R1 needs. If you believe Axis 3 will land soon anyway, building the ledger twice is waste.
  - 👎 Forfeits the cheapest user-visible win: R1 hot-reload — billed as the lowest-risk Phase-1 quick win (report:138,245) — cannot ship until the full layer refactor lands, which drags in fixed-binding identity (A3-Q1/Q3: 168-257 nameless sites), the precedence rewrite (A3-Q4/Q6), Unbound state (A3-Q9/Q10), and the parallel custom-action collections (A3-Q8). If the layer slips, hot-reload slips with it. NOTE: the original steelman's claim that 'the Tracked invariant already lives there' is weak — the ledger doesn't need Tracked, so co-locating it with the layer buys nothing on that axis.
- **Two-stores (Axis 1 ships its own ledger now, defer unification entirely)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Axis 1 builds a standalone last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind> owned app-side (e.g. in keyboard state), seeded at launch, used purely for reload diff/reset, with no agreed contract. Axis 3 later builds whatever layer it wants; the two are not required to converge.
  - 👍 Cheapest path to R1: the ledger is a tiny in-memory map and the watcher has a proven settings template (emit WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Keybindings, handle in an init-side subscriber with &mut AppContext, A1-Q8). Ships in days, fully decoupled from any engine decision. Now that the Tracked trap is debunked and there's no persisted ledger data, backing this out later is a small in-memory rewire, not a migration — so the cost the original brief feared is mostly illusory.
  - 👎 With no agreed boundary, the moment Axis 3 lands you have two structures that can both claim authority over 'what the user set,' and during the release where Axis 3's layer ships but the diff still reads last_applied they can transiently disagree (a reset/precedence bug invisible in the settings page, which refreshes via explicit ctx.notify not autotracking, A3-Q7). The failure is a coordination/process risk, not a data-loss class — but it is real if the rewire isn't done atomically. Strictly dominated by Interface-first, which gets the same speed with a compiler-enforced boundary at trivial extra cost.
- **Shared-store-Axis1-first (one matcher-side Tracked store, full written contract up front)** _(med effort · easy)_ — Both teams agree up front a written contract: ONE write API (set/remove/snapshot-diff) AND ONE ownership location on the matcher/Keymap side, Tracked-ready, BEFORE either ships. Axis 1 ships the watcher against it now; the layer absorbs the same object later with no rewire.
  - 👍 In an org with weak cross-team coordination, a written contract + the engine owner's explicit buy-in is the surest way to guarantee Axis 3 actually adopts the API and the abstraction doesn't drift. Converts A1-Q8's open 'where is it owned' into a decided fact in one sync. If you're going to need the engine owner's sign-off eventually, getting it before any code ships removes a future renegotiation.
  - 👎 Over-commits up front given the corrected facts: it blocks Axis 1's start on an engine-owner sign-off to (a) put the store on the matcher side and (b) make it Tracked — but the ledger doesn't NEED to be Tracked (the override store does, and that's Axis-3's), and matcher-side is only one of three viable homes (A1-Q8). It also implicitly bets on an A3-Q4 layer-shape outcome that is still UNSETTLED — the brief's own what_would_settle_it admits the spike may have to escalate if the store can't be Tracked without deciding A3-Q4. So it pays a real coordination/blocking cost to lock decisions that are either unnecessary or premature.
- **Interface-first (ship the ledger behind a 3-method trait now; defer ownership/Tracked commitment) [ADDED]** _(low effort · easy)_ — Axis 1 ships last_applied in Phase 1 behind a minimal trait — set(name,kind) / remove(name) / snapshot_diff(prior, parsed_file)->revert_set — owned app-side (keyboard state or a tiny model), NOT necessarily Tracked because it doesn't need to be. The watcher and load_custom_keybindings (lib.rs:2557) write only through that trait; remove_custom_trigger-on-reload becomes 'remove from store.' Axis 3 later supplies a layer-backed impl of the SAME trait and, if its layer must be Tracked for render hints (its own A3-Q7 concern), makes its impl Tracked then. The only Phase-1 cross-team artifact is the agreed 3-method signature; the compiler enforces the boundary so it cannot silently degrade into Two-stores.
  - 👍 Captures the one-source-of-truth / no-desync benefit of Shared-store WITHOUT the up-front blocking sync, WITHOUT the throwaway of Two-stores, and WITHOUT committing to matcher-side+Tracked ownership or betting on the unsettled A3-Q4 shape. It satisfies every settled constraint: seeded at launch (A1-Q7), name+AppliedKind shape sufficient for diff-based reset (A1-Q3/Q4), and live hints preserved for free because the impl mutates overrides via the existing Tracked set/remove_custom_trigger (A3-Q7 — the trap simply doesn't reach the ledger). If the layer slips, Axis 1 still has a fully working reload; if it lands, swapping the impl is a code change behind a stable signature. Most defensible to a skeptical reviewer: nothing is locked that the code doesn't force, and the boundary is type-checked, not a promise.
  - 👎 A thin trait can leak the wrong abstraction: it is name-keyed, so it cannot express the nameless fixed-binding key-scoped disable (A3-Q3/A3-Q15) — but so is Shared-store's API; both must reserve room for a key-scoped path, and this is out of scope for the editable hot-reload surface anyway. If Axis 3's layer model wants per-context or per-layer membership (A3-Q4/A3-Q8), the trait's snapshot_diff may need widening — a signature change, but a localized one, not a data migration. Residual coordination still needed: the two teams must agree the signature, and if cross-team trust is low a written contract (Shared-store) may still be warranted on top.

**→ Recommendation: Interface-first (ship the ledger behind a 3-method trait now; defer ownership/Tracked commitment to Axis-3 kickoff).** The code forces a new owned store either way (A1-Q8), so the only question is one-vs-two and how much to commit up front. The original brief reached the right family of answer — one store behind one API, Axis 1 ships first — but justified it with a debunked catastrophe (the 'non-Tracked ledger silently breaks hints' A3-Q7 trap) and an overstated migration cost, which together pushed it toward an over-committed Shared-store contract that blocks Axis 1 on engine-owner sign-off and bets on the still-open A3-Q4 layer shape. Once corrected, the facts point one notch cheaper: distributed hints stay live in every option because the reload handler mutates the per-binding Tracked override store via set/remove_custom_trigger (verified matcher.rs:137-152 → tracked.rs:50-55), so the ledger needs no Tracked-ness and no mandatory matcher-side home; and because last_applied is in-memory launch-seeded state with no persisted artifact (A1-Q3), unification is later a code rewire, not a data migration. Interface-first keeps the genuine win — exactly ONE store reached through exactly ONE compiler-enforced API, so no desync and no informal-agreement degradation — while dropping the premature commitments: it does NOT require the up-front cross-team sync, does NOT pin the store as Tracked, and does NOT depend on A3-Q4 being decided. It ships R1 in Phase 1 unblocked, and Axis 3 absorbs the same trait with a layer impl later. Shared-store remains the right escalation if cross-team coordination is weak and a written contract is worth the blocking cost; Two-stores is acceptable but strictly dominated since Interface-first adds the type-checked boundary at trivial cost. This decision is deferrable past Phase 1 in the sense that the ledger ships regardless and the eventual owner is decided at Axis-3 kickoff — but the trait SIGNATURE must be fixed in Phase 1, because that boundary is the entire mechanism preventing desync.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the coordination one Shared-store front-loads: a 3-method trait agreed only at the signature level (not a written contract with engine-owner buy-in) can still drift if Axis 3's layer model turns out to need a fundamentally different diff shape (per-context or per-layer membership, A3-Q4/A3-Q8) — in which case Interface-first's 'absorb with zero change' promise becomes 'absorb with a signature change,' eroding part of its advantage over Two-stores. The trait is also name-keyed and genuinely cannot express the nameless fixed-binding key-scoped disable (A3-Q3/A3-Q15); that surface is deferred, not solved, by every option here, and if 'remappable fixed bindings' is pulled forward the boundary must be revisited. Finally, if the org's real failure mode is teams ignoring un-written interfaces, the compiler boundary helps but a written contract (Shared-store) is the safer bet — so the recommendation is conditional on at least moderate cross-team trust.
- _Dependencies:_ Directly answers A1-Q8 (where the applied-names set is owned — now: app-side behind a trait, re-homeable later) and A1-Q3 (diff vs nuke — both consume the same last_applied/AppliedKind shape this defines; DIFF). Constrains A1-Q4 (tri-state reconcile reads this store: keys=>set, 'none'=>set Empty, absent=>remove) and A1-Q7 (seed from launch load, lib.rs:2557). A3-Q7's Tracked invariant is RECLASSIFIED as a dependency of the Axis-3 LAYER (the override store), NOT of this ledger — so it no longer gates Axis 1. A3-Q4 (layer shape) is NOT a gate for Interface-first (the trait boundary sidesteps it) but WOULD gate the Unify and Shared-store options. Under-covers A3-Q3/A3-Q1 (nameless fixed bindings) and A3-Q15 (file schema for disabling a nameless default), which the name-keyed store cannot express and must reserve, not build. Must keep both parallel custom-action collections fed (A3-Q8) — handled automatically since the impl writes via the existing update_custom_trigger lockstep.
- _What would settle it:_ A short cross-team sync fixing ONLY the trait signature — set(name,kind) / remove(name) / snapshot_diff(prior, parsed_file)->revert_set — plus confirmation that load_custom_keybindings (lib.rs:2557) and the new WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Keybindings watcher (native.rs + init.rs subscriber, A1-Q8) write exclusively through it and that remove_custom_trigger-on-reload becomes 'remove from store.' Then a thin spike proving four things on real data: (1) a removed file entry reverts to default via the diff; (2) the spike issues the revert by calling remove_custom_trigger and a distributed keystroke hint invalidates WITHOUT the ledger being Tracked — directly demonstrating the A3-Q7 trap lives in the override store, not the ledger; (3) matcher state and the store never disagree after an interleaved sequence of file edits and in-app edits; (4) the 3-method trait is sufficient for a stub layer-backed impl to satisfy the same signature. If (4) reveals the layer needs a richer diff shape (per-context/per-layer, gated on A3-Q4/A3-Q8), escalate the signature — not the ownership — back to the tech lead before Axis 3 commits.
- _Red-team added option:_ Interface-first (ship the ledger behind a 3-method trait now; defer ownership/Tracked commitment to Axis-3 kickoff)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all 5 code_grounding facts against the answers doc and source — every file:line holds (A1-Q3/Q4/Q7/Q8, A3-Q1/Q3/Q7). The facts are sound; the brief's REASONING had two load-bearing errors I corrected. (1) DEBUNKED THE 'Tracked trap' on the ledger. The brief's strongest argument for one matcher-side Tracked store now — 'if the app-side ledger is NOT Tracked, the layer migration silently breaks distributed keystroke hints (A3-Q7)' — is a conflation. I confirmed in code that hints are driven by the per-binding Tracked<EditableBinding>.custom_trigger (get_binding_by_name→editable_bindings, keymap.rs:378-385), and that ANY reload handler mutates overrides through set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-152), which already route through DerefMut→track_update (tracked.rs:50-55). The applied-names ledger is in NEITHER path — it is only the diff-source the handler reads to decide which mutators to fire. So the ledger's Tracked-ness is irrelevant to hint liveness; the A3-Q7 invariant binds the OVERRIDE STORE (a future layer that overrides MOVE INTO), which is an Axis-3 refactor, not Axis 1 adding a side ledger. This removes the catastrophe framing from the Two-stores 'what_breaks'. (2) DOWNGRADED the 'reconciliation migration' cost. last_applied is launch-seeded IN-MEMORY state, never persisted (the on-disk file stays the flat keyboard.rs:169 map). Unifying it into a layer later is an in-memory code rewire (point the diff at layer.membership() instead of self.last_applied) with ZERO user-data migration — so Two-stores reversibility is 'easy', not 'costly' as billed. (3) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY surfaced: the Shared-store recommendation silently commits 'the ledger IS the layer' before A3-Q4 (layer shape: tag-field-on-flat-Vec vs per-layer collections) is decided — and A3-Q4 is still an open readiness question, not settled. The brief's own what_would_settle_it admits the spike may escalate back if the store can't be Tracked without A3-Q4. (4) DEFERABILITY: the binding decision CAN be deferred past Phase 1 — Axis 1 ships its ledger in Phase 1 for R1 regardless, and Axis 3's layer is Phase 2; only the API boundary must be fixed in Phase 1. Net: core thesis ('one store behind one API, Axis 1 ships first') survives and is strengthened, but the up-front matcher-side+Tracked+full-contract commitment is over-engineering once the Tracked argument is debunked.

### A3-Q18 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product DRI for the keybindings initiative owns the scope call (which Phase-1 honesty is required: warn vs block vs nothing). Because the live new-chord paths are hot-reload YAML and (later) G4/R8 capture, the Phase-1/Axis-5 eng lead owns the guard mechanism and its placement at the apply seam, with the warpui_core/matcher eng lead consulted on the validate_bindings hook and the concrete-context point-eval interaction (and owning the dispatch contract if a timing option is ever chosen). The Axis-4 modal-editing eng lead is the tiebreaker on deferral: as the only confirmed consumer of coexisting prefix chords, they ratify whether the R4 timeout can wait (it can — Axis 4 is Phase 3). The input-latency/perf owner signs off ONLY if a dispatch-timing option (global timeout or per-binding opt-in) is later adopted; the recommended guard touches no hot path so it needs no perf sign-off.

**Decision.** Whether the chord-ambiguity fix (a complete short binding always shadows a longer prefix-sharing chord via the early return at matcher.rs:329-331) is in-scope for Axis 3 now or deferred to R4, and — if in-scope — which mechanism delivers it: do-nothing/Defer, a low-cost apply-time shadow guard that surfaces the overlap honestly without changing dispatch, a full surface-as-conflict ConflictMap rekey, or a dispatch-timing wait policy (global timeout longest-match, or per-binding 'prefix' opt-in). The split forced by the code: the HONESTY half (stop the silent shadow) is in-scope for Phase 1; the COEXISTENCE half (make a short binding and a longer chord both fire, which requires a timer) is the genuinely expensive R4/Axis-4 work and should be deferred.

**Stakes.** The early return fires a complete short binding immediately and shadows any longer chord sharing its prefix, order-independently and silently — the exact 'the UI lies about what your keys do' dishonesty this initiative targets. CORRECTION to the brief: this is not a future risk that only appears once G4/R8 UI chord-capture lands — it is reachable TODAY. The keybindings file already round-trips multi-key chords (keyboard.rs:196-211) and applies them as full multi-key triggers (keyboard.rs:46-47: set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Keystrokes(keystrokes.to_vec()))), and hot-reload YAML is Phase-1. So a user who edits keybindings.yaml to bind an action to 'g d' while a default binding owns bare 'g' in the same context gets a binding that silently never fires — in Phase 1, before any deferred fix. Conversely, every wait/timeout-based coexistence fix converts the synchronous push_keystroke into timer-driven async dispatch, taxes the keystroke hot path with felt input latency power users notice, and collides with the pending-context-clear on mode switches (A2-Q12). So this is a real honesty-vs-latency tradeoff, and it gates whether Axis 3 can claim its stated 'predictable chords' end-state (end-goals:79-80). The honesty win is cheap and Phase-1; the coexistence win is expensive and belongs with Axis 4.

_Constrained by:_
- push_keystroke returns MatchResult::Action the instant a binding's full trigger equals the pending buffer (the else-branch only sets retain_pending and keeps scanning), so a complete short binding ALWAYS wins over a prefix-sharing longer chord regardless of registration order — the function returns before the retain_pending check is reached. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-345 (early return 329-331); confirmed by direct read`
- DECISIVE / brief-correcting: the new-shadow path is live TODAY via the file, not gated on G4/R8 UI capture. A file-defined chord is parsed into a multi-element Vec1<Keystroke> and applied as the full multi-key trigger: app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Keystrokes(keystrokes.to_vec())). The settings UI flattens captures to a 1-element vec (bindings.rs:492-494), but the file does not, and hot-reload YAML is Phase-1. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:46-47, :196-211; bindings.rs:492-494 (per A5-Q7 answer); end-goals-design.md:139 (hot-reload Phase 1)`
- No ambiguity infrastructure exists: Pending is a per-EntityId keystroke buffer, push_keystroke is synchronous and returns a MatchResult immediately with no timer/event-loop hook. Any wait/timeout policy is a change to the dispatch contract, not a local edit — and must resolve the pending-buffer clear on context change (matcher.rs:313-321) and via set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger (138/150), which strands a timeout-held chord across a mode switch (A2-Q12). — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:32-36,307-346,313-321,138,150; A2-Q12 (answers:506-544)`
- Correction to brief: 'ships into ZERO chord tests' is overstated. matcher_tests.rs has 3 tests but DOES cover chord pending + context-reset (matcher_tests.rs:13-66 and 97-158 per A2-Q12); the reusable test_keystroke helper + chord fixtures exist. Missing coverage is the shadow / longest-match / precedence / unbind scenarios specifically — so the harness foundation exists and the penalty against the timeout options is smaller than implied. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:5,13-66,89,97-158,179,230; A3-Q16 (answers:1183-1189); A2-Q12 (answers:526)`
- Correction to brief: the full 'surface-as-conflict' option is not cleanly Phase-1/medium. ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize>, built from editable bindings only and flattening chords to the first keystroke (keys.first()), so it cannot represent prefix-shadow nor see the fixed defaults that do the shadowing. A5-Q7's own answer states the required context-awareness 'is the Axis-2/R6 dependency' (Phase 2), and naming the colliding fixed binding needs A5-Q9 + a stable identity fixed bindings lack. So that option couples to A5-Q7 + A5-Q9 + R6/Axis-2 — effort:high, Phase-2-entangled. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-132; app/src/util/bindings.rs:236; A5-Q7 (answers:1906-1935, esp. 1916,1929); A5-Q9 (answers:1862-1903)`
- Scope contradiction the question settles: roadmap files R4 (per-context trie + ambiguity policy) as 'medium' and '(Optional)' and 'deferred behind cheaper prereqs', yet Axis-3 end-goal bullet 4 lists 'predictable chords / explicit ambiguity policy / timeout (L5, R4)' as part of the Axis-3 end-state. X12 separately argues binding-count growth may force R4 earlier. — `architecture-report:144,258,270; end-goals-design.md:79-80; X12 (readiness:107-111)`

**Options:**

- **Defer entirely to R4 (keep first-match-wins, no Phase-1 change)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Make no change in Axis 3. Leave matcher.rs:329-331 as-is, document short-shadows-chord as known behavior, and put the whole fix in the deferred R4 trie+ambiguity work.
  - 👍 Zero hot-path risk, zero added latency, no new tests for unchanged behavior. The report explicitly sequences R4 as optional/medium/deferred (report:258,270). If the broadened spike shows ~0 plausible prefix collisions among the 168 user-reachable contexts, the shadow is a non-problem and Axis 3 ships its high-value layer/Unbound/precedence work uncluttered.
  - 👎 Worse than the brief painted it: the silent-shadow path is reachable in Phase-1 RIGHT NOW via the file (keyboard.rs:46-47 applies a file chord as a full multi-key trigger; hot-reload YAML is Phase-1) — not merely 'before a deferred fix.' So Defer ships the initiative's signature dishonesty in the very phase that elevates the file to a first-class flow, and directly contradicts end-goal bullet 4 (end-goals:79-80). Forecloses nothing technically but leaves a known, user-reachable correctness gap.
- **Apply-time prefix-shadow guard (warn at chord apply/capture; no ConflictMap rekey, no dispatch change) — ADDED** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Keep the early-return dispatch exactly as-is (zero latency, no async). At the single point where a chord enters the applied keymap — set_custom_trigger / the file hot-reload apply path, reusing the existing validate_bindings seam (matcher.rs:181-203) — run a one-shot scan over the live bindings (get_bindings/bindings_for_context already exist) applying matcher.rs:326's starts_with against the binding's concrete context, and warn (optionally block) when the new chord is prefix-shadowed by, or would shadow, an existing binding. Same hook later covers the G4/R8 UI capture.
  - 👍 Targets exactly the only user-reachable path (file/hot-reload today, UI capture later) at the lowest cost: no change to the synchronous dispatch contract, no timer plumbing, no ConflictMap data-model rekey, and it sidesteps the Axis-2/R6 satisfiability gate by using concrete-context point-eval (in_context already exists) at apply time rather than general predicate satisfiability. It can name the shadowing binding from the lens at scan time without giving fixed bindings stable ids. Removes the SILENCE (the north-star) in Phase 1, rides with the hot-reload/capture work that creates the risk, reuses the existing validate_bindings hook + matcher.rs:326 predicate, and needs little of the A3-Q16 harness. Makes Axis 3 honest now and lets the expensive coexistence machinery wait for Axis 4.
  - 👎 Removes the silence but not the shadow — a warned chord still will not FIRE until R4's timeout lands, so 'predictable chords' is delivered as 'honestly reported as non-functional,' not 'works.' Concrete-context point-eval is best-effort: can miss a shadow that only manifests in a context the user is not currently in. Does nothing for default-vs-default shadows among the 397 fixed sites (the guard only fires on user-initiated apply), so if the spike finds those non-zero they stay silently broken until R4. 'Warn-but-allow' leaves a non-functional binding on screen; 'block' would forbid the legitimate standalone-key-that-is-also-a-chord-lead-in that Axis-4 mode-packs want — so warn (not hard-block) is the honest default.
- **Surface prefix-shadow as a first-class catalog conflict (full ConflictMap rekey) + block at capture** _(high effort · costly)_ — Keep dispatch as-is but rekey the persistent ConflictMap from HashMap<Keystroke,usize> to Vec<Keystroke> + an explicit strict-prefix index, union fixed bindings into the conflict universe, give the colliding fixed binding a nameable identity, and render the overlap as a first-class conflict in settings.
  - 👍 The durable, complete honesty surface: every shadow (including default-vs-default and default-vs-user) shows in the catalog continuously, not just at apply time. Aligns with Axis-5's north-star and reuses the ConflictMap as the one conflict detector in the codebase.
  - 👎 Not cleanly Phase-1 and not 'medium': ConflictMap today is editable-only and flattens chords to the first keystroke (keybindings.rs:104-132; bindings.rs:236), so it cannot even see the fixed defaults that do the shadowing. Surfacing the fixed-default-shadows-user-chord case needs A5-Q7 (rekey) + A5-Q9 (union fixed bindings) + fixed-binding naming/identity + R6/Axis-2 context-awareness — which A5-Q7's own answer (line 1929) labels the Axis-2/R6 dependency = Phase 2. So this is effort:high and entangled with multiple unsettled Axis-2/Axis-5 decisions; pulling it into Axis 3 for the Phase-1 urgency over-builds. The apply-time guard achieves the urgent honesty win at a fraction of the cost.
- **Global wait-timeout longest-match** _(high effort · costly)_ — Change Pending to a candidate set; when a key exact-matches a short binding but a longer prefix-sharing candidate also exists, hold for a configurable timeout (vim-style ~300-500ms) before firing the short one; the next keystroke continues the chord.
  - 👍 The only policy that makes a short binding and a longer chord genuinely COEXIST and fire — the industry-standard model (vim timeoutlen, VS Code, Emacs). Matches end-goal bullet 4's 'timeout' verbatim and gives one uniform mental model. The harness lift is smaller than the brief implied (chord pending/reset tests already exist).
  - 👎 Adds felt input latency to every prefix-key short binding — the adversarial angle's exact objection. Converts the synchronous push_keystroke into timer-driven async dispatch (a real dispatch-contract change), and collides with the pending-context-clear on mode switches (matcher.rs:313-321; A2-Q12): a mode-switching first keystroke clears the buffer, stranding/misfiring a timeout-held chord. Needs the A3-Q16/A3-Q17 golden harness for the new timing paths, and a wrong timeout value is a one-way-feeling UX regression once muscle memory forms. Genuinely belongs in R4 alongside Axis-4, whose mode-packs are the only confirmed consumer of coexistence.
- **Per-binding 'prefix' opt-in wait (immediate for everything else)** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Bindings explicitly declare themselves a chord-prefix; only flagged prefix keys get candidate-set + timeout treatment; every other short binding keeps the immediate first-match return and zero latency.
  - 👍 Targets the latency cost precisely — only declared prefix keys pay the wait tax, so the vast majority of short bindings stay instant; the explicit flag is self-documenting for conflict detection and enables coexistence exactly where mode-packs need it.
  - 👎 Still needs the same async timer plumbing and A2-Q12 mode-switch resolution as the global timeout, so it carries most of that cost. Adds a per-binding field the record schema (X2/R3b) and the ~397 FixedBinding sites must learn, and pushes disambiguation onto authors who may misflag and re-create silent shadows by omission. Higher complexity than the global timeout for a latency win the apply-time guard already avoids paying. R4/Axis-4 work.

**→ Recommendation: Apply-time prefix-shadow guard (warn at chord apply/capture; no ConflictMap rekey, no dispatch change) — ADDED.** Split the question the way the code forces, then ship the cheapest thing that satisfies the honesty half in Phase 1. The HONESTY half is genuinely in-scope: end-goal bullet 4 demands 'predictable chords' (end-goals:79-80) and the silent-shadow path is reachable TODAY — not after some deferred fix — because the file already applies multi-key chords (keyboard.rs:46-47,196-211) and hot-reload YAML is Phase-1. So Defer ships the initiative's signature dishonesty. But the brief's chosen mechanism (full ConflictMap rekey + catalog surface) over-builds: A5-Q7's own answer puts the required context-awareness in Axis-2/R6 (Phase 2), and surfacing the fixed-default-shadows-user-chord case also needs A5-Q9 + fixed-binding naming — effort:high, Phase-2-entangled. The COEXISTENCE half (a timer that lets both fire) is the real architectural cost: it turns synchronous push_keystroke into async dispatch (matcher.rs:307-346), collides with the pending-context-clear on mode switches (A2-Q12), and is the only thing genuinely gated on the A3-Q16 harness — that belongs in R4 alongside Axis-4, the only confirmed consumer of coexisting prefix chords. The minimum that kills the dishonesty in Phase 1 is therefore the added apply-time guard: keep the early-return dispatch (zero latency), and at the single seam where a chord enters the keymap (set_custom_trigger / hot-reload, reusing the existing validate_bindings hook and matcher.rs:326's starts_with against the concrete context) warn the user that the chord is shadowed/unreachable. This removes the silence at low cost and easy reversibility, without rekeying any persistent map, without an async dispatch change, and without depending on Axis-2 satisfiability. It is explicitly NOT 'just do longest-match' (correctly attacked for taxing every short binding) and NOT the heavier catalog rekey (defer to Axis-2/R6 where its context-dependency is satisfied). If the broadened spike shows the collision set is ~0, even this guard is cheap insurance and the decision is near-deferrable; if non-trivial, the guard is required Phase-1.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument: the apply-time guard removes the SILENCE but not the SHADOW. End-goal bullet 4 reads 'a short binding no longer silently shadows a longer chord' — the guard satisfies 'no longer silently' but a warned chord still will not FIRE until R4's timeout lands, so a purist can argue Axis 3 still ships incomplete against its own end-state, delivering 'honestly broken chords' rather than 'working chords.' Two amplifiers: (1) the guard is concrete-context best-effort (point-eval), so it can miss a shadow that only manifests in a context the user is not currently in, and 'warn-but-allow' leaves a non-functional binding on screen; (2) the guard only fires on user-initiated apply, so if the broadened spike finds default-vs-default prefix collisions among the 397 fixed sites are non-zero, those stay silently broken until R4 regardless. The rebuttal — bullet 4 is the AXIS end-state, not the Phase-1 deliverable; full coexistence (the timer) is legitimately R4/Axis-4-adjacent and removing the silence is the north-star honesty win — holds, but the residual is real and is exactly what the Axis-4 stakeholder check must price.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated-with: hot-reload YAML + G4/R8 chord-capture (the live/forthcoming new-chord paths; the guard rides with them and reuses validate_bindings, matcher.rs:181-203). Explicitly DEFERRED out of Phase 1: the full catalog conflict-surface (brief's heavy option) is gated by A5-Q7 (ConflictMap rekey to Vec<Keystroke>+strict-prefix index), A5-Q9 (union fixed bindings + nameable identity), and R6/Axis-2 context-awareness — all Phase 2 per A5-Q7's own answer (line 1929) — so it must NOT be pulled into Axis 3 just for the Phase-1 urgency. Also deferred: the dispatch-wait coexistence policy (global timeout or per-binding 'prefix' opt-in), gated by A2-Q12 (mode-switch context-clear), the A3-Q16/A3-Q17 golden/snapshot harness, and X2/R3b (a per-binding 'prefix' flag must fit the record schema); sequenced with Axis-4 mode-packs (R4) and possibly pulled earlier by X12 (matcher budget). Hidden dependency made explicit: the brief's recommended option silently assumed A5-Q7's rekey + A5-Q9 + R6 all land in Phase 1; they do not — that is why the lighter apply-time guard, not the rekey, is the Phase-1 mechanism.
- _What would settle it:_ Run the spike A3-Q18 prescribes, BROADENED to the file path the brief missed: instrument over the 168 user-reachable contexts (A3-Q1) and enumerate (1) default-vs-default short-prefixes-longer-chord collisions among the 397 fixed sites (brief's original), AND (2) every bare single-keystroke binding that a plausible user-authored chord (file or future UI) would be prefixed by — the shadow that is live today via keyboard.rs:46-47. If both sets are ~0/rare, the apply-time guard is cheap insurance and Defer is nearly defensible; if (2) is non-trivial, the apply-time guard is a required Phase-1 honesty fix. (3) A latency spike measuring the felt cost of a 300-500ms wait on prefix keys quantifies what the deferred timeout options would impose if ever adopted. (4) Stakeholder input from the Axis-4 lead: do the shipping vim/helix mode-packs require a short binding to COEXIST-AND-FIRE with a longer prefix-sharing chord in the SAME context? If yes, the timeout/opt-in policy becomes a hard Axis-4/R4 requirement (not optional, confirming X12's pressure) and validates the split — Axis 3 surfaces/warns the shadow now, R4 alongside Axis 4 resolves coexistence.
- _Red-team added option:_ Apply-time prefix-shadow guard (warn at chord apply/capture; no ConflictMap rekey, no dispatch change)
- _Red-team:_ Direction upheld (honesty in Phase 1, defer the dispatch-wait to R4), but mechanism and grounding hardened on four fronts. (1) MISSING OPTION added: an apply-time prefix-shadow guard that warns when a newly-applied chord is prefix-shadowed by an existing binding in the same concrete context, hooked at set_custom_trigger / the existing validate_bindings pass — no ConflictMap rekey, no catalog surface, no dispatch change. This is a strict, cheaper subset of the brief's recommended option. (2) STAKES re-grounded: I disproved the brief's premise that the new-shadow path is gated on G4/R8 UI chord-capture. keyboard.rs:46-47 applies a file-defined chord as the full multi-key Trigger::Keystrokes today; the parser (keyboard.rs:196-211) builds a multi-element Vec1; hot-reload YAML is Phase-1. The dishonesty is reachable NOW via the file, which makes the urgency higher than stated AND relocates the correct hook to apply/load time (covers file + future UI), not a UI capture widget. (3) OPTIMISM corrected: the brief's recommended 'surface-as-conflict' option is not cleanly Phase-1/medium — A5-Q7's own answer (line 1929) puts the required context-awareness in Axis-2/R6 (Phase 2), ConflictMap is editable-only and flattens chords to the first keystroke (keybindings.rs:104-132, bindings.rs:236), and surfacing the fixed-default-shadows-user-chord case also needs A5-Q9 (union fixed bindings) + fixed-binding naming. So that option is effort:high and Phase-2-entangled, not medium. I downgraded it. (4) HAND-WAVING corrected: 'ships into ZERO chord tests' is overstated — matcher_tests.rs:13-66 and 97-158 already cover chord pending + context-reset (A2-Q12); the test_keystroke helper + chord fixtures are reusable, so the harness lift for any option is smaller than the brief's penalty against the timeout options implied. DEFERABILITY: the decision is mostly deferrable — only the apply-time guard must ride with the Phase-1 hot-reload/capture work; the catalog surface defers to Axis-2/R6 and the dispatch-wait to R4/Axis-4.

### A3-Q19 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Keybindings/settings-surface PM is the decider and must make the SCOPE call EARLY (is 'remappable fixed bindings' a committed Axis-3 deliverable -> forces B; deferable -> allows C/D/A) — the only non-deferable input. Co-signed by the client/UI eng lead, who owns the badge render off lens.original_trigger, the new 'reset all' affordance, and specifically the requirement that reset-all be a FILE WIPE (clear custom-keybindings file + reload defaults) with a confirmation step, not a name-keyed loop. Design owns the visual language distinguishing user-unbound vs default-no-key (and, if rendered, unavailable).

**Decision.** What settings-UI surface the new layer/unbind model must ship so that (1) a user-unbound editable binding is visually distinguishable from a never-bound/default-no-key one, and (2) the model can never strand a user in a disabled state they cannot recover. Corrected framing: for EDITABLE bindings recovery is already universal today (the per-row 'Default' reset button at keybindings.rs:436 is unconditional), so sub-question (1) is a legibility/trust requirement, not a recoverability one; the only genuine get-stuck surface is nameless FIXED bindings (no row, A3-Q1/Q3) and any FUTURE key-scoped-disable schema (A3-Q15). The decision is therefore: ship a cheap user-unbound badge (buildable today from lens.original_trigger), add a universal FILE-WIPE 'reset all' escape hatch (none exists today), and explicitly DEFER surfacing/naming the 168 fixed keys (the A3-Q3 identity migration) out of this axis.

**Stakes.** The UI surface is the contract of the layer model: if a blank key cell silently means three different things (user-unbound vs default-no-key vs unavailable), it violates Axis 3's 'trust what the UI shows' north star even though the user is not technically stuck. The actual get-stuck (unrecoverable) risk is narrower than the brief claims — it is confined to fixed bindings, which have no row and which the current name-keyed file format physically cannot disable (A3-Q15), so it only materializes if a future key-scoped-disable schema is added without a matching reversible surface. The scope of the answer also decides whether Axis 3 stays a contained Unbound+precedence+badge refactor or balloons into a 160-257-site fixed-to-editable identity/migration project (A3-Q1/Q3) — committing to the latter (Option B) blocks the cheap, high-value badge+reset-all behind an unsolved identity decision and non-portable platform forks.

_Constrained by:_
- DECISIVE / MISSED BY BRIEF: per-row recovery is universal for editable bindings. In get_edit_button_row the 'Clear' button is gated on trigger.is_some() (line 397, pushed at 413 inside the if), but the 'Default' reset button (RESET_BUTTON_TEXT, rendered line 423) is pushed UNCONDITIONALLY at line 436 outside the if. So an unbound (trigger=None) editable row still shows 'Default' and can always be reset — the 'permanently stuck' failure does NOT apply to editable bindings. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:397,413,436`
- The badge needs NO unified classify_state()/tombstone (brief's hard dependency on A3-Q10/Q12 is overstated): as_lens sets original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger) iff a custom override is present, so user-unbound (resolved Empty + original_trigger Some) is already distinguishable from default-no-key (original_trigger None) at the lens today. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:741-748`
- reset_keybinding_to_default is name-keyed and restores the exact default by calling remove_custom_trigger then reading the now-resolved (default) trigger via trigger_to_keystroke — it does NOT consume original_trigger. Substance (exact restore, row kept) holds; the brief's 'exact via original_trigger' is imprecise about mechanism. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:511-527; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:617-640`
- The UI collapses every 'no key' state into one Option<Keystroke>: trigger_to_keystroke maps Trigger::Empty => None (util/bindings.rs:256). Verified. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:256`
- No global 'reset all keybindings to default' affordance exists today (grep for reset_all/reset-all/restore-default in keybindings.rs returns nothing). CORRECTION: to be a real safety net it must be a FILE WIPE (clear the custom-keybindings file + reload built-in defaults); a name-keyed loop only re-does per-row Default and cannot reach nameless/fixed or a corrupt file. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs (grep, no match); app/src/keyboard.rs:15,167-169`
- The name-keyed on-disk file physically cannot express disabling a nameless fixed default (FixedBinding has no name; no schema/version field; platform baked at parse so a key-scoped disable is non-portable). So an irreversible FIXED disable is structurally impossible UNLESS a new key-scoped-disable schema is added — which is the Option-B project, not something A/C/D enable. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286,914-919,502-506 (A3-Q15); app/src/keyboard.rs:15,167-169`
- Surfacing/naming fixed rows is a 160-257-site migration gated on the UNSOLVED A3-Q3 identity decision: 168 of 397 FixedBinding sites are user-reachable nav/edit keys; derive-an-id collides (one enum=one TypeId); 13 new_per_platform forks make any key-scoped disable platform-specific. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs (13 new_per_platform verified); A3-Q1 (168/397), A3-Q3`

**Options:**

- **A. Editable-only third state; rely on structural impossibility of fixed unbind** _(low effort · easy)_ — Add a distinct user-unbound badge to the editable rows that already exist, distinguishing user-unbound from default-no-key. Do NOT surface fixed rows. Add NO global escape hatch. Rely on the fact that recovery is already universal (unconditional 'Default' button, keybindings.rs:436) and that the name-keyed file cannot express a fixed disable (A3-Q15).
  - 👍 Cheapest legible answer. The badge is buildable TODAY from lens.original_trigger (keymap.rs:741-748) with no dependency on a unified classify_state(). Because per-row 'Default' is unconditional and the file format cannot disable a fixed binding, there is no reachable irreversible state to recover from — A needs no new 'forbidding' code, it just doesn't add the hole. Closes the 'blank cell means three things' trust gap for every row the UI can address. Fully reversible.
  - 👎 Adds NO universal escape hatch, so the moment a future key-scoped-disable schema is added (the B project) or a user hand-corrupts the file, there is no global recovery and the get-stuck hole opens. Delivers none of the remappable-fixed end-goal (168 core keys stay invisible). 'Third state' is realistically a binary (user-unbound vs default-no-key) — the 'unavailable/context' state cannot be rendered in the context-less settings list, so don't over-promise three states.
- **B. Surface and name fixed defaults; full per-fixed restore** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Add the badge AND surface fixed-binding rows by authoring ~160-257 stable &'static str names (or promoting Fixed to Editable), so each fixed default can be disabled and restored per-row, with platform-fork handling.
  - 👍 Only option that delivers remappable fixed bindings — users finally rebind enter/tab/arrows (the keys they actually want, A3-Q1) with a per-key safe restore. No binding category stays invisible-and-irreversible. If product has committed 'remappable fixed bindings' as an Axis-3 deliverable, B is forced regardless of cost.
  - 👎 Massive scope: a 160-257-site fixed->editable migration gated on the UNSOLVED A3-Q3 identity decision (derive-an-id non-viable; needs hand-authored names that become a permanent compatibility surface). 13 new_per_platform forks + platform-baked parse make a key-scoped disable non-portable, and the name-keyed file has no schema field to express it (A3-Q15). Turns a focused axis into a multi-quarter effort and blocks the cheap badge+reset-all behind it. One-way once names ship.
- **C. Editable badge now + FILE-WIPE 'reset all' safety net; defer fixed naming** _(med effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Ship A's user-unbound badge AND add a single 'Reset all keybindings to default' escape hatch implemented as a FILE WIPE (clear the custom-keybindings file + reload built-in defaults), with a confirmation step. Explicitly defer per-fixed rows and the A3-Q3 naming project.
  - 👍 Answers BOTH sub-questions at low cost: the badge (sub-q1) closes the trust/legibility gap and is cheap (lens already has the data); the file-wipe reset-all (sub-q2 surrogate) is a genuine universal escape hatch that recovers from ANY current OR future on-disk state including a hand-edited file or a future key-scoped disable — coverage a name-keyed loop or per-row Default cannot give. 'Restore all defaults' is also a standard, expected affordance that simply doesn't exist today. Keeps the door open to B without committing to the migration now. Highest safety-per-dollar that still fully answers the decision.
  - 👎 Still no selective fixed rebind — reset-all is all-or-nothing and destructive (wipes ALL customizations), so it needs a confirm step and is a minor footgun. The get-stuck JUSTIFICATION is weaker than the original brief claimed: under current code there is no reachable irreversible EDITABLE state, so reset-all is future-insurance + product hygiene, not a present-hole fix. Slightly more surface than A/D. If product has committed remappable-fixed, C under-delivers and B is forced.
- **D. File-wipe 'reset all' only; defer the badge AND fixed naming (MISSING — added)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Add only the universal file-wipe 'Reset all keybindings to default' escape hatch; DEFER the user-unbound badge to land alongside the resolver's classify_state() (A3-Q10/Q12) it naturally pairs with. Don't surface fixed rows.
  - 👍 Cheapest option that fully satisfies the get-stuck north star — universal recovery from ANY state, name-agnostic, minimal Phase-1 surface. Because recovery is already universal for editable rows and the badge is pure legibility (not recoverability), deferring it loses no correctness; the badge can ship later with the resolver work without rework risk. The floor option if eng wants to minimize Phase-1 UI churn.
  - 👎 Under-answers the decision: sub-question (1) explicitly asks for the user-unbound display state, and D punts it, leaving the 'blank cell means three things' trust violation in place during the window where unbind ships but the badge hasn't. Leaves the legibility north-star gap open even though the fix is cheap and decoupled.

**→ Recommendation: C. Editable badge now + FILE-WIPE 'reset all' safety net; defer fixed naming.** C is the only option that answers BOTH halves of the decision at low, defensible cost, and it survives the attack with two hardening edits. Half one (the user-unbound badge) is the direct fix for the 'trust what the UI shows' north star and is genuinely cheap and DECOUPLED — buildable today from lens.original_trigger (keymap.rs:741-748) with no hard dependency on a unified classify_state(), correcting the brief's overstated A3-Q10/Q12 gate. Half two must be re-specified: the escape hatch only delivers safety as a FILE WIPE (clear the custom-keybindings file + reload defaults), not the brief's 'name-keyed loop,' which would merely duplicate the already-unconditional per-row 'Default' button and could never reach a nameless/fixed or corrupt-file state. With that correction, C closes the only residual hole (a future key-scoped disable or hand-edited file) for pennies while keeping the axis contained. The expensive half — per-fixed rows — stays correctly deferred: gated on the unsolved A3-Q3 identity decision and non-portable platform forks (A3-Q15), so committing to B now blows Axis 3 into a 160-257-site migration. A is even cheaper but ships no universal escape hatch; D is the honest floor but punts sub-question (1) even though the badge is cheap and decoupled. Crucially, none of this is on the Phase-1 critical path: the surface is additive UI, recovery for editable rows already exists (keybindings.rs:436), and the file format cannot disable a fixed binding (A3-Q15), so A3-Q19 can land late Phase 1 / early Phase 2. The ONE input that must be settled early is the product scope ruling — if 'remappable fixed bindings' is a committed deliverable, B is forced and C/D under-deliver. Recommend hardened C; D is the acceptable minimal fallback if Phase-1 UI surface must be minimized; A only if a global escape hatch is judged unnecessary.

- _Residual risk:_ If product HAS already committed 'remappable fixed bindings' as an Axis-3 deliverable, both C and D under-deliver and B becomes mandatory regardless of its very-high/one-way cost — so the recommendation is contingent on a scope ruling that is not an engineering fact. Secondary surviving risks: (a) building the badge now on lens.original_trigger may need light rework if A3-Q10 later introduces a first-class Unbound tombstone, so the badge and resolver must be kept aligned (soft dependency, not a blocker); (b) a file-wipe reset-all is destructive with no undo and wipes ALL customizations, so without a confirmation step it is itself a minor footgun; (c) A3-Q9 (whether 'unbind' also kills menu/Custom dispatch) changes what the 'user-unbound' badge promises — if unbind leaves the action menu-reachable, the badge must say 'no keystroke' not 'disabled.'
- _Dependencies:_ SOFT (not hard) on A3-Q10/Q12: the badge ships today from as_lens.original_trigger (keymap.rs:741-748) without a unified classify_state(); if A3-Q10 builds a tombstone/classify_state(), the badge must MIRROR it to avoid matcher/badge disagreement, but it is not BLOCKED on it — correcting the brief's hard gate. HARD on A3-Q3/A3-Q1 ONLY for Option B (fixed identity/naming); A/C/D do not need it. A3-Q15: the name-keyed file structurally cannot express a fixed disable, so C/D's safety guarantee relies on NOT adding a key-scoped-disable schema (a non-action) plus the file-wipe reset-all as the universal catch; reset-all MUST be a file wipe or it provides no coverage beyond per-row Default. Interacts with A3-Q9 (does 'unbind' also kill menu/Custom dispatch) — changes the badge's promise. Feeds Axis 5's conflict map (Unbound must stay invisible to ConflictMap, already true per A3-Q10). NOT a Phase-1 critical-path blocker: additive UI, recovery for editable rows already exists.
- _What would settle it:_ (1) A PRODUCT scope ruling — is 'remappable fixed bindings' a committed Axis-3 promise? Yes forces B; no makes C the answer (or D as the minimal floor). This is the only input that must be settled before Phase-1 work proceeds. (2) A fast usability spike focused on DISCOVERABILITY, not possibility (recovery already exists via the unconditional per-row 'Default' button): can 5 users (a) tell 'I unbound this' from 'this never had a key' without a badge, and (b) find a recovery path? If they can't find recovery, the badge + a visible file-wipe 'reset all' earn their keep (validates C); if users specifically demand per-fixed selective restore, B is justified. (3) Eng confirmation (already done here): no reset-all exists today, the file-wipe version is small, and the badge is buildable now from lens.original_trigger with no resolver rewrite.
- _Red-team added option:_ D. File-wipe 'Reset all to default' safety net only; defer the user-unbound badge
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every cited anchor against source and the settled answers doc (A3-Q1/Q3/Q12/Q15); the brief's grounding is substantially accurate. Three corrections that change the analysis, plus one decisive missed fact: (1) MISSED FACT — recovery is already UNIVERSAL for editable rows. In get_edit_button_row the 'Default' reset button (RESET_BUTTON_TEXT) is pushed UNCONDITIONALLY at keybindings.rs:436; only the 'Clear' button is gated on trigger.is_some() (:397). So an unbound editable row can always be reset by opening it and clicking 'Default'.
