The 'user can disable a default and get permanently stuck' framing therefore does NOT apply to editable bindings — it is exclusively a FIXED-binding problem (no row) plus any FUTURE key-scoped-disable schema. The editable badge is a legibility/trust win, not a get-stuck recovery fix. (2) RESET-ALL SEMANTICS — the brief conflates 'loops the existing name-keyed reset' with 'name-agnostic.' A name-keyed loop only re-does what per-row Default already does and CANNOT reach anything nameless/fixed or a hand-corrupted file — zero coverage beyond Option A. The safety-net value only exists if reset-all is a FILE WIPE (clear the whole custom-keybindings file + reload built-in defaults). Corrected. (3) HIDDEN-DEPENDENCY REMOVED — the brief asserts the badge 'must mirror classify_state()' and is gated on A3-Q10/Q12 building a tombstone. Verified false: as_lens already sets original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger) iff a custom override is present (keymap.rs:741-748), so user-unbound is distinguishable from default-no-key at the lens TODAY with no unified classify_state(). Dependency is soft, not a hard gate. (4) DEFERABILITY — the whole A3-Q19 surface is additive UI; nothing in Phase-1 resolver/layer plumbing is blocked by it, and the name-keyed file physically cannot express a fixed disable (A3-Q15), so an irreversible fixed disable is structurally impossible unless someone adds a key-scoped-disable schema (the Option-B project). Only early-required input is the product SCOPE ruling. (5) Minor: reset_keybinding_to_default restores the exact default by remove_custom_trigger then reading the resolved trigger (util/bindings.rs:511-527), NOT by consuming original_trigger; substance holds. (6) 'three states' is over-promised — in the context-less settings list the 'unavailable (context)' state mostly can't be rendered; the achievable load-bearing distinction is the binary user-unbound vs default-no-key.

---

## Axis 4 — Modality as a first-class, pluggable choice

### A4-Q1 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product: the keybindings initiative product owner (PM for the editor experience), who owns the v1 scope promise — with sign-off from the editor/keybindings engineering lead on the Phase-2/Phase-3 boundary AND on the Helix-proof depth (A4-Q3), since that, not editability, is the dominant effort driver (A4-Q6/Q7). The A4-Q1 'How to answer' routes the editability cut to the initiative owner; the added Helix-depth sub-call (full vs single-selection) is a joint product+eng decision.

**Decision.** Whether "per-mode keymaps are themselves scoped bindings the user can edit/override" is in scope for Axis-4 v1, or cut to a post-Phase-2 milestone so v1 ships hardcoded, non-editable per-mode keymaps. Two refinements the original brief omitted are part of this same call: (a) how deep the "Helix proof" goes (single-persistent-selection vs full multi-selection), because that — not editability — is the dominant cost driver of the engine deliverable (A4-Q6); and (b) whether the design doc commits the forward-compatible Modal:{engine}:{submode} seam SHAPE now, or states editability-OUT now and finalizes the seam against Phase-2's R3b schema at Phase-2 exit (since Axis 4 is sequenced after Phase 2 in the spine).

**Stakes.** Sets the Axis-4 v1 acceptance bar and the Phase-2/Phase-3 boundary. Putting editability in scope pulls two unrelated, deep Phase-2 refactors (Axis-2 context-scoped override schema R3b, Axis-3 binding layers/precedence R2) plus a context-aware conflict map (R6) into a Phase-3 spec that is already the highest-risk axis — and does so to deliver an editor over content that does not exist (only the Vim engine is in code; A4-Q14). Under-scoping the other way (hardcoded keymaps with NO forward-compatible seam) discards near-free forward compatibility and risks entrenching a vim-shaped, name-keyed binding path. The part that is genuinely "forced now" is narrow: the design doc must state that editability is OUT of Axis-4 v1. The part that is NOT forced now — and should not be bet — is the exact seam shape, which can be confirmed against R3b once Phase 2 lands, because the spine sequences Axis 4 after Phase 2. The larger latent stakes the original brief understated: the engine + Helix proof that Option A keeps is itself high-effort (net-new selection-set model per A4-Q6; mid-session swap semantics per A4-Q7; two duplicated VimHandler impls to collapse per A4-Q4), so the real scope lever is the Helix-proof depth (A4-Q3), not editability.

_Constrained by:_
- The report explicitly sequences per-mode editability behind Phase 2: report:270 'Phase 3's per-mode bindings are *unusable* without Phase 2's context-scoped overrides and conflict detection'; report:228 names context-scoped override (R3) and context-aware conflict map (R6) as prerequisites 'for per-mode keymaps to be editable.' The end-goals dependency spine independently states Axis 4 (Phase 3) 'depends on the Phase-2 context model' and that Axes 2 & 3 are 'the prerequisite that makes Axis 4's per-mode bindings actually editable.' All three lines verified. — `.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:270,228 + 2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-end-goals-design.md:117-128`
- Today overrides are action-NAME-keyed and context-blind: update_custom_trigger loops `.filter(|b| b.name == name)` and overwrites custom_trigger on EVERY matching binding (verbatim, lines 422-433), so a single override clobbers the same action across all mode-contexts (vim-normal and helix-normal cannot be independently rebound). Editing per-mode keymaps is impossible until R3b. NOTE: file is crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs, not crates/vim — the brief's bare 'keymap.rs' was imprecise. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433 (= report:128 L3)`
- Only the Vim engine exists in code: 'kakoune' appears nowhere in the repo; 'helix' appears only in external-editor CLI file:line:column parsing. VimMode is a closed 4-variant enum {Normal,Insert,Visual(MotionType),Replace}. A VimCodeEditor-gated dropdown can today offer only {Off, Vim}. There are no Helix/Kakoune keymaps for a user to edit in v1 — editability would be a UI over empty content. — `A4-Q14 (crates/vim/src/vim.rs:14-20; app/src/util/file/external_editor/mod.rs:267-268)`
- The mode-selection dropdown is cheap and independent: a reusable Dropdown<FeaturesPageAction> template (code_editor_line_number_mode) exists — field, action variant + set_value handler, update fn, render widget all present beside the line-number widget. CAVEAT: the swap is not a free side-effect — it must rework BOTH the command-palette ToggleSettingActionPair AND the settings VimModeWidget, and the runtime status bar (render_vim_status) is terminal-only with an EXHAUSTIVE VimMode match that won't compile if new submodes are added. — `A4-Q14 (app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:1385,490-504,6105-6213,3014-3047,5789-5820; terminal/input/common.rs:46-87)`
- The non-editable engine deliverable is mechanically supported: Entity::Event is a free associated type so one shared Entity emitting EditorCommand = one subscribe_to_model + one blanket ModalEditExecutor (generalizing impl<T> VimSubscriber). Step D already prescribes registering per-mode bindings under Modal:{engine}:{submode} contexts, expressible TODAY at compile time via the existing ContextPredicate macro grammar (id!/eq!/ne!). NOTE: 'context.rs' refers to TWO files — core/model/context.rs:67-86 (subscribe machinery) and keymap/context.rs (the predicate grammar) — the brief conflated them. — `A4-Q4 (entity.rs:39-40; core/model/context.rs:67-86; vim.rs:1849-1942) + crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-17 + report:265`
- DECISIVE, MISSED BY THE BRIEF: the report's '~90% shared / no new buffer algorithm' is wrong for a real Helix. Per-selection APPLY is already multi-selection and reusable (selection.rs:642 Vec1; operators map over the set), but selection-set CREATION/MANAGEMENT (s/S regex-split, x line-split, keep/remove-matching, ;/, collapse, rotate-primary) has ZERO primitives — a grep for these over crates/editor crates/vim app/src returns exactly 2 hits, both an unrelated notebooks collapse-to-one-cursor hack. There is also no 'primary-selection' pointer (model exposes only first/last of the Vec1). A real Helix proof is NET-NEW crates/editor model surface, raising the engine deliverable's effort. — `A4-Q6 (crates/editor/src/content/buffer.rs:164-197; crates/editor/src/selection.rs:642,499; app/src/notebooks/editor/model.rs:1221-1234)`
- DECISIVE, MISSED BY THE BRIEF: mid-session mode switching does not exist today. VimModel is add_model'd once and subscribe_to_model'd once; the AppEditorSettings observer body is just ctx.notify (no rebuild/reset/re-subscribe). In-flight FSA state (pending_action, register, dot_repeat_event, submode — vim.rs:37-54) persists and resumes stale. The cheap dropdown does not work at runtime until net-new swap/reset/re-subscribe + pending-state-discard logic is built — a cost Option A inherits. — `A4-Q7 (app/src/code/editor/view.rs:312-314,359-360; crates/vim/src/vim.rs:37-54)`
- Contexts are &'static str with NO FromStr parser; predicates are Rust-only (L6). So hardcoded Modal:{engine}:{submode} contexts are cheap today (string literals via the macro grammar) but USER-EDITABLE contexts fundamentally require R3a (FromStr parser + Cow/interned strings) before R3b — confirming editability is code-blocked, and confirming the forward-compat seam is a compile-time construct that becomes data-driven only in Phase 2. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:3-17 (= report:128 L6) + report:255 (Phase-2 R3a)`

**Options:**

- **Cut editability to post-Phase-2; ship engine + Helix proof + dropdown with a forward-compatible seam whose final shape is confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit (recommended)** _(high effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Axis-4 v1 = ModalEngine + EditorCommand IR + a Helix proof + a one-setting editing_mode dropdown. Per-mode keymaps are hardcoded and registered under Modal:{engine}:{submode} contexts (Step D, via the existing compile-time macro grammar) but NOT user-editable. The design doc states editability is OUT of v1 and RESERVES the seam; it does not BET the seam's final shape now — that is finalized against Phase-2's R3b record schema at Phase-2 exit, since the spine sequences Axis 4 after Phase 2.
  - 👍 Matches the report's own dependency spine — Phase-3 per-mode bindings are 'unusable' without Phase 2 (report:270), and editable contexts are code-blocked by name-keyed overrides (keymap.rs:422-433) and &'static-str contexts with no FromStr (L6). Only Vim exists (A4-Q14), so editability would be a UI over empty content. The dropdown is cheap (infra exists). Registering under Modal:{engine}:{submode} now uses the existing macro grammar, so Phase-2 editability is purely additive. Crucially, because Axis 4 follows Phase 2 in the spine, deferring the seam's final shape to Phase-2 exit removes the only real risk (schema divergence) at zero schedule cost.
  - 👎 Users expecting to rebind Vim/Helix keys in the first modal release cannot; 'pluggable modality' may look half-delivered if marketed as editable. Effort is HIGH, not medium: the retained engine is non-trivial — a real Helix proof needs net-new selection-set primitives (A4-Q6), mid-session switching needs net-new swap/reset/re-subscribe logic (A4-Q7), and two duplicated VimHandler impls must collapse (A4-Q4); the dropdown also touches two UI surfaces + a vim-shaped exhaustive-match status bar (A4-Q14). If product pulls the Helix proof EARLIER than Phase 2 to demo the flagship, the seam stops being free-and-known and becomes a real R3b bet.
- **Cut editability AND scope the Helix proof to single-persistent-selection (defer net-new selection-set primitives; finalize seam against R3b at Phase-2 exit) — ADDED** _(med effort · easy)_ — Same editability cut as the recommended option, but the Helix proof is deliberately degenerate: select-then-act ordering/persistence using ONLY the existing reusable per-selection apply path, explicitly NOT shipping multi-selection-set creation (s/S/x/keep/;/,/rotate), which has zero primitives today (A4-Q6). Editability deferred; seam reserved and shape-confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit.
  - 👍 The genuine cheapest forward-compatible Axis-4 v1. It de-risks BOTH costs the original brief omitted: the net-new selection-set model (A4-Q6) and the schema bet. It still validates the core IR thesis (one engine, one executor, select-then-act) without the unbounded Helix-multi-selection tail or the empty-content editability surface. Strictly cheaper than the recommended option and avoids its multi-selection exposure. Lets the engine land first and empirically inform the full Helix command set.
  - 👎 A single-selection 'Helix' is a degenerate proof — multi-selection IS Helix's identity (A4-Q6, A4-Q3 killer question) — so it validates only ordering/persistence, not that the IR scales to Helix's defining commands; whether that counts as 'the proof' is A4-Q3's product call. Deferring the seam shape means the doc states a reserved-but-unfinalized seam, which some reviewers dislike. Risks a second, larger Helix milestone later once selection-set primitives are built.
- **Include user-editable per-mode keymaps in Axis-4 v1** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — v1 must deliver per-mode keymaps as scoped bindings the user can edit/override, forcing R2 (layers + precedence + Unbound), R3a (ContextPredicate FromStr parser, since contexts are &'static str today) + R3b (context-scoped override schema threaded through set/update_custom_trigger), and R6 (context-aware conflict map) into the Axis-4 spec.
  - 👍 Delivers the complete 'pluggable modality' vision as one shippable unit — a user who picks Helix can immediately tailor it, no second migration. If modal editing is the flagship bet, shipping it editable maximizes launch impact and avoids re-opening the engine later.
  - 👎 Inverts the dependency spine: pulls two unrelated Phase-2 axes plus R6 into the highest-risk Phase-3 spec. Editability is code-blocked — name-keyed clobbering (keymap.rs:422-433) AND &'static-str contexts with no FromStr (L6, requiring R3a before R3b). And there are NO Helix/Kakoune keymaps to edit (A4-Q14, only Vim), so most of the surface is an editor over empty content. Maximizes schedule risk and couples the Helix proof to the deepest refactors in the initiative.
- **Minimal cut — hardcoded keymaps with NO forward-compatibility commitment** _(high effort · costly)_ — Ship engine + Helix proof + dropdown the cheapest way; register per-mode bindings ad-hoc (not necessarily under Modal:{engine}:{submode}); defer all editability and make NO pre-commitment to the override-schema seam.
  - 👍 Avoids betting on a Phase-2 override schema that isn't designed yet; lets the engine land first and empirically inform the editability schema rather than guessing now.
  - 👎 Saves almost no effort versus the recommended option — the engine + Helix proof are common to both; the ONLY delta is the binding-registration seam, which is near-free under the existing macro grammar (so this option's 'low effort' is illusory — the engine itself is high). Meanwhile it discards near-free forward compatibility: each mode is 'naturally a layer' (report:229) and Step D already prescribes Modal:{engine}:{submode} (report:265). Risks recreating the L3 context-blindness problem inside the new engine. Dominated by the recommended option.

**→ Recommendation: Cut editability to post-Phase-2; ship engine + Helix proof + dropdown with a forward-compatible seam whose final shape is confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit (recommended).** Editability is not merely deprioritized — it is blocked by code facts I re-verified: update_custom_trigger filters by action name and clobbers all matching bindings (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433), and contexts are &'static str with no FromStr parser (L6), so per-mode keymaps cannot be cleanly editable until R3a+R3b+R6 exist (Phase 2). Only the Vim engine is in code (A4-Q14), so shipping an editor for Helix/Kakoune keymaps in v1 is a UI over content that does not exist — Option B spends very-high effort and the most schedule risk to deliver an empty surface while inverting the dependency spine. The minimal-no-seam option is dominated: it saves no meaningful effort (the engine is the cost, not the seam) yet discards near-free forward compatibility. Among the surviving cuts, the recommended option ships the real Axis-4 thesis (one engine, one EditorCommand IR, one ModalEditExecutor, Helix as proof — A4-Q4/Q5 mechanically supported) plus a cheap dropdown, and keeps the Modal:{engine}:{submode} seam clean.

Two corrections distinguish this from the original brief's Option A. First, effort is HIGH, not medium: the engine the option KEEPS includes net-new selection-set primitives for a real Helix (A4-Q6 corrects the report's '~90% shared') and net-new mid-session swap/reset logic (A4-Q7, the model is wired once and never reacts). Second, the seam is NOT a bet: because the spine sequences Axis 4 (Phase 3) after Phase 2, the design doc should state editability-OUT now but finalize the seam SHAPE against the then-known R3b schema at Phase-2 exit — eliminating the schema-divergence risk at zero cost. If effort pressure rises, the added Option (degenerate single-selection Helix) is the legitimate cheaper floor; choosing between it and a fuller proof IS the A4-Q3 decision, which this question is gated WITH. DEFERABILITY: this decision blocks neither Phase 1 nor Phase 2 — only the one-line 'editability OUT of v1' statement is needed now.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument is product-sequencing, not code: IF the initiative chooses to pull the Helix proof EARLY (before Phase 2 lands) to de-risk or demo the flagship modal bet — which the stakes themselves call 'the actual point of Axis 4' — then the forward-compatible seam stops being free-and-known and becomes a genuine bet that R3b's record schema {name,trigger,context?,platform?} can host Modal:{engine}:{submode} predicates. In that world some seam rework is possible and the 'reversible-toward-editable' claim weakens. A secondary residual: even with editability cut, the retained engine is high-effort (net-new selection-set model A4-Q6 + swap semantics A4-Q7), so 'cut editability' does NOT make Axis 4 cheap — it only removes the very-high editability tail; if the org expected the cut to make Axis 4 a quick win, that expectation is wrong and the real cost lever is the A4-Q3 Helix-proof depth, not this decision.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated WITH (same v1 acceptance bar): A4-Q2 (which surfaces get modality + flag-gating) and A4-Q3 (the Helix command set — now elevated to the primary effort lever, since single-selection vs full multi-selection determines whether the net-new selection-set primitives of A4-Q6 are in scope). Depends ON (deferral target): Phase-2 Axis 2 (context-scoped overrides R3b, plus R3a FromStr parser, since contexts are &'static str today) and Axis 3 (binding layers/precedence/Unbound R2) plus conflict map R6 — these gate the editability sub-goal per the spine (end-goals-design.md:117-128). The seam SHAPE should be confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit rather than committed now. Feeds: A4-Q14 (the mode dropdown — cheap but touches two UI surfaces + a vim-shaped status bar) and A4-Q7 (mid-session switch semantics — net-new, not free; required for the dropdown to function at runtime). The IR/executor shape (A4-Q4) and vim-vocabulary IR choice (A4-Q5) are the engine deliverable this option keeps in scope; A4-Q6 sizes its true cost.
- _What would settle it:_ A product call from the initiative owner, resolved by four inputs (the brief had three): (1) initiative-owner sign-off that 'pick your mode via one setting + a working Helix proof' is an acceptable v1 WITHOUT user-editable mode keymaps; (2) an audit of any committed external/customer/marketing promise that the first modal release be user-rebindable per mode — likely none, since no Helix/Kakoune engine exists yet (A4-Q14); (3) an eng estimate confirming R2+R3a+R3b+R6 would balloon the Axis-4 spec; and (4) NEW — a decision on Helix-proof depth (A4-Q3): full multi-selection (pulls the net-new selection-set primitives of A4-Q6 + a primary-selection pointer into scope) vs single-persistent-selection (the degenerate floor, the added option). On the seam: confirm whether Axis-4 engine work is sequenced AFTER Phase-2 R3b (per the spine) — if yes, no seam bet is needed and the doc simply states 'seam finalized against R3b at Phase-2 exit'; if the Helix proof is pulled early, run the 1-page check that R3b's {name,trigger,context?,platform?} record can host Modal:{engine}:{submode} predicates before committing the seam.
- _Red-team added option:_ Cut editability AND scope the Helix proof to single-persistent-selection (defer net-new selection-set primitives; finalize seam against R3b at Phase-2 exit)
- _Red-team:_ Direction of the recommendation (cut editability from Axis-4 v1) survives every attack and is grounded in code I re-verified myself. But the brief was optimistic and hand-waved three things, which I corrected:

1) OPTIMISM — Option A effort is NOT "medium." The cut removes editability's very-high tail, but the ENGINE+Helix-proof that Option A keeps is itself high. The brief's grounding fact #5 sells the engine as cleanly supported (A4-Q4: one Entity, one subscribe, one blanket executor — TRUE) but omits A4-Q6, which I confirmed: per-selection APPLY is already multi-selection and reusable, but Helix selection-set CREATION/MANAGEMENT (s/S/x/keep/remove/;/,/rotate + a "primary-selection" pointer) has ZERO primitives — a grep over crates/editor crates/vim app/src returns exactly 2 hits, both an unrelated notebooks "collapse-to-one-cursor" hack. So a real Helix proof is NET-NEW crates/editor model surface, correcting the report's "~90% shared." Plus A4-Q7: the engine model is wired once and never reacts to settings, so mid-session Vim→Helix switching needs net-new swap/reset/re-subscribe logic before the cheap dropdown even functions. Raised Option A to HIGH and added these as grounding.

2) MISSING OPTION — added Option D (the genuine cheaper-80% floor): cut editability AND scope the Helix proof to single-persistent-selection (select-then-act only), explicitly deferring the net-new selection-set primitives. This is the cheapest forward-compatible cut and de-risks the two costs the brief omits.

3) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY / DEFERABILITY — Option A's "forward-compatible seam" is sold as a BET on Phase-2's R3b schema. But the dependency spine sequences Axis-4 (Phase 3) AFTER Phase-2 R3b (end-goals-design.md:117-128). If that sequencing holds, the seam is NOT a bet — you build against a known R3b. The bet only exists if product pulls the Helix proof EARLY (before Phase 2). Made this explicit and split the residual risk accordingly. This decision does NOT block Phase 1 or Phase 2; the editability-OUT intent must be stated now, but the seam SHAPE should be confirmed against R3b at Phase-2 exit, neutralizing A's only real risk.

CITATION FIXES: fact #2's "keymap.rs:422-433" is crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs (NOT crates/vim); mechanism is action-NAME-keyed (filter |b| b.name == name), so the real failure is "same action across mode-contexts clobbered together," which I tightened. Fact #5 blurs two different context.rs files (keymap/context.rs grammar vs core/model/context.rs subscribe) under one basename — disambiguated. All report line cites (228, 229, 265, 270) and L3/L6 (report:128) verified real.

### A4-Q2 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: The keybindings-initiative product owner (PM) decides surface scope and which engines count as v1 (same owner as A4-Q1/A4-Q3). The editor/terminal-input eng lead is co-owner for two engineering constraints the PM cannot adjudicate: (1) the flag mechanics, and (2) the A4-Q4 coupling — specifically that the shared blanket dispatch impl (vim.rs:1849) means code-editor-only scope does not fully isolate the terminal, so the lead must say whether the terminal rides a legacy path or is ported.

**Decision.** For Axis-4 v1, which editor surfaces get pluggable modality (code editor only vs code editor + terminal command editor), and under what feature-flag strategy do the new Helix/Kakoune engines roll out (reuse VimCodeEditor, one umbrella ModalEditing flag, or per-engine flags)? Reframed by the attack: the decision actually FORCED now is (a) surface scope and (b) whether the editing setting migrates from vim_mode:bool to an editing_mode enum — both gate the executor-trait (A4-Q4) and settings-UI (A4-Q14) rework. The flag-multiplication strategy is largely DEFERRABLE because no second engine exists to gate yet.

**Stakes.** This sets the v1 build surface and the rollout safety net. A wrong scope choice either (a) drags an unfinished Helix into stable by sharing vim's flag, (b) regresses every existing terminal-vim user by re-gating a currently always-on feature (input.rs:2734), or (c) commits to building selection-first modal editing on a single-line shell prompt where Helix/Kakoune's differentiating multi-selection commands (s regex-split, x line-select, % whole-file) are degenerate (A4-Q6). It must be decided before the executor trait surface (A4-Q4) and settings-UI rework (A4-Q14) are designed. The attack lowers the stakes of one branch: the 'doubles executor work' fear was overstated (the executor is one shared blanket impl, A4-Q4), so the real stakes are user value and regression, not engineering N-fold cost.

_Constrained by:_
- Code editor's vim is feature-flag-gated/opt-in: supports_vim_mode = FeatureFlag::VimCodeEditor.is_enabled(). — `app/src/code/editor/view.rs:357`
- Terminal command editor's vim is ALWAYS-ON, not flag-gated: supports_vim_mode is hardcoded true; the only gate is the user setting via AppEditorSettings::vim_mode_enabled(). Re-gating behind a flag would regress existing terminal-vim users. (Brief's cited gate mod.rs:4920-4921 is wrong/empty; actual user-setting gates are input.rs:7490 and terminal/view.rs:13435.) — `app/src/terminal/input.rs:2734 (gate at input.rs:7490)`
- CORRECTION to brief: 'both surfaces doubles executor work 2x N' is overstated. The dispatch executor is ONE shared blanket impl (impl<T> VimSubscriber for T where T: VimHandler). The two VimHandler impls are per-SURFACE glue that already BOTH exist and do NOT multiply by engine. 'Both surfaces' costs a one-time terminal-glue port + threading an extend/keep_selection flag, not N-fold work. — `A4-Q4 (vim.rs:1849-1942; impls at vim_handler.rs:23 and editor/view/mod.rs:1959)`
- HIDDEN COST: that blanket dispatch impl is shared by BOTH surfaces, so refactoring it to the proposed ModalEditExecutor cannot cleanly isolate the terminal — code-editor-only scope either ports the terminal glue anyway or maintains a parallel legacy VimEvent dispatch during the transition. The brief's 'only one of the two impls is touched' is optimistic. — `A4-Q4 (vim.rs:1849; blanket impl serves CodeEditorView and EditorView)`
- DECISIVE VALUE FACT (brief under-weighted): per-selection APPLY is already multi-selection, but selection-set CREATION/MANAGEMENT primitives are ABSENT — s/S regex-split, x line-split, keep/remove, rotate-primary, and a primary-selection pointer are all net-new crates/editor surface. These differentiating Helix/Kakoune commands are inherently multi-line/whole-file, hence largely degenerate in a single-line terminal prompt — the strongest reason to keep the terminal out of v1. — `A4-Q6 (answers doc lines 1366,1389)`
- No Helix/Kakoune engine exists (kakoune absent; helix only in external-editor CLI parsing), and the settings dropdown can today only offer {off, Vim} (A4-Q14). Any flag gating new engines gates vaporware — so the per-engine-vs-umbrella flag choice is deferrable until the first engine is actually built. — `A4-Q14 (answers doc line 1659); features.rs:259`

**Options:**

- **Code-editor-only now; migrate setting bool->enum now; lazily add one dark flag per engine (deferred flag-multiplication)** _(med effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Lock surface scope to the code editor (the already-flag-gated opt-in surface) and migrate vim_mode:bool -> editing_mode enum now, because both are forced inputs to the executor-trait (A4-Q4) and settings-UI (A4-Q14) rework. Keep vim under the existing VimCodeEditor flag. Do NOT pre-create Helix/Kakoune flags or an umbrella flag; when the FIRST new engine is actually built, add exactly one dark flag for it (e.g. HelixCodeEditor) that gates only its dropdown option. Terminal keeps its always-on vim, untouched.
  - 👍 Strictly dominates the brief's Option 1: same small blast radius (reuses view.rs:357 + dropdown infra), same ability to ship an unfinished Helix dark on its own cadence, but it does NOT commit to a flag-proliferation policy before any engine exists (the flag would gate vaporware, A4-Q14). It separates the genuinely-forced decisions (surface scope + setting bool->enum, needed to design the trait surface) from the deferrable one (per-engine vs umbrella flags), so the team can move on Phase-1 design without prematurely choosing a flag taxonomy. Leaves terminal's always-on vim (input.rs:2734) untouched = zero regression. Avoids the single-line-prompt UX trap entirely.
  - 👎 Leaves the boolean(terminal)-vs-enum(code editor) surface inconsistency: a user who selects 'Helix' still gets vim in the terminal command line. Does not validate the shared executor against the terminal surface, so a later terminal port could surface the keep_selection/extend trait-surface gap late (terminal uses move_cursors_by_offset, a distinct method from the code editor's vim_move_horizontal_by_offset). Because the blanket dispatch impl (vim.rs:1849) is shared by both surfaces, even 'code-editor-only' must decide whether the terminal rides a parallel legacy VimEvent path or is mechanically ported — bounded but not free.
- **Code-editor-only; create per-engine experimental flags up front (staged) [brief's original recommendation]** _(med effort · easy)_ — v1 modality on the code editor only; eagerly create per-engine flags (HelixCodeEditor, KakouneCodeEditor) so each engine can ship dark and promote independently. Terminal vim untouched.
  - 👍 Same small blast radius as the recommended option and the same independent-promotion property. Per-engine flags are explicit and self-documenting.
  - 👎 Creates flags that gate vaporware — no Helix/Kakoune code exists and the dropdown can only offer {off, Vim} today (A4-Q14), so the KakouneCodeEditor flag (and arguably HelixCodeEditor until Helix lands) is dead flag-lifecycle overhead created before it is needed. The lazy variant gets the same benefit without the premature taxonomy commitment.
- **Both surfaces, unified under one ModalEditing umbrella flag** _(high effort · costly)_ — Pluggable modality covers code editor AND terminal command editor; all engines gated by a single ModalEditing flag; one editing_mode enum drives both surfaces.
  - 👍 Resolves the boolean-vs-enum inconsistency in one move and forces the shared-executor design (A4-Q4) to prove it generalizes across both VimHandler glue impls early. With A4-Q4 corrected, the cost is lower than the brief feared: the executor is one shared blanket impl, and the terminal's second VimHandler glue impl ALREADY exists — so this is a one-time port plus threading a keep_selection/extend flag, not 2x N work. A staff engineer could reasonably argue for doing both now to avoid carrying a parallel legacy VimEvent dispatch for the terminal through the transition.
  - 👎 The real objection is VALUE, not cost: Helix/Kakoune's differentiating commands (s regex-split, x line-select, % whole-file, multi-cursor) are inherently multi-line and degenerate in a single-line shell prompt (A4-Q6) — you build net-new selection-set primitives whose payoff barely exists there. Re-gating the terminal's always-on vim (input.rs:2734) regresses existing users; keeping it always-on while gating only Helix reintroduces the very inconsistency this option set out to remove. Pulls in the terminal-only vim status-bar rework (render_vim_status has no Helix submode/selection-count concept, A4-Q14c). A single umbrella flag forces vim/Helix/Kakoune to promote in lockstep.
- **Code-editor-only; reuse the existing VimCodeEditor flag (no new flag)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Extend the VimCodeEditor-gated dropdown to add Helix/Kakoune as options with no new flag; all engines ride VimCodeEditor on the code editor only.
  - 👍 Lowest effort — flag, dropdown infra, and gated surface all already exist (features.rs:259, A4-Q14). No flag proliferation, no terminal work, no regression. Ships the Helix proof fastest.
  - 👎 Couples Helix's rollout to vim's: sharing one flag means promoting VimCodeEditor toward stable drags an unfinished Helix option into the dropdown for users who already have vim, or you hold vim back for Helix. Makes 'ship Helix dark independently' structurally impossible. Separating them later requires retroactively introducing a flag and possibly clawing back a shipped engine. Same surface inconsistency as the code-editor-only options.

**→ Recommendation: Code-editor-only now; migrate setting bool->enum now; lazily add one dark flag per engine (deferred flag-multiplication).** Code-editor-only scope still wins, but on USER VALUE and REGRESSION grounds, not the brief's executor-cost grounds (which A4-Q4 shows were overstated: the executor is one shared blanket impl, and the terminal's VimHandler glue already exists, so 'both surfaces' is a one-time port, not 2x N work). The durable reasons to exclude the terminal from v1: (1) Helix/Kakoune's differentiating select-then-act commands are multi-line/whole-file and require net-new selection-set primitives (A4-Q6) whose value is degenerate in a single-line shell prompt; (2) re-gating the terminal's hardcoded always-on vim (input.rs:2734) regresses existing users, and not re-gating it reintroduces the inconsistency. The recommended option improves on the brief's Option 1 by DECOUPLING the forced decisions (surface scope + migrating vim_mode:bool to an editing_mode enum, both needed to design the trait surface and settings rework) from the DEFERRABLE one (per-engine vs umbrella flags). Since no second engine exists and the dropdown can only offer {off, Vim} today (A4-Q14), any engine flag gates vaporware — so create exactly one dark flag per engine when that engine is actually built, rather than committing to a flag taxonomy now. This keeps the required new flags at zero in Phase 1 (vim keeps VimCodeEditor) while preserving the ability to ship the first Helix dark. One honesty correction over the brief: 'only one of the two impls is touched' is optimistic — the shared blanket dispatch impl (vim.rs:1849) means even code-editor-only scope must decide whether the terminal rides a parallel legacy path or is mechanically ported when A4-Q4 lands.

- _Residual risk:_ Because A4-Q4 makes the executor a single shared blanket impl AND that impl already serves both surfaces, the marginal engineering cost of including the terminal in v1 is smaller than the brief originally claimed — so a skeptical staff engineer could argue for doing both surfaces in one pass: it fixes the boolean-vs-enum inconsistency immediately and avoids carrying a parallel legacy VimEvent dispatch for the terminal through the transition. The counter that keeps code-editor-only on top is that this is a VALUE/regression argument, not a cost argument: Helix/Kakoune's differentiating multi-selection features are degenerate in a single-line prompt (A4-Q6) and re-gating always-on terminal vim regresses users (input.rs:2734) — and those costs do not shrink just because the executor is shared. If telemetry showed heavy terminal-vim usage AND product judged single-line select-then-act valuable, the both-surfaces option could overtake.
- _Dependencies:_ Forced-now inputs: surface scope + migrating vim_mode:bool -> editing_mode enum (A4-Q11 owns the cloud-sync migration). Gated by A4-Q4 (shared executor) — sharpened: the blanket impl vim.rs:1849 is shared by both surfaces, so A4-Q4's resolution determines whether code-editor-only truly isolates the terminal or forces a terminal trait port/parallel legacy path regardless. Gated by A4-Q1 (whether user-editable per-mode keymaps are in v1 — sets overall surface). Deferrable: the per-engine-vs-umbrella flag strategy can wait until the first new engine is built (no engine exists today; dropdown offers only {off, Vim}, A4-Q14). Coupled to A4-Q6/A4-Q3 (selection-set value only materializes where multi-line selection is meaningful — i.e. not the single-line terminal prompt). Gates A4-Q7 (live mode-switch semantics multiply with surface count) and A4-Q14/A4-Q14c (dropdown, vim sub-settings, and terminal-only status-bar rework expand if terminal is in scope).
- _What would settle it:_ Three inputs: (1) product call on whether the single-line terminal command line is even a target for selection-first modal editing — A4-Q6 shows the differentiating Helix/Kakoune commands are multi-line/whole-file and degenerate there, so this is the crux; (2) telemetry on how many users enable vim in the terminal command editor vs the code editor today, quantifying the regression blast radius of ever re-gating the terminal (input.rs:2734); (3) the resolution of A4-Q4 — specifically whether the shared blanket dispatch impl (vim.rs:1849) forces the terminal's VimHandler glue to be ported regardless of scope (mechanical, bounded) or can be left on a parallel legacy path. If (3) lands such that the terminal must be ported anyway and (1)+(2) show terminal value/usage, the both-surfaces option should be revisited; otherwise code-editor-only stands and the flag-multiplication decision is taken just-in-time per engine.
- _Red-team added option:_ Code-editor-only now; migrate setting bool->enum now; lazily add one dark flag per engine (deferred flag-multiplication)
- _Red-team:_ Recommendation (code-editor-only) UPHELD, but its load-bearing rationale was wrong and is replaced. (1) FALSE PREMISE REMOVED: the brief's core cost claim — 'covering both surfaces literally doubles executor work (2 impls x N engines)' — is contradicted by its own cited source A4-Q4. The dispatch executor is ONE shared blanket impl (impl<T> VimSubscriber for T where T: VimHandler, vim.rs:1849-1942); the two VimHandler impls are per-SURFACE glue that already both exist and do NOT multiply by engine. 'Both surfaces' adds a one-time terminal-glue port + threading a keep_selection/extend flag, not an N-fold cost. I re-priced the 'both surfaces' option from very-high to high and rewrote its what_breaks. (2) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY surfaced: that blanket impl is shared by BOTH surfaces, so refactoring it to the proposed ModalEditExecutor (A4-Q4) cannot cleanly isolate the terminal — code-editor-only either ports the terminal glue anyway or runs a parallel legacy VimEvent dispatch during the transition. The brief's 'only one of the two impls is touched' is optimistic. (3) The recommendation now rests on durable arguments: USER VALUE (Helix/Kakoune's differentiating multi-selection commands are multi-line and degenerate in a single-line shell prompt — A4-Q6) and REGRESSION risk (re-gating always-on terminal vim, input.rs:2734), neither of which depends on the false cost claim. (4) CITATION FIX: brief cited mod.rs:4920-4921 for the terminal gate; that range is empty — actual sites are input.rs:7490 / terminal/view.rs:13435 via AppEditorSettings::vim_mode_enabled(). (5) DEFERABILITY: the per-engine-vs-umbrella flag sub-decision gates vaporware (no engine exists; dropdown can only offer {off, Vim}, A4-Q14) and is deferrable; only surface-scope + the setting bool->enum migration are forced now. Added a decoupled/lazy-flag option as the recommendation.

### A4-Q3 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product decision (doc tags it user-product-decision): the keybindings-initiative product owner sets the acceptance bar / target user, co-signed by the editor + vim-crate eng-lead who owns the crates/editor selection-model scope the bar implies. The choice of which single probe command is an eng-lead call within the product-set tier (and the verified-correct answer is s/select-regex-within, not C/add-cursor, since C reuses existing AddCursorAt). Because Axis 4 is Phase 3, the TIMING owner is whoever sequences Phase-3 entry — the bar need not be set until Phase 2 is landing, and should be set by the spike result.

**Decision.** What is the acceptance bar for "Helix ships as the proof that the EditorCommand IR generalizes" — minimal select-then-act (single persistent selection + d/c/y), full multi-selection Helix (s/S/x/%/;/,/C + primary-selection pointer), or a tiered bar where minimal ships plus exactly one REGEX-driven selection-set-creation command (recommend s/select-regex-within) is built end-to-end as the IR probe — AND, given Axis 4 is Phase-3 work, whether the bar must be set now at all or deferred to Phase-3 entry and decided by a spike rather than opinion.

**Stakes.** "Helix as the proof" is the artifact meant to validate the Axis-4 claim that the FSA→VimEvent→VimHandler pipeline generalizes into one mode-agnostic IR + one ModalEditExecutor per engine. Set the bar too low and you declare the IR "proven" while never exercising the one truly net-new surface — regex/pattern-driven selection-set CREATION (zero primitives today) plus a primary-selection pointer (no model state) — and risk retrofitting multi-selection after the IR has ossified, producing a false "proven" stamp Helix users will call broken. Set it too high and you hostage Axis-4 sign-off to a multi-month crates/editor selection-model build plus an unprofiled per-keystroke N-selection perf question (A4-Q16, still open) that is largely orthogonal to whether the engine is pluggable. CRUCIAL REFRAME: Axis 4 is Phase 3 (end-goals:120,132), so this bar gates nothing in Phases 1-2 — the cost of getting it wrong is concentrated in Phase 3 (executor trait surface, IR shape, regression net). The real present risk is committing the bar — and with it the costly-to-reverse primary-index model decision (A4-Q6) — by opinion now instead of by a Phase-3-entry spike. Note the apply path is NOT the gap (already multi-selection), and raw add-cursor/add-selection primitives already exist; only regex-driven creation + primary-pointer + collapse/rotate are absent.

_Constrained by:_
- Per-selection APPLY is already fully multi-selection, so it is NOT the Helix gap: selections() returns Vec1 (selection.rs:642); update_selections_internal merges overlapping then iter/zip/map/unzip over the whole Vec1 (selection.rs:683,694-697); vim operators map over the set then write via vim_set_selections->BufferSelectAction::SetSelectionOffsets (vim_handler.rs:237,364-372). — `crates/editor/src/selection.rs:642,683,694-697; app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:237,364-372`
- CORRECTION to the brief's 'ZERO primitives' claim: selection-set CREATION is partly built. BufferSelectAction includes AddCursorAt and AddSelection creation variants, reachable from the code editor (model.rs:1811; selection.rs:489/499 add_cursor, :579 AddSelection). Genuinely absent: REGEX/pattern-driven creation (s/S/x/keep/remove), a primary-selection pointer, collapse-each/rotate. So C/add-cursor is a NON-probative probe (reuses AddCursorAt); s/select-regex-within is the only probe that hits truly net-new surface. — `crates/editor/src/content/buffer.rs:164-197 (AddCursorAt:177, AddSelection:182); app/src/code/editor/model.rs:1811; crates/editor/src/selection.rs:489,499,579`
- Regex/split/keep/rotate selection-set primitive grep across crates/editor, crates/vim, app/src returns EXACTLY 2 hits, both notebooks collapse_selections_to_cursors (collapses ALL selections to one cursor at the first head — neither Helix ; nor ,). Search/find selects a SINGLE match only (search.rs:172,199,243); no multi-match regex selection exists. — `grep crates/editor crates/vim app/src => 2 hits (app/src/notebooks/editor/{model.rs:1221,view.rs:2026}); crates/editor/src/search.rs:172,199,243`
- Minimal single-persistent-selection ExtendSelection is cheap: the extend/collapse switch already exists one layer below the motion fan-out — vim_move_horizontal_by_offset takes keep_selection:bool and sets `tail: if keep_selection { selection.tail } else { head }` (model.rs:2315-2321,2410-2413); navigate_* hardcode false (vim_handler.rs:40,49). So minimal proof = threading an existing bool, no new buffer algorithm. — `app/src/code/editor/model.rs:2315-2321,2410-2413; app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:40,49`
- The IR-to-new-primitive seam is largely DECOUPLED: A4-Q6 establishes selection-set ops can be 'new BufferSelectAction variants vs free functions computing a Vec1 then calling the existing SetSelectionOffsets — both viable on today's substrate.' So the new primitive is mostly self-contained crates/editor algorithm code; the IR carries only intent. This narrows how much a single selection-set probe actually tests 'does the IR generalize.' — `answers doc A4-Q6 residual (:1404); crates/editor/src/content/buffer.rs SetSelectionOffsets:193-197`
- There are ZERO FSA/IR-level golden tests: crates/vim/src/vim.rs (the FSA) has no test module; the 173 handler tests (49 code-editor + 124 terminal) assert only buffer_text/cursor/mode, never the emitted VimEvent/EditorCommand stream, across two duplicated harnesses (34 shared names) with no shared keystroke-trace fixture. Any 'IR generalizes' bar requires net-new test scaffolding regardless of tier. — `answers doc A4-Q13 (:1633); crates/vim/src/vim.rs (no #[test]); app/src/{code/editor/view,editor/view}/vim_handler_tests.rs`
- A4-Q16 (per-keystroke N-selection re-merge/re-normalize/re-render budget for %/x/s on a large file) is an OPEN, UNANSWERED question — it appears only as a question (readiness:733) with NO answer section in the answers doc. The perf risk is genuinely unprofiled. — `docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md:733; no A4-Q16 section in the answers doc`
- Axis 4 — ModalEngine, EditorCommand IR, and the Helix proof — is PHASE 3, the last/highest-risk phase, explicitly gated on the Phase-2 context model. So A4-Q3 blocks neither Phase 1 nor Phase 2 and is deferrable to Phase-3 entry. The doc also lists 'Helix/Kakoune grammar depth: multi-selection fit onto ExtendSelection/ApplyOperator is unproven beyond basic cases' as an honest unknown. — `docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-end-goals-design.md:120,132,154`

**Options:**

- **Minimal select-then-act proof** _(low effort · easy)_ — Sign-off = single persistent selection: existing vim motions (w/e/b/...) extend one selection via keep_selection=true, d/c/y/p consume it, plus mode/cursor/status-bar/context wiring and the structural refactor (collapse the two VimHandler impls into one blanket ModalEditExecutor on a shared Entity emitting EditorCommand). No multi-selection, no regex commands, no primary pointer.
  - 👍 Cheapest, lowest-risk, and it actually proves the Axis-4 THESIS as written: the structural risk lives in the Entity/Event/executor collapse (one subscription, one blanket impl replacing vim.rs:1849) and the motion/operator vocabulary (A4-Q5) — both fully exercised here — not in the selection-set algorithm layer, which A4-Q6 shows is decoupled crates/editor work bolted on via the existing SetSelectionOffsets write action. Reuses the already-multi apply path (selection.rs:694-697) and the existing keep_selection bool (model.rs:2410-2413), so 'no new buffer algorithm required' is literally true. Ships fast; multi-selection becomes a clean fast-follow once the seam is proven.
  - 👎 Validates a Helix nobody recognizes: it never touches regex-driven selection-set creation (s/S/x/keep), the missing primary pointer, or collapse/rotate — the only genuinely net-new surface. If multi-selection later forces an IR reshape (parametric SelectRegex, or neutral motion/operator types per A4-Q5) you discover it AFTER declaring victory on a hardened IR. Helix refugees will call it broken. The risk is mitigated (not eliminated) by A4-Q6 showing selection-set ops compose via SetSelectionOffsets without IR reshape in the common case.
- **Full multi-selection Helix proof** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Sign-off requires Helix's identity: s/S regex-select-and-split, x line-select, % whole-file, ; collapse-each / , keep-primary, C add/copy-cursor, rotate-primary — backed by a new primary-selection pointer in the model and the regex-driven selection-set primitives.
  - 👍 The only bar that honestly tests whether the IR + the net-new crates/editor primitives compose at Helix's actual identity, forcing the missing primitives and the primary-index model state to be designed while the IR is still malleable rather than retrofitted onto a frozen IR.
  - 👎 Couples Phase-3 sign-off to a multi-month crates/editor project (new regex-driven BufferSelectAction variants/free-functions, a primary-index on a Vec1 model that has none, the regex-DFA compute path that search.rs only does single-match today) PLUS the unprofiled A4-Q16 per-keystroke N-selection re-normalization cost (selection.rs:683,694-697 re-merge every update, untested at large N). Balloons scope; the primary-pointer model decision is costly to reverse. Much of this is feature work orthogonal to engine pluggability — over-pays for the IR-generalization signal.
- **Tiered proof — minimal ships, one regex selection-set command is the IR probe** _(med effort · costly)_ — Bar = the minimal set PLUS exactly one REGEX-driven selection-set-creation command — s/select-regex-within — built fully end-to-end (new crates/editor regex-split primitive + skeletal primary-index + the IR command that drives it), but NOT the full suite. C/add-cursor is explicitly rejected as the probe because AddCursorAt already exists and would not exercise net-new surface.
  - 👍 Gets the minimal option's de-risking AND forces the one genuinely net-new seam — regex-compute over per-selection bytes producing a Vec1 of sub-selections — to be exercised once, surfacing whether selection-set intent fits the IR as a clean intent variant or forces a parametric/neutral reshape (A4-Q5), and giving a free A4-Q16 perf sniff on s/% over a large buffer, all while the IR is still malleable. s is the correct single probe: it hits the regex-compute primitive that has no analog today, where C merely reuses AddCursorAt.
  - 👎 Still not 'real Helix' to a refugee, so it does not settle the target-user question alone. Its IR-generalization value is narrower than advertised: A4-Q6 shows the regex-split can be implemented as 'compute Vec1, call existing SetSelectionOffsets,' so the probe largely proves 'we can write a regex feature' rather than stressing the IR — it mainly de-risks one IR intent variant and the primary-index design. One probe may not surface the A4-Q16 perf cliff that only appears at large N. 'Exactly one' invites scope-creep negotiation, and even one regex-split command commits to the costly-to-reverse primary-pointer design in v1.
- **Refactor-first / defer the command-set bar to Phase-3 entry (ADDED)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Recognize that Axis 4 is Phase 3 and commit NOTHING on the command set now. The early deliverable is the structural generalization — single shared Entity emitting EditorCommand, one blanket ModalEditExecutor replacing the two VimHandler impls, plus net-new keystroke->EditorCommand golden fixtures proving vim behavior is byte-preserved through the VimEventType->EditorCommand rename. The A4-Q3 bar (minimal vs tiered vs full) is explicitly deferred and decided at Phase-3 entry by the spike + a product target-user call.
  - 👍 Honest to the documented Phase-3 gating (end-goals:120,132): the bar blocks nothing in Phases 1-2, so locking it now is premature. De-risks the high-value structural change (the actual generalization claim) and builds the golden net that A4-Q13 says is net-new regardless — before spending any product capital on a target-user/command-set call that depends on a spike result, not opinion. Keeps the costly-to-reverse primary-index decision out of the critical path until it's actually needed.
  - 👎 A refactor with exactly one implementor (vim) is the classic single-implementor-abstraction smell — it does not, by itself, prove pluggability; you still owe a second-engine probe before you can claim 'the IR generalizes.' So this defers the question rather than answering it, and risks an IR that looks generic but quietly bakes in vim assumptions only a real second engine would expose. Must be paired with a committed Phase-3 probe (the tiered option) or it becomes an open-ended 'we'll prove it later.'

**→ Recommendation: Tiered proof — minimal ships, one regex selection-set command (s/select-regex-within) is the IR probe — sequenced as a Phase-3-entry decision, set by the spike, not committed now.** The decision exists to make 'Helix proves the IR generalizes' true, not slogan-true — but it is a Phase-3 decision (end-goals:120,132) that blocks nothing in Phases 1-2, so the actionable now is NOT to lock a command set but to sequence: (a) land the structural generalization + net-new golden net (the added Refactor-first option, which all tiers share and which carries the real IR-generalization signal), then (b) at Phase-3 entry run the spike and set the tier. The minimal option proves only the part that was already near-free (apply is multi-selection per selection.rs:694-697; ExtendSelection is threading an existing keep_selection bool per model.rs:2410-2413) and never touches regex-driven creation — risking a false 'proven' stamp. The full option over-commits sign-off to a multi-month editor build plus the still-open A4-Q16 perf question, largely orthogonal to pluggability. The tiered option is the cheapest way to make the proof probative WHEN Helix ships: build exactly one regex selection-set command, and make it s/select-regex-within — NOT C/add-cursor, because (verified) AddCursorAt already exists (buffer.rs:177; model.rs:1811) so C reuses existing substrate and proves nothing, whereas s hits the only genuinely net-new surface (regex-compute over per-selection bytes; search.rs is single-match only). Bind any tier with a net-new keystroke->EditorCommand golden fixture (A4-Q13) since no IR-level regression net exists. The honest caveat, carried into residual_risk: A4-Q6 shows even s can compose via the existing SetSelectionOffsets write action, so the probe's incremental IR-confidence over minimal is modest — its main jobs are to keep one selection-set IR variant malleable-while-deciding and to provide a free A4-Q16 perf sniff.

- _Residual risk:_ A4-Q6 shows that a regex selection-set command can be implemented as 'compute a Vec1 of offsets with regex over buffer bytes, then call the EXISTING SetSelectionOffsets write action' — both viable on today's substrate. That means the s probe largely lives in self-contained crates/editor algorithm code and touches the IR only as a single intent variant; it mostly proves 'we can write a regex-split feature,' which was never the IR-generalization risk. The real generalization risk is concentrated in the Entity/Event/executor refactor and the motion/operator vocabulary (A4-Q5), both of which the minimal option already exercises. So a skeptic can argue the tiered probe buys little incremental IR-confidence over minimal while committing, in v1, to the costly-to-reverse primary-index model design — and that the cleaner move is Refactor-first + defer the bar to a genuine product target-user call. The probe is justified mainly as (1) a forcing function to keep one selection-set IR command malleable while the IR is decided and (2) a free A4-Q16 perf sniff — not as deep architectural validation. If the spike shows s composes via SetSelectionOffsets without IR reshape, the IR-generalization worry largely evaporates and even the full option is cheaper than feared.
- _Dependencies:_ PHASE-GATED: Axis 4 (ModalEngine, EditorCommand IR, Helix proof) is Phase 3 (end-goals:120,132), so A4-Q3 blocks neither Phase 1 nor Phase 2 — it is deferrable to Phase-3 entry. Gated by A4-Q6 (which selection-set primitives are net-new — regex creation + primary-pointer + collapse/rotate, NOT raw add-cursor which exists; and whether the new op is a BufferSelectAction variant vs a free-function over the existing SetSelectionOffsets — the latter de-risks the IR seam). Tightly coupled to A4-Q5 (does the selection-set IR command carry vim enums or neutral motion/operator types — a fuller proof pressures this harder). Coupled to A4-Q4 (shared Entity/Event + blanket ModalEditExecutor + ExtendSelection reusing the navigate fan-out via the existing keep_selection bool) which sets the executor seam the proof rides on. Constrained by A4-Q13 (no IR golden tests exist; the bar must add a net-new shared keystroke->EditorCommand fixture regardless of tier). A4-Q16 (per-keystroke N-selection perf) is OPEN/unanswered and becomes blocking only if multi-selection at scale enters scope (Option 2, or the probe extended). Bounded by A4-Q1/A4-Q2 (per-mode keymap editability and which surfaces/flag-gating) which frame the surrounding deliverable.
- _What would settle it:_ Two inputs, both schedulable at Phase-3 entry (NOT a Phase-1 blocker). (1) A product call on the target user: v1 Helix for 'Helix-curious vim users' (minimal suffices) or 'Helix refugees' (multi-selection mandatory) — narrows minimal vs full. (2) A 1-2 week eng spike building exactly the s/select-regex-within primitive end-to-end — regex-split compute over per-selection bytes + skeletal primary-index + the IR command — measuring: (a) whether it composes via the EXISTING SetSelectionOffsets write action (A4-Q6) or forces an IR reshape / parametric SelectRegex / neutral types (A4-Q5); and (b) a quick A4-Q16 perf sniff on s/% over a large buffer. Decision rule: if the spike composes cleanly via SetSelectionOffsets and stays in frame budget, the IR-generalization worry largely evaporates and scoping up toward the full suite (Option 2) is cheaper than feared; if it forces IR rework or hits the re-normalization perf cliff, lock the minimal bar (Option 1) and keep s as the only multi-selection command (Option 3). Until then, execute the Refactor-first deliverable (structural generalization + golden net) which is shared by every tier and carries the real IR-generalization signal. The spike result, not opinion, picks the final tier.
- _Red-team added option:_ Refactor-first / defer the command-set bar to Phase-3 entry (prove generalization structurally via shared Entity+Event+blanket executor+golden net; commit no Helix command set now)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code anchor directly; the brief's recommendation (tiered: minimal + one regex selection-set probe) survives but is corrected and sharpened on four points. (1) GROUNDING CORRECTION: the headline claim "selection-set CREATION has ZERO primitives" is overstated. BufferSelectAction (crates/editor/src/content/buffer.rs:164-197) already has AddCursorAt and AddSelection creation variants, reachable from the code editor (app/src/code/editor/model.rs:1811; selection.rs:489/499/579). What is genuinely net-new is REGEX/PATTERN-driven creation (s/S/x/keep/remove), a primary-selection pointer, and collapse-each/rotate. This STRENGTHENS the brief's preference for s over C as the probe: C/add-cursor would mostly reuse existing AddCursorAt and is therefore non-probative. The brief called C merely "lighter"; it is in fact largely already built. (2) DEFERABILITY (missing framing): the end-goals doc (lines 120, 132, 154) puts Axis 4 — ModalEngine, EditorCommand IR, and the Helix proof — in PHASE 3, the last/highest-risk phase, gated on the Phase-2 context model. So A4-Q3 blocks neither Phase 1 nor Phase 2; the bar need not be set now. The brief reads as if the bar must be locked immediately. (3) NARROWER PROBE VALUE (residual): A4-Q6 establishes selection-set ops can be "compute a Vec1 over buffer bytes/regex, then call the EXISTING SetSelectionOffsets write action" — both viable on today's substrate. So the new primitive lives almost entirely in self-contained crates/editor algorithm code and touches the IR only as one intent variant; the s probe mostly proves "we can write a regex feature," which was never the IR-generalization risk (that lives in the Entity/Event/executor refactor + A4-Q5 vocabulary, which minimal already exercises). (4) A4-Q16 is genuinely UNANSWERED in the answers doc (only a question at readiness:733), so the per-keystroke N-selection perf risk is real and unprofiled — correctly priced, kept as a spike item. Added a fourth option (refactor-first / defer the bar) that the brief omitted.

---

## Axis 5 — Discoverability & truthful conflicts

### A5-Q1 — FLIPPED · _product_ · owner: The Keybindings initiative product DRI decides (user-product-decision), with required sign-off from the Resource Center / docs owner, who owns how much hand-curation (Essentials membership and section ordering) must be preserved as data — and since this option KEEPS that curation rather than deleting it, that sign-off is low-friction. The warpui engine owner is NOT load-bearing for the Phase-1 increment: the resolvability test and the shared app-side builder use the existing get_key_bindings/editable_bindings APIs with no engine change. The engine owner's call is deferred to the separate fixed-binding-identity decision (A5-Q3/Q4) that gates the eventual union.

**Decision.** For catalog-v1, decide how to make the keybindings catalog structurally truthful given that trigger data already comes from ONE live source — the keymap, exposed as editable_bindings() for the settings editor and get_key_bindings() for the resource-center cheatsheet — while the static name arrays in resource_center/utils.rs and PRIORITIZED_KEYBINDINGS act only as a hand-maintained sectioning/ordering overlay (the cheatsheet's triggers are already live-derived; only its sectioning is curated). Choose among: (a) replace everything with one unioned live manifest and delete the arrays, sectioning by BindingGroup; (b) reconcile by build-time codegen; (c) converge the editor's and cheatsheet's independent source walks onto one shared, filter-parameterized walk over the live keymap, keep the curation arrays as CI-guarded data, and defer the fixed+editable union; (d) coexist with only a resolvability test; or (e) defer and declare the settings catalog canonical.

**Stakes.** The North Star is 'what the UI shows is the truth.' The brief's framing — three drifting sources that cannot all be right — is half true: trigger data already has one source (the keymap), the cheatsheet's triggers are already live-derived, and the cheatsheet's Fundamentals catch-all guarantees no live binding is orphaned. The only hand-maintained, drift-prone artifact is the curation overlay (which name goes in which section); a curated name that no longer resolves silently disappears with no test to catch it. So the genuinely contestable design question is narrower than implied: (a) whether to converge the editor's and cheatsheet's independent source walks so they provably read the same keymap, and (b) whether to add the missing resolvability test. The maximal 'delete the arrays and unify fixed+editable into one complete manifest' move is real but gated on fixed-binding identity (A5-Q3/Q4, ~167 user-reachable sites) and would REGRESS the cheatsheet if attempted editable-only — so it is not a Phase-1 prerequisite. Getting this wrong additively (bolt on a fourth catalog) re-creates the discoverability gap; over-scoping it (force the union now) blocks Phase 1 on a large separate refactor for a benefit that is partly a category error, since the editor and cheatsheet legitimately show different filtered sets for different jobs.

_Constrained by:_
- The cheatsheet's TRIGGER data is already live-derived (build_bindings walks get_key_bindings then filters trigger.is_some() && non-empty description); only its SECTIONING is hand-curated via name-membership in the static arrays. So there is no independent third source of trigger data — only a curation overlay over the live list. The arrays' own header says they 'should always align with documentation' (drift policed by hand). — `app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:136-154,229-253; app/src/resource_center/utils.rs:1-2,8-114`
- The cheatsheet's Fundamentals section is a CATCH-ALL: every binding not in any other static array is chained into Fundamentals, so NO live binding passing the trigger+description filter is orphaned. The only invisibility is the build_bindings filter (triggerless / empty-description), not section membership — so 'live bindings in no section' is structurally zero and does not discriminate between the options. — `app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:255-277`
- BindingGroup cannot serve as a complete sectioning dimension: lens.group is Option<&'static str> with many editable bindings None, and ALL FixedBinding constructors set group:None. 'Derive sections from BindingGroup' therefore dumps fixed bindings and None-group editables into a catch-all, so a name-keyed sectioning overlay must survive — 'delete the arrays' is illusory. — `A5-Q2 (bindings.rs:797-810; group Option at keymap.rs:316); A5-Q3 (FixedBinding constructors all set group:None, keymap.rs:511-593)`
- The editor and cheatsheet are two FILTERED VIEWS of one keymap, not two independent sources: settings builds from editable_bindings() via from_editable_lens (never returns None, so includes unbound editables); cheatsheet builds from get_key_bindings() via from_lens (returns None for description-less bindings) then filters trigger.is_some(). A single manifest would still feed both with different filters, so Replace does not eliminate the set-difference. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:748-784; app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:136-154; app/src/util/bindings.rs:749-771`
- An editable-only v1 manifest would regress the cheatsheet by at most the ~65 description-bearing fixed bindings (44 custom + 21 empty constructors) that currently survive from_lens; the other ~404 fixed bindings (new/standard/new_per_platform) are description-less and already dropped today. This bounds the cheatsheet-regression risk of any source switch and is why the cheatsheet must stay on get_key_bindings until A5-Q3/Q4. — `A5-Q3 (keymap.rs:511-593,633; from_lens bindings.rs:749-758)`
- The resolvability test is low-risk and uniquely correct: an existing test (App::test + ctx.editable_bindings().find(name)) already spot-checks curated names, so asserting every array name resolves is a direct generalization; unlike build-time codegen it runs against the true cfg/platform-gated registered set. No completeness/conflict/resolvability test exists today across 147+120=267 register sites in 111 files. — `A5-Q13 (bindings_tests.rs:76-116; register sites 147 fixed + 120 editable across 111 files)`
- The only durable catalog key is the editable binding name (already the keybindings.yaml key); fixed bindings are nameless (FixedBinding::as_lens sets name = Default::default()), so a name-keyed manifest is editable-only until A5-Q3/Q4 assign fixed bindings a stable name. — `A5-Q12 (keymap.rs:294; keyboard.rs:155-187); A5-Q3 (keymap.rs:633)`

**Options:**

- **Replace — one live manifest is the only source; delete static arrays, section by BindingGroup** _(high effort · costly)_ — catalog-v1 is a single live walk keyed on name; the cheatsheet becomes a view over the same manifest with sections derived from BindingGroup; the hand-maintained name arrays are deleted; editorial prioritization survives only as a thin overlay; full fixed-binding coverage is phased in once A5-Q3/Q4 give fixed bindings a stable name.
  - 👍 The only option that forces the full fixed+editable union and an honest single complete catalog, and keeps the team committed to finishing the union rather than letting 'defer' become 'never.' Much machinery already exists (the live walk, the durable name key, a semantic group field).
  - 👎 Its two defining moves are not viable as stated. (1) BindingGroup cannot section fixed bindings (all group:None, A5-Q3) or the many None-group editables, so 'section by BindingGroup' silently dumps most bindings into a catch-all and a name-keyed sectioning overlay must survive anyway — 'delete the arrays' is illusory. (2) The full union is gated on A5-Q3/Q4 (~167 user-reachable fixed-binding sites, X5), and until it lands an editable-only 'replace' would REGRESS the cheatsheet by dropping the ≤65 description-bearing fixed bindings it shows today. The 'removes the structural contradiction' premise is partly a category error: the editor and cheatsheet filter the same keymap differently because they have different jobs, and a single manifest still feeds differently-filtered views. Highest coordination cost across product/engine/docs.
- **Reconcile by codegen — generate the static lists from the manifest at build time** _(high effort · costly)_ — keep curation in source as annotations but emit/assert the section and Essentials arrays from a build-time walk of the binding registry, so a static list can never reference a name that does not resolve; runtime keeps the two renderers fed by a generated artifact.
  - 👍 Preserves human curation and both renderers while making name-membership drift impossible by construction, with no runtime engine change.
  - 👎 Bindings are registered imperatively at 267 sites across 111 files, many behind cfg/platform gates, so a build-time generator cannot see the true runtime set without effectively running app init — the snapshot can itself diverge. It is strictly dominated by a RUNTIME resolvability test (App::test, as bindings_tests.rs already does), which sees the real gated set. Adds build-pipeline complexity, still leaves two renderers and two filter policies, and does nothing about nameless fixed bindings.
- **Coexist + resolvability guard — keep all surfaces, add the missing CI test** _(low effort · easy)_ — leave the settings catalog, cheatsheet, and static arrays as-is, but add the resolvability test that does not exist today: assert every static curated name resolves to a live binding.
  - 👍 The cheapest credible step and the correct first increment. The test is a direct generalization of an existing test (App::test + editable_bindings().find(name), bindings_tests.rs:76-116), so it is low risk/effort, and it kills the ONLY real silent-drift vector — a dangling curated name — because the cheatsheet's Fundamentals catch-all already guarantees no live binding is orphaned. Ships this release; preserves all curation and UX; buys time for A5-Q3/Q4.
  - 👎 Stops short of converging the source: the editor and cheatsheet keep walking the keymap independently (editable_bindings vs get_key_bindings), so they can still diverge in which collection/filter they apply even though neither is 'wrong.' Cheatsheet still hides unbound actions and settings still hides fixed bindings — but note that gap is largely by-design and the fixed-clobber risk is A5-Q6's job, not this decision's.
- **Converge the source builder + keep curation as CI-guarded data (defer fixed+editable union to A5-Q3/Q4)** _(med effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Ship the resolvability CI test (the Coexist increment) as the non-negotiable first step. Refactor the settings editor and the cheatsheet to draw their CommandBinding list from ONE shared source walk over the live keymap, parameterized by an explicit filter policy (editor: editable incl. unbound; cheatsheet: triggered), so the two surfaces provably read the same underlying set. KEEP the static name arrays + PRIORITIZED as a name-keyed curation/ordering overlay, now CI-guarded so names cannot dangle, rather than deleting them. Leave the cheatsheet's source on get_key_bindings so it keeps its described fixed bindings. Defer the full fixed+editable union and any BindingGroup re-sectioning until A5-Q3/Q4 give fixed bindings a stable name/group.
  - 👍 Delivers the achievable part of the North Star NOW — one source walk, curation that cannot dangle — at low/medium cost and easy reversibility, without blocking on the 167-site fixed-binding project or the unsolved scope primitive. It formalizes what the grounding shows is already reality (the arrays are a curation overlay over a live list) instead of pretending to introduce a green-field manifest. It sidesteps the BindingGroup-sectioning trap (group:None for fixed and many editables) and avoids the cheatsheet regression. The shared builder is exactly where the future union plugs in, so it keeps the maximal option open without paying for it prematurely.
  - 👎 Does not produce a single complete surface: the cheatsheet still won't list unbound actions and settings still won't list fixed bindings, so a user expecting ONE place to see everything is under-served until the deferred union lands. The shared-builder refactor is real work touching both build paths and must preserve the editor's ConflictMap/dedup behavior (keybindings.rs:782). It converges/manages the filter divergence rather than eliminating it — though that divergence is largely appropriate to the two surfaces' jobs.
- **Defer — declare the settings catalog canonical, leave cheatsheet and arrays untouched** _(low effort · easy)_ — name the live settings catalog the source of truth for rebinding and conflicts, leave the cheatsheet and its static arrays as a separate quick reference, and do nothing to reconcile them this cycle.
  - 👍 Zero new work; the settings catalog is already live and is where editing happens, so other A5 workstreams proceed without blocking on catalog unification.
  - 👎 Ships the exact additive anti-pattern this question names: two 'live' catalogs both claim authority while the static arrays keep rotting with no guard (a dangling curated name silently vanishes). Cheapest in the short term, most expensive in accumulated silent drift.

**→ Recommendation: Converge the source builder + keep curation as CI-guarded data (defer fixed+editable union to A5-Q3/Q4).** The brief's premise — pick a single source of truth among three drifting surfaces — is partly false on the code. There is already ONE source of trigger data (the keymap), exposed as two filtered views (editable_bindings for the editor, get_key_bindings for the cheatsheet), plus a hand-maintained curation overlay (the name arrays). The cheatsheet's triggers are already live-derived (grounding fact 1), and its Fundamentals catch-all (keybindings_page.rs:255-277) guarantees no live binding is orphaned, so the only genuine silent-drift vector is a curated name that no longer resolves — which a runtime resolvability test (generalizing the existing bindings_tests.rs:76-116 App::test pattern) closes at low cost. Replace's headline claim that it 'removes the structural ability for two catalogs to disagree' is overstated: the editor and cheatsheet filter the same keymap differently because they have different jobs (an edit surface that must show unbound editables you can bind; a read-only reference of what keys are bound), and a single manifest would STILL feed differently-filtered views — so the set-difference is not eliminated by Replace. Worse, Replace's two defining moves are not viable as stated: BindingGroup cannot section fixed bindings (all group:None, A5-Q3) or the many None-group editables, so a name-keyed sectioning overlay must survive regardless; and the fixed+editable union is gated on A5-Q3/Q4 (~167 user-reachable sites, X5), during which an editable-only 'replace' would REGRESS the cheatsheet by dropping the ≤65 description-bearing fixed bindings it shows today. The brief's own phasing concedes exactly this by keeping the cheatsheet on get_key_bindings — which is this hybrid, not Replace. Therefore: ship the resolvability test now (mandatory, low risk), converge the two build paths onto one parameterized source walk, keep curation as CI-guarded data, and defer the union and any BindingGroup re-sectioning to A5-Q3/Q4. This is NOT blocking for Phase 1 beyond the cheap test. Codegen (Option 2) is dominated by a runtime test that actually sees the cfg-gated set; Defer (Option 5) ships the additive anti-pattern; pure Coexist (Option 3) is the correct first increment but stops short of converging the independently-walked source.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the product one: if the initiative truly wants ONE surface where a user can find ANY bindable action — bound or unbound, editable or fixed, the literal North Star — then this hybrid does not deliver it. It keeps the editor and cheatsheet as complementary partial views and defers the only thing that produces a single complete catalog (the fixed+editable union) behind A5-Q3/Q4. A staff engineer reading the North Star maximally could argue we are choosing convenience over the stated end-state, and that committing to Replace's direction now — even if executed incrementally — keeps the team honest about finishing the union rather than letting 'defer' become 'never' (the Defer option's failure mode). Mitigant: the union is genuinely blocked on fixed-binding identity (A5-Q3/Q4, ~167 sites) and on entry-granularity (A5-Q8, one name maps to N context-distinct bindings); building it before those land would be building on sand, and the hybrid's shared builder is precisely the seam where the union plugs in later at no rework cost.
- _Dependencies:_ Non-blocking for Phase 1 except the resolvability CI test, which should ship this cycle. The deferred source-union consumes A5-Q3/Q4 (stable fixed-binding name/id; ~167 user-reachable sites per X5), A5-Q12 (manifest key = editable name), and A5-Q8 (one name maps to N context-distinct bindings — governs whether a unified entry is one row or N). A5-Q2/Q8 (catalog has no scope dimension) and A5-Q10 (platform-triggerless) define what 'complete' means but do not block the test or the shared builder. The shared-builder refactor must preserve the editor's ConflictMap and same-name+same-description dedup behavior (keybindings.rs:782) and the cheatsheet's Fundamentals catch-all.
- _What would settle it:_ Run the one-off startup walk dumping get_key_bindings() and editable_bindings() and diff against the union of the five static arrays (BLOCKS/INPUT_EDITOR/TERMINAL/FUNDAMENTALS + PRIORITIZED). ONE number is decisive: how many curated names no longer resolve to a live binding (dangling curation). If greater than zero, coexistence is already silently lying and the resolvability test is mandated now, not optional. The brief's second number — 'live bindings in no section' — is structurally ZERO because the cheatsheet's Fundamentals catch-all (keybindings_page.rs:255-277) absorbs every uncategorized binding, so it does not discriminate between options; replace it with the number that actually prices the regression risk of any source switch: how many description-bearing FIXED bindings the cheatsheet renders today (≤65 candidates from the custom/empty constructors that survive from_lens, A5-Q3) — these are exactly what an editable-only manifest would drop, confirming why the cheatsheet must stay on get_key_bindings until A5-Q3/Q4. In parallel, get the docs owner's call on curation depth: if 'derivable from priority/order,' the overlay is thin; if 'substantial hand-curation,' keep the arrays verbatim as CI-guarded data (this option already does). The engine owner's A5-Q3/Q4 timeline sets when fixed bindings can join the union — the phasing boundary.
- _Red-team added option:_ Converge the source builder + keep curation as CI-guarded data (defer fixed+editable union to A5-Q3/Q4)
