- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every cited file plus the cheatsheet build path directly. Three load-bearing corrections flip the recommended option from Replace to a cheaper hybrid: (1) The brief's own grounding (fact 2) says the cheatsheet's TRIGGERS are already live-derived from get_key_bindings; only its SECTIONING is hand-curated. So there is no independent third 'source of truth' for trigger data — there is one keymap exposed as two filtered views (editable_bindings for the editor, get_key_bindings for the cheatsheet) plus a name-keyed curation overlay. The 'three drifting sources that cannot all be right' framing overstates the problem. (2) I read keybindings_page.rs:255-277 directly: the Fundamentals section is a CATCH-ALL that absorbs every uncategorized binding, so NO live binding passing the trigger+description filter is ever orphaned. The brief's settle-it metric '(2) how many live bindings appear in no section' is structurally zero and does not discriminate between options — the only real silent-drift vector is a dangling curated NAME, which a low-cost runtime resolvability test (a direct generalization of the existing bindings_tests.rs:76-116 App::test pattern) closes. (3) Replace's two defining moves are not viable as stated: BindingGroup cannot section fixed bindings (all constructed group:None, A5-Q3) or the many None-group editables, so 'delete the arrays + section by BindingGroup' is illusory — a name-keyed sectioning overlay must survive regardless; and the full fixed+editable union is gated on A5-Q3/Q4 (~167 user-reachable sites, X5), during which an editable-only 'replace' would REGRESS the cheatsheet by dropping its ≤65 description-bearing fixed bindings. The brief's own phasing concedes this by keeping the cheatsheet on get_key_bindings — which IS the hybrid, not Replace. I also challenged the 'removes the structural contradiction' rationale: the editor and cheatsheet filter the same keymap differently because they have different jobs, and a single manifest still feeds differently-filtered views, so set-divergence is not actually eliminated by Replace. Honest overlap disclosure: my recommended hybrid shares its first increment (the CI test) and its editable-only v1 scope with the brief's phased-Replace; the flip is in rejecting 'delete the arrays / BindingGroup sectioning', dropping effort high→medium and reversibility costly→easy, and declaring the decision non-blocking for Phase 1 beyond the test.

### A5-Q10 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team. The keybindings product owner OWNS the in/out-of-scope declaration and the caveat-scoping copy (and must decide whether the catalog is ever an off-machine/published artifact — the pivot for residual risk). The warpui_core keymap engine lead co-owns feasibility (confirms the inactive side is unbuildable today, A6-Q6) and whether/when R5 is built. The Axis-6 platform-foundation owner gates Option D and owns the Keystroke struct-serde migration for the two synced hotkeys (A6-Q4). Product decides scope and the caveat surface; engine confirms sequencing. No new owner needed.

**Decision.** Whether Phase-1 conflict detection and the action catalog claim cross-platform (mac vs linux/windows) truthfulness or explicitly scope to the running OS — and, if scoped, the minimum honesty surface required. Corrected framing: the data model can only conflict-check the running OS today (A6-Q6), and the editable bindings that feed the conflict map are stored locally per machine as OS-resolved strings (not cross-OS synced), so the in-app conflict UI is already truthful for the machine it shows. The real decision is therefore narrow: (a) MUST the design doc explicitly declare cross-platform binding-editing/conflict-checking as out of scope for Phase 1 (vs implying authority it lacks), and (b) does Phase 1 add any runtime caveat, and if so where — a blanket 'this is macOS only' banner, or a caveat scoped to the genuinely cross-OS surfaces (the cloud-synced QuakeMode/ActivationHotkey hotkeys; device-sync). The heavyweight cross-platform feature (Option D / R5) is out of Phase 1 regardless.

**Stakes.** The axis North Star is 'what the UI shows is the truth.' Corrected: single-OS scope does NOT inherently violate it — the settings view only ever displays the running OS's bindings (A6-Q6) and only claims conflicts for that OS, both of which are true, and the editable bindings feeding the ConflictMap live in a local keybindings.yaml as OS-resolved strings (keyboard.rs:95-96,167-187), not synced cross-OS, so a rebind on one machine cannot silently mis-resolve on another through this path. The residual exposure is a lie of OMISSION: the UI never warns that the catalog/conflict result is one-platform, so (i) a user re-applying a binding on a second OS gets no advance warning (but that second machine flags the real conflict when they do), and (ii) if the catalog is ever EXPORTED off-machine as a 'portable' reference (docs, or the R9 change-keybinding agent manifest), the single-OS artifact would be presented as canonical cross-platform — that is the one place the omission becomes a genuine false claim. So the stakes are: avoid OVERCLAIMING cross-platform truthfulness in the design doc and in any off-machine catalog artifact. The cost of getting this wrong is a credibility hit on the axis ('truthful conflicts' that aren't, off-machine), not a live in-app falsehood for the typical single-machine user.

_Constrained by:_
- ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke, usize>, populated only from binding.trigger.clone() (a single fully-resolved Keystroke with no platform/context dimension); has_conflict keys on that one Keystroke. Verified literally. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133, :795-800`
- cmdorctrl is resolved to cmd(mac)/ctrl(else) at parse time via OperatingSystem::get(); the Keystroke struct is {ctrl,alt,shift,cmd,meta,key} — no cmdorctrl and no platform field — so the inactive-OS keystroke is never stored. Verified literally. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919 (struct :320-328)`
- new_per_platform picks keystroke.mac or .linux_and_windows by OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() and discards the other; with_mac/with_linux_or_windows NOOP on the inactive OS; only with_key_binding runs Keystroke::parse — so the inactive side is never parsed. OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time cfg per target_os on native. Verified literally. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:497-508,688-726; A6-Q6`
- Genuine (non-cmd<->ctrl) divergence is the MAJORITY: cmdorctrl is the only pure swap (~31-34%); cmd_or_ctrl_shift (~140 actual binding call sites, NOT the inflated 181), with_mac/with_linux (101+56=157), new_per_platform (13) are full-chord divergence that cannot be synthesized from the mac side. Brief correctly uses the corrected ~140 figure. — `A6-Q5 (answers doc :2150-2168)`
- Any STRUCT-shape change to Keystroke touches the cloud-synced/UserDefaults serde form (QuakeModeSettings.keybinding and ActivationHotkeyKeybinding are Globally synced), so it must be additive (#[serde(default)]/Option) or carry a migration. Note: this constrains Option D's schema, NOT the general editable bindings, which use the insulated PersistedTrigger string. — `A6-Q4 (answers doc :2103-2148)`
- DECISIVE OMISSION the brief missed: the user-editable keybindings that populate the ConflictMap are written to a LOCAL keybindings.yaml under config_local_dir(), serialized as OS-resolved normalized strings (CustomKeybindings = HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>, PersistedTrigger built from Keystroke::normalized()), with no cloud-sync wiring in keyboard.rs. So a rebind does not propagate cross-OS through this path — bounding the cross-platform false-negative to near-zero in practice and confirming the in-app conflict UI is truthful for the running machine. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:95-96,103-121,167-187`

**Options:**

- **Honest scope with sync-targeted caveat (doc declaration mandatory, no blanket banner)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Make the design doc explicitly declare cross-platform binding-editing/conflict-checking out of scope for Phase 1 (free, words only). In the UI, do NOT slap a blanket 'macOS only' banner on a conflict view that is already truthful for the machine it shows; instead surface a one-line caveat only where another OS is genuinely reachable (the two cloud-synced QuakeMode/ActivationHotkey hotkeys, and/or when device-sync is on) and, critically, label any OFF-MACHINE catalog export (docs/R9 agent manifest) as single-OS. No engine change.
  - 👍 This is the minimal honest surface that maps to what the data model can deliver (A6-Q6) AND to where the omission actually misleads. It keeps the cheap, strictly-dominant part of Option A (the doc scope declaration) while dropping the part that has cost and risk (a blanket runtime banner that cries wolf at the single-machine majority for whom the check is already true). It directly closes the only genuine false-claim vector — a single-OS catalog presented off-machine as canonical cross-platform. Zero blast radius, ships in Phase 1, fully reversible, and forecloses nothing for R5.
  - 👎 Requires deciding the caveat's exact trigger condition (sync-on detection / which surfaces), which is slightly more design thought than 'one banner everywhere.' A user editing standard (non-synced) bindings still gets no other-OS warning — but per keyboard.rs:95-96/167-187 that binding isn't active on another OS through this path anyway, so the warning would be noise. Advances the real cross-platform feature by zero.
- **Honest single-OS scope (blanket label) — original Option A** _(low effort · easy)_ — Conflict map and catalog operate on the running OS only; the design doc and a blanket UI label declare the scope (e.g. 'Showing macOS bindings; conflicts checked for this platform'). No engine change.
  - 👍 Dead simple and unambiguous: one label, no conditional logic, tells the literal truth about its own limits. Forward-compatible — when R5 lands the label is dropped.
  - 👎 Conflates two separable things (free doc declaration vs costed runtime banner). The blanket banner cries wolf at the single-machine majority, for whom the conflict check is already fully truthful (the UI never shows another OS), making the product look more broken than it is. Its forward-compat claim is overstated: dropping the label is easy, but making the surfaces actually cross-platform IS the full Option-D effort, so A advances D by zero, not 'no rework.'
- **Silent single-OS (do-nothing)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Scope to the running OS with no caveat anywhere; leave today's behavior unchanged and treat cross-platform as informally out of scope.
  - 👍 Cheapest possible. Re-framed honestly: for the typical single-machine user the in-app conflict UI is already truthful (it only shows and checks this OS), the editable bindings are local-only (keyboard.rs:95-96), and nobody is presently promised cross-platform conflicts — so this is not the flagrant North-Star violation the original brief painted.
  - 👎 Still leaves a lie of omission in two places that matter: (i) the design doc would implicitly imply cross-platform truthfulness it can't deliver, and (ii) any off-machine catalog export (docs / R9 manifest) would present a one-OS list as canonical. Costs essentially nothing to fix via the doc declaration, so accepting this purely to save one sentence is a bad trade.
- **cmdorctrl synthesis** _(med effort · costly)_ — At catalog/conflict time, re-run the cmd<->ctrl swap to synthesize the inactive-OS keystroke for the pure cmdorctrl case and surface best-effort other-OS conflicts, no schema change.
  - 👍 In principle catches the single most common primary-modifier swap class without touching the data model.
  - 👎 Near-useless, not merely lossy: the conflict TARGET set (default bindings) is ALSO single-OS — the other-OS default chords were never constructed (A6-Q6) — so synthesizing the user's binding's other-OS form gives almost nothing real to compare against. It can only catch collisions in the tiny intersection where BOTH the user's binding AND the colliding default are pure cmdorctrl, while presenting as comprehensive. Manufactures false confidence over the ~66% genuine-divergence majority and is lossy for meta/super (A6-Q7). Strictly worse than an honest scope statement.
- **Full cross-platform via R5** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Build the Axis-6 platform-in-data-model: a structured per-platform record storing both mac and linux/windows sides so the catalog and conflict map are genuinely cross-platform.
  - 👍 The only option that actually delivers a portable catalog and truthful cross-OS conflicts, and unblocks a cross-platform binding editor — the correct end-state for the North Star.
  - 👎 Very high effort, gated on Axis 6: needs a canonical per-platform schema (A6-Q2/Q9/Q10), re-keys ConflictMap per platform and grows the catalog an other-OS column (A5-Q1), and mutates the cloud-synced/UserDefaults Keystroke STRUCT serde form so it must be additive/migrated (A6-Q4) — effectively one-way. Cannot ship in Phase 1; making it a precondition stalls the whole axis behind an engine refactor. Note the persisted-format risk applies only to the two struct-serde hotkeys; the general editable bindings stay on the insulated string format.

**→ Recommendation: Honest scope with sync-targeted caveat (doc declaration mandatory, no blanket banner).** The data model can only conflict-check the running OS (A6-Q6, confirmed: new_per_platform discards the inactive side at keymap.rs:497-508; ConflictMap keys on one resolved Keystroke at keybindings.rs:104-133), and the editable bindings feeding the conflict map are stored LOCALLY as OS-resolved strings (keyboard.rs:95-96,167-187), so cross-OS scope is unbuildable in Phase 1 and the in-app conflict UI is already truthful for the machine it shows. That makes the whole Phase-1 decision narrow: avoid OVERCLAIMING. The mandatory, free move is the design-doc out-of-scope declaration — this strictly dominates do-nothing (B) for the price of one sentence. The runtime caveat, however, should NOT be a blanket banner (original Option A): a 'macOS only' banner cries wolf at the single-machine majority for whom the check is already true, and the only place the single-OS result becomes a genuine false claim is when it is reasoned about for another machine (the synced QuakeMode/ActivationHotkey hotkeys) or exported off-machine (docs / R9 manifest). So scope the caveat to exactly those surfaces. cmdorctrl-synthesis (C) is rejected harder than the brief did — it is near-useless because the conflict target set is also single-OS, so it manufactures false confidence. Full cross-platform (D) is the right end-state but very-high-effort, one-way on the synced serde format (A6-Q4), and gated on Axis 6; it is out of Phase 1 regardless, so it does not gate this decision. This option is fully reversible and forecloses nothing for D. Honest caveat: D is still full effort later — this option does not pre-build any of it; the only 'forward compatibility' claim that survives is that it doesn't make D harder.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument: if the action catalog is intended to be a PUBLISHED, off-machine 'portable' reference — i.e. it is exported into documentation or frozen into the R9 change-keybinding agent's action manifest (A5-Q12) and consumed on a different OS than where it was generated — then a single-OS catalog presented as canonical IS a real (not merely omitted) cross-platform falsehood, and a doc-level caveat the user never reads does not fix the artifact itself. In that consumption path, only Option D produces a correct portable catalog, and a per-surface caveat is a band-aid. This is why the recommended option explicitly requires labeling any off-machine catalog export as single-OS — but if product later decides the catalog/agent-manifest must be authoritative cross-platform, this decision flips toward prioritizing D and the 'low effort / defer' framing collapses. Settling the catalog's off-machine consumption model (A5-Q1, A5-Q12) is therefore the pivot.
- _Dependencies:_ Option D is gated by Axis-6 R5 / A6-Q5 / A6-Q6 (platform-in-data-model), the canonical-schema calls A6-Q2/A6-Q9/A6-Q10, and constrained by A6-Q4 (the struct-serde Keystroke for QuakeMode/ActivationHotkey is cloud-synced/persisted; per-platform record must be additive or migrated — note this does NOT constrain the general editable bindings, which use the insulated PersistedTrigger string). This decision is the cross-platform slice of the A5-Q11 Phase-1/2 scope line and must stay consistent with where R6 (truthful conflicts) lands. The newly-decisive dependency: A5-Q1 (catalog source of truth) AND A5-Q12 (R9 agent manifest key/snapshot) jointly determine whether the catalog is consumed off-machine — which is what converts the omission into a real false claim and is the pivot for whether D must be prioritized. It compounds A5-Q5 (conflict overlap rule) and A5-Q6 (binding universe) since cross-platform multiplies the conflict universe. NOT a blocker for Phase 1: the cross-platform feature defers past Phase 1 regardless; the only Phase-1 deliverable here is the doc scope declaration plus a scoped caveat.
- _What would settle it:_ 1) A product declaration the design doc must make: is cross-platform binding editing/conflict-checking a stated v1 goal? Out of scope -> recommended option (honest scope + scoped caveat); in scope and prioritized -> Option D. 2) THE decisive new question: is the action catalog ever consumed OFF the running machine — published in docs, or frozen into the R9 agent manifest (A5-Q1/A5-Q12) and applied on a different OS? If yes, a single-OS catalog is a genuine cross-platform falsehood and pushes toward D; if no (in-app only), the in-app UI is already truthful and the scoped-caveat option fully suffices. 3) A usage/sizing input: do users edit keybindings for an OS they are not running, AND does any cross-OS sync of the editable keybindings.yaml exist or get added (today it's a local file, keyboard.rs:95-96)? If that population/path stays negligible, the scoped caveat is sufficient; if cross-OS sync of editable bindings is planned, re-evaluate — that is precisely what would make the single-OS conflict map start lying in-app. The A6-Q5 sizing (~34% pure / ~66% genuine) already quantifies how little Option C could ever cover.
- _Red-team added option:_ Honest scope with sync-targeted caveat (doc declaration mandatory, no blanket banner)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all five grounding facts against source — every one holds at the cited lines (ConflictMap HashMap<Keystroke,usize> populated from binding.trigger.clone() at keybindings.rs:104-133/795-800; cmdorctrl resolved at parse via OperatingSystem::get() at keymap.rs:914-919; Keystroke struct has no platform field at :320-328; new_per_platform/with_mac/with_linux only build the active side at :497-508,688-713; A6-Q5 counts use the corrected ~140/157/13 figures, not the inflated 181). The recommended DIRECTION (single-OS scope, declared honestly) survives, so this is hardened not flipped. But three things are materially wrong in the brief and I corrected them:

(1) STAKES OVERSTATED. The decisive fact the brief omits: the editable keybindings that populate the ConflictMap are written to a LOCAL keybindings.yaml under config_local_dir() as OS-resolved normalized strings (keyboard.rs:95-96, CustomKeybindings/PersistedTrigger :167-187), with no cloud-sync path in keyboard.rs. Only QuakeModeSettings.keybinding and ActivationHotkeyKeybinding (two single hotkeys) are cloud-synced (A6-Q4). So a Mac user's rebind of cmd-X does NOT propagate to their Linux machine, and even if the file were copied the resolved string would be misread there. The conflict map is therefore fully truthful for the machine in front of the user; the settings view never displays another OS's bindings (A6-Q6), so it makes no false claim about another OS. The North Star 'what the UI shows is the truth' is SATISFIED by single-OS scope — the only gap is a lie of OMISSION about a platform the UI never shows, and it only bites a user who manually re-applies a binding on a second machine, where THAT machine's conflict check then runs correctly. This narrows the A-vs-B gap to roughly one sentence and downgrades Option B from 'directly violates the North Star' to 'fails to volunteer a caveat.'

(2) C IS WORSE THAN 'LOSSY' — IT'S NEAR-USELESS. The brief rejects cmdorctrl-synthesis as lossy for the ~66% genuine-divergence majority. Stronger point: the conflict TARGET set (the default bindings you'd check a synthesized user binding against) is ALSO single-OS — the other-OS default chords were never constructed (A6-Q6). So synthesizing the user's binding into its other-OS form gives nothing real to compare against. C can only catch a collision inside the tiny intersection where BOTH the user's binding AND the colliding default are pure-cmdorctrl — while presenting as comprehensive. That manufactures false confidence; reject harder.

(3) 'NO REWORK' FORWARD-COMPAT CLAIM IS OPTIMISTIC. The rationale says when R5 lands you 'drop the label and the identical surfaces become cross-platform with no rework.' Dropping a label is easy; making the surfaces cross-platform IS the full Option-D effort (re-key ConflictMap per platform, grow the catalog an other-OS column per A5-Q1, build R5). A is forward-compatible only in that it doesn't FORECLOSE D — it advances D by zero. Corrected the claim.

(4) ADDED A CHEAPER, BETTER-TARGETED OPTION the brief conflates away: Option A bundles a free design-doc scope declaration with a blanket runtime 'macOS only' banner. Those are separable, and the banner crying-wolf on the single-machine majority (for whom the check is already truthful) is net-negative. The added option keeps the mandatory free doc declaration and scopes any runtime caveat to where another OS is actually reachable (the two synced hotkeys / device-sync surface).

(5) DEFERABILITY made explicit: the cross-platform FEATURE defers past Phase 1 regardless of this decision (the data model can't do it today); the only Phase-1 action this decision gates is whether to add a one-line honest scope statement. That is the whole decision.

### A5-Q11 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Three-party, synthesized by the Axis-5 design-doc tech lead, with a decoupling the brief missed: the Tier-0 honest relabel is owned solely by the SETTINGS-UI owner (settings_view/keybindings.rs render/copy) + PRODUCT/DESIGN (the interim-promise wording) and needs NO engine sign-off — it touches no warpui_core code and no persisted schema, so it can proceed immediately, in parallel with the cross-team negotiation. The Tier-1+ pieces need the full three parties: (1) ENGINE owner (warpui_core keymap/Matcher) is gatekeeper for the gated dimension — they alone decide whether/when to expose ContextPredicate (make .context pub/accessor, add Hash derive) and own the A5-Q5 overlap-primitive spike, and they own the once-vs-twice ConflictMap re-key call; (2) SETTINGS-UI owner owns the chord/Unbound rekey; (3) PRODUCT/DESIGN owns whether 'duplicates within the scope you're editing (this OS)' is an acceptable G1 interim promise. Engine owner remains gatekeeper for the context dimension because it is theirs to unblock — but is explicitly NOT on the critical path for the Phase-1 floor.

**Decision.** Where to draw the Phase-1/Phase-2 line for 'truthful conflicts' — which dimensions of conflict-truthfulness can ship in Phase 1 without the Axis-2 context-scoped engine work, and which are structurally gated on it. Re-verification confirms the answer is per-DIMENSION, not per-feature: (context dimension → Axis 2) and (every other dimension → Phase 1), PLUS a newly-separated zeroth tier — the honest relabel that makes today's claim match today's computation — which is mandatory, free, and gates nothing.

**Stakes.** Decides what the Axis-5 doc can promise as the headline G1 'truthful conflicts' feature without waiting on the Axis-2 engine refactor. Mis-scope one way and Phase 1 ships a conflict UI that still lies (false-positives on context-forked same-key editable bindings; silent passes on fixed-binding collisions). Mis-scope the other way and the whole discoverability axis stalls behind the context-model refactor, foregoing cheap real wins. CRITICAL re-frame from the attack: today's ConflictMap already LIES on the context axis, so 'freeze it' (Option A) is not a neutral default — it ships an active lie. The cheapest correct move is therefore not 'add detection' but 'stop the existing UI from claiming more than it computes' (the relabel), which costs no engine work and is independently shippable today. Everything beyond that floor is an optional, independently-deferrable increment.

_Constrained by:_
- The settings ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize>, context-blind, populated only from binding.trigger, flags count>1, and is the ONLY keybinding conflict detector. It is also EPHEMERAL: #[derive(Debug,Clone,Default)] with no Serialize, rebuilt in populate() from self.bindings — so re-keying it persists nothing and is freely reversible. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133 (def), :794-800 (populate)`
- CommandBinding has no context/scope field (only name/description/trigger/action/group/id) and from_editable_lens DROPS lens.context; EditableBindingLens.context is private. So context-truthfulness is necessarily a warpui_core change (expose + thread ContextPredicate), confirming Option B's 'Phase-1 slice' is mislabeled engine work; the question's premise of an existing scope field is false. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541, :762-770; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:311`
- ContextPredicate has only point-eval (eval(&Context)); no overlap/satisfiability routine exists. It derives Eq/PartialEq but NOT Hash, so re-keying a map on it needs an added Hash derive; And/Or are ORDERED, so even 'exact structural equality' false-negatives on commutatively-equal predicates. A real can-both-be-true overlap check is a net-new engine primitive (spike). — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:3,9-18,100-119`
- Two dimensions need NO context model: (a) chord/prefix-shadow — ConflictMap keys one Keystroke and trigger_to_keystroke collapses a chord via keys.first(), false-flagging prefix-siblings and hiding true prefix-shadow; (b) triggerless/Unbound bindings never conflict and never get the warn border (FromIterator flatten drops None). HIDDEN DEP: chord-CONFLICT detection has no value until chord capture (R8) ships, so it is co-gated on R8. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:236; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:106,139; report R8 (Phase 1)`
- Universe-union (naming fixed/custom-action collisions) is gated on fixed-binding stable identity: Binding::as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default(), and the conflict map is editable-only. The refactor is ~146 FIXED-binding sites (A5-Q3), NOT the brief's ~469 — that figure is the TOTAL bind-site count (grep bind!/add_binding/FixedBinding ≈ 471, incl. editable). Sources disagree (146/265/437/469); qualitatively large, correctly deferred behind a separate project. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:633; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:754-800; grep total bind-sites=471`
- Report places R6 (context-aware conflict that names the collision) in Phase 2 (line 257) and defines Phase 1 as 'no schema/engine changes' (line 244) — supporting the engine-gate on the context dimension. BUT report's own R6 text (line 165) proposes keying on BindingGroup, which A5-Q2 proves is 12 semantic categories (Settings/Navigation/Terminal/...) NOT context scopes — an UNSOUND shortcut (both false-positives and false-negatives). The (Keystroke,group) hack a skeptic will propose must be explicitly rejected. — `/home/mhb/warp/.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:165,244,257,270`
- No Phase-1-eligible option touches persisted schema (ConflictMap and CommandBinding are both ephemeral, no Serialize derive). The A1 forward-incompatibility meta-finding bites only on persisted CustomKeybindings records (Phase-2 R3b), so it does NOT gate the Phase-1 relabel/chord/unbound work — correcting the brief's claim that 'any scope id chosen here must be the one Axis-2 will persist.' — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104; app/src/util/bindings.rs:530 (Clone,Debug only)`

**Options:**

- **A. Hard gate — all conflict-truthfulness waits for Axis 2** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship no conflict-logic OR copy changes in Phase 1; keep the context-blind, editable-only, single-keystroke ConflictMap exactly as-is and defer every R6 improvement to Phase 2.
  - 👍 Cleanest dependency story, matches the report's literal phasing, lets the engine team touch the volatile keymap exactly once co-designed with the Phase-2 record schema. Lowest coordination cost. If the empirical audit shows near-zero same-key/cross-context editable pairs AND chords aren't enterable until R8 anyway, doing ConflictMap's full re-key (chord+context together) ONCE in Phase 2 genuinely avoids a double-touch.
  - 👎 It freezes an ACTIVE LIE, not a clean slate: today's map already false-flags context-forked editable same-key bindings and silently passes fixed-binding collisions. It leaves the UI making a claim ('conflict') it cannot back — and the fix for that costs zero engine work (just narrowed copy), so 'do nothing' needlessly ships a known-false UI for the whole of Phase 1. Empirically the question's own answer ('is ALL truthfulness gated on Axis 2?') is NO, so blanket gating is an over-restriction.
- **B. Pull a minimal exact-context slice into Phase 1** _(high effort · costly)_ — Expose ContextPredicate as a comparable scope id, add a scope field to CommandBinding, add a Hash derive to ContextPredicate, and re-key the conflict map on (Keystroke, structural-predicate-equality).
  - 👍 Exact-context equality is the cheapest CORRECT-by-construction context truthfulness: no SAT solver, never false-flags the forked id!(Vim) vs !id!(Vim) golden case, and lands the data model the rest of Axis 2 needs anyway, so the schema is co-designed up front.
  - 👎 A5-Q2 + context.rs confirm this IS the Axis-2 engine change (expose+thread ContextPredicate across the warpui_core boundary), so 'Phase 1' is mislabeled — it drags engine risk into the quick-win phase and blurs the exact line the decision must draw. Worse than the brief stated: ContextPredicate isn't even Hash yet, and And/Or are ORDERED so structural equality false-negatives on commutatively-equal predicates — so it trades a context false-positive for TWO classes of false-negative (ordering + differently-spelled overlap). Does nothing for nameless fixed-binding collisions or chords.
- **C. Context-independent truthfulness now; gate only the context dimension on Axis 2 (brief's original)** _(high effort · easy)_ — Decompose by dimension: Phase 1 ships chord/prefix-shadow rekeying + triggerless/Unbound first-classing (plus universe-union IF fixed identity is in scope), with honest copy scoping the claim; the ONLY gated dimension is same-key/different-context truthfulness, which waits for Axis 2.
  - 👍 Directly answers the question and ships real user value (chords, unbound discoverability) without a context claim it can't back. Each sub-slice is independently golden-testable. Correctly sequences the expensive engine work behind its prerequisites.
  - 👎 Conflates the zero-cost honest relabel with the new detection, treating it as one 'high effort' bundle when the relabel is the cheap, mandatory floor that should ship regardless. Over-rates reversibility as 'costly': ConflictMap/CommandBinding are ephemeral, nothing persisted, so it's actually EASY. Under-states that chord-rekey is co-gated on R8 (no chords to conflict pre-R8) and STILL inherits today's context lie until Axis 2, so 'honest scoping' copy is load-bearing not optional. Universe-union is only cheap in principle (~146 fixed-identity sites).
- **D. Honesty-relabel only (cheap defer)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Build no new detection; make the existing UI HONEST by narrowing its claim to 'duplicate keystroke (editable, this OS, single-key)'. Ship the rest of Phase 1 (catalog/search/validators) and defer all conflict DETECTION to Phase 2.
  - 👍 Cheapest move that removes the lie without touching the engine: today's map computes the wrong thing only because it CLAIMS more than it computes, so narrowing the claim is a zero-engine-risk honesty fix. Buys time to co-design R6 with the Phase-2 schema and dodges the A1 trap entirely.
  - 👎 Delivers little of the G1 'truthful conflicts' value users asked for — a label that says 'this list only' reads as an admission of incompleteness. It unblocks nothing AND leaves the genuinely-free wins (Unbound chip) on the table even though they need no engine work. Treating it as an ALTERNATIVE to detection (rather than the floor under it) is the brief's framing error.
- **E. Honest floor (mandatory relabel) + cheap detection increment; chord rides R8; context+universe-union to Phase 2 — ADDED** _(med effort · easy)_ — Two-tier. TIER 0 (mandatory, this-OS/editable/single-key honest relabel, D's content) ships in Phase 1 regardless and makes the existing UI stop lying at zero engine cost. TIER 1 (optional, independently-deferrable) layers the context-independent detection that is genuinely cheap — the Unbound chip (A5-Q9) — and lets chord/prefix-shadow detection (A5-Q7) ride along WHEN chord capture (R8) lands, not before. The context dimension and universe-union both go to Phase 2 (engine exposure + overlap spike; fixed-identity ~146 sites).
  - 👍 The synthesis a skeptic signs off on: it separates the thing that MUST ship (stop the active lie — free, no engine sign-off, owned by settings-UI+product alone) from optional increments, so Phase 1 is truthful even if every increment slips. It prices each piece correctly: relabel = low/easy; Unbound = low/easy; chord = gated on R8 (don't build conflict for chords nobody can type yet); context = Axis-2 engine; universe-union = separate fixed-identity project. It touches NO persisted schema, so it's fully reversible and carries no A1 forward-compat risk — the Phase-2 context re-key replaces the ephemeral ConflictMap freely. It explicitly rejects the report's own (Keystroke,group) shortcut as unsound (BindingGroup ≠ context scope, per A5-Q2). The audit can then decide whether even chord-rekey is worth a double-touch or should fold into the Phase-2 re-key.
  - 👎 Requires disciplined honest copy (load-bearing, since chord-rekey if shipped still over-reports across distinct contexts until Axis 2). The 'is duplicate-within-the-scope-you're-editing an acceptable G1 interim promise?' question is a real product call that must be answered, not finessed. If the audit shows many forked same-key cross-context pairs, the relabel-only floor will read as visibly incomplete and product may want to pull B's exact-equality slice forward (accepting it is engine work). Adds light cross-team coordination for the Tier-1 increments (none for the Tier-0 floor).

**→ Recommendation: E. Honest floor (mandatory relabel) + cheap detection increment; chord rides R8; context+universe-union to Phase 2.** The verified facts uphold the brief's per-dimension thesis but reveal it mis-bundled the work. Decisive re-frame: today's ConflictMap is an ACTIVE LIE (context-blind, editable-only — keybindings.rs:104-133, 794-800), and the fix for a UI claiming more than it computes is to NARROW THE CLAIM, which costs zero engine work. So the cheapest correct move is not 'add detection' but 'stop lying' — and that belongs in Phase 1 unconditionally. That makes the honest relabel a mandatory FLOOR (Tier 0), not one of several mutually-exclusive options. On top of it, the genuinely context-independent, genuinely-free win is the Unbound chip (A5-Q9). Chord/prefix-shadow (A5-Q7) is context-independent BUT co-gated on chord capture R8 — no chords to conflict until users can enter them — so it rides R8 rather than standing as a committed Phase-1 deliverable. The context dimension is genuinely engine-gated: EditableBindingLens.context is private and CommandBinding has no scope field (bindings.rs:531-541, 762-770; keymap.rs:311), and there is no overlap primitive (context.rs) — confirming Option B's 'Phase-1 slice' is mislabeled engine work, made worse by the missing Hash derive and And/Or ordering false-negatives. Universe-union is gated on ~146-site fixed-identity work (A5-Q3), correctly deferred. Two corrections strengthen E over the brief's C: (1) all Phase-1-eligible work touches NO persisted schema (ConflictMap/CommandBinding are ephemeral, no Serialize), so reversibility is EASY and the A1 forward-compat coupling the brief leaned on does not apply — the Phase-2 context re-key freely replaces the ephemeral map; (2) the report's own R6 proposal to key on BindingGroup is unsound (A5-Q2) and must be explicitly rejected so a skeptic doesn't 'just use the pub group field.' Net: E ships a truthful Phase-1 UI on day one (the floor), adds the one free detection win, and sequences everything expensive behind its real prerequisite — without persisting anything it would regret.

- _Residual risk:_ Option A's double-touch argument is the strongest survivor: if the empirical audit shows near-zero same-key/cross-context editable pairs, the context dimension barely matters in Phase 1, and since chords aren't enterable until R8 anyway, the engine team could re-key ConflictMap ONCE in Phase 2 (chord + context together) instead of twice (chord-rekey now, context-rekey later). In that world E's Tier-1 chord increment is wasted motion and the truly minimal correct scope collapses to 'relabel + Unbound only, defer ALL detection re-keying to Phase 2.' E partly absorbs this by making chord ride R8 (not a committed deliverable) — but it still leaves chord-rekey on the Phase-1 table when the audit might prove it should fold into Phase 2. The audit is therefore not just a tie-breaker between C and B; it also decides whether chord-rekey belongs in Phase 1 at all. Second survivor: the honest relabel, however truthful, may read to users/reviewers as a bug-shaped admission of incompleteness ('this list only'), under-delivering on the G1 'truthful conflicts' headline — a product-acceptability risk the floor cannot engineer away.
- _Dependencies:_ Context dimension gated by A5-Q2 (expose+thread ContextPredicate — the engine change) and A5-Q5 (operational rule: exact-equality vs real overlap; a prototype-spike, made harder by ContextPredicate lacking Hash and And/Or being order-sensitive). Universe-union gated by A5-Q6 + A5-Q3/A5-Q4 (fixed-binding stable identity, ~146 fixed sites — NOT the ~469 the brief cited, which counts all bind-sites). Chord-conflict (A5-Q7) is CO-GATED on R8 (chord capture) — newly made explicit: no chords to conflict before R8. Unbound chip co-dependent with A5-Q9 and R7 (filter chips). Phase-1 copy must also scope to current OS (A5-Q10: cross-OS collisions invisible) and to the editable universe. CORRECTION to the brief's cross-axis claim: the A1 forward-incompatibility meta-finding does NOT bind this decision's Phase-1 work — ConflictMap and CommandBinding are ephemeral (no Serialize; rebuilt in populate()), so no scope id is persisted in Phase 1; A1 binds only the Phase-2 CustomKeybindings record schema (R3b). The Phase-1 floor/increment is therefore freely replaceable when Axis-2 lands.
- _What would settle it:_ Run, via a one-off instrumented debug build that reads the currently-private lens.context (no shipping exposure needed), a startup walk over editable_bindings() counting (a) how many distinct ContextPredicates exist and (b) how many same-keystroke editable pairs sit in DIFFERENT contexts. Near-zero ⇒ real same-key/cross-context collisions are rare, the context dimension barely matters in Phase 1, E's honest-floor approach is clearly safe, AND the engine should prefer a SINGLE Phase-2 ConflictMap re-key (chord+context together) over E's double-touch — i.e. drop the Tier-1 chord increment. Large ⇒ the Phase-1 UI would be visibly noisy under honest copy, and product should weigh pulling B's exact-equality slice forward (accepting it is engine work). Pair with the A5-Q13 golden test (id!(\"Vim\") vs !id!(\"Vim\") must produce NO conflict; same-context collision DOES) run against today's map to measure the current false-positive rate empirically. Plus TWO rulings: (1) PRODUCT — is 'conflicts within the scope you're editing (this OS)' an acceptable interim G1 promise, or does the headline require the context dimension on day one? (2) ENGINE — once vs twice on the ConflictMap re-key, decided by the same audit.
- _Red-team added option:_ E. Honest floor (mandatory relabel) + cheap detection increment (Unbound now, chord rides R8), context+universe-union to Phase 2
- _Red-team:_ Core thesis SURVIVES code re-check: truthfulness genuinely decomposes by dimension, the context dimension is genuinely engine-gated, and the non-context dimensions genuinely are not. Five corrections harden it: (1) MISSING OPTION: the brief frames D (honest relabel) and C (new detection) as mutually exclusive. They are complementary. The honest relabel is a ZERO-ENGINE FLOOR that removes today's active lie unconditionally and IS the actual fix for the G1 'truthful' promise; cheap detection layers on top. Added Option E = relabel-as-mandatory-floor + Unbound chip, with chord-conflict riding R8 and context/universe-union to Phase 2. E becomes the recommendation. (2) REVERSIBILITY CORRECTION: the brief rates C 'costly' and ties the decision to the A1 forward-compat meta-finding. But ConflictMap (keybindings.rs:104, no Serialize) and CommandBinding (bindings.rs:530, no Serialize) are EPHEMERAL — rebuilt in populate() (:754-800), never persisted. The only persisted schema is CustomKeybindings (yaml, Phase-2 R3b). Every Phase-1-eligible option here touches ZERO persisted state and carries NO forward-incompatibility risk. C/E reversibility = EASY, not costly. The brief's 'any scope id chosen here must be the one Axis-2 will persist' is a false coupling for Phase-1 work. (3) GROUNDING NUMBER: the brief's '~469 constructor sites' for fixed-identity is the TOTAL bind-site count (grep: 471, incl. editable); the fixed-binding subset that needs stable identity is ~146 (A5-Q3). Brief overstates universe-union cost ~3x (sources disagree 146/265/437/469; stays deferred either way). (4) HAND-WAVING on 'exact structural equality is cheap': ContextPredicate derives Eq/PartialEq but NOT Hash (context.rs:3,9), so re-keying needs an added Hash derive; And/Or are ORDERED, so structural equality false-negatives on commutatively-equal predicates — Option B is even leakier than stated. (5) INTERNAL CONTRADICTION surfaced: the report's own R6 (line 165) proposes keying on BindingGroup (already pub, zero engine), but A5-Q2 proves BindingGroup is 12 semantic categories, not context scopes — so it both false-flags and false-negatives: an unsound shortcut a skeptic will propose and must be explicitly rejected. HIDDEN DEPENDENCY: chord-CONFLICT has zero value before R8 (chord capture) ships — cannot conflict on chords nobody can enter — so chord-rekey is co-gated on R8, not free-standing. DEFERABILITY: the context dimension can be deferred past Phase 1 with zero blocking cost; nothing in Phase 1 (catalog/search/validators) depends on it. Only the honest relabel MUST ship to keep Phase 1 from lying.

### A5-Q14 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Keybindings PM owns the policy and whether any future save-gate is opt-in (default-off) vs default. The warpui/keymap eng-lead is the truthfulness gatekeeper: B or C cannot be greenlit until eng confirms a same-context overlap predicate (A5-Q5) and context threading (A5-Q2) are landed and truthful, AND confirms the concrete Phase-1 enabler for A/D — that ConflictMap can become an identity-bearing lookup (keystroke -> colliders) rather than the current count map, and whether scroll-to-row exists for D. Primary owner: keybindings PM.

**Decision.** On save of an edited keybinding that collides with another binding, what does the editor do: (a) warn-only and always persist, (b) block save on a same-context hard conflict, (c) one-click swap that unbinds the loser, or (d) warn + non-destructive jump-to-collider — and what does the 'Conflicts with: <description>' line render when N bindings collide. Decision: ship A (named, non-blocking warning) as the Phase-1 floor; strongly consider D within Phase-1 if scroll-to-row is cheap; reject B as a hard gate for Phase-1; defer C to Phase-2. All naming/jump features are limited to the editable collider universe and the warning text must not imply it has seen every collision.

**Stakes.** This is the user-visible contract of Axis 5 (truthful conflicts). The end-goal advertises an 'optional save-gate on hard conflicts' and 'one-click rebind/swap,' so the policy defines the save path (confirm_keystroke_editing) and the conflict render path. Wrong both ways: today the save path silently overwrites with zero conflict consultation, so users can lose an intended binding without notice; but a blocking gate built on today's signal would refuse legitimate saves, because that signal is context-blind, deduped-editable-only, and identity-free — it cannot tell a real same-context collision from a benign cross-context coexistence (e.g. the Vim-normal vs terminal fork), nor can it see fixed/custom or cross-OS colliders at all. A blocking gate on an untruthful metric is worse than no gate. Equally, even the non-blocking 'Conflicts with' string is a trust surface: because the count universe is partial, a confidently-worded warning that names one editable collider while silently omitting a fixed/custom one gives false assurance — the honesty bug is naming, not just blocking.

_Constrained by:_
- The save path performs NO conflict check: confirm_keystroke_editing unconditionally calls set_custom_keybinding when unsaved_binding is Some — it never consults conflict_map. So warn-only is literally the current behavior and the cheapest path. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:669-699 (re-verified)`
- The only conflict signal is render-side, generic, names no action: has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger) at :1064 drives a 2px warn border (:270-276) plus a static bold string SHORTCUT_CONFLICT_WARNING_TEXT='This shortcut conflicts with other keybinds' (:48, rendered in render_clicked :313-321). No machinery fills in 'Conflicts with: <description>'. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:48,270-276,313-321,1064 (re-verified)`
- ConflictMap is COUNT-ONLY: map: HashMap<Keystroke,usize>; has_conflict = count>1. It records that a collision exists, NOT who collides — there is no collider identity and no context dimension. CommandBinding has fields name/description/trigger/action/group/id and NO context field. So naming a collider (A) or jumping to it (D) needs a net-new identity-bearing lookup, and the 'hard vs soft' same-context distinction B/C presuppose is uncomputable from this data. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133; app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541 (re-verified)`
- The conflict universe is DEDUPED-editable-only: conflict_map is built from self.bindings (:795-800), and self.bindings is ctx.editable_bindings() (:754) deduped by (name,description) (:782). Collisions with FixedBinding/custom-action bindings are never counted (has_conflict==false), and FixedBinding's as_lens name is empty (Default::default()), so a fixed collider cannot even be named. The universe is narrower than 'editable-only' — linked same-(name,description) bindings are collapsed before the map sees them. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:754,782,795-800; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:631-642 (re-verified; as_lens references self.command_description, a FixedBinding-only field at :280)`
- The dedup/save key is (name,description) with no scope dimension; one action NAME spans several bindings in distinct contexts/views and changes are saved/loaded by name, so 'unbind the loser' by name is not surgical — it can strip a binding from an unrelated view. — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:760-782 (re-verified)`
- ContextPredicate DOES exist on EditableBinding (keymap.rs:297) and FixedBinding (:281), but EditableBindingLens.context is private (:311) and from_lens discards it / it is never threaded onto CommandBinding. So 'same context' is computable in principle but unexposed: A5-Q2 is expose+thread, not invent — the genuinely hard part for B/C is A5-Q5's overlap primitive (predicate intersection without a SAT solver), not the data's existence. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:281,297,311; answers doc 2026-06-13-...-answers.md:1763`
- No transactional undo exists on the save path: set_custom_keybinding -> set_custom_trigger persists to disk immediately (write_custom_keybinding). C's promised 'one-click swap with undo' therefore requires building an undo stack that does not exist today, on top of resolving by-name surgical targeting — raising C's true cost above the brief's 'high'. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506; answers doc 2026-06-13-...-answers.md:1836`

**Options:**

- **A. Warn-only, advisory (always persist) — Phase 1** _(low effort · easy)_ — Keep save non-blocking (confirm_keystroke_editing keeps calling set_custom_keybinding), but upgrade the generic static string to name the colliding EDITABLE action(s): 'Also bound to: <description> (+N more)'. Requires turning ConflictMap from count-only into an identity-bearing lookup (keystroke -> colliders), or a per-render row scan. Phrase to NOT imply completeness; fixed/custom/cross-OS colliders are invisible today and must not be falsely summarized. No gate, no auto-unbind.
  - 👍 It matches the only signal the code can compute, ships in Phase 1 with low risk, and never refuses a legitimate save, so it cannot damage trust by blocking on a context-blind false positive (e.g. the Vim/!Vim fork the count map wrongly buckets together). It is forward-compatible: when A5-Q2 (context scope) and A5-Q5 (overlap primitive) land, the same warning upgrades in place to a true same-context message, and a gate/swap layers on later. It delivers a real Axis-5 win over the generic string at the cost of one small lookup refactor.
  - 👎 It does not honor the 'optional save-gate' promise, so a determined user can still silently overwrite a binding they care about — the warning is easy to ignore. Effort is low but NOT free: ConflictMap is count-only, so collider identity is net-new work. Because the universe is deduped-editable-only, the named warning is partial — it can confidently name one editable collider while silently omitting a fixed/custom or cross-OS one, giving false assurance unless phrased as non-exhaustive. Forecloses nothing.
- **B. Soft save-gate on same-context hard conflict (block + override)** _(high effort · costly)_ — On save, if the candidate collides within the SAME context, present a blocking confirmation ('Conflicts with <action> — save anyway?') and only persist on override; cross-context/unknown-scope collisions stay warn-only. Opt-in setting.
  - 👍 This is the literal end-goal feature and the strongest user protection: it stops the silent-overwrite footgun at the moment of harm and forces an informed choice, while opt-in preserves power-user speed.
  - 👎 Not Phase-1 shippable truthfully: 'same context' is not exposed in the data the save path can see — CommandBinding has no context field, the context predicate is private/discarded, and ConflictMap keys a bare Keystroke over a DEDUPED-editable-only universe. 'Hard' would compute as exact-keystroke-equality, a known false-positive generator (blocks the legitimate Vim/!Vim fork) and false-negative source (never sees fixed/custom/cross-OS). Blocking on an untruthful metric trains users to reflexively override the gate. Hard-depends on A5-Q2 (thread ContextPredicate), A5-Q5 (overlap primitive, the real hard part), A5-Q6 (universe union). Until those land, B ships a wrong block — strictly worse than today's silent overwrite, which at least never blocks a correct action. Reversibility costly: once users build override muscle-memory, removing the gate is itself a behavior change.
- **C. One-click swap/rebind (unbind the loser) — Phase 2** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Never block, but render a button that on save assigns the key to the new action and unbinds the previous holder, with undo.
  - 👍 Most ergonomic resolution — turns a dead-end warning into a one-click fix and directly delivers the end-goal's 'one-click rebind/swap.' Respects user intent, keeps the keymap conflict-free, and being non-blocking avoids B's wrong-refusal trust damage.
  - 👎 'The loser' is ambiguous: dedup is by (name,description) and a name spans contexts/views with shared save/load, so unbinding by name can silently strip a binding from an unrelated view. It cannot target or name a FixedBinding collision (empty lens name, 'can't be reconfigured'), so the swap is undefined for the largest collider class — which today's count map can't even see. And there is NO transactional undo on the save path (set_custom_keybinding persists immediately), so the 'with undo' promise is a new undo-stack build, not a flag. Needs A5-Q8 (per-(name,context) identity) and A5-Q6 (fixed/custom identity) first or it is a surprising destructive action. True effort is above the brief's 'high'.
- **D. Warn + non-destructive jump-to-collider — Phase 1 stretch (ADDED)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Build on A: make the named conflict warning clickable so it scrolls/navigates to the colliding editable row, letting the user decide. No unbind, no block, no persisted change. Reuses A's identity lookup (you already need keystroke -> colliders to name it).
  - 👍 Captures the Axis-5 discoverability goal far better than bare A while being strictly safer than C — it has zero destructive-by-name risk and needs no undo stack, no context model, and no overlap primitive. It is the 80% of C's ergonomics (find-and-fix-yourself) at a fraction of the cost and risk, and it never refuses a save. Because the identity lookup is shared with A, the marginal cost over A is mostly a click handler.
  - 👎 Navigation depends on a scroll-to-row capability in the ClippedScrollable that may not exist yet (clipped_scroll_state) — if absent, D needs UI-framework plumbing, pushing effort up. Like A, it can only jump to EDITABLE colliders; fixed/custom/cross-OS colliders are invisible, so the jump is undefined for them and must be omitted (not shown as a dead button). It does not satisfy the literal 'swap' promise.

**→ Recommendation: A. Warn-only, advisory (always persist) — Phase 1; with D (jump-to-collider) as the recommended within-Phase-1 stretch if scroll-to-row is cheap. Reject B as a Phase-1 hard gate; defer C to Phase 2..** The decisive constraint holds after attack: the system cannot today compute the 'same-context hard conflict' that B and C presuppose. ConflictMap is bare-keystroke, COUNT-ONLY equality over a DEDUPED-editable-only universe (keybindings.rs:104-133, :782, :795-800); fixed/custom/cross-OS collisions are invisible and unnameable (keymap.rs:631-642); the save/dedup key is (name,description) with no scope (:760-782); and the ContextPredicate that would make 'same context' real is private/discarded (keymap.rs:311, answers:1763). A save-gate (B) on that signal would refuse legitimate cross-context saves and train distrust — strictly worse than today's silent overwrite, which never blocks a correct action. C's 'unbind the loser' is destructive-by-name across views and additionally needs an undo stack that does not exist (bindings.rs:491-506). So ship A: it delivers real Phase-1 value (names the colliding editable action instead of the generic string), never blocks, and is the in-place foundation B/C bolt onto once A5-Q2/Q5/Q6 make 'hard' truthful. Two corrections to the original brief, both hardening not flipping: (1) A is low-effort but NOT free — ConflictMap is count-only, so collider identity is net-new work; size it honestly. (2) For the 'Conflicts with: <description>' sub-question, render the matched EDITABLE colliders' descriptions, truncate with '+N more', and do NOT claim completeness or attempt to flag fixed colliders as 'unnameable' — the count never sees them, so phrase it as 'Also bound to: …' (non-exhaustive), not an authoritative 'Conflicts with.' Finally, prefer D (clickable jump-to-collider) over bare A inside Phase 1 if scroll-to-row is already supported: it captures Axis-5 discoverability with no destructive risk and reuses A's lookup. This decision is partially DEFERABLE: the gate/swap half (B/C) is correctly gated by A5-Q2/Q5/Q6/Q8 and does not block Phase 1 at all; only the warn/name/jump half (A/D) is on the Phase-1 path, and even that could be reduced to the existing generic warning if needed without blocking other Axis-5 work.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the honesty bug in A itself, not in B. Because the conflict universe is deduped-editable-only, A's confidently-named 'Conflicts with: Find' can be the LEAST important collision while the user's real problem — a fixed-binding or cross-OS collider — is silently omitted. A partial truth stated with authority can be worse than the old generic string, which at least did not pretend to enumerate. Mitigation (non-exhaustive 'Also bound to:' phrasing) reduces but does not eliminate this. Second surviving counter: recommending warn-only for Phase 1 means the initiative's two headline Axis-5 features (the save-gate and the swap) do not ship in Phase 1; if A5-Q2/Q5/Q6 slip, 'truthful conflicts' degrades to 'a better warning string,' and leadership expecting the gate should be told it is data-gated, not punted. Both are arguments for prioritizing the A5-Q2/Q5/Q6 data-model work, not for forcing B/C onto an untruthful signal now.
- _Dependencies:_ Gate/swap half is gated by A5-Q2 (expose+thread ContextPredicate into a scope field — note the predicate already exists on EditableBinding/FixedBinding but is private/discarded), A5-Q5 (the operational overlap primitive — the genuinely hard part, a non-SAT And/Or/Not predicate intersection), A5-Q6 (union fixed+custom into the conflict universe and give those lenses nameable identity), and A5-Q8 (per-(name,context) identity to make a swap surgical and avoid stripping linked views). Sequenced by A5-Q11 (A = Phase-1 deliverable; B/C = Phase-2). Co-located with A5-Q4 (release-safe validate_trigger on the edit path is the natural home for any future gate). A5-Q7 (chords/prefix-shadow) extends the conflict notion once chord capture (G4/R8) lands. NET-NEW, not gated by another A-question: A/D require refactoring ConflictMap from HashMap<Keystroke,usize> to an identity-bearing map (local work the brief's 'render path already exists' framing omitted); C additionally requires a transactional undo stack that does not exist today.
- _What would settle it:_ Run a startup walk of ctx.editable_bindings() bucketed by keystroke and measure: (1) how many count>1 buckets are actually DISTINCT contexts (the id!('Vim') vs !id!('Vim') fork at actions.rs:51-60) vs genuine same-context collisions — a high cross-context rate empirically rules out B until A5-Q2/Q5 land; (2) how many real collisions involve FIXED/custom bindings that the count map never sees — this sizes how dangerously PARTIAL A's named warning is and whether 'non-exhaustive' phrasing is mandatory. Pair with telemetry: KeybindingChanged (keybindings.rs:681) plus a 'reverted within session' follow-up signal to estimate overwrite-regret — if rare, warn-only suffices and a gate is over-engineering; if common, prioritize the opt-in save-gate for Phase 2. For D specifically: confirm whether ClippedScrollable/clipped_scroll_state supports scroll-to-index (cheap) or needs new plumbing. Final inputs: PM decision on whether 'optional save-gate' means a default-off user setting; eng confirmation from A5-Q5 that a non-SAT overlap check is feasible and from A5-Q6 that fixed/custom can be given nameable identity.
- _Red-team added option:_ D. Warn + non-destructive jump-to-collider (clickable conflict that scrolls to the offending row; no unbind, no block)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all 5 grounding facts against source — every file:line holds (confirm_keystroke_editing :669-699 unconditional set_custom_keybinding; ConflictMap :104-133; render-only signal :48/:270-276/:313-321/:1064; dedup-by-(name,description) :782; FixedBinding as_lens name empty keymap.rs:631-642). Core recommendation (ship A, reject B as a Phase-1 hard gate, defer C to Phase-2) survives intact and is well-grounded — UPHELD on substance. Hardened in four ways: (1) Corrected an OPTIMISM/HAND-WAVING error in A's steelman: the brief claims naming the collider is near-zero work because 'the render path already exists' and 'the description is in-hand.' It is not in-hand — ConflictMap is count-only (HashMap<Keystroke,usize>); it knows a collision EXISTS but not WHO. Naming requires a net-new identity-bearing lookup (multimap keystroke->colliders, or a per-render row scan). A stays low-effort but is not free. (2) Corrected an incoherent sub-answer: the brief says to 'mark fixed-binding colliders as unnameable until A5-Q6.' You cannot mark them — the count universe is deduped-editable-only and never SEES fixed/custom or cross-OS colliders, so it can't even produce a '+N more' for them. The warning must therefore NOT imply completeness (use 'Also bound to: X' phrasing, not an exhaustive 'Conflicts with'), or it gives false assurance. (3) Added MISSING OPTION D (warn + non-destructive jump-to-collider) — strictly safer than C (no destructive by-name op) and more aligned with Axis-5 discoverability than bare A; reuses A's identity lookup. (4) Priced in two omitted costs: no transactional undo exists on save (set_custom_keybinding persists to disk immediately), which inflates C's 'with undo' to a new undo-stack build; and the ContextPredicate actually EXISTS on EditableBinding/FixedBinding but is private/discarded, so A5-Q2 is 'expose+thread,' not 'invent' — the real Phase-2 hard part is A5-Q5's overlap primitive, not the data's existence. Considered and rejected a pure-defer (ship nothing, keep generic string): dominated by A, which is genuinely cheap and delivers real value.

### A5-Q15 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product owner of the keybindings initiative, with design review on the display affordance (tagged user-product-decision in the spec). Engineering owns only the trivial implementation: the name fuzzy-score line in app/src/util/bindings.rs (filter_bindings_including_keystroke) and, for reveal-on-detail, a copyable token inside the existing render_clicked region in app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs.

**Decision.** When search-by-name is added (R7 extends filter_bindings_including_keystroke, bindings.rs:625-687, which today scores only keystroke and description and never reads binding.name), do we (a) index the raw internal namespace:action id as a search key, and separately (b) display that id to the user — and if so, where (always-visible per-row badge vs reveal-only inside the expanded/clicked row vs not at all)? The index and the display are two separable sub-decisions; the contested part is the display.

**Stakes.** The axis headline promises 'search by action name / internal id.' The name is the only stable, persistable identifier (descriptions are display text; the per-instance BindingId is volatile and non-serializable), the R9 change-keybinding agent's manifest is name-keyed (A5-Q12), and keybindings.yaml round-trips by name (A1). Two distinct needs ride on this: (A) SEARCH/CONFIRM a token you already know — satisfied by indexing name alone; (B) DISCOVER a token you don't know (find a row by description, learn its canonical id to type into yaml or hand to the agent) — satisfied only by displaying the id somewhere. Getting the display wrong either forecloses the discovery need (index-only) or injects ~hundreds of machine strings into a list users currently scan by clean human descriptions (always-visible badge). A concrete extra win the brief missed: today the settings catalog contains unbound editable bindings with empty descriptions that are unfindable by any text search (answers :1985); indexing name makes them findable.

_Constrained by:_
- filter_bindings_including_keystroke scores ONLY binding.trigger (bindings.rs:638) and binding.description.in_context (bindings.rs:650-651); it never references binding.name. Adding name-search is a small edit to this one function. Independently confirmed by the answers doc: 'the function never reads binding.name ... this also answers A5-Q15.' — `bindings.rs:638-651,681 + answers :1985`
- GROUNDING CORRECTION: the display-layer struct app/src/util/bindings.rs CommandBinding.name is `String` (bindings.rs:532), NOT `&'static str` as the brief stated; the &'static str lives on upstream registration structs (keymap.rs EditableBinding::new :646, keymap CommandBinding.name :308). Substance holds: CommandBinding has fields name/description/trigger/action/group/id (bindings.rs:531-541) with NO separate human display-label field — the name IS the raw namespace:action id. — `bindings.rs:531-541; keymap.rs:308,646`
- Search runs OVER the already-deduped list: on_page_selected builds self.bindings via dedup_by(|a,b| a.name==b.name && a.description==b.description) (keybindings.rs:782), and filter_bindings feeds self.bindings.iter().flatten() into the filter (keybindings.rs:538-539). So name-search cannot resurface dedup-hidden rows; hidden rows are exact (name,description) twins that remap identically under name-keyed update. The question's 2nd clause ('does name-search expose hidden bindings?') = NO for the settings path. VERIFIED. — `keybindings.rs:759-784,536-545`
- MISSED DECISIVE FACT (strongest pro-index argument, omitted by brief): the settings catalog includes unbound editable bindings and applies NO description-empty filter on the settings path; the answers doc states 'an unbound editable binding with an empty description would be included in the settings catalog but unfindable by text' today. Indexing binding.name makes this currently-unsearchable class findable. — `answers :1985`
- The reveal host already EXISTS: clicking a row expands it via render_clicked (keybindings.rs:307-366), which renders an inline detail region (conflict warning, 'Press new keyboard shortcut', edit buttons via get_edit_button_row :394). A small copyable monospace id token can be added there at genuinely low cost — no new detail panel is built. This rehabilitates the recommended option's 'low effort' claim. — `keybindings.rs:307-366,394`
- HIGHLIGHT LIMITATION (fixes recommended option's what_breaks): the filter returns highlight indices (Option<Vec<usize>>) computed by match_indices_case_insensitive OVER the description string (bindings.rs:650-651). A name-only match therefore has no description span to highlight, so the brief's 'mitigated by highlighting the matched token' is not achievable in the collapsed list — the matched token can only be surfaced after expansion (render_clicked). — `bindings.rs:650-653,685-686`
- CommandBinding carries no context/scope dimension: from_editable_lens (bindings.rs:762-771) copies description/trigger/action/name/group/id but NOT the lens context_predicate. A name maps to N context-distinct bindings but remap is uniform (one keystroke per name). The 9 same-name/different-description rows survive dedup as parallel rows (keybindings.rs:775-782 comment: find-in-block vs find-in-terminal) and would all show the SAME id. — `bindings.rs:762-771,531-541; keybindings.rs:775-782`
- Fixed (read-only) bindings have an EMPTY name: FixedBinding's BindingLens hardcodes name: Default::default() (keymap.rs:633). So a name-index over the editable settings catalog covers ONLY editable bindings until A5-Q3/Q4 give fixed bindings a stable id; the answers doc sizes that work at 93 files / 146 register calls / 456 constructor sites (the brief's alias option mis-cited '109 registration files'). — `keymap.rs:633; answers :1809`
- The resource-center cheatsheet is a SEPARATE catalog that dedups by description ONLY (resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:152: dedup_by(|a,b| a.description==b.description)) over fixed+editable bindings filtered to trigger.is_some(). A name-index on the settings path does nothing for it. — `resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:140-152`

**Options:**

- **Hidden search key + reveal-on-detail** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Extend filter_bindings_including_keystroke (bindings.rs:625-687) to also fuzzy-score binding.name, but do NOT add a name column to the collapsed list. Surface the raw id only inside the EXISTING expanded/clicked row (render_clicked, keybindings.rs:307-366) as a small copyable monospace token, so users who need the canonical id (for keybindings.yaml or the R9 agent) can copy it while the default scannable list stays description-driven.
  - 👍 Delivers BOTH needs at near-zero UI cost. The index serves search-by-name and rescues the currently-unsearchable unbound/empty-description editables (answers :1985); the reveal token serves the discovery need (learn the canonical token from a description-found row) that index-only cannot. Crucially, render_clicked already exists as the host (keybindings.rs:307), so no detail panel is built — the effort really is one fuzzy-score line plus a token in an existing region. Both real consumers of the id (name-keyed R9 manifest A5-Q12, name-keyed keybindings.yaml A1) need it copyable, not prominent, which is exactly what reveal-on-detail provides.
  - 👎 CORRECTED: a user who types a name but sees only the description in the collapsed row gets a 'why did THIS match?' moment that CANNOT be mitigated by highlighting — the filter's highlight indices are computed over the description (bindings.rs:650-651), so a name-only match has no description span to highlight; the matched token can only appear after the user expands the row (render_clicked), not in the scannable list. Fixed bindings have empty names (keymap.rs:633) so name-search silently covers only editable bindings until A5-Q3/Q4 — a partial-coverage gap to document. The 9 same-name/different-description rows show the SAME id on expansion (looks duplicative). Does nothing for the resource-center cheatsheet (separate description-only dedup, keybindings_page.rs:152).
- **Index-only now, defer display (telemetry-gated)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Add binding.name as a fuzzy key in filter_bindings_including_keystroke and ship NOTHING else — no badge, no detail token. This delivers the literal axis headline ('search by action name / internal id') as a single-function edit, and defers the contested display sub-decision (reveal-token vs always-visible badge) to telemetry plus the power-user/R9 interview the brief's own what_would_settle_it prescribes.
  - 👍 Cleanly unbundles the uncontested, trivial, high-value index from the design-reviewed display affordance. It is the cheapest option that satisfies the headline, rescues the currently-unsearchable unbound/empty-description editables (answers :1985), and avoids pre-committing to 'users need to see/copy the id' before that has been validated — which is precisely the open question the brief flags. If the reveal-token design review stalls, Phase 1 still ships search-by-name. This is the documented minimal floor / fallback.
  - 👎 Serves 'search/confirm a token you already know' but NOT 'discover a token you don't' — a user who finds a row by description still cannot learn its canonical namespace:action id, so the discovery half of the stated need (stakes B) is unmet until a later display decision. Because render_clicked makes the reveal token genuinely cheap (keybindings.rs:307), deferring display saves little real effort while leaving the discovery gap open. Same collapsed-list 'why did this match' ambiguity as Option 1, unmitigated.
- **Visible raw-id badge** _(low effort · easy)_ — Render the namespace:action id as a persistent secondary/subtitle line or monospace badge on every catalog row, and index it in search.
  - 👍 Maximum truthfulness and power-user/agent affordance: the canonical token is always visible and copyable, removes all ambiguity about which row matched a name query, and directly supports hand-authoring keybindings.yaml or instructing the R9 agent without expanding a row. Treats the keybindings settings page as the developer tool its audience largely is.
  - 👎 Injects ~hundreds of machine strings (code_editor:find, ai_block:rich_content_secret_first_char_position) into a list users scan by human description — visual clutter and a leaky-abstraction feel. Names were never designed as display strings; some are unflattering implementation detail. Couples the UI to an internal naming convention, making future renames user-visible/breaking. The 9 same-name/different-description rows show identical badges, which reads as a bug rather than intended linked-binding behavior. Fixed bindings have empty names (keymap.rs:633) so badges would be blank for them until A5-Q3/Q4.
- **Humanized alias index** _(high effort · costly)_ — Don't surface raw ids; build a curated/synthesized human-friendly synonym set (or Title-Case-from-name labels) and index those for search, keeping descriptions as the only visible text.
  - 👍 Best pure-UX outcome: users search in natural language, never see namespace:action, and matches feel intuitive — the cleanest possible discoverability without exposing internals.
  - 👎 No alias data source exists — name is the sole id and description already serves as the human label, so this is net-new content to author and maintain across the registration surface the answers doc sizes at 93 files / 146 register calls / 456 constructor sites (the brief's '109 files' is not the verified figure), with drift risk. It also fails the stated need: the R9 agent and keybindings.yaml require the REAL name (A5-Q12, A1), so an alias index that hides the canonical token leaves power users unable to discover what to type. High effort for a benefit description already mostly provides.
- **Defer name-search entirely (description+keystroke only)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship v1 with search unchanged (description + keystroke only); do not index OR display names at all.
  - 👍 Zero code change and zero clutter; descriptions already cover most natural-language queries, and the conflict/catalog work lands without touching the search path. Lets the team defer the fixed-binding-id (A5-Q3/Q4) and scope (A5-Q8) questions that complicate truthful name-search.
  - 👎 Foregoes the axis headline AND the agent-discoverability value. Users who know the id (from yaml or agent output) can't find the row by it; the gap between what the agent speaks (names) and what the UI lets you search (descriptions) persists. Also leaves the currently-unsearchable unbound/empty-description editables (answers :1985) unfindable. Cheap now, but re-opens the same decision later with no learning gained. Strictly dominated by Index-only, which costs the same one-function edit but delivers the headline.

**→ Recommendation: Hidden search key + reveal-on-detail.** Decompose the decision: indexing binding.name is the non-negotiable Phase-1 FLOOR — a single fuzzy-score line in filter_bindings_including_keystroke (bindings.rs:633-687) that delivers the literal axis headline and rescues the currently-unsearchable unbound/empty-description editables (answers :1985); every non-defer option includes it. The contested half is DISPLAY, and reveal-on-detail wins because (a) it is the only low-cost option that satisfies the DISCOVERY need — learning a canonical token from a description-found row — which index-only structurally cannot; (b) the host already exists (render_clicked, keybindings.rs:307-366), so the marginal cost over index-only is a copyable token in an existing region, not a new panel; (c) the two real consumers (name-keyed R9 manifest A5-Q12, name-keyed keybindings.yaml A1) need the id copyable, not prominent. The always-visible badge pays an ongoing clutter and abstraction-coupling cost for a benefit only power users need occasionally, and its same-name/different-description duplicate-badge artifact reads as a bug. The alias option invents a maintenance burden across ~456 constructor sites with no data source and still hides the canonical token the agent requires. Defer/defer-name-search bank no learning and are dominated by Index-only. The safety worry is answered: search runs over the post-dedup list (keybindings.rs:538-539,782) so it provably cannot resurface dedup-hidden twins. Two honest caveats now corrected: the 'highlight the matched token' mitigation is NOT implementable in the collapsed list (highlight indices are over the description, bindings.rs:650-651) — the token only appears on expansion; and name-search covers editable bindings only until A5-Q3/Q4 give fixed bindings a stable id. If design review on the token stalls, fall back to the added 'Index-only' option to keep Phase 1 unblocked, then promote to reveal/badge per telemetry — reversibility is easy in both directions.

- _Residual risk:_ The recommendation commits to building the reveal-on-detail token BEFORE running the very power-user/R9 interview that what_would_settle_it prescribes ('do you need to see/copy the canonical id, or only search by it?'). If the answer is 'we only search by it, never copy', the token is wasted effort (cheap and reversible, but premature); if the answer is 'we need it visible while scanning the list', reveal-on-detail under-delivers and the always-visible badge was the right call. So the choice is a bet that the copy-on-expand middle is what's needed, made ahead of the evidence. It is defensible only because render_clicked makes the token nearly free and reversibility is easy both ways (drop it, or promote it to a badge) — but a disciplined owner could equally ship the added Index-only option first and let the interview decide the display, accepting that the discovery need stays unmet for one release.
- _Dependencies:_ NOT a Phase-1 blocker: the name-index edit is shippable standalone (the added 'Index-only' option), and the display affordance can defer. Gated-by: A5-Q3/A5-Q4 (fixed bindings have empty names, keymap.rs:633 — name-search covers editable bindings only until they get stable ids; that work is sized at 93 files / 146 register calls / 456 constructor sites, answers :1809). A5-Q1 (which surface gets search — settings path vs resource-center cheatsheet; the cheatsheet dedups description-only at resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:152 and needs separate work). Interacts-with: A5-Q8 (entry granularity — whether a name query returns 1 deduped row or N scope rows; today rows are scope-less (name,description), and the 9 same-name/different-description rows return in parallel). Informs/raises-value-of: A5-Q12 (name-keyed R9 manifest — a copyable id is the human bridge to it). Orthogonal: the forward/reverse get_binding_by_name precedence asymmetry (answers :882) does not affect search/display, which iterate self.bindings directly.
- _What would settle it:_ Ship the name-index (the uncontested floor) in a dogfood build FIRST — it is needed even to measure name-shaped demand. Log hashed query strings and zero-result rates: if a meaningful fraction of queries look like namespace:action tokens, or description-only search shows high zero-result rates a name-index would catch, the index is justified immediately.
