Then ask the R9 agent team and 3-5 power users the single deciding question: 'do you need to SEE/COPY the canonical action id in the UI, or only search by it?' — that answer, not an A/B on perceived clutter, settles reveal-on-detail vs always-visible badge vs index-only. Sequencing the index before the interview means Phase 1 is never blocked on the contested display call.
- _Red-team added option:_ Index-only now, defer display (telemetry-gated)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code anchor against bindings.rs, keybindings.rs, keymap.rs, resource_center/keybindings_page.rs, and the answers doc. The core recommendation survives but is materially strengthened. Corrections: (1) GROUNDING ERROR — display-layer CommandBinding.name is `String` (bindings.rs:532), not `&'static str`; the &'static str is on upstream registration structs (keymap.rs:308,646). Substance (no separate display-label field; name IS the raw id) holds. (2) OPTIMISM — the recommended option claimed the 'why did this row match?' confusion is 'mitigated by highlighting the matched token', but the filter's highlight indices are computed over the description string (bindings.rs:650-651); a name-only match has no description span to highlight, so the matched token can only surface after the row is expanded (render_clicked), never in the scannable list. Fixed the what_breaks. (3) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY (resolved in the option's favor) — 'reveal-on-detail' silently assumed a detail/edit affordance exists; it does: render_clicked (keybindings.rs:307-366) already renders an expanded inline row, so the copyable token is a cheap add, not a new panel. This rehabilitates the 'low effort' claim. (4) MISSED FACT — settings catalog includes unbound editable bindings with no description-empty filter; answers doc (:1985) says such rows are 'unfindable by text' today, so name-indexing rescues a currently-unsearchable class (the strongest pro-index argument, omitted by the brief). (5) Alias option cited '109 registration files'; verified sizing is 93 files / 146 register calls / 456 constructor sites (answers :1809). (6) SAFETY upheld — search iterates post-dedup self.bindings (keybindings.rs:538-539,782), so name-search cannot resurface dedup-hidden (name,description) twins; question's 2nd clause = NO, confirmed by answers :1985. (7) DEFERABILITY — the decision bundles a trivial uncontested index edit with a contested display sub-decision; I unbundled them and added the middle option as a Phase-1-shippable floor.

---

## Axis 6 — Cross-cutting foundation (platform-in-data-model)

### A6-Q1 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product owner for the keybindings/settings initiative (the PM who owns R5), with co-sign from the warpui/settings engineering lead — because the edit-vs-display line directly sets whether the Axis-2 record schema, a release-path validator, and a platform-parameterized conflict engine become hard prerequisites, and because the engineering lead must confirm the cheaper boundary (faithful inactive display from retained-registration data + portable write path WITHOUT those three) is real. The investigation doc classifies A6-Q1 as user-product-decision; note the 'rule out B for Phase 1' half is largely engineering-cost-driven, while the display-vs-portable-edit-vs-defer half is the product/demand call.

**Decision.** For R5's "show/edit both mac and linux/windows bindings" end state, decide the editability boundary for the INACTIVE (non-running) platform's binding. The load-bearing, must-decide-before-schema call is binary: is full INDEPENDENT editing of the inactive platform in Phase-1 scope (which forces the Axis-2 record schema, a release/edit-path platform-parameterized validator, and a platform-parameterized conflict engine) or NOT? Given that answer is "not yet," a second, smaller, reversible call follows: how does the cross-platform surface treat the inactive side and what does a current-platform edit PERSIST — read-only display of defaults with edits persisted resolved (status quo write path, inactive column is decorative); read-only display PLUS edits persisted in unresolved/portable form so a customization resolves correctly on the other OS and the inactive display stays truthful; or no cross-platform surface at all this phase (engine-only).

**Stakes.** This boundary is the single biggest scope multiplier in Axis 6, but the truly blocking decision is narrower than the brief implied. Full independent inactive editing silently pulls in (a) the Axis-2 record-based file schema (the flat one-string-per-name file physically cannot hold a {mac:'cmd-[', other:'ctrl-shift-{'} pair — keyboard.rs:169,174), (b) a net-new release/edit-path platform-parameterized validator (today's edit path validates nothing; the only validator is debug-only, one-directional — keybindings.rs:669-699, bindings.rs:918-933), and (c) a target-OS-parameterized conflict engine (ConflictMap is keyed on the current-OS-resolved keystroke — keybindings.rs:104-133) — and it asks a user to edit, match, and conflict-check a binding their compile-time-locked build can never run or test (OperatingSystem::get is cfg-fixed, no native setter — platform/mod.rs:669-683). RULING THAT OUT for Phase 1 is what keeps the schema flat and Axis 6 parallel; both display variants and full-defer satisfy that. The remaining display-vs-portable-edit-vs-defer choice is reversible and cheap, but it has hidden teeth: a faithful inactive display of the genuine-divergence MAJORITY (~66% of sites — A6-Q5) is only possible if A6-Q3 commits to the {mac,other} pair representation; if A6-Q3 picks the cmd_or_ctrl bool instead, the inactive column cannot be rendered truthfully and the whole display value collapses. The doc rightly flags that "show both" was conflated with "edit both"; the sharper correction is that "edit both" was further conflated with "edit the inactive side independently" — most multi-OS users actually want "my one config works on both machines," which is a portability/persistence property, not an inactive-platform editor.

_Constrained by:_
- OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time-fixed per native build (cfg_if over target_os/family) with NO parameter and NO runtime setter; the only non-compile-time path is a wasm OnceLock set once from the browser user agent. A single native build literally cannot resolve, match, run, or test the inactive platform's binding, so EDITING a platform you cannot exercise is inherently riskier and locally unverifiable, while DISPLAY carries no such hazard. — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:669-683; wasm.rs:8 (A6-Q15 answers:2447)`
- Keystroke has fields ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key and NO cmdorctrl/platform field; the cmdorctrl token is resolved to cmd (mac) or ctrl (else) at PARSE time. So a binding's cmd-vs-ctrl identity is OS-baked before display or persistence; a faithful cross-platform surface needs the unresolved binding plus a target-OS resolver, not glyph rendering alone. — `keymap.rs:320-328, 914-919 (verified directly)`
- cmdorctrl is the ONLY pure cmd<->ctrl swap (~139 sites) and is resolved away at parse time. The MAJORITY of divergence is genuine key/chord difference (cmd_or_ctrl_shift ~140 CORRECTED from 181; with_mac/with_linux 157; new_per_platform 13) and the inactive side is never constructed or stored because the builders no-op on the OS they don't match. 'Show both' cannot be synthesized by a cmd<->ctrl rule for most bindings; both sides must be carried through registration. — `A6-Q5 answers:2156 (keymap.rs:688-713, 914-919)`
- The persisted file is HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger(String)> (one slot per name) and the ONLY write path maps via Keystroke::normalized(), which emits resolved ctrl-/cmd- with no cmdorctrl branch. Therefore: (a) a full {mac,other} pair cannot be persisted without Axis-2's record schema; (b) a cmdorctrl token can be READ from disk but cannot be WRITTEN/round-tripped today; (c) crucially, a user's current-platform edit is persisted in RESOLVED form, so it is not config-portable and does not survive being carried to the other OS — the brief's Option A is silent on this. — `keyboard.rs:169,174,176-180; normalized() keymap.rs:970-993 (A6-Q9 answers:2311,2315)`
- The settings edit path runs NO validator: confirm_keystroke_editing unconditionally calls set_custom_keybinding with no has_conflict gate; ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize> keyed on the CURRENT-OS-resolved keystroke and does same-platform duplicate detection only; is_binding_cross_platform is debug-only, registration-time, one-directional (returns Yes on mac, only flags a stray cmd on non-mac). Editing the inactive platform safely requires a brand-new release/edit-path, platform-parameterized validator AND a target-OS-parameterized conflict map that do not exist today. — `keybindings.rs:104-133,669-699; util/bindings.rs:918-933 (answers:1836; A6-Q13:2393,2409)`
- displayed() has no target-OS parameter, branches on OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() five times, and is called across 21 files / 41 sites (all current-OS hint surfaces). A read-only displayed_for(target_os) is additive and confined to ~3 settings-adjacent callers; threading a target-OS param through displayed() would be 21-file mechanical churn. This bounds the display path as cheap relative to the edit path. — `keymap.rs:998-1065 (A6-Q15 answers:2451-2462)`
- HIDDEN DEPENDENCY: a faithful inactive-platform display of the genuine-divergence majority is only possible if A6-Q3 commits to a {mac,other} pair representation carried through registration; option (a) cmd_or_ctrl: bool on the flat Keystroke struct cannot express key-level divergence (cmd-[ vs ctrl-shift-{), so under (a) the inactive column degrades to lossy cmd<->ctrl synthesis — the very truthfulness regression the brief warns against. The recommendation silently assumes A6-Q3 = (b). — `A6-Q3 design-doc:901-906; A6-Q5 answers:2154; A6-Q6 answers:2180`

**Options:**

- **Read-only display of inactive platform (edits persist resolved, status quo write path)** _(med effort · easy)_ — The settings/catalog surface faithfully shows the inactive platform's DEFAULT binding via an additive displayed_for(target_os) over retained-at-registration {mac,other} data; only the current-platform binding is editable, and that edit persists as today (resolved current-OS string).
  - 👍 Strictly inside what one native build can honestly assert, and the cheapest way to land the 'here is what your shortcut does on the other OS' value. Avoids all four edit blast-radius items (Axis-2 record schema, edit-path validator, target-OS conflict map, target-OS test seam). Ships in Phase 1 in parallel with the engine. displayed_for(os) is ~3 callers.
  - 👎 Decorative, not portable: because the edit path persists RESOLVED strings (keyboard.rs:180), a user who rebinds on Mac gets NO change to their Linux column and their customization does NOT follow a carried/synced config — so for CUSTOMIZED bindings the value is only 'see the default of the other OS,' the weakest slice of R5. Faithfulness of the inactive column depends on A6-Q3 landing the {mac,other} pair; under cmd_or_ctrl bool it degrades to lossy synthesis. Customized rows must label the inactive value as the platform DEFAULT, not the user's choice.
- **Read-only inactive display with portable current-platform edits (RECOMMENDED)** _(med effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Same read-only inactive display, PLUS the current-platform edit persists in unresolved/portable form (cmdorctrl token where symmetric) so the customization resolves correctly on the other OS when the config is carried/shared, and the inactive display reflects that resolution rather than a stale default. The inactive side is still never independently editable.
  - 👍 Delivers the actual North-Star value most multi-OS users want — 'my one config works on both machines' — which is a persistence/portability property, not an inactive editor, so it sidesteps the entire edit blast radius (no per-platform pair to persist for the symmetric class, no target-OS conflict engine, no edit-a-binding-you-cannot-run hazard). For the symmetric cmdorctrl class a single portable edit effectively rebinds both OSes; for the genuine-divergence class it cleanly degrades to display-only. Incremental cost over plain Option A is just the unresolved write-path that A6-Q9 already flags the engine needs anyway, plus the A6-Q12 read rule the portability promise needs regardless. Still a strict subset of full edit, forecloses nothing.
  - 👎 Adds two dependencies over plain display: the unresolved write-path change (A6-Q9: cmdorctrl can be read but not round-trip-written today) and the A6-Q12 back-compat read rule (so an existing resolved 'cmd-d' is not misread as a deliberate mac-only override). If the Phase-1 engine ships portability only for DEFAULTS and not for the user-edit write path, this collapses back to plain Option A. It does NOT let a power user set DIFFERENT bindings per OS (the asymmetric case) — that still needs full edit. Faithful inactive display still depends on A6-Q3 = {mac,other} pair.
- **Full independent editing of inactive platform** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — The user can edit, conflict-check, and persist the Linux binding while running on Mac (and vice versa), as a true symmetric per-platform editor with asymmetric overrides.
  - 👍 Matches the literal 'edit both' phrasing and is the ONLY option that satisfies asymmetric demand — a power user who wants cmd-K on Mac but alt-K on Linux for ergonomic reasons. Most complete realization of platform-in-the-data-model; avoids a future second feature if that demand turns out to dominate.
  - 👎 Pulls in the whole downstream spine: persisting an inactive edit needs the Axis-2 record schema (flat one-slot-per-name file cannot hold a pair); a net-new release/edit-path platform-parameterized validator (today's edit path validates nothing, is_binding_cross_platform is debug-only/one-directional); a target-OS-parameterized conflict engine (ConflictMap is current-OS-resolved); and a target-OS test-injection seam or CI cannot assert the other platform resolves/conflicts/validates. The user edits a binding the build can never run or test (OperatingSystem::get is compile-time-fixed) with no obvious capture UX for a mac chord on Linux hardware. Migration-bearing schema/serde change. High regression risk; serializes Axis 6 behind Phase 2.
- **Defer the cross-platform surface entirely (engine-only this phase)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship the portability/unresolved-model engine (so a binding is correct on whatever OS it runs on and a carried config resolves sanely) but expose NO inactive-platform display or edit yet; the settings UI stays current-OS only.
  - 👍 Most of R5's real value (a config that is automatically right on both OSes via the cmdorctrl/unresolved model) is delivered WITHOUT any cross-platform editor. Fully de-risks Phase 1, lets the Axis-2 record schema settle before any cross-platform surface is committed, validates demand before investing, and — by the brief's own 'display is additive/reversible' logic — forecloses nothing because display can be layered later. The honest fallback if the display-feasibility spike fails or demand data is absent.
  - 👎 Leaves R5's visible 'show both' promise unfulfilled; the discoverability/truthfulness win (a Mac user seeing what a Linux teammate gets) is absent. Defers the display-vs-edit learning to later with the same uncertainty. Still requires the separate A6-Q3 unresolved-representation decision to land for portability — it removes only the UI surface, not the model work.

**→ Recommendation: Read-only inactive display with portable current-platform edits (RECOMMENDED).** The decision that MUST be made now is to rule out full INDEPENDENT inactive-platform editing for Phase 1 (Option B): only B forces the Axis-2 record schema, a release/edit-path validator, and a target-OS conflict engine as hard prerequisites, and only B asks a user to edit a binding their compile-time-locked build can never run, match, or test (platform/mod.rs:669-683). Ruling B out keeps the file schema flat, keeps Axis 6 parallel with Phase 1, and is robustly supported by code. Among the remaining read-only options, the portable-edit variant beats plain display because the brief's Option A is silent on a load-bearing fact a skeptical reviewer will immediately probe: with today's write path the user's edit persists RESOLVED (keyboard.rs:180), so it is not config-portable and the inactive column is mere decoration — whereas the actual value multi-OS users want ('my one config works on both machines') is a portability/persistence property the engine already needs (A6-Q9 flags the cmdorctrl write-path change; A6-Q12 flags the read rule). Portable-edit delivers that for the symmetric class and degrades cleanly to display for the genuine-divergence majority, all without the edit blast radius. It beats full-defer (Option D) because the incremental cost over the shared engine is small and it realizes R5's visible 'show both' promise; it beats full-edit (Option B) because B's very-high, migration-bearing cost should be paid only after demand for inactive editing — specifically ASYMMETRIC per-OS overrides — is demonstrated, not assumed from the word 'edit.' Two honest caveats make this signable rather than optimistic: (1) the recommendation is conditional on A6-Q3 committing to the {mac,other} pair representation — under the cmd_or_ctrl-bool alternative, faithful display of the ~66% genuine-divergence majority is impossible and the recommendation must fall back to Option D; (2) if the Phase-1 engine does not extend portability to the user-edit write path, portable-edit collapses to plain Option A and full-defer becomes the cleaner choice. Because the display-vs-portable-edit-vs-defer refinement is itself cheap and reversible, it can be finalized on a demand signal without blocking the start of Phase-1 engine work.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument: the genuine-divergence majority (~66% of sites — A6-Q5) is precisely the population a power user might want to customize DIFFERENTLY per OS (e.g. cmd-K on Mac, alt-K on Linux for ergonomics). Neither read-only display NOR portable-edit can satisfy that asymmetric demand — only full independent editing (Option B) can. So if the product demand data comes back showing the wanted capability is asymmetric per-platform overrides rather than 'trust portability and see the resolution,' the recommendation's cost-deferral was wrong and B's Axis-2-schema + validator + conflict-engine cost must be paid after all, having lost the time spent on the display surface. Secondary residual: the whole read-only family's truthfulness is hostage to A6-Q3 landing the {mac,other} pair; if A6-Q3 picks the cmd_or_ctrl bool, the inactive column degrades to lossy cmd<->ctrl synthesis (wrong for meta/super and for cmd-[ vs ctrl-shift-{), turning the headline feature into a confident-but-wrong glyph — at which point Option D (engine-only, no surface) is the safer ship.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated-by / couples-with: A6-Q3 (canonical unresolved representation — the recommendation REQUIRES this land on the {mac,other} pair carried through registration; the cmd_or_ctrl-bool alternative breaks faithful display of the genuine-divergence majority). A6-Q9 + A6-Q10 (full-pair persistence is gated behind Axis-2's record schema; the portable-edit refinement needs the cmdorctrl write-path change A6-Q9 flags as not-yet-round-trippable). A6-Q12 (back-compat read rule — NEW, omitted by the brief: needed so an existing resolved 'cmd-d' is not misread as a deliberate mac-only override, which gates faithful display of customized bindings and the portable-edit persistence). A6-Q2 (whether scope also covers QuakeMode/global-hotkey and slash-command PerPlatformKeystroke surfaces — and note those, unlike keybindings.yaml, ARE cloud-synced, so the sync story differs). A6-Q15 (displayed_for(os) vs param). A6-Q16 (carrying both sides through registration changes the Trigger data the matcher hot-path scans — a shared engine concern). Gates ONLY if full independent Edit (Option B) is later chosen: A6-Q11 (R6 conflict detection must become target-OS-parameterized — Axis 5); A6-Q13 + R10 (new release/edit-path platform-parameterized validator); A6-Q14 (target-OS test-injection seam). Upstream: Axis-1 portability-promise decision frames how much cross-platform visibility users actually need. Sequencing: only the 'reject independent inactive editing for Phase 1' half blocks schema work; the display-vs-portable-edit-vs-defer refinement is deferrable past the start of Phase-1 engine work and is reversible.
- _What would settle it:_ Three inputs. (1) Product/stakeholder data: among customizing users who run Warp on more than one OS, what is the dominant need — (a) merely TRUST portability and SEE the other-OS resolution (then portable-edit/display suffices), (b) set ASYMMETRIC different bindings per OS (then only full Option B suffices), or (c) no real multi-OS customizing population (then Option D, engine-only, is correct)? This is the pivotal fork. (2) An engineering spike proving the cheap boundary is real: that a faithful read-only inactive display can be produced from retained-at-registration {mac,other} data + displayed_for(target_os), AND that the current-platform edit can persist in unresolved/portable form, WITHOUT the Axis-2 record schema, the edit-path validator, or the conflict-map parameterization. (3) Confirmation that A6-Q3 commits to the {mac,other} pair representation — if it instead picks the cmd_or_ctrl bool, faithful display of the genuine-divergence majority is impossible and the recommendation must fall back to Option D. If the spike shows even display/portable-edit forces the three edit-blast-radius dependencies, the cost gap between this recommendation and Option B collapses and the decision should be revisited.
- _Red-team added option:_ Read-only inactive display with portable current-platform edits (edits persist in unresolved form so they apply on both OSes / survive a carried config; no independent inactive editing)
- _Red-team:_ Grounding survives scrutiny. I re-verified all five code_grounding facts against the answers doc AND directly against source: Keystroke has no cmdorctrl/platform field (keymap.rs:320-328); cmdorctrl resolves at parse time to cmd/ctrl (keymap.rs:914-919); the persisted file is HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger(String)> with a resolved write path via Keystroke::normalized (keyboard.rs:169-180); ConflictMap = HashMap<Keystroke,usize> keyed on resolved keystroke (keybindings.rs:104-133); confirm_keystroke_editing calls set_custom_keybinding with no validator/conflict gate (keybindings.rs:669-699); is_binding_cross_platform returns Yes on mac and only flags a stray cmd on non-mac (bindings.rs:918-933). The cmd_or_ctrl_shift count in the brief (~140) correctly uses the A6-Q5 CORRECTED figure, not the inflated 181. Citation hygiene: the brief's "A6-Q17 answers doc:1836" is mislabeled (that line lives in the R10/edit-path answer; no A6-Q17 section exists), and "A6-Q11 ... :2283" points to a one-line cross-reference inside the A6-Q8 answer (A6-Q11/Q14 have no standalone answer sections) — but in both cases the underlying FACT is supported, so I corrected the labels rather than deleting the facts. Four real attacks landed: (1) MISSING OPTION — the brief never says what a current-platform edit PERSISTS; with today's write path (keyboard.rs:180 emits resolved cmd-/ctrl-) a Mac user's customization is NOT config-portable and the inactive column is decorative. I added a hybrid that persists edits in unresolved/portable form so a carried config resolves correctly on the other OS. (2) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY — the recommendation silently assumes A6-Q3 lands on the {mac,other} pair representation (option b); if A6-Q3 picks the cmd_or_ctrl bool (option a), faithful display of the genuine-divergence majority (~66% of sites) is impossible and display degrades to the lossy cmd<->ctrl synthesis the brief itself calls a truthfulness regression. Made explicit. (3) DEFERABILITY — the load-bearing content of A6-Q1 is narrower than "pick display": it is "rule OUT full independent inactive editing for Phase 1." Both Display and Defer keep the schema flat and Axis 6 parallel; only Edit forces the Axis-2 record schema. So the must-decide-now is "reject B for now"; choosing Display-now vs Defer-display vs portable-edit is a smaller, reversible refinement that can ride product data. (4) Surfaced A6-Q12 (back-compat read rule) as a dependency the brief omitted — it gates faithful display of customized/synced bindings and the portable-edit refinement. Verdict hardened, not flipped: read-only-for-inactive and reject-full-edit-for-Phase-1 both stand; I tightened the recommendation, added a dominating-for-target-user option, and made the A6-Q3 assumption load-bearing and explicit.

### A6-Q2 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Keybindings/Settings product lead (user-product-decision per A6-Q2), with the warpui_core/settings engineering lead as REQUIRED co-signer on the serialization constraint — A6-Q4 makes the Keystroke struct a cloud-synced + UserDefaults-persisted format, so the serde-additive guard (#[serde(default)] vs Trigger-level pair vs versioned migration) is an eng-scoped gate. Additionally name a DRI for the separate global-hotkey resolution bug (cmd->ctrl vs cmd->Super policy + optional per-platform override) so the decoupled item does not go unowned.

**Decision.** Fix the scope boundary of R5 'platform in the data model' / the cross-platform editor: whether it covers only the keymap (keybindings.yaml), or also the global-hotkey/QuakeMode and slash-command keystrokes that persist as Option<Keystroke> in cloud-synced settings.toml. Re-scoped after attack into two separable sub-decisions: (1) the DATA-MODEL serde constraint (non-deferrable, owned by A6-Q3), and (2) the EDITOR/feature scope for the global hotkey (deferrable; the global-hotkey cross-platform divergence is a settings-resolution decision, not a keymap-editor display feature).

**Stakes.** Keystroke is a shared type and the two global-hotkey settings are the only cloud-synced keystroke consumers. The genuinely non-deferrable risk is the silent-reset hazard: the Keystroke STRUCT serde form is a live persisted format with NO #[serde(default)] — the platform-native store (UserDefaults/registry) ALWAYS serializes struct-form (and cloud does too when FeatureFlag::SettingsFile is off), so if the A6-Q3 representation adds a non-defaulted field to the struct, users' synced/locally-persisted global hotkeys silently reset to default. That hazard is owned by A6-Q3 and must be gated regardless of product scope. The product stakes are NARROWER than the brief implied: the synced mac cmd-` global hotkey is NOT unmatchable on Linux — global_hotkey.rs:105-107 registers cmd as SUPER and displayed() renders it as Logo, so its display already matches its behavior. The real divergence is (a) cmd->Super differs from the keymap's cmd->ctrl (cmdorctrl) convention, and (b) Super-combos are frequently intercepted by the Linux/Windows WM — both settings-resolution concerns. So choosing scope wrong risks either (a) over-investing Phase-1 keybindings-editor effort in a cosmetic global-hotkey display that closes no truthfulness gap, or (b) widening R5 into a settings per-platform schema + cloud-sync migration that can reset users' synced hotkeys. This must be fixed before schema/UI work because it sets the blast radius and the dependency spine.

_Constrained by:_
- Keystrokes persist in TWO surfaces: keybindings.yaml stores PersistedTrigger(String) via Keystroke::normalized() (a local file, HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>, with no SyncToCloud registration), while global-hotkey keystrokes persist as Option<Keystroke> — QuakeModeSettings.keybinding and ActivationHotkeyKeybinding. — `app/src/settings/mod.rs:334-338; app/src/terminal/keys_settings.rs:13-50; app/src/keyboard.rs:33,169`
- Both global-hotkey keystroke settings are cloud-synced SyncToCloud::Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes), private:false; keybindings.yaml is not. So cross-platform divergence is concentrated on the synced global hotkey. — `app/src/terminal/keys_settings.rs:14-50`
- DECISIVE CORRECTION to the brief: a mac cmd-based global hotkey synced to Linux is NOT 'unmatchable'. The winit backend maps keystroke.cmd -> Modifiers::SUPER (registers as Super-`), and displayed() renders cmd -> 'Logo' on non-mac (Logo == the Super key). So display and behavior already AGREE on Linux; there is no display-truthfulness gap. The real divergence is that cmd->Super differs from the keymap's cmd->ctrl (cmdorctrl) convention and Super-combos are often intercepted by the WM — a settings-resolution problem, not a keymap-editor display problem. — `crates/warpui/src/windowing/winit/delegate/global_hotkey.rs:99-110; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:1024-1029`
- The Keystroke STRUCT serde form is a live persisted/synced format with NO #[serde(default)]: the platform-native store (UserDefaults/registry) ALWAYS serializes the struct (settings/lib.rs:493-498), and cloud receives struct-form when FeatureFlag::SettingsFile is off (manager.rs:224-250). Adding a non-defaulted field to Keystroke (A6-Q3 option a) silently resets these synced/persisted settings to default. This hazard is unconditional for the platform-native store regardless of cloud rollout state. — `A6-Q4 (answers doc:2103-2148); crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-354; crates/settings/src/manager.rs:224-250`
- Slash-command divergence is a THIRD construct, PerPlatformKeystroke{mac,linux_and_windows}, used only as COMPILE-TIME DEFAULTS collapsed at construction via OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() in FixedBinding::new_per_platform — NOT a user-persisted settings surface. User customizations of slash commands flow into keybindings.yaml like any other keymap binding. So the global hotkey is the ONLY genuinely separate synced/persisted keystroke surface. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:488-508; app/src/search/slash_command_menu/static_commands/bindings.rs:11-29`
- The global-hotkey settings store a SINGLE resolved Option<Keystroke> with no per-platform field, so a per-platform OVERRIDE (e.g. ctrl on Linux instead of Super) needs a new settings schema — but cross-platform DISPLAY of the one stored value is derivable from a displayed_for(target_os) helper with NO schema change. Inclusion in 'show both platforms' is therefore cheap; inclusion in 'edit/override per platform' is the expensive part. — `app/src/settings/mod.rs:334-338; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:998-1030`

**Options:**

- **Keymap-only (defer the settings surfaces) — as written in brief** _(low effort · easy)_ — R5 scope = Keystroke usage in the keymap + keybindings.yaml + matcher/displayed/validators only. Global-hotkey/QuakeMode and slash-command keystrokes keep current single-resolved behavior and are explicitly OUT of 'show/edit both platforms'. No serde constraint imposed on A6-Q3.
  - 👍 Smallest blast radius and fastest ship. keybindings.yaml is not synced, so if A6-Q3 also avoids touching the Keystroke struct, this slice is migration-free.
  - 👎 As WRITTEN it omits the load-bearing guard: if A6-Q3 picks the cmd_or_ctrl-on-Keystroke struct field (its option a), 'keymap-only scope' is a fiction — the struct change unavoidably hits the two synced + UserDefaults-persisted Option<Keystroke> settings and, without #[serde(default)], silently resets them. The defect is not the scope, it is the absence of the serde constraint. Option D fixes exactly this.
- **All Keystroke-backed surfaces (unified model + unified editor)** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — One platform-aware Keystroke model; the cross-platform editor shows AND edits keybindings.yaml, global-hotkey/QuakeMode, and slash-command bindings, all per-platform.
  - 👍 The only option that puts every keystroke a user sees, including the synced global hotkey, in one place with consistent per-platform editing — no second platform model maintained in parallel, and it directly enables a per-platform override (ctrl-on-Linux instead of Super) which is the actual global-hotkey fix users want.
  - 👎 Widens scope into settings serialization + a cloud-sync migration: the two Option<Keystroke> settings need a per-platform schema they lack today, plus migration of already-synced struct-form values. Slash-command per-platform bindings are compile-time defaults, so making them user-editable per-platform is a separate persistence project. Pulls settings into Axis 2's record schema. Highest risk of resetting synced hotkeys if the migration is botched. Most of this is low-value relative to cost given the global-hotkey display is already truthful.
- **Type-coherent model, staged editor (global hotkey in Phase-1 DISPLAY) — brief's original recommendation** _(high effort · easy)_ — Declare the platform DATA MODEL covers all Keystroke-backed surfaces; force A6-Q3 to keep the Keystroke struct serde-stable; stage the editor: keybindings.yaml editing first, include the global hotkey in cross-platform DISPLAY from Phase 1, defer inactive-platform editing and slash-command editing.
  - 👍 Honest about the shared-type reality (touching Keystroke touches the synced settings) and keeps everything in one user-facing surface, so a user never sees an editor that conspicuously omits the one synced binding. The serde-additive constraint is correct and non-negotiable.
  - 👎 Its distinctive Phase-1 add — cross-platform DISPLAY of the global hotkey as 'the highest-truthfulness win' — does not survive the code: on Linux the global hotkey already registers as Super (global_hotkey.rs:105-107) and displays as Logo (keymap.rs:1024-1029), so its display already MATCHES its behavior; there is no truthfulness gap to close by displaying it. Worse, if Phase 1 tries to display it as ctrl-` (the keymap convention) to look 'more correct', that display would NOT match the Super-` that actually fires — a NEW truthfulness regression. So the Phase-1 global-hotkey work is either cosmetic or risks lying. The genuinely valuable global-hotkey fix (cmd->ctrl vs cmd->Super resolution, per-platform override, WM-conflict avoidance) is a settings-layer decision this option does not deliver.
- **Keymap-only editor + serde-additive constraint on A6-Q3 + global-hotkey routed to a separate settings fix (ADDED — decouple)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Two separable outputs. (1) DATA-MODEL constraint (non-deferrable): hand A6-Q3 the hard requirement that the canonical representation keep the Keystroke STRUCT serde-additive — carry the platform pair at Trigger level, or use additive #[serde(default)]/Option — so the synced + UserDefaults-persisted global-hotkey settings are never silently reset. (2) EDITOR scope: keymap-only for Phase 1 (matches everyone's agreement that global-hotkey editing and slash-command persistence are expensive/low-value). (3) Route the global-hotkey cross-platform divergence (cmd->Super vs cmd->ctrl, WM conflicts, no per-platform override) to a SEPARATE, named settings-resolution bug, not the keybindings editor. A cheap displayed_for(os) dual-platform readout of the single stored hotkey value can be added later with no schema change, but it is not load-bearing for Phase 1 because the existing display already matches behavior.
  - 👍 Captures option C's only load-bearing element (the serde-additive constraint) at option A's effort, while discarding C's illusory display work. It is honest about the code: the global hotkey's display is already truthful, and its real problem is a resolution-policy mismatch fixable in the settings layer independently of the keymap editor and independently of the Axis 2 record schema. It keeps Phase 1 schema-free, does not fork into two platform models, and puts the real fix where it can actually change matching behavior (per-platform override / cmd->ctrl resolution) rather than a cosmetic editor readout.
  - 👎 Creates a settings-UX seam: keymap bindings show both platforms while the global hotkey shows one (option C's steelman). Decoupling risks the separate settings bug falling through the cracks, so the 'cross-platform keybindings' product could still launch visibly omitting the single synced binding — mitigated only if the settings fix is a named, tracked item in the overhaul's definition-of-done. Like every option except A-as-written, it still HARD-depends on A6-Q3 actually honoring the serde-additive constraint.

**→ Recommendation: Keymap-only editor + serde-additive constraint on A6-Q3 + global-hotkey routed to a separate settings fix (ADDED — decouple).** The only non-deferrable code constraint is the silent-reset hazard: any change to the Keystroke STRUCT touches the two cloud-synced + UserDefaults-persisted global-hotkey settings, and a non-defaulted field resets them (A6-Q4; keymap.rs:320-354; manager.rs:224-250). That constraint is owned by A6-Q3, so A6-Q2's substantive job is to hand A6-Q3 that requirement and set the editor scope to keymap-only for Phase 1 — both options C and D do this and it is the part a skeptical staff engineer must sign. Where C and D diverge is C's claim that cross-platform DISPLAY of the global hotkey is 'the highest-truthfulness win' and belongs in Phase 1. The code falsifies that: on Linux the global hotkey already registers as Super (global_hotkey.rs:105-107) and displays as Logo (keymap.rs:1024-1029), so display already matches behavior — there is no truthfulness gap, and an attempt to display it as the 'ctrl' convention would actually mismatch the Super-` that fires. The genuine global-hotkey problem (cmd->Super vs the keymap's cmd->ctrl convention, Super-combos swallowed by the WM, and no per-platform override) is a settings-resolution/storage decision, not a keymap-editor display feature, and is best filed as a separate named settings bug. D therefore delivers C's load-bearing element at lower effort, keeps Phase 1 schema-free and migration-free, avoids forking into two platform models, and routes the real fix to the layer that can change matching behavior. Note the decision is largely deferrable: the global-hotkey INCLUSION sub-decision can wait, while the serde-additive constraint (the only thing that blocks safe Phase-1 work) is registered against A6-Q3 now.

- _Residual risk:_ Even with the 'display is already truthful' correction, the global hotkey IS the one cloud-synced binding, and decoupling its fix into a separate settings track (D) risks it never shipping — leaving the 'cross-platform keybindings' product launched while visibly omitting the single synced binding, the exact partial-editor embarrassment the brief warned about. Option C guards against this by forcing the global hotkey into Phase 1; D relies on org discipline. Mitigation: make the separate global-hotkey resolution bug a named, tracked item in the overhaul's definition-of-done, not an unowned backlog entry. Second, the highest-severity failure mode survives every option: D (like B and C) depends on A6-Q3 actually honoring the serde-additive constraint — if engineering picks the flat cmd_or_ctrl struct field and forgets #[serde(default)], the synced + UserDefaults-persisted global hotkeys silently reset to default. This must be a gating checklist item with a round-trip migration test (old struct-form payload -> deserialize -> value preserved), independent of which scope option is chosen.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by A6-Q3 (canonical Keystroke representation): a struct field forces the synced settings into scope and mandates #[serde(default)] or a migration; a Trigger-level {mac,other} pair leaves the struct — and thus the synced settings — untouched, giving clean keymap isolation. Coupled to A6-Q1 (show vs edit). Built on A6-Q4 (blast radius — the serde struct-form hazard). NOT gated by A6-Q9/A6-Q10 (record schema) for Phase 1, since a keymap-only editor plus a display-only global-hotkey readout need no new settings schema; full per-platform global-hotkey OVERRIDE would pull in a settings schema (option B). The separate global-hotkey resolution fix is coupled to A6-Q7 (cmd->ctrl vs cmd->meta/Super lossiness) and surfaces a real existing inconsistency: the codebase already has TWO cmd-mappings — cmdorctrl->ctrl in the keymap vs cmd->SUPER in the global-hotkey backend (global_hotkey.rs:105-107).
- _What would settle it:_ (1) The A6-Q3 eng spike: does the chosen representation force a Keystroke STRUCT change? If yes, the serde-additive constraint (or a versioned migration) is mandatory; if it is a Trigger-level pair, the struct is untouched and keymap isolation is clean. Plus the current FeatureFlag::SettingsFile rollout state — determines whether struct-form is already live in the cloud, though struct-form is unconditionally live in the platform-native store regardless. (2) A product decision on global-hotkey RESOLUTION POLICY: should a mac cmd-hotkey resolve to ctrl (the keymap cmdorctrl convention) or Super (current global-hotkey backend behavior) on Linux/Windows, and should the setting gain a per-platform override? This is the real global-hotkey fix and is independent of the keybindings editor. (3) Support/telemetry: how many users have a CUSTOM (non-default) global hotkey AND sync across mac + a non-mac platform — sizes the priority of the separate settings bug and whether the option-B per-platform override is worth building. (4) A UX decision on whether a settings page that shows keymap bindings cross-platform but the global hotkey single-platform (D's seam) is acceptable, or whether a cheap displayed_for(os) dual readout should be added.
- _Red-team added option:_ Keymap-only editor + serde-additive constraint on A6-Q3 + global-hotkey divergence routed to a separate settings-layer fix (decouple)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code_grounding fact against source. The silent-reset hazard (Keystroke struct serde, no #[serde(default)], always struct-form in UserDefaults/registry) is REAL and is the one non-deferrable constraint — upheld (answers doc A6-Q4:2103-2148; keymap.rs:320-354). The two-surface, synced-global-hotkey, and slash-command-is-compile-time-default facts all check out. BUT I falsified the brief's central PREMISE: it calls the synced global hotkey 'unmatchable/broken' and treats cross-platform DISPLAY of it as 'the highest-truthfulness win.' Reading the backend: global_hotkey.rs:105-107 maps keystroke.cmd -> Modifiers::SUPER on Linux/Windows, and displayed() (keymap.rs:1024-1029) renders cmd -> 'Logo' on non-mac. SUPER is the Logo/Super key, so a mac cmd-` synced to Linux is MATCHABLE (registers as Super-`) and its display (Logo-`) already MATCHES its behavior. There is no display-truthfulness gap to close. The genuine global-hotkey problem is a resolution-policy mismatch (cmd->Super here vs cmd->ctrl/cmdorctrl in the keymap) and WM interception of Super — a settings-layer fix, not a keybindings-editor display. This collapses option C's distinctive Phase-1 value-add. Added option D (decouple): keymap-only editor + hand A6-Q3 the serde-additive constraint + file the global-hotkey resolution divergence as a SEPARATE settings bug. D keeps C's only load-bearing element at lower effort and routes the real fix to the layer that can fix it. Also clarified that A6-Q2's substantive output is just (a) a one-line serde constraint owned by A6-Q3 and (b) editor-scope=keymap-only — so the global-hotkey INCLUSION sub-decision is largely deferrable, the serde constraint is not.

### A6-Q10 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team. The keybindings initiative lead (product) owns the split-vs-monolith sequencing and the ruling on whether the L7 'edit both platforms' editor is a committed deliverable or a stretch. Co-decided with the eng-lead of warpui_core/keymap (owner of Keystroke/Trigger/parse/normalized/displayed/matcher and of the A6-Q12 read rule + A6-Q2 representation spike) and the eng-lead of the settings-view/keybindings-UI team (owner of keyboard.rs on-disk schema and the Phase-2 cross-platform editor). The Axis 2 (R3b record schema) owner must ratify that R5's full pair is parented onto R3b and that R3b's field set includes the platform field.

**Decision.** Whether to SPLIT R5 "platform-in-data-model" into a cheap Phase-1 portability slice — the A6-Q12 back-compat READ/resolution rule that makes the common cmd<->ctrl case portable with NO new on-disk representation — shipped alongside Axis 1, and a Phase-2 full {mac,other} pair + cross-platform editor parented on Axis 2's R3b record schema; rather than (a) the brief's heavier Phase-1 round-trippable-cmdorctrl WRITE-representation, (b) a single monolithic R5 deferred entirely behind R3b, (c) cmdorctrl-only with the editor dropped, or (d) front-loading R3b. The label correction itself — Axis 6 is "independent" only for the cmd<->ctrl minority, while its expressive majority is physically gated behind Axis 2's schema — is settled by code (keyboard.rs:169 single-value-per-name; report:231) and is upheld under every option.

**Stakes.** A sequencing call that decides whether a shippable cross-platform portability foundation lands early or is over-serialized behind the engine work, and it corrects a mislabel ("Axis 6 independent") that propagates through the whole initiative timeline. The sharpened stakes: the cheapest path to the portability headline ("drop a colleague's config and it just works") is a read/resolution-rule change (A6-Q12), not a new representation — so the real risk is building TOO MUCH in Phase 1 (a cmdorctrl write-representation that prematurely commits the undecided A6-Q2 choice and forces R3b to special-case a transitional encoding), or building too little (serializing a near-free read-rule win behind months of layer/context/conflict engine work). Compounding the uncertainty: the divergence-site counts measure APP DEFAULTS, not user customizations, so whether even the cheap slice is worth Phase-1 cycles is telemetry-gated. Get the Phase-1 content wrong and you either entrench a throwaway encoding or tease a portability promise the product cannot honestly keep for the majority of divergent bindings until the Phase-2 editor exists.

_Constrained by:_
- cmdorctrl is the ONLY pure cmd<->ctrl swap and is resolved at PARSE time via OperatingSystem::get() (cmd=true on Mac else ctrl=true); the Keystroke struct has only {ctrl,alt,shift,cmd,meta,key} and NO cmdorctrl/platform field, so the unresolved intent is discarded on parse. — `keymap.rs:914-920, keymap.rs:320-328 (verified)`
- The flat file is one-value-slot-per-name: CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger(String)>). The only write path From<UserDefinedKeybinding> emits Keystroke::normalized() (resolved cmd-/ctrl- only, no cmdorctrl branch); the read path TryFrom uses Keystroke::parse (accepts cmdorctrl). So the flat file can READ a hand-authored cmdorctrl token but cannot round-trip-WRITE it, and cannot hold a {mac,other} pair at all. — `keyboard.rs:169,174,176-213; keymap.rs:970-993 (verified)`
- REFRAME: the 'drop a colleague's config and it just works' portability win for the common cmd<->ctrl case is primarily a READ/resolution-rule change, NOT a write-representation change. Existing files already store resolved cmd-d/ctrl-d; porting them requires reinterpreting bare resolved cmd-X/ctrl-X as platform-agnostic on load (today is_binding_cross_platform flags cmd-on-non-mac as invalid). This needs no Keystroke field, no Trigger marker, and no schema, and round-trips symmetrically. — `A6-Q12 (keyboard.rs:176-187, keymap.rs:914-919); bindings.rs:918-933 is_binding_cross_platform`
- Round-trip-WRITING an explicit unresolved cmdorctrl token (vs only reading) needs a representation change whose form is UNDECIDED per A6-Q9 residual: a Keystroke cmd_or_ctrl field changes a struct whose derived serde is cloud-synced + UserDefaults-persisted (QuakeModeSettings.keybinding, ActivationHotkeyKeybinding) so any field must be #[serde(default)]/Option; a Trigger-level marker avoids that but adds plumbing through UserDefinedKeybinding/editor. The brief's claim that the Trigger marker 'sidesteps the blast radius' is spike-pending, not settled. — `A6-Q9 residual item 3 (undecided); A6-Q4 (answers:2146-2147); keymap.rs:320 (Keystroke derives Serialize/Deserialize)`
- The 45 cmdorctrl + 101 with_mac + 56 with_linux (=157 full-pair) + ~140 cmd_or_ctrl_shift counts are APP-DEFAULT registration call sites (compiled-in builders), NOT entries in any user's keybindings.yaml. They settle the CANONICAL-MODEL choice (full pairs needed for defaults, A6-Q5) but do NOT measure user-portability coverage, which is a telemetry question. The brief's 'slice covers only ~45 of ~200+ sites -> users get a partial story' conflates the two. — `A6-Q9/A6-Q5 verified greps (keymap.rs:688-713; bindings.rs:863-888)`
- The full {mac,other} pair is physically impossible on the flat file and is gated behind Axis 2's R3b record schema; the inactive platform's keystroke is never constructed (with_mac/with_linux NOOP; new_per_platform discards the other side; OperatingSystem::get() compile-time fixed). A real cross-platform editor must store both sides and additionally needs platform-parameterized conflict detection (A6-Q11), a target-OS test seam that does not exist (A6-Q14), and a displayed_for refactor over 21 callers (A6-Q15). Report:231 states R5 is 'independent but a hard prerequisite for any cross-platform binding editor'; report:270 places R5 in the deferred bucket. — `keymap.rs:688-713,497-508; platform/mod.rs:668-683; report:146,231,270 (verified)`

**Options:**

- **Read-rule-first split (Phase-1 A6-Q12 portability rule, no new representation; Phase-2 representation + full editor on R3b)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Ship ONLY the A6-Q12 back-compat read/resolution rule in Phase 1 alongside Axis 1: on load, treat a bare resolved cmd-X/ctrl-X as platform-agnostic so a colleague's config ports for the common cmd<->ctrl case, with NO change to the on-disk representation, the Keystroke struct, or the write path. Defer the choice of unresolved representation (A6-Q2: Keystroke field vs Trigger marker), the full {mac,other} pair, and the cross-platform editor to Phase 2 on Axis 2's R3b record schema.
  - 👍 This is the cheapest path to the exact headline value the Split was justified by. The portability promise is a read-time reinterpretation (existing files already hold resolved cmd-/ctrl-, A6-Q12), so it needs none of the write-representation machinery the brief's slice commits to: no Keystroke serde change (A6-Q4 risk avoided entirely, not merely 'sidestepped'), no UserDefinedKeybinding/editor plumbing, no A6-Q2 decision, and crucially no transitional encoding that R3b must later special-case. It is symmetric and round-trip-stable (mac cmd-d <-> Linux ctrl-d). It preserves the L7 end-state (beats drop-editor), does not over-serialize (beats Monolith), keeps maximal freedom to pick any Phase-2 schema (most reversible), and the divergence-site counts (which describe defaults, not user files) do not undercut it because user customizations plausibly skew toward the cmd<->ctrl case the read rule fully covers.
  - 👎 A read rule is a HEURISTIC: it cannot distinguish an intentional mac-only or genuine Linux meta/super binding (A6-Q7 lossiness) from a portable one, and it silently changes resolution semantics for existing users' files without an explicit migration UI, so a rare user who truly wanted super-d on Linux is broken. It delivers NO new user-visible capability in Phase 1 (no editor, no 'see both platforms'), so the win is invisible-by-design and hard to validate or market. If telemetry shows users rarely share configs across OSes, even this cheap rule is low-value and the cycles are better spent on the engine. The escape hatch for genuine super bindings, if required, pulls representation forward and erases the cost gap with the brief's heavier slice.
- **Round-trippable-cmdorctrl-representation split (the brief's original recommendation)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Ship a round-trippable cmdorctrl WRITE-representation in Phase 1 via a Trigger-level platform marker (so the flat file can persist an unresolved cmdorctrl-X token), and defer the full {mac,other} pair + editor to Phase 2 on R3b.
  - 👍 Distinguishes 'intentionally platform-agnostic' from 'happened to be cmd' that the read-rule heuristic conflates, and gives the editor a real unresolved token to write later, validating the representation early. Two halves match the code's two cost profiles.
  - 👎 Prematurely commits the UNDECIDED A6-Q2 choice (A6-Q9 residual item 3) and asserts the Trigger-marker sidesteps the A6-Q4 cloud-synced serde blast radius as if settled, when it is spike-pending and the marker carries its own UserDefinedKeybinding/editor plumbing. Creates a transitional on-disk encoding that the Phase-2 R3b record must special-case and migrate (A6-Q12 double-format). Spends more than read-rule-only to deliver the SAME headline portability, since the value is in the read rule not the write token. If the spike shows a Keystroke field is needed, blast radius balloons into QuakeMode settings.
- **Monolith deferred (all of R5 in Phase 2 behind R3b)** _(high effort · costly)_ — Treat R5 as one unit; build cmdorctrl + full pair + editor together in Phase 2 on R3b. Nothing platform-related ships in Phase 1.
  - 👍 One schema, one migration, one editor, one review; no transitional encoding to invent and migrate away from. The editor is the real deliverable, and shipping anything half-portable arguably teases a promise the product cannot fully keep until the editor exists.
  - 👎 Needlessly serializes a near-free read-rule portability win (no schema change at all) behind all of Phase 2's layer/ContextPredicate/conflict engine work, delaying any cross-platform value to the back half. Read-rule-first dominates it: same no-double-format property PLUS an early cheap win. Bundles a low-risk change with high-risk schema work.
- **cmdorctrl-only, drop/indefinitely-defer the editor** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship a cmdorctrl representation for portability and do NOT commit to the full {mac,other} pair or the 'edit both platforms' editor (L7) in this initiative.
  - 👍 Lowest commitment to the end-state: delivers the common-case portability without touching R3b, the matcher hot path (A6-Q16), platform-parameterized conflicts (A6-Q11), the test seam (A6-Q14), or displayed_for (A6-Q15). If telemetry shows most customizations are pure cmd<->ctrl, captures most value for a fraction of effort.
  - 👎 Abandons the R5 end-state: the majority genuine-divergence bindings never get a portable representation or any cross-platform editing, and 'show/edit both platforms' is silently shelved against the stated North Star. Still spends on a cmdorctrl representation that read-rule-only avoids, so it is the worst of both: pays representation cost AND drops the editor. cmd->ctrl synthesis is lossy for real Linux meta/super (A6-Q7).
- **Pull R3b forward, build the full editor early (monolith-early)** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Declare the cross-platform editor a Phase-1 priority and pull R3b forward to build the full {mac,other} representation, displayed_for, platform-parameterized conflicts, and the target-OS test seam up front.
  - 👍 If the editor is the single most-valued deliverable, this gets it to users soonest and avoids any throwaway transitional encoding; forces the hardest schema/test-seam questions (A6-Q11, A6-Q14) to be answered with full context.
  - 👎 Inverts the dependency spine: R3b is coupled to context-scoped overrides (R3a/R3b) and conflict detection (R6), and the editor additionally needs platform-parameterized conflicts (A6-Q11), a target-OS injection seam that does not exist (A6-Q14), and a displayed_for refactor over 21 callers (A6-Q15). Front-loads the riskiest, most-coupled work, forecloses Phase-1 de-risking, and makes the initiative's first deliverable its hardest.

**→ Recommendation: Read-rule-first split (Phase-1 A6-Q12 portability rule, no new representation; Phase-2 representation + full editor on R3b).** unused

- _Residual risk:_ The Monolith's 'teases a promise / heuristic risk' argument applies with full force to read-rule-only and is the strongest surviving counter. A read rule that silently reinterprets bare cmd/ctrl as platform-agnostic (a) cannot distinguish a genuine Linux meta/super binding or an intentional mac-only override from a portable one (A6-Q7 lossiness), and (b) changes resolution semantics for existing users' files with no explicit migration UI — so a user who truly wanted super-d on Linux is silently broken. It also ships NO new user-visible capability in Phase 1 (no editor, no 'see both platforms'), making the win invisible-by-design and hard to validate or market. If the A6-Q7 ruling demands an escape hatch for genuine super bindings, that requires representation and erases the cost advantage over the brief's heavier slice. And if telemetry shows users rarely load cross-OS configs at all, the honest move is to defer ALL of R5 (Monolith) and spend Phase-1 cycles on the engine — at which point the read rule is a low-value distraction, not a de-risking win.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by A6-Q9 (flat file cannot carry a full pair -> full pair needs Axis 2's R3b schema). A6-Q12 is now the LOAD-BEARING Phase-1 dependency (the read/resolution rule IS the portability win), not a mere phase-bridge. A6-Q2 (representation choice: Keystroke cmd_or_ctrl field vs Trigger-level marker) is DEFERRED to a Phase-2 spike under this recommendation, NOT decided in Phase 1 as the brief had it. A6-Q4 (cloud-synced QuakeModeSettings.keybinding serde blast radius) binds ONLY if a Keystroke-field representation is chosen — read-rule-first avoids it entirely. A6-Q7 (cmd->ctrl synthesis is lossy for real Linux meta/super) bounds the read rule's correctness and may force an escape-hatch representation. A6-Q13 (is_binding_cross_platform validator contract under the new read rule). The Phase-2 editor half is gated by A6-Q1 (edit vs read-only inactive platform), A6-Q11 (R6 must become platform-parameterized for honest inactive-platform conflicts), A6-Q14 (target-OS test injection seam), A6-Q15 (displayed_for refactor over 21 callers). Gates the R3b schema field set (must include platform) and corrects the end-goals 'Axis 6 independent' label.
- _What would settle it:_ Four inputs. (1) Telemetry on USER customizations specifically (not app defaults): (a) what fraction of entries in real keybindings.yaml / synced configs are pure cmd<->ctrl vs genuine key/chord divergence, and (b) how many users ever load a config authored on a different OS. If cross-OS sharing is near-zero, defer all of R5 (Monolith); if it is common and skews cmd<->ctrl, the read rule captures nearly all value cheaply. (2) A6-Q2/A6-Q12 spike: confirm the read rule alone delivers acceptable, round-trip-stable portability with NO representation change, and — only if a representation is still wanted — whether a Trigger-level marker round-trips a cmdorctrl token WITHOUT touching the cloud-synced Keystroke serde (A6-Q4) and is forward-compatible with R3b. (3) A6-Q7 ruling: is silently mapping bare cmd->ctrl on non-mac (losing the meta/super distinction) acceptable, or must Phase-1 preserve an escape hatch for genuine super bindings (which pulls representation forward and erases the read-rule cost advantage)? (4) Initiative-owner ruling on whether the 'edit both platforms' editor (L7) is committed or a stretch — this keeps or removes the drop-editor option.
- _Red-team added option:_ Phase-1 read-rule portability only (A6-Q12 back-compat resolution rule, no new representation); defer representation choice (A6-Q2) + full {mac,other} editor to Phase 2
- _Red-team:_ The brief's core framing — R5 is two changes with two cost profiles welded under one mislabeled "independent" tag, and should be split — is fully grounded and survives (verified report:231, keyboard.rs:169-213, keymap.rs:914-920/320-328/688-713, platform/mod.rs:668-683). I hardened the RECOMMENDATION, not the framing. Five attacks: (1) MISSING OPTION — the brief's own value attribution betrays it: the "drop a colleague's config" win for the common cmd<->ctrl case is delivered by A6-Q12's back-compat READ/resolution rule (reinterpret a bare resolved cmd-X/ctrl-X as platform-agnostic on load), NOT by inventing a round-trippable cmdorctrl WRITE-representation. Read-rule-only ships the same headline portability at strictly lower cost (no Keystroke field, no Trigger marker, no A6-Q2 commitment, no transitional encoding R3b must migrate away from) and is symmetric/stable on round-trip (mac writes cmd-d -> Linux reads ctrl-d -> saves ctrl-d -> mac reads cmd-d). I added it as the new recommended Phase-1 content. (2) GROUNDING CORRECTION — the brief leans on "45 of ~200+ sites" to argue users get "a partial story," but 45/157/140 are APP-DEFAULT registration call sites (compiled in), not what a user's keybindings.yaml contains; user-portability coverage is a telemetry question, not derivable from these counts. Those counts inform the CANONICAL-MODEL choice (A6-Q5: defaults need full pairs), not user value. (3) OPTIMISM — the brief asserts a "Trigger-level platform marker sidesteps the cloud-synced serde blast radius (A6-Q4)" as settled; A6-Q9 residual (item 3) lists Keystroke-field-vs-Trigger-marker as UNDECIDED and spike-pending, and a Trigger marker still adds plumbing through UserDefinedKeybinding/editor. Demoted the brief's "round-trippable cmdorctrl representation" Phase-1 slice for prematurely committing A6-Q2 and creating a double-format. (4) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY — made A6-Q12 explicit as the LOAD-BEARING Phase-1 dependency, not a mere phase-bridge. (5) DEFERABILITY — the representation choice (A6-Q2) and full editor are deferrable to Phase 2 without blocking Axis 1; the read rule is the only thing that needs to land early, and even it is telemetry-gated. Verified the supposedly-decisive A6-Q4 serde risk only bites if a Keystroke FIELD is chosen — read-rule-only sidesteps it entirely.

### A6-Q11 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team. The warpui_core engine lead owns R6's API shape (whether target_os is in the signature from the first commit), the {mac,other} representation (A6-Q2), the A6-Q14 test seam, AND the newly-surfaced question of whether platform-conditional FixedBinding registration (is_mac()/is_linux() guards, #[cfg(target_os)] gates) must be de-gated into always-registered platform-tagged bindings to close the residual honesty gap. The Settings/UI eng lead owns ConflictMap/R6 wiring in app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs and the inactive-display caveat UX. Product owns the truthfulness bar: whether 'conflicts checked for bindings present on both platforms, with an explicit caveat for OS-only bindings' is acceptable for v1, or whether fully honest cross-platform conflicts (requiring registration de-gating) are a launch requirement.

**Decision.** Decide whether R5's 'edit/show both platforms' cross-platform editor requires R6 (conflict detection) to take an explicit target-OS parameter — resolving and conflict-checking the inactive platform's bindings — as a hard prerequisite, or whether the editor ships with current-OS-only conflict feedback. Reframed by the attack into a two-part question: (a) the cheap design-now decision — does R6's API shape get a target_os parameter reserved at design time so it is not a retrofit; and (b) the expensive build decision — when (and whether) the inactive-platform conflict resolution is actually implemented, given it depends on storing both platform sides (A6-Q2), is Phase-2 work, and CANNOT be made fully honest by the param alone because some bindings are registered only on one platform.

**Stakes.** This sets the dependency spine, but the attack narrows where the real cost lives. Locking the param into R6's signature at design time is cheap and prevents an expensive retrofit later (R6 is still unbuilt, report:257). The expensive part — materializing the inactive side (A6-Q2 record schema, pulling in Axis 2) and, for TRUE honesty, de-gating platform-conditional registration — is Phase-2 and is NOT required to ship Phase 1 (report:244-251 contains no cross-platform editing). If we defer the param entirely and later add cross-platform editing, R6 must be re-cut to thread target_os: a real retrofit. If we over-build now, we pay for inactive-side storage and registration de-gating before measuring whether the conflict gap is even non-trivial. The truthfulness stake is real but bounded: even a fully built target_os ConflictMap leaves a residual gap (collisions against other-platform-only bindings like the mac-only character palette), so 'honest inactive conflicts' is achievable only for the shared binding set unless registration is also de-gated — a fact that must be stated honestly rather than claimed away. Critically: this is a Phase-2 implementation decision whose ONLY now-cost is a one-line API-shape commitment; it does not gate Phase 1.

_Constrained by:_
- ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize> keyed on the bare RESOLVED current-OS Keystroke; has_conflict = count>1 (lines 123-128); update() only inc/decrements counts; it is context-blind and never gates save (purely advisory, confirmed independently by A5-Q4). — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133`
- R6's planned model re-keys conflicts on (Keystroke, context-group) using each binding's STATIC context predicate/group (EditableBindingLens.context, keymap.rs:311; BindingGroup, bindings.rs:797) — NOT live runtime context. R6 is explicitly Phase-2, item 4, still unbuilt. — `report:165 and report:257`
- Conflict detection is context-blind and scope is not engine-exposed: ConflictMap is populated from binding.trigger only and EditableBindingLens.context is private, so making conflicts context-aware (R6) is an engine change. This A5-Q2 cost is R6's cost regardless of platform and must NOT be re-counted as a platform-param cost. — `A5-Q2`
- cmdorctrl resolves at parse time via OperatingSystem::get(), compile-time-fixed on native with no runtime override; the inactive platform's Keystroke is never constructed/parsed/stored, so it cannot be fed into ConflictMap. cmdorctrl is the ONLY pure cmd<->ctrl swap; the majority of divergence (cmd_or_ctrl_shift ~140 sites, with_mac/with_linux 157 sites) is genuine key/chord divergence whose inactive side must be STORED to be resolvable. — `A6-Q5`
- DECISIVE, MISSED BY BRIEF: whole bindings — not just keystrokes — are registered only on one platform via runtime is_mac()/is_linux() guards around register_fixed_bindings: mac-only `ctrl-cmd-space -> ShowCharacterPalette` (462-472) and Linux-only `ctrl-e -> EmacsBinding` (475-483). On a single-OS build the other platform's binding object is never constructed, so a target_os ConflictMap that re-resolves the CURRENT build's registered bindings still misses collisions against other-platform-only bindings. The param does NOT make inactive conflicts 'exactly as honest as current-platform'; closing the residual gap requires de-gating these (and any #[cfg(target_os)]) registrations into always-registered platform-tagged bindings. — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:462-483`
- The conflict signal is advisory only and the edit/save path (confirm_keystroke_editing -> set_custom_keybinding -> set_custom_trigger) runs no validation or conflict gate today; there is no existing release-path validation machinery to extend per-platform. Note: is_binding_cross_platform already exists (bindings.rs:918-933, debug-only) and could be a cheaper per-OS-safety enforcement than full inactive-conflict display. — `A5-Q4 and A6-Q13`

**Options:**

- **Reserve R6 target_os seam now; stage the build (measure-then-implement, honest caveat) [RECOMMENDED, added by attack]** _(low effort · easy)_ — Make the cheap design-now decision only: commit that R6's API will accept a target_os parameter and key on the resolved (target-OS Keystroke, static context-group) so it is not a retrofit. Do NOT build inactive-side resolution until the measurement spike (what_would_settle_it #2) quantifies the collision gap on the SHIPPED keymap, including collisions against other-platform-only bindings. Ship the cmdorctrl-mirror Phase-1 slice (Option 3) for early cross-platform value. In Phase 2, implement inactive-OS keystroke resolution for the shared binding set, and ship an HONEST caveat ('conflicts checked for bindings present on both platforms; <OS>-only bindings shown but not cross-checked') for the residual gap that the param provably cannot close without de-gating platform-conditional registration.
  - 👍 This captures Option 1's only real benefit — no expensive R6 retrofit — at the cost of a one-line API-shape commitment, while refusing to pay for A6-Q2 record-schema storage, registration de-gating, and inactive resolution before the spike proves the gap is non-trivial. It is the only option whose truthfulness claim is defensible: it tells the user exactly what is and is not cross-checked, instead of the brief's overstated 'exactly as honest as current-platform.' It is deferral-safe (nothing in Phase 1 is blocked, report:244-251) yet retrofit-safe (param reserved at design time). It dominates Option 1 because it yields the identical no-retrofit guarantee with strictly less committed effort and an honest stance on the residual gap.
  - 👎 Requires discipline to actually reserve the seam in the R6 design doc now rather than discovering it in Phase 2. The honest caveat is a UX admission of incompleteness that Product may dislike. If the spike shows the gap is large AND dominated by other-platform-only bindings, the caveat is not enough and you are pushed toward the expensive registration de-gating anyway — this option does not escape that, it just defers and measures it. Still depends on A6-Q2 (store both sides) and A5-Q2 (R6's own context exposure) for the Phase-2 build.
- **Platform-parameterized R6, build it as a hard prerequisite (full)** _(high effort · costly)_ — Design AND build R6 to take target_os: resolve each binding's stored {mac,other} pair (A6-Q2) to the target-OS Keystroke and bucket with R6's static (Keystroke, context-group) key. Treat the platform-parameterized R6 as a hard prerequisite for the full 'edit both' editor, coupling Axis 6 to Axis 5 Phase 2.
  - 👍 The question's 'cannot evaluate runtime context' objection is half a strawman: R6 compares STATIC scope, not live context (A5-Q2/report:165), so the impossible piece is not part of R6. Target-OS keystroke resolution from the stored pair is achievable and catches the named failure (two mac bindings that collide only after mac resolution, viewed from Linux). Because R6 is unbuilt, the param is incremental, not a retrofit.
  - 👎 Effort is HIGH, not medium: it requires R5 to carry both platform sides first (A6-Q2, pulls in the Axis-2 record schema), the A6-Q14 test seam, and — to actually deliver the 'honest' promise — de-gating platform-conditional registration (editor/view/mod.rs:462-483 and similar) so other-platform-only bindings are enumerable; without that, the build still cannot see collisions against mac-only/linux-only bindings, so its central claim 'exactly as honest as current-platform conflicts' is OVERSTATED. Kills the 'Axis 6 independent' label and reorders the spine. Over-commits before the spike measures whether the gap is non-trivial.
- **Read-only inactive display, current-OS conflicts only (defer entirely)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship R5 view-only for the inactive platform with a 'conflicts checked on <current OS> only' caveat; leave ConflictMap current-OS, do not parameterize R6.
  - 👍 Fastest; no dependency on unbuilt R6 or the A5-Q2 refactor; keeps Axis 6 independent. Most users edit on the OS they run; the inactive column is informational; the caveat is honest about what it checks.
  - 👎 Editing a cmdorctrl or current-side binding can still create an inactive-platform collision invisible on the current OS — the exact regression the question warns about. It also leaves R6 with no target_os seam, so enabling inactive editing later forces a real R6 retrofit (the cost the recommended option avoids for one line). Its main hole (cmdorctrl edits) is exactly what Option 3 plugs, so it is dominated by 'Option 3 + reserved seam.'
- **cmdorctrl-mirror Phase-1 slice (no separate inactive conflict map)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Restrict Phase-1 cross-platform editing to the pure cmdorctrl-mirror case: the inactive side is a deterministic cmd<->ctrl relabel, so the existing current-OS ConflictMap covers it with a small cmd/ctrl key-equivalence check. Defer genuine-divergence conflicts to platform-parameterized R6 in Phase 2.
  - 👍 Genuinely cheap: ships on the flat file (A6-Q9) with no R6 change, aligns with the A6-Q10 split, proves the spine without paying for the hard case. For pure-cmdorctrl bindings the two conflict graphs are the same graph with cmd<->ctrl relabeled.
  - 👎 Covers only the ~31-34% pure-swap minority (A6-Q5); cmd_or_ctrl_shift (~140 sites) and with_mac/with_linux (157 sites) cannot be edited cross-platform under this slice. cmd and ctrl are different Keystroke keys (keymap.rs:320-328), so the 'isomorphic' claim needs an equivalence check. Sharpened by the attack: the isomorphism holds ONLY on the binding set SHARED by both platforms — mac-only/linux-only bindings (editor/view/mod.rs:462-483) exist on one graph and not the other, so even the cmdorctrl slice cannot claim a complete inactive conflict graph. Good Phase-1 value, not the end state.
- **Live per-platform runtime-context conflict (full fidelity) [REJECT]** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Evaluate the inactive platform's live runtime context too, so conflicts reflect that platform's actual UI/focus state.
  - 👍 Maximal truthfulness; zero fidelity gap between platforms.
  - 👎 Not buildable on a single-OS native build: OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time-fixed (A6-Q5) and live UI/focus state for an OS you are not running cannot be simulated. R6 deliberately uses static scope, not live context (A5-Q2). Conflates static scope with runtime state; burns effort on an unreachable target.

**→ Recommendation: Reserve R6 target_os seam now; stage the build (measure-then-implement, honest caveat).** Literal answer to the question: YES, the FULL 'edit both platforms' editor needs R6 to carry a target_os parameter — but the attack splits this into a cheap now-decision and an expensive deferrable build, and corrects the brief's overstated honesty claim. Lock NOW only the cheap, retrofit-avoiding part: reserve target_os in R6's API shape (present in the signature from R6's first commit, defaulting to current-OS) so Phase-2 R6 is not re-cut later (R6 is unbuilt, report:257). Defer the expensive part — inactive-side storage (A6-Q2, pulls in the Axis-2 record schema) and resolution — to Phase 2, gated on the measurement spike, because nothing in Phase 1 (report:244-251) is blocked by it. Ship the cmdorctrl-mirror slice (Option 3) for early cross-platform value; it also plugs Option 2's main hole. Reject Option 1-as-written: it over-commits relative to its own staged rationale and rests on an overstated claim. The decisive correction: a target_os ConflictMap can only re-resolve bindings that EXIST in the current build, but whole bindings are registered only on one platform via runtime is_mac()/is_linux() guards (verified at editor/view/mod.rs:462-483: mac-only ShowCharacterPalette, Linux-only EmacsBinding), so collisions against other-platform-only bindings are invisible regardless of the param — the same platform-fixity A6-Q5 proves for keystrokes, now proven for whole bindings. Therefore 'honest inactive conflicts' is achievable only for the shared binding set unless every platform-conditional registration is also de-gated (a large, separate change). The honest end-state is param (close the resolvable majority) + explicit caveat (be truthful about the residual platform-only gap), not a false 'exactly as honest as current-platform' promise. Reject Option 4 as unbuildable. The A5-Q2 engine refactor is R6's cost regardless of platform and is not a marginal platform-param cost.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument: a one-line API-shape 'reservation' is only as good as the team's discipline to honor it — if R6 is actually designed and built in Phase 2 without re-reading this decision, the reservation buys nothing and you pay the full retrofit anyway, which is exactly the cost Option 1's 'hard prerequisite' framing was trying to force. A skeptical staff engineer could argue the only reliable way to prevent the retrofit is to make the param non-optional in R6's first commit (Option 1's design half), even while deferring the build. That is a fair point and the recommended option accommodates it: 'reserve the seam' means the target_os parameter is present in R6's type signature from its first commit (defaulting to current-OS), not merely a doc note. Second residual risk: if the spike shows the conflict gap is dominated by other-platform-ONLY bindings rather than re-resolvable shared bindings, then neither the param nor the cmdorctrl slice helps, and the honest caveat may be judged an unacceptable truthfulness regression by Product — pushing the cost back up toward de-gating all platform-conditional registration, which the staged option defers but does not eliminate.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by A6-Q2 (canonical representation must carry BOTH platform sides, or there is no inactive-OS keystroke to resolve). Shares R6's engine cost A5-Q2 (expose context-group) — this is R6's cost regardless of platform, not a marginal platform-param cost. Sequencing gated by A6-Q9/A6-Q10 (flat-file cmdorctrl Phase-1 slice vs record-schema full-pair Phase-2). Softened by A6-Q1 (view-only inactive display lowers the bar; full inactive editing hardens it). Requires A6-Q14 (target-OS test seam to assert inactive conflicts on single-OS CI). Sibling to A6-Q15 (displayed_for(target_os), same parameterization pattern). NEW dependency surfaced by the attack: for the conflict feedback to be more than resolvable-subset honest, depends on de-gating platform-conditional binding REGISTRATION (editor/view/mod.rs:462-483 and similar runtime is_mac()/is_linux() guards) so other-platform-only bindings are enumerable on a single-OS build — otherwise an explicit caveat is mandatory. Related: A6-Q13/R10 (the existing cross-platform validator could enforce per-OS binding safety on the edit path more cheaply than full inactive-conflict display). This decision blocks NOTHING in Phase 1 (report:244-251).
- _What would settle it:_ Three inputs. (1) Product fixes the truthfulness bar: is 'conflicts checked for bindings present on both platforms, inactive shown read-only with an explicit caveat for OS-only bindings' acceptable for v1, or must inactive conflicts be fully honest (which requires de-gating platform-conditional registration)? (2) A measurement spike on the SHIPPED default keymap, EXPANDED by the attack: walk every binding, resolve for the NON-current OS, and count (a) collisions that exist only after inactive-OS resolution among bindings present on BOTH platforms — these the param fixes — and SEPARATELY (b) collisions against bindings registered only on the other platform (via is_mac()/is_linux() guards or cfg gates) — these the param CANNOT fix without registration de-gating. If both counts are ~0, Option 2/3 + reserved seam is safe; if (a) is non-trivial, build the param in Phase 2; if (b) is non-trivial, the honest-caveat-vs-de-gating tradeoff goes to Product.
