(3) Confirm the cheap commitment: R6's first-commit type signature carries a target_os parameter (defaulting to current-OS), validated through the A6-Q14 seam by asserting two mac-resolved bindings collide when viewed from a Linux build — proving the seam exists without yet building the full inactive resolution.
- _Red-team added option:_ Reserve the R6 target_os seam now; stage the build (measure-then-implement, honest caveat for the residual gap)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code_grounding fact against live code and the settled-answers doc — all six are real and not overstated (ConflictMap at keybindings.rs:104-133; R6 static (Keystroke,context-group) at report:165 + Phase-2 placement at report:257; cmdorctrl-only-swap / inactive-never-stored at A6-Q5; advisory-only/no-save-gate at A5-Q4). The brief's core literal answer (full 'edit both' needs R6 to carry a target_os param) survives.

But I found a DECISIVE fact the brief missed that punctures its strongest claim. The brief asserts Option 1 makes inactive-platform conflicts 'exactly as honest as current-platform conflicts.' FALSE. The same compile/runtime platform-fixity that A6-Q5 proves for KEYSTROKES also applies to WHOLE BINDINGS: bindings are registered behind runtime is_mac()/is_linux() guards (and #[cfg(target_os)] gates), so the other platform's binding OBJECTS are never constructed in a single-OS build. Verified example: app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:462-483 registers mac-only `ctrl-cmd-space -> ShowCharacterPalette` and Linux-only `ctrl-e -> EmacsBinding`. A target_os ConflictMap that re-resolves the CURRENT build's registered binding list to the other OS still cannot surface a collision against `ctrl-cmd-space` on a Linux build, because that binding is not in the Linux process at all. So Option 1 closes the resolvable gap (re-resolving bindings present on both platforms) but leaves a residual honesty gap (other-platform-ONLY bindings) that the param alone does NOT close — that needs de-gating every platform-conditional registration into always-registered, platform-tagged bindings, a much larger change. This raises Option 1's true effort from 'medium' to 'high' and corrects its overstated steelman.

DEFERABILITY: re-read report §7 — R5, R6, and the full-pair editor are ALL Phase 2 (report:253-258); Phase 1 (report:244-251) is R1/R7/R8/R10/R9-partial and contains NO cross-platform editing. So this decision blocks NOTHING in Phase 1. The only thing worth locking NOW is reserving the param in R6's API shape (design-readiness is now even though R6 implementation is Phase 2); the build is deferrable and should be gated on the measurement spike. The brief's recommended OPTION (Option 1, 'hard prerequisite, build it') overcommits relative to its own staged RATIONALE (ship cmdorctrl slice first, gate on spike). I named that staged option and made it the recommendation.

Also corrected: A5-Q2's engine refactor (expose context-group) is R6's cost REGARDLESS of platform, so it must not be double-counted as a platform-param cost; the param's MARGINAL cost is target_os threading + the A6-Q2 stored-pair dependency + (for true honesty) registration de-gating. Sharpened Option 3: its 'isomorphic graph' claim holds only on the binding set SHARED by both platforms; mac-only/linux-only bindings break the isomorphism, the same gap as above.

---

## Cross-axis — Cross-axis / sequencing

### X2 — FLIPPED · _cross-team_ · owner: Primary (option C, do now): the keybindings/keymap-engine tech lead (owns app/src/keyboard.rs) — C is a self-contained loader bug-fix requiring NO cross-team sign-off, which is part of why it is preferred. The settings-UI/import-export owner is informed (their Phase-1 R7 ships over the flat map). Escalation to records-now (A-family) is owned by Product via the X3 cross-platform-v1 ruling, and only then requires sign-off from the Axis-2 context-schema owner (ContextPredicate representation), the Axis-6 platform owner (populated platform field + Keystroke/write-path changes), and the settings-UI/import-export co-sign — none of which should gate Phase 1.

**Decision.** Whether Phase-1 keybindings work must commit the Phase-2 RECORD file schema ({name, trigger, context?, platform?}, context/platform reserved-and-None in v1) plus a duplex back-compat deserializer from day one, OR ship the Phase-1 quick wins over today's flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> while (a) hardening the loader against the silent-reset + truncate-clobber data-loss path now and (b) deferring the record schema to Phase 2, where Axis 2's ContextPredicate grammar and the X1 one-primitive spike have settled and the flat→record migration ships once, version-detected and safe.

**Stakes.** keybindings.yaml is the one artifact that is real, shared (import/export) user data, and the most expensive thing to change twice. Two distinct risks are conflated in the original brief and must be separated:

(1) DATA-LOSS (must fix now, every option): read_custom_keybindings returns None on BOTH NotFound and parse-error (conflated, keyboard.rs:129,132-138); startup load applies triggers only inside if-let-Some (silent reset, :37-57); the write-back path does unwrap_or_default + create_file→fs::File::create which TRUNCATES (:71,89,103-122; util/file.rs:143). This is a LIVE bug today: a hand-edit YAML typo silently resets the keymap at next launch, and the very next in-app edit permanently clobbers the file. No test guards it (keyboard_tests.rs covers only conversions). This must be fixed before Phase 1 regardless of the schema decision.

(2) FORWARD-COMPAT (the actual X2 debate, deferable): whether to commit the record container now so Phase-1-window export files never need migration. The catch: the legacy-flat reader is mandatory whenever records land (A1-Q12), because the installed base already has flat files; committing the record envelope before Axis 2 has chosen the ContextPredicate representation (no FromStr/Deserialize path exists; identifiers are &'static str — A6-Q9) and before X1 settles the one-primitive question risks shipping a v1 record that itself needs migration. Get THIS wrong and you commit to a context/platform grammar Axes 2/6 have not designed.

_Constrained by:_
- On-disk schema is a flat top-level YAML map: CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>) and PersistedTrigger(String) with only plain serde derives (no version field, no untagged, no alias, no hand-written Deserialize), so any record-shaped change makes serde_yaml fail on legacy files. — `keyboard.rs:167-174 (verified); A1-Q12`
- The data-loss path is real and LIVE today, not merely an upgrade artifact: read returns None on parse-error AND NotFound (conflated); startup load applies only inside if-let-Some (silent reset); write/remove do unwrap_or_default and save_custom_keybindings calls create_file→fs::File::create which truncates. A hand-edited YAML typo therefore silently resets the keymap, and the next in-app edit permanently clobbers the whole file. No test guards this. — `keyboard.rs:37-57,71,89,103-122,128-139 (verified); util/file.rs:132-145 (verified create_file→fs::File::create truncates); keyboard_tests.rs:1-79`
- The flat format ALREADY supports multi-keystroke chords (space-separated string, documented), so chord capture (R8) writes through the existing PersistedTrigger with NO schema change. Combined with hot-reload reset being an in-memory applied-names set and import/export serializing the existing flat map, NONE of the Phase-1 writers require a record schema — every Phase-1 win ships over the flat map. — `keyboard.rs:160-161,166,180 (verified); report L245-249 Phase-1 items`
- The whole pipeline (UI edit→persist→load→apply) is name-keyed end to end; the flat file is isomorphic to what the engine can represent; there is NO file-vs-UI capability gap. A per-context override cannot even be CREATED today because update_custom_trigger is per-name and rewrites all N same-name registrations. So the record schema buys forward-compat ONLY, not any v1 capability. — `A1-Q9 (keymap.rs:28-30,422-433; keyboard.rs:169) — verified`
- The legacy-flat back-compat deserializer is MANDATORY under every option because the installed base already has flat files; ‘Axis 2 inherits this even if Axis 1 ships only the watcher over today's flat map.’ Therefore committing the record schema in Phase 1 (A) does not AVOID the reader — it only front-loads it, before X1 and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar exist (no FromStr/Deserialize; identifiers are &'static str). — `A1-Q12 _Unblocks_ (verified); A6-Q9 (context grammar undefined)`
- The full {mac,other} platform pair physically cannot ride the flat string AND a record envelope with platform reserved-and-None delivers no cross-platform capability either — that requires a POPULATED platform field plus Axis-6 Keystroke/write-path changes (normalized() emits resolved ctrl-/cmd- only; cmdorctrl is read-only). So even for a cross-platform-v1 product mandate, the brief's specific A (reserved-None) is a half-measure; the real requirement is the heavier records+populated-platform variant. — `A6-Q9 (keyboard.rs:169,174,180; keymap.rs:320-328,914-920,970-993) — verified`
- The hot-reload ‘applied custom names’ set is IN-MEMORY bookkeeping (does not exist yet; seed point lib.rs:2557), not an on-disk field. It is name-keyed in Phase 1 and must become name+context in Phase 2 once Axis 2 lands — gated on the engine (update_custom_trigger needs a context selector, A1-Q9), NOT on the file schema. So a record file with context=None does not let the Phase-1 applied-set be name+context; A buys nothing for X8 in Phase 1. — `A1-Q9; X8 readiness; report L245; lib.rs:2557 — verified`

**Options:**

- **A: Ship the record schema now (forward-compatible v1)** _(high effort · one-way)_ — Define the canonical R3b record envelope ({name, trigger, context?, platform?}, context/platform reserved-and-None in v1) plus a duplex/untagged back-compat deserializer that reads today's flat entries as records. Build every Phase-1 writer/reader against it. Commit only the ENVELOPE; leave the context/platform grammar opaque for Axis 2/6.
  - 👍 The on-disk CONTAINER becomes a stable contract decoupled from the engine refactor, so files exported during the Phase-1 window are already record-shaped and never need a container migration. If product is committed to the cross-platform north-star, a record container is a prerequisite regardless and doing it once on a clean schema is cleaner than under live-data pressure later. One commitment nominally unblocks Axes 1/2/5/6 file work simultaneously.
  - 👎 Buys ~zero v1 capability (A1-Q9, A6-Q9 — engine cannot express context or platform), so you pay container cost for no behavior. The legacy-flat reader is mandatory anyway (A1-Q12), so A does not avoid it — only front-loads it before X1 and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar exist (no FromStr/Deserialize; &'static str identifiers). If the envelope guesses wrong (Axis-2 needs nested predicate structure, or X1 rules a layer-based not name-based primitive), you ship a v1 record that itself needs migration = the file changes TWICE, the exact failure the premise warns against. Untagged map-vs-record discrimination is an unproven serde unknown (A1-Q12 residual). With platform reserved-and-None it does NOT deliver cross-platform import either. Effectively one-way: exported files propagate the schema to other users.
- **B: Ship flat now, migrate when Axis 2/5/6 land (defer, unguarded)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Keep the flat map for all Phase-1 wins; switch to records with a one-time migrate-on-load when the engine schema work lands in Phase 2 — without committing to harden the loader first.
  - 👍 Cheapest Phase-1 path; matches the report's literal phasing (R3b in Phase 2, L256). Axis 2 settles the context-predicate representation by then, so the schema is designed against real requirements rather than guesses.
  - 👎 This is the trap A1-Q12 documents AND it leaves the live data-loss bug unfixed in the meantime. Unless Phase-2 ships BOTH the duplex deserializer AND the absent-vs-parse-error split, upgrade silently resets every user and the first edit truncate-clobbers the file. The defect is doing nothing to the loader NOW while the hand-edit-typo footgun is already live. Dominated by C, which is B plus the mandatory hardening done early.
- **C: Harden the loader now, keep flat for Phase 1, records land once in Phase 2 (recommended)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Make ZERO on-disk format change in Phase 1. Immediately fix the two hazards: split absent vs parse-error at keyboard.rs:129/132-138, and never unwrap_or_default-then-truncate a present-but-unparseable file (so a typo or future-unknown schema can never silently empty the file). Ship all Phase-1 wins over the flat map. In Phase 2, when Axis 2's ContextPredicate grammar is settled, introduce records + a top-level version discriminant + the mandatory legacy-flat duplex reader in ONE migration.
  - 👍 Removes the real risk — catastrophic, LIVE data loss — for ~10 lines, today, with no schema commitment. Changes the on-disk format exactly ONCE (in Phase 2, against known requirements), honoring the expensive-file premise better than A (which risks two changes). A top-level version tag added in that single Phase-2 migration makes the legacy/record discrimination explicit and sidesteps A's untagged-ambiguity unknown. Fully reversible (pure loader fix). Does not block any Phase-1 win — the writers all serialize the flat map, and chord capture already round-trips multi-keystroke strings. Mandatory under every option anyway, so do it now and stop there until Axis 2 forces the schema's real shape.
  - 👎 Files exported during the Phase-1 window are flat and need the (safe, version-detected, deterministic) flat→record migration when records land — undercutting a maximal reading of the import/export ‘win,’ though same-platform import/export works fully in Phase 1. Does not deliver cross-platform portability in v1 (neither does A-reserved-None). Leaves the X8 applied-set-key/file-key alignment to Phase 2 (where it is gated on the Axis-2 engine regardless). If product later mandates cross-platform-v1, C must be escalated to records+populated-platform, incurring the schema change then.
- **D: Split the decision (decompose-and-sequence)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Recognize X2 as two questions: X2a = fix the catastrophic loader (decide now, do now — unblocked, mandatory); X2b = record-envelope timing/shape (defer the DECISION itself to resolve jointly with X1's one-primitive spike and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar, both of which X2 is already declared to depend on).
  - 👍 The brief treats X2 as one atomic call that must precede Phase 1, but only X2a must. X2b cannot be answered correctly before X1 and Axis-2 resolve (the envelope should match the one-primitive structure and the real context grammar), so forcing it now invites a wrong guess. Splitting lets Phase 1 proceed immediately with the data-loss fix while the genuinely-premature sub-decision waits for its inputs.
  - 👎 Requires discipline to actually revisit X2b when X1/Axis-2 land (calendar a hard gate), else it silently becomes unguarded B. Adds one tracking item. Functionally D is the sequencing wrapper around C; it is not a different on-disk outcome.

**→ Recommendation: C: Harden the loader now, keep flat for Phase 1, records land once in Phase 2 — sequenced per D (split X2a do-now / X2b defer-to-Axis-2).** Three verified facts flip this from A to C. (1) The decision is DEFERABLE: none of the Phase-1 writers need a schema change — hot-reload reset is an in-memory applied-names set, import/export serializes the existing flat map, and chord capture writes into the flat format that already supports multi-keystroke (keyboard.rs:160-161,166). So nothing in Phase 1 is blocked by deferring records. (2) A buys ~zero over C: it adds no v1 capability (A1-Q9, A6-Q9), and the legacy-flat back-compat reader it relies on is mandatory under every option anyway (A1-Q12), so A only front-loads that reader — before X1 and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar exist — while its sole marginal payoff (forward-compat of Phase-1-window export files) is redundant with the mandatory reader. (3) The brief's own premise (the file is the most expensive thing to change twice) argues against A: A commits an envelope blind to Axis 2 and risks a SECOND change if it guesses wrong; C changes the on-disk format exactly once, in Phase 2, against known requirements, and is easily reversible. The genuinely urgent item is C's hardening, because the silent-reset + truncate-clobber is a LIVE bug today (hand-edit typo → permanent file loss), not just an upgrade artifact. So harden now (C), defer the record schema to Phase 2 where it is designed against real Axis-2 requirements with a version discriminant for a clean detected migration. A is dominated unless product issues a cross-platform-v1 mandate (X3) — and even then the answer is NOT the brief's reserved-None envelope but a heavier records+populated-platform variant requiring Axis-6 work, which is X3/Axis-6's call to escalate, not X2's to pre-commit. B is dominated by C (same defer, but leaves the live bug unfixed).

- _Residual risk:_ If product commits to the cross-platform north-star (X3 = ‘drop in a colleague's config and it just works,’ end-goals L54) as a v1 must-have, then a platform-bearing record container becomes a prerequisite and committing it early (A-family) would save a coordinated container+engine change in Phase 2; under C that change is bundled into the Phase-2 migration. Note this scenario does NOT validate the brief's specific A (reserved-None platform delivers no cross-platform behavior) — it requires the heavier records+populated-platform+Axis-6 variant — so even here the brief's recommendation is not the right instrument. Secondary residual: the Phase-2 legacy/record discrimination could be ambiguous to serde_yaml (the open A1-Q12 unknown); C neutralizes this by adding an explicit top-level version tag in the same Phase-2 migration, but that tag and the duplex reader are still unwritten and untested today.
- _Dependencies:_ Option C is gated by NOTHING — it is a pure loader hardening plus a deferred Phase-2 migration, and it unblocks all Phase-1 work over the flat map. The record-schema escalation (A) is gated by: X1 (one-primitive vs three — the envelope must match the chosen primitive), the Axis-2 ContextPredicate FromStr/Deserialize grammar (R3a — defines the reserved context field), Axis-6 platform model (A6-Q9 — defines the platform field and the Keystroke/write-path change needed for any real cross-platform value), X3 (cross-platform import/export — the only thing that can force records into Phase 1), and X8 (applied-set key, which is engine-gated on Axis 2 regardless of file schema). The documented A1-Q1/R3b scope contradiction (end-goals L52-53 places R3b in Axis 1; report L256 places it in Phase 2) is resolved by C in favor of the Phase-2 placement, with the loader hardening pulled forward into Phase 1.
- _What would settle it:_ (1) The X3 product decision is the real pivot, not a secondary gate: if v1 import/export is explicitly same-platform-only (label it so), C is the correct floor and records stay in Phase 2; if cross-platform-v1 is a must-have, escalate — and scope it as records+populated-platform+Axis-6, not the reserved-None envelope. (2) A throwaway test reproducing the live hand-edit-typo data-loss path (typo the file → launch → in-app edit → confirm the file is truncated to one binding) — settles that C's hardening is urgent-now and must land before any Phase-1 writer regardless of X2's schema outcome. (3) Resolution of X1 (one-primitive spike) and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar — the inputs X2b is genuinely waiting on; only after these can a record envelope be committed without risking a second migration. (4) A serde spike proving an explicit top-level version discriminant cleanly separates legacy-flat from versioned-record in serde_yaml — needed for the Phase-2 migration under C anyway (the A1-Q12 residual), and the safer alternative to A's unproven untagged per-entry discrimination.
- _Red-team added option:_ D: Split the decision — harden the loader now (X2a, unblocked), defer the record-envelope choice (X2b) to resolve jointly with X1 and the Axis-2 context grammar
- _Red-team:_ All five code-grounding facts are real and verified against source (keyboard.rs:128-139,71,89,103-122; util/file.rs:143 create_file→fs::File::create truncates; A1-Q9, A6-Q9, A1-Q12 confirmed). The catastrophe is real. But the brief's RECOMMENDATION (A: ship the record envelope now) is over-recommended; I flip it to C. Four attacks land:

1) DEFERABILITY (decisive): The brief's stakes claim "every Phase-1 writer serializes the file format, so this must be decided before any of them land" is false in the load-bearing sense. None of the Phase-1 writers need a schema change. Hot-reload reset is an IN-MEMORY applied-names set (not on-disk). Import/export (R7) serializes the existing flat map. Chord capture (R8) writes a space-separated multi-keystroke string into the EXISTING PersistedTrigger(String) — the flat format already documents and supports multi-keystroke (keyboard.rs:160-161, example "cmd-shift-D escape" :166). So every Phase-1 win ships over the flat map. The decision IS deferable past Phase 1; the only thing that must happen before Phase 1 is C's loader hardening.

2) A buys ~ZERO over C. The brief's own grounding (A1-Q9, A6-Q9) says the engine cannot express context or a {mac,other} platform pair in v1, so A's reserved-None envelope delivers no new capability. Its only marginal benefit is forward-compat of files exported during the Phase-1 window — but the legacy-flat back-compat reader is MANDATORY under every option anyway (A1-Q12 _Unblocks_: "Axis 2 inherits this even if Axis 1 ships only the watcher over today's flat map"), because every existing user already has a flat file. A does not avoid that reader; it only front-loads it. The flat format is already frozen/known, so writing the reader in Phase 2 (C) is no harder than now — A's "pay it on greenfield" advantage is rhetorical.

3) The expensive-file premise argues AGAINST A, not for it. "The file is the most expensive thing to change twice" ⇒ commit the LEAST until requirements are known. A commits a record envelope NOW, before X1 (one-primitive spike) and the Axis-2 ContextPredicate grammar are settled — risking the very "ship a v1 record that itself needs migration" failure the brief admits (2 changes). C changes the on-disk format exactly ONCE, in Phase 2, against known requirements. Reversibility also favors C strongly: A is effectively one-way (exported files propagate the schema to other users — a shared artifact), C is a pure loader fix with zero on-disk change.

4) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY / live bug. (a) A is gated on X1 and Axis-2 grammar ratification — both unresolved — so "ship the envelope now" cannot be executed correctly before them; C is gated on nothing. (b) The "catastrophic failure" is not only an upgrade artifact: it is a LIVE data-loss bug today — a user who hand-edits keybindings.yaml and makes a YAML typo gets silent-reset on next launch, and the next in-app edit hits unwrap_or_default→truncate and permanently clobbers the whole file. That makes C's hardening urgent NOW independent of X2's outcome. Also corrected: even if product wants cross-platform-v1, A-as-specified (platform reserved-and-None) does NOT deliver it — that needs a POPULATED platform field plus Axis-6 Keystroke/write-path changes (A6-Q9), i.e. a heavier option than the brief's A.

### X3 — HARDENED · _product_ · owner: Product owner for the keybindings initiative sets the user-facing portability promise (tagged user-product-decision), co-signed by the Settings-UI eng lead who owns the import/export surface, the file-picker/path-parameterized I/O, the same-platform label, and the import-warning copy. The Axis-6 / platform-in-data-model owner is consulted (confirms whether the cmdorctrl slice is cheap enough to reconsider). Crucially, because A' makes NO on-disk encoding commitment, the export-envelope/provenance question is handed entirely to the X2 schema owner — it is NOT a Phase-1 cross-team commitment under A' (it would be under brief-Option-A). The X2 owner additionally owns the legacy-tolerant duplex deserializer that Phase-2 import must reuse for long-lived Phase-1 exports.

**Decision.** What portability promise Phase-1 import/export (R7) is allowed to make, and what — if anything — it commits on the export file format. Either: (A') ship import/export as a literal reuse of the flat keybindings.yaml format, label it same-platform-only, warn on cross-OS import, and make NO on-disk encoding commitment — forward-compat is delegated to the legacy-tolerant deserializer X2 must build for the installed base anyway; (A) same, but additionally write a provenance tag into an export envelope; (B) pull the cmd-or-ctrl slice of R5 forward so the common cmd-d/ctrl-d swap ports cross-OS; or (C) defer import/export entirely until the Phase-2 record schema (R3b/X2) + R5 land. The portability-promise half (same-OS vs cross-OS) must be decided before Phase-1 code lands; the on-disk-encoding half is deferrable to X2 and should not be pre-committed.

**Stakes.** Import/export is billed as a flagship Phase-1 quick win and the north-star is "drop in a colleague's config and it just works" (end-goals L54). But the file persists RESOLVED current-OS strings, so a mac export imported on Linux is literally cmd-d — a chord Linux rarely emits and that the debug validator flags invalid (bindings.rs:918-933). Shipping silently makes the headline promise false cross-OS: mixed-team users get dead bindings and file bugs while the feature "looks done." Two corrections to the framing: (1) The "quick win" is thinner than billed — for personal same-OS backup it is largely a discoverable wrapper over copying keybindings.yaml; its genuine added value is the named shareable artifact plus an import UI that surfaces conflicts/validation. (2) The forward-compat exposure is real but mis-located: ANY flat-format export is forward-incompatible with the Phase-2 R3b record schema, but so is every existing user's keybindings.yaml, so Phase 2 must ship a duplex/migrating deserializer regardless (A1-Q12). The decision therefore sets the user-facing promise and decides whether Phase 1 pre-commits an export encoding (it should not) — not whether Phase 2 inherits a migration (it inherits one anyway).

_Constrained by:_
- R7 is sequenced as a Phase-1 quick win whose stated implementation is 'Add Import/Export buttons reading/writing keybindings.yaml via the existing keyboard.rs:95-139 save/read functions' — no schema/engine change assumed. The north-star it serves is 'Export / import a keymap; drop in a colleague's config and it just works (R7)'. — `report:167; end-goals L54`
- The persisted format is a flat top-level HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> with only plain serde derives (no version/untagged/alias field); export of an arbitrary user-chosen file is not literally keyboard.rs:95-139, which hardcode keybinding_file_path(). A provenance tag added as a top-level YAML key deserializes as a BINDING named the tag and is then fed to Keystroke::parse and fails — so a machine-readable tag requires an envelope struct, a reserved key, or a serde-dropped comment, i.e. it is NOT free and is not byte-identical reuse. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:95-97,167-174,189-211`
- DECISIVE / brief missed it: because the flat file has no version field, the Phase-2 R3b record schema cannot deserialize ANY legacy flat file, and the entire installed base already has flat keybindings.yaml, so a duplex/migrating deserializer is MANDATORY for Phase 2 regardless of X3. Therefore a Phase-1 flat export adds zero new migration burden, a provenance tag does not reduce Phase-2 work, and the real forward-compat obligation is that Phase-2 IMPORT reuse that legacy-tolerant deserializer — else a long-lived Phase-1 export imported on a Phase-2 build hits parse-failure-None (conflated with absent) and the next in-app edit truncates the file, permanently clobbering it. — `A1-Q12 (app/src/keyboard.rs:129,132-138,37-57,71,89,103-122; app/src/util/file.rs:132-145)`
- The import warning that Option A proposes to 'reuse' is ONE-DIRECTIONAL: is_binding_cross_platform returns Yes immediately on mac and flags No only for cmd-on-non-mac. So it catches a mac export imported on Linux, but a Linux export (ctrl-d) imported on a mac is unflagged — a wrong-but-live binding (ctrl-d valid on mac) that no validator catches. The function itself is release-callable; only its default-validator install is debug-gated. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933; A6-Q13`
- The flat file physically cannot encode a full {mac,other} pair (one value slot per name); only a narrow cmdorctrl slice fits, it covers the minority of divergence sites (45 cmdorctrl vs 101 with_mac + 56 with_linux full-pair), and even the slice needs a write-path/Keystroke change because cmdorctrl is resolved at parse and never re-emitted by normalized(). — `A6-Q9 (app/src/keyboard.rs:169; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919,970-993; counts 101/56/45)`
- On read, a mac-written 'cmd-d' parses to a fully-resolved Keystroke{cmd:true} byte-identical to a deliberate mac-only binding, and the flat file carries no platform/provenance tag, so the two are indistinguishable from stored data — any cross-OS resolution needs an imposed back-compat read rule plus migration, which the X2 schema work owns. — `A6-Q12 (app/src/keyboard.rs:169,189-211)`
- R5 (platform-in-data-model) is sequenced as a Phase-2 optional/stretch foundation, explicitly 'a hard prerequisite for any cross-platform binding editor' — so genuine cross-OS portability is unavailable until R5, which Phase 1 does not deliver. — `report:256-258; end-goals L111-113`

**Options:**

- **A' — Literal flat reuse, same-platform-only, one-directional cross-OS warn, NO provenance tag (NEW)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship Phase-1 import/export over the unchanged flat keybindings.yaml format (a file picker plus path-parameterized read/write reusing the existing PersistedTrigger conversion). Export writes the current-OS resolved strings exactly as on disk — no envelope, no tag. The UI labels import as same-platform-only and warns on cross-OS import using is_binding_cross_platform (catches the high-harm mac-export-on-Linux direction) plus a static 'imported from another OS may not work' note covering the reverse direction the predicate misses. Make ZERO on-disk encoding commitment; the canonical encoding and any envelope are decided by X2 when R3b lands, and Phase-2 import reuses the mandatory legacy-tolerant deserializer (A1-Q12) so long-lived Phase-1 exports keep importing.
  - 👍 This is strictly cheaper and better-grounded than the brief's Option A: it deletes the one part of A that does not survive attack (the tag is not free on the flat schema, does not reduce Phase-2 migration because the installed base forces that migration anyway, and prematurely couples X3 to X2). It captures the real same-OS value now, converts the worst silent failure (dead cmd- bindings on Linux) into an explicit labeled limitation, and leaves the export-encoding decision where it belongs — with the X2 schema owner — so Phase 1 commits nothing it must later reconcile. The forward-compat story is concrete and honest: a Phase-1 export is just another flat file the mandatory R3b duplex deserializer already handles.
  - 👎 The north-star 'just works' is downgraded to 'just works same-OS' — a visible gap on mixed teams. The warning is one-directional (a Linux export on mac is unflagged and silently wrong), so the label, not the predicate, is doing the real protective work. Import must NOT blindly write a foreign file through the truncate-prone save path on a future build; the long-lived-artifact obligation (Phase-2 import reuses the legacy deserializer) is a note that must actually be honored in X2, or a Phase-1 export becomes unimportable / clobber-prone later. Same-OS backup is only a thin wrapper over copying the file.
- **A — Same-platform-only v1 WITH export provenance tag (brief's original recommendation)** _(low effort · costly)_ — As A', but additionally wrap the export in an envelope carrying a provenance tag (e.g. exported_platform: macos) so a future R5-aware importer can detect and resolve cross-OS files.
  - 👍 A deterministic source-OS tag is more reliable than heuristic detection (a file with no cmd bindings is platform-ambiguous by heuristic but unambiguous by tag), and putting it in the export envelope rather than the per-binding schema is a smaller commitment than Option B. If X2's envelope is decided first, the tag is a genuinely forward-compatible hedge.
  - 👎 The tag is not free on the flat schema (envelope struct / reserved key / serde-dropped comment — no longer byte-identical reuse, contradicting R7's framing). It does NOT reduce Phase-2 migration: every existing untagged keybindings.yaml forces the migrator to handle untagged-source detection anyway (A1-Q12), so the tag's marginal value is low. It commits an export format before X2 exists, re-introducing — in miniature — the exact forward-incompatibility the brief faults in Option B; if X2 later picks a different envelope, in-the-wild Phase-1 exports must be reconciled. Reversibility is therefore worse than A'.
- **B — cmd-or-ctrl slice of R5 in Phase 1** _(med effort · costly)_ — Pull the cmdorctrl token slice of R5 forward: add a way to persist/round-trip cmdorctrl (a Keystroke field or Trigger-level marker), emit cmdorctrl- for bindings whose only divergence is the primary modifier, and resolve per-OS on import so the cmd-d/ctrl-d swap ports cross-OS.
  - 👍 It attacks the single most common cross-OS case (the primary-modifier swap is exactly what a naive mac->linux import gets wrong) and lets Phase-1 import make a partially-true 'it just works' claim for the keys users most often share. parse already round-trips the token (keymap.rs:914-919), so the read side is nearly free.
  - 👎 Covers only the minority of divergence (45 cmdorctrl vs 101+56 full-pair, A6-Q9) — bindings like alt-left/ctrl-left or cmd-left/home still won't port, so 'it just works' is true for some keys and silently false for others, arguably worse than an honest same-OS label. It forces a back-compat read rule NOW (a bare resolved cmd-d is ambiguous between cmdorctrl and a deliberate mac-only binding, A6-Q12). It introduces a partial on-disk/Keystroke encoding the Phase-2 full {mac,other} record schema (X2) must reconcile and migrate — the exact forward-incompatibility the cross-axis review warns against — and it is no longer a 'no schema change' Phase-1 item.
- **C — Defer import/export to Phase 2 (gate on R5/record schema)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Do not ship import/export in Phase 1. Wait until the R3b record schema (X2) and R5 land so export carries the full {mac,other} pair and import is genuinely portable across OSes.
  - 👍 Only option that can honestly satisfy the L54 north-star and never ships a portability promise the data model can't keep; avoids any throwaway Phase-1 I/O glue or shim.
  - 👎 Throws away a near-free, genuinely-used Phase-1 win (same-OS sharing, backup/restore) that needs none of R5. Weakens the Phase-1 de-risking rationale (independently shippable wins that prove the catalog + I/O path). Couples a small valuable feature to R5, sequenced as Phase-2 optional/stretch (report:256-258) — likely a multi-quarter wait. High opportunity cost for a purity same-OS sharers don't need.

**→ Recommendation: A' — Literal flat reuse, same-platform-only, one-directional cross-OS warn, NO provenance tag.** The flat file physically cannot encode a portable cross-OS binding (A6-Q9: one value slot per name; the full {mac,other} pair needs the Phase-2 R3b schema), so genuine portability is unavailable in Phase 1 no matter the investment — Option B buys only partial truth (45 of ~200 divergence sites) at the cost of a new Keystroke encoding, a forced back-compat read rule (A6-Q12), and a forward-incompatible on-disk format that Phase-2 R5 must reconcile. Option C sacrifices a near-free, genuinely-used win by chaining it to the slowest part of the roadmap. The brief's Option A is the right direction but carries one piece that fails attack: the provenance tag. The decisive fact (A1-Q12) is that the entire installed base already has flat files with no version field, so Phase 2 MUST ship a duplex/migrating deserializer regardless — which means (a) a Phase-1 flat export adds zero new migration burden and (b) the tag does not reduce Phase-2 work because untagged legacy files force untagged-source handling anyway. The tag is therefore not the cheap forward-compatible move it's billed as; it is a non-free schema deviation (a top-level YAML key deserializes as a bogus binding, keyboard.rs:189-211), and committing an export envelope before X2 exists re-creates, in miniature, the very forward-incompatibility the brief faults in Option B. Option A' captures the same real same-OS value, converts the worst silent failure (dead cmd- bindings on Linux) into an explicit labeled limitation, and makes ZERO encoding commitment — the export-format decision is correctly deferred to X2 (the encoding half of X3 is genuinely deferrable; only the same-OS-vs-cross-OS promise must be decided now, and it is cheap). The single concrete forward-compat obligation is that Phase-2 import reuse the mandatory legacy-tolerant deserializer so long-lived Phase-1 exports keep importing without hitting the A1-Q12 truncate-on-edit clobber. Honest answer to X3: Phase-1 import is explicitly same-platform-only with no on-disk format commitment, and that is acceptable precisely because cross-platform requires R5, which Phase 1 cannot deliver anyway.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the one for Option A's tag: a deterministic source-OS marker is genuinely more reliable than heuristic detection for the cross-OS files R5 will eventually want to resolve, and adding it later (after exports are already in the wild, untagged) means R5's importer must support untagged-source detection forever. A' bets that heuristic detection (is_binding_cross_platform / presence of cmd-* plus an explicit 'which OS?' import prompt) is good enough and that the installed base forces that untagged path anyway — true, but it means A' never delivers a clean tagged path even when R5 lands. Second residual: the one-directional warning leaves the Linux-export-on-mac direction silently wrong (ctrl-d overriding expected cmd-d, unflagged by any validator), so on mixed teams the feature's honesty rests entirely on the static label, which power users may dismiss. If telemetry shows cross-OS sharing actually dominates (the opposite of the assumed same-OS-dominant case), A' is the wrong call and Option B's partial cross-OS port — or holding for C — becomes defensible.
- _Dependencies:_ Coupled to X2 (canonical record schema {name,trigger,context?,platform?}) but, under A', only by inheritance: a Phase-1 flat export is just another legacy flat file that X2's MANDATORY duplex/migrating deserializer (forced by the installed base, A1-Q12) must already handle — A' adds no new schema seam. Concrete A1-Q12 obligation to honor: Phase-2 import must route export files through that legacy-tolerant deserializer and must split absent-vs-parse-error so a failed parse of an old export cannot empty-and-truncate the user's file on the next edit (keyboard.rs:103-122; util/file.rs:132-145). Gated by code facts A6-Q9 (full pair needs the record schema), A6-Q12 (bare resolved cmd-d is ambiguous; needs a back-compat read rule — owned by X2, not Phase 1), A6-Q13 (validator is one-directional and debug-install-only). Option B is exactly the A6-Q10 cmdorctrl-slice split. Touches A1-Q14 (cross-platform import) and the Axis-1 killer question (R3b in Phase-1 scope — no). Does not gate Axis 2/3/4 engine work.
- _What would settle it:_ Three concrete inputs. (1) Telemetry: instrument import to record source-platform vs current-platform and measure what fraction of shared/imported keybindings.yaml files are cross-OS vs same-OS — if same-OS dominates (the likely case for personal backup and same-team sharing), A'/A is decisively right and B/C are over-engineering; if cross-OS dominates, reopen B vs C. (2) A throwaway spike: import a real mac keybindings.yaml on a Linux build and count how many bindings land as cmd-* that is_binding_cross_platform flags invalid / that Linux never emits, AND repeat the reverse (Linux file on mac) to quantify the unflagged wrong-but-live direction the warning misses — this sizes both the silent-failure severity and whether the cmd/ctrl slice (B) rescues a meaningful share or a sliver. (3) A 1-day prototype of X2's duplex deserializer fed a Phase-1 flat export, to confirm the load-bearing A' claim that a flat export round-trips through the mandatory legacy path with no extra migration and no truncate-on-edit clobber. The product owner then rules on the promise — 'same-OS portable now, cross-OS when R5 ships' — and explicitly defers the export-encoding/envelope question to X2.
- _Red-team added option:_ Literal flat-file reuse, same-platform-only, one-directional cross-OS import warning, NO provenance tag (forward-compat delegated to X2's mandatory legacy deserializer)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code anchor against source and the answers doc — all of the brief's load-bearing facts hold (A6-Q9 one-slot-per-name 101/56/45; A6-Q12 bare cmd-d ambiguity; A6-Q13 validator; keyboard.rs:167-211; report:167; end-goals L54). The core call (ship same-platform-only v1, defer real cross-OS to R5) is UPHELD. But the recommendation's load-bearing detail — the "cheap forward-compatible provenance tag" — does not survive attack, and I added a decisive fact the brief ignored:

(1) HAND-WAVING on the tag. Option A says the tag is "one metadata line, e.g. exported_platform: macos" over the existing reader. The on-disk format is a flat top-level HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> with plain serde derives (keyboard.rs:167-174). A top-level metadata key deserializes as a BINDING named "exported_platform" whose value is then fed to Keystroke::parse (keyboard.rs:189-211) and fails to parse. So a machine-readable tag is NOT free: it needs an envelope struct (a new serde type, distinct from CustomKeybindings — no longer "reuse keyboard.rs:95-139"), a reserved/special-cased key, or a YAML comment serde silently drops on read. The brief's own what_breaks half-admits this ("no longer byte-identical") but the rationale still leans on it as the key forward-compat move.

(2) MISSED DECISIVE FACT (A1-Q12). The flat file has no version/untagged/alias field, so when Phase-2's R3b record schema lands it CANNOT deserialize ANY legacy flat file — and the entire installed base already has flat keybindings.yaml files. A duplex/migrating deserializer is therefore MANDATORY for Phase 2 regardless of X3. Consequences the brief got backwards: (a) a Phase-1 flat export adds ZERO new migration burden (it is just one more flat file the mandatory migrator already must handle); (b) the provenance tag does NOT reduce Phase-2 work — Phase 2 must support untagged legacy files anyway; (c) the REAL forward-compat obligation is concrete and different: a long-lived Phase-1 export imported on a Phase-2 build hits the A1-Q12 hazard (parse-failure None is conflated with absent, and the next in-app edit truncates the file — permanent loss), so Phase-2 IMPORT must reuse the same legacy-tolerant deserializer. The tag is at best optional sugar and at worst added migration surface and a premature X2 coupling.

(3) OPTIMISM on the warning. Option A says the cross-OS warning "reuses the existing is_binding_cross_platform predicate." That predicate short-circuits to Yes on mac and only flags cmd-on-non-mac (bindings.rs:919-932). So it is ONE-DIRECTIONAL: it catches a mac export on Linux, but a Linux export (ctrl-d) on a mac is unflagged (ctrl-d is valid on mac), silently overriding the mac user's expected cmd-d with a wrong-but-live binding that no validator catches. The "warn cross-OS" claim is only half-true.

(4) Verified the function itself is release-callable (only its default-validator install is debug-gated, A6-Q13), so the import-time warning is feasible.

Net: same direction, cheaper/cleaner variant, one hand-wave fixed, one decisive fact added, what_breaks hardened.

### X4 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team (the readiness doc labels X4 a `cross-team-decision`): the settings-UI owner and the change-keybinding agent-skill owner (Warp AI / agent-platform) jointly define the manifest's stable key, field-stability tiers, versioning story, and whether UI and agent consume the same serialization; the keybindings-engine lead arbitrates the schema (engine introspection produces it) and owns the X11 alias-map dependency. Add: the agent-platform owner additionally owns running the coverage prototype that gates whether the skill ships IN Phase 1.

**Decision.** Define the action-catalog (R9) contract serving both the settings UI and the change-keybinding agent skill. Decide whether Phase-1 ships an explicitly versioned manifest (schema_version field from day one, with a documented FROZEN vs BEST-EFFORT field tier and an agent contract bound only to the editable-name stable key), versus a "v1-is-done" catalog, a full up-front catalog, two separate catalogs, or shipping only the versioned schema now and deferring the published agent skill until a coverage prototype runs.

**Stakes.** The catalog is one contract feeding two consumers with opposite tolerances: the agent needs a frozen, build- and OS-invariant key to map "description -> action id" without guessing; the UI tolerates dynamic descriptions and current-OS triggers. The verified Phase-1 catalog can only be a startup walk of editable_bindings(), context-less and current-OS (CommandBinding drops the ContextPredicate and has no platform field). The three things the agent wants most — stable ids for nameless fixed bindings, per-context scope, and platform variants — only exist after Phases 2-3. The report's own Phase-1 R9 tuple lists a "context" field that provably cannot exist yet, so a "done in Phase 1" framing is false on the roadmap's own terms. Getting this wrong either locks the agent onto an incomplete manifest (forcing a re-key when Phase 2/3 extend it, and silently orphaning agent references when Axis-4 Step-D renames actions in Phase 3), or stalls the cheap Phase-1 catalog behind the ~469-site fixed-id project. The decision sets whether/what the agent skill can truthfully promise in Phase 1 — and the editable-only coverage (how many real "rebind X" requests target Fixed/context/platform keys it cannot serve) is currently UNMEASURED, with X5 suggesting the Fixed surface includes user-facing code-editor navigation.

_Constrained by:_
- CommandBinding has NO scope/context field and NO platform field — fields are name, description, trigger, action, group, id only; both constructors (from_lens, from_editable_lens) drop the ContextPredicate. A Phase-1 catalog is therefore structurally context-less and current-OS-only and cannot represent one-entry-with-N-scopes. Verified by direct read. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541, 749-771 (and A5-Q8)`
- The report's OWN Phase-1 R9 deliverable describes the manifest as a (name, description, group, CONTEXT, default) tuple, but the verified code (A5-Q8) shows the context field cannot exist in Phase 1. The roadmap overpromises a field Phase-1 code can't produce — decisive support for 'the catalog is inherently versioned, not v1-done', and proof the naive 'v1' framing is internally inconsistent. — `report L8/L171/L249 section 7 vs A5-Q8`
- Read-only fixed bindings have NO stable persistable catalog id: as_lens hardcodes name=Default (empty string, keymap.rs:633), the only id is a process-local non-Serialize atomic BindingId (keymap.rs:262-272), synthesis from existing fields is non-viable, and from_lens drops the ~404 description-less fixed bindings. Giving them stable identity is a ~469-constructor-site / 146-register-call / 93-file change — a Phase 2/3 capability, not Phase 1. — `A5-Q3 (keymap.rs:633, 262-272, 277-286, 497-508)`
- The editable `name` is the faithful name-keyed persistence key end-to-end with no file-vs-UI capability gap today — making it a viable frozen agent key NOW for editable (user-rebindable) actions, and it is both build- AND OS-invariant (only the resolved trigger is OS-specific). BUT one name maps to N bindings and update_custom_trigger rewrites ALL N (keymap.rs:422-433); ~9 names carry multiple descriptions and some carry multiple default triggers, so name->trigger is N-valued and the agent cannot target one-of-N contexts — the same limit the UI has. — `A1-Q9 (keymap.rs:30, 422-433; keyboard.rs:169)`
- Search never reads binding.name — the internal action id is unsearchable today, so the agent's name-based description->action mapping does not exist yet and is net-new work, not a free read of the existing catalog. This is the real (medium) cost in A/B/D/eventually-C. — `A5-Q8 / report L59 (app/src/util/bindings.rs:625-687)`
- Action names are an implicit public API shared by the file loader, catalog, and agent skill; Axis-4 Step-D RENAMES a batch (Vim-prefixed actions collapse into ModalKeystroke), orphaning name-keyed references unless a shared alias/migration map is consulted at catalog generation. Step-D is Phase 3 (report section 7), so the alias map is a Phase-3 GATE on the rename, NOT a Phase-1 blocker on shipping the catalog — in Phase 1 no rename has occurred, so the editable-name key is stable. — `X11 (actions.rs:50-294); report section 7 Phase 3 Step D; report L230`
- The report's claim that Fixed bindings are 'internal/transient only' is an explicitly UNCONFIRMED honest-unknown and is contradicted by X5: the code editor registers its ENTIRE navigation set as Fixed under id!("CodeEditorView"). So the editable-only agent's coverage gap may be larger than 'just internal/transient', and the fraction of real 'rebind X' requests it cannot serve is currently unmeasured. — `report section 6 honest-unknown; readiness doc X5 (L61)`

**Options:**

- **A. Versioned manifest, tiered guarantee (editable-name stable key)** _(med effort · easy)_ — Ship the Phase-1 catalog now as an explicitly versioned manifest (schema_version field from day one) with a documented two-tier guarantee: FROZEN = editable action `name` (the existing persistence key, build- and OS-invariant) + group; BEST-EFFORT = description + current-OS default trigger. The agent contract binds ONLY to the frozen tier, scoped to editable bindings + current OS. Context column, platform variants, and fixed-binding stable ids are declared explicit 'coming in vN' fields. UI and agent consume the SAME serialization. The X11 alias map is named as a Phase-3 GATE on Step-D, not a Phase-1 deliverable.
  - 👍 Matches every verified constraint instead of fighting it: the catalog provably cannot carry scope/platform/fixed-id in Phase 1 (A5-Q8, A5-Q3) — even the report's own Phase-1 tuple wrongly lists 'context' — so a 'done' framing is demonstrably false; versioning makes that honest and the schema_version field is ~free and reversibility-easy. It gives the agent the one key it actually needs today (the editable name, already the persistence key, A1-Q9) and that key is uniquely immune to BOTH the OS-resolution and build-instability problems. Editable bindings ARE the rebindable surface (fixed 'can't be reconfigured', keymap.rs:275), so the agent can do its core job on day one against the frozen tier while honestly labeling its same-context/current-OS limits. One serialization keeps UI and agent from drifting (report L230). Aligns with the report's actual Phase-1 plan ('persist for the agent skill').
  - 👎 The v1 agent is blind to per-context scope and the other platform, so it can over/under-report and must self-label same-context/current-OS-only — an honesty burden on the skill UX. Even for editable bindings the name->trigger map is N-valued (~9 multi-description, multi-trigger names; update_custom_trigger rewrites all N), so the contract test must handle 1-or-N. It is worthless for fixed bindings until A5-Q3's ~469-site project lands — and that gap is likely LARGER than 'internal only' because X5 shows user-facing code-editor navigation is Fixed, so the editable-only coverage of real 'rebind X' requests is unmeasured and could be low. The frozen-key promise is contingent on X11's alias map landing before Step-D in Phase 3; if A is read as 'publish and ship the full skill in Phase 1' before the coverage prototype runs, it risks shipping a frustrating 'I can't do that' agent.
- **B. Full catalog up front** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Build the complete (id, description, group, context, default, platform, fixed-vs-editable) manifest before exposing the agent — adding a stable id to all ~469 fixed-binding sites and threading context/platform into CommandBinding now. One catalog, never re-keyed.
  - 👍 The agent never re-keys or version-negotiates; fixed bindings, context, and platform are all present so the skill is fully capable day one and 'the catalog is the truth' holds across consumers. Avoids shipping an agent contract that immediately needs a v2.
  - 👎 Pulls the ~469-site fixed-id project (A5-Q3), the context-column engine refactor (A5-Q8/A5-Q6, R3a/R3b — a real Phase-2 engine change), and the platform data-model (R5, X3) all onto the Phase-1 critical path, destroying the 'independently shippable, no schema/engine change' premise of the quick wins (report section 7 Phase 1). Worse, it freezes the catalog schema BEFORE the Phase-2/3 designs that DEFINE context/platform/unbind semantics are settled, so any later change is a costly breaking edit to a shipped agent contract. Highest cost, highest lock-in.
- **C. Defer the agent contract; UI-only Phase-1 catalog** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship the Phase-1 editable-only catalog purely as read-display data for the settings UI. Do NOT expose it as a contract to the agent yet. Build the change-keybinding skill later, after Phase 2/3 produce context, fixed-binding identity, and platform.
  - 👍 Cheapest path and zero risk of locking the agent onto an incomplete or soon-renamed manifest. The agent later gets the full, stable, context/platform-aware catalog in one shot, sidestepping X11 churn and the same-context/current-OS caveats entirely.
  - 👎 Forfeits a Phase-1-achievable capability (description->editable-name->trigger over the whole user-rebindable surface, A1-Q9) and departs from the report's own plan to 'persist for the agent skill' in Phase 1. Deferral does NOT escape the X11 rename problem — it just postpones it; the rename still orphans whatever the agent eventually keys on unless the alias map is built regardless. Crucially, C tends to DROP the ~free schema_version field, so when the agent is later wired up the catalog serialization changes are an unversioned breaking edit for the UI consumers — strictly worse than E for the same deferral.
- **D. Two separate catalogs (UI dynamic, agent frozen)** _(med effort · costly)_ — UI consumes a dynamic editable-only catalog; the agent consumes its own independently-versioned frozen manifest, decoupling the two consumers.
  - 👍 Each consumer gets exactly its tolerance: the UI keeps fully dynamic descriptions with no stability obligation; the agent gets a hand-curated frozen id set that never moves. Decoupling avoids the UI's churn forcing agent re-keys.
  - 👎 Two serializations of the same name-keyed registry WILL drift — the report explicitly wants ONE catalog feeding both (L230). The divergent agent manifest must still regenerate from the same registry, so it inherits the IDENTICAL X11 rename and fixed-id problems while doubling maintenance and the surface where UI and agent disagree about what an action IS. Adds B's curation cost without B's completeness.
- **E. Versioned-schema-only now; agent skill as coverage-gated fast-follow** _(low effort · easy)_ — In Phase 1, land ONLY the cheap, irreversible-protection part: add the schema_version discriminant to the single catalog serialization and document the editable-name FROZEN key + tier table. Do NOT publish/ship the agent skill as a Phase-1 deliverable; gate its publication on (a) the X11 code investigation enumerating Step-D renames and (b) a coverage prototype measuring what fraction of real 'rebind X' requests the editable-only/current-OS v1 can actually serve. Build the skill as a fast-follow once those clear.
  - 👍 Captures ~100% of A's cheap, reversibility-easy insurance (versioned schema so Phase-2/3 can add context/platform/fixed-id columns without breaking consumers, and one serialization for UI+agent) while NOT betting on an agent whose coverage is unmeasured — directly honoring the brief's own what-would-settle-it (run the X11 spike + prototype the skill before committing). Strictly dominates C by keeping the schema_version field C drops; decouples X4 from X11's timeline and from the Fixed-surface uncertainty (X5). If the coverage prototype comes back high, you proceed to A's published contract with evidence; if low, you've spent only a near-free field.
  - 👎 Slips the agent-skill ship date past the spike, so the agent-platform team waits on a deliverable the report nominally placed in Phase 1 — a minor schedule cost if the coverage prototype is quick. Carries a small risk of analysis-paralysis if the spike is treated as a blocker rather than a fast, time-boxed check. Does not by itself solve the N-valued name->trigger or fixed-binding gaps; those remain vN work either way.

**→ Recommendation: A. Versioned manifest, tiered guarantee (editable-name stable key) — with E's decoupling folded in.** The verified facts make the adversarial premise correct and stronger than the brief stated: the catalog is inherently versioned, not 'done in Phase 1' — context, platform, and fixed-binding identity are structurally absent (A5-Q8), fixed ids are a ~469-site project (A5-Q3), and the report's OWN Phase-1 tuple overpromises a 'context' field the code cannot produce. Option A is the only choice that accepts this instead of denying it (the 'v1-is-done' framing) or paying to erase it now (B/D). It also unlocks real value: the editable name is already the faithful name-keyed persistence key (A1-Q9) and is uniquely build- AND OS-invariant, and editable bindings are exactly the rebindable surface (fixed 'can't be reconfigured'), so the agent can do its core job against the frozen tier while honestly labeling limits. The schema_version field plus alias-map awareness are cheap, additive, reversibility-easy insurance. BUT A must be adopted with two corrections from the attack: (1) decouple the two costs — land the versioned schema + frozen-key tier (LOW effort) in Phase 1, but treat the PUBLISHED, shipped agent skill as gated on the X11 enumeration spike and a coverage prototype (this is E's posture, and it matches the brief's own what-would-settle-it); (2) make explicit that X11's alias map is a Phase-3 GATE on Step-D, not a Phase-1 blocker, so the catalog ships in Phase 1 regardless. So the operative recommendation is 'A's schema now, E's gate on the shipped skill.' E is the preferred fallback if Phase-1 agent capacity is unavailable — it strictly dominates C by keeping the ~free schema_version field. B and D are rejected: B destroys the no-engine-change Phase-1 premise and freezes the schema before Phase-2/3 semantics are settled; D contradicts the one-catalog goal (L230) and doubles the drift surface while inheriting the same rename/fixed-id problems.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter is the deferral/coverage case: A's claim of 'real value on day one' is UNPROVEN until the coverage prototype runs, and could be small. The report's 'Fixed = internal/transient' is an unconfirmed honest-unknown contradicted by X5 (the code editor's entire navigation set is Fixed under CodeEditorView), so a meaningful, surprising fraction of user-facing 'rebind X to Y' requests may target Fixed keys the editable-only v1 agent cannot serve — and the same-context/current-OS frozen tier also can't serve context- or platform-specific rebinds. If that unserveable fraction is high, a shipped Phase-1 agent delivers a frustrating 'I can't do that' experience that erodes trust more than waiting would. This is precisely why the shipped skill (not the schema) is gated on the coverage prototype; the residual after that gate is bounded because the schema_version field itself is near-free, so even a low-coverage outcome wastes almost nothing.
- _Dependencies:_ Co-dependent on X11 (action-name alias/migration registry) — the frozen editable-name key is only stable across the Axis-4 Step-D renames if the alias map is consulted at catalog generation; CORRECTION: Step-D is Phase 3, so X11 is a Phase-3 GATE, not a Phase-1 blocker (in Phase 1 no rename has occurred, so the key is stable). Gated by A5-Q3 (fixed-binding stable id, ~469 sites) for the 'read-only fixed bindings' column — a vN deferral. Gated by A5-Q8/A5-Q6 + R3a/R3b (CommandBinding has no scope dimension; context is a Phase-2 engine change) for the context column. Tied to X2 (record file schema) and X3/Axis 6 / R5 (platform variants) for the platform column. NEW: tied to X5 (FixedBinding magnitude audit) — needed to size the editable-only coverage gap, since X5 shows user-facing code-editor navigation is Fixed. Tied to X1 (single shared resolved-binding store) — a unified store makes the catalog walk and versioning cleaner but is not a hard prerequisite. Builds on A1-Q9 (editable name is today's faithful stable key). Feeds Axis-5 search-by-name (R7), which currently does not read binding.name at all (A5-Q8). Note: the catalog is regenerated/persisted at startup, so its schema_version protects consumer CODE and any persisted manifest from missing-field assumptions; the user's durable keybindings.yaml safety is a SEPARATE concern governed by X2/A1-Q12.
- _What would settle it:_ A joint settings-UI + agent-skill design session producing a written catalog schema with: (a) a schema_version discriminant; (b) a field-stability table (FROZEN: name, group; BEST-EFFORT: description, current-OS trigger; ABSENT-UNTIL-vN: context, platform, fixed-binding id) with the agent contract bound only to FROZEN; and (c) a contract test asserting description->name->trigger resolution for editable bindings on the current OS, EXPLICITLY covering the N-trigger-per-name case (~9 multi-description names). To decide whether the agent skill ships IN Phase 1: (1) run the X11 code investigation enumerating the Step-D renames and confirm the alias map can be seeded before any Phase-3 rename; (2) run the X5 FixedBinding magnitude audit to size how much user-facing surface is Fixed (and thus outside the editable-only agent); and (3) prototype the skill against the v1 manifest and MEASURE what fraction of intended 'rebind X to Y' requests target fixed bindings or context/platform-specific keys it cannot serve. Ship the skill in Phase 1 only if that unserveable fraction is acceptably low; otherwise land the versioned schema now (Option E) and ship the skill as a coverage-gated fast-follow.
- _Red-team added option:_ E. Versioned-schema-only now; agent skill as coverage-gated fast-follow
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all five grounding facts against the answers doc and source; all hold. Direct read of app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541 + 749-771 confirms CommandBinding has no context/platform field and both from_lens/from_editable_lens drop the ContextPredicate. A5-Q3 confirms the ~469-site / 146-register / 93-file fixed-id project and ~404 description-less drops. A1-Q9 confirms editable-name is the faithful name-keyed persistence key. Hardenings applied: (1) DECISIVE NEW FACT — the report's OWN Phase-1 R9 deliverable (report L8/L249, section 7) describes the manifest as "(name, description, group, CONTEXT, default)", but A5-Q8 proves the context field structurally cannot exist in Phase 1. The roadmap itself overpromises a field the verified code can't produce, which is the strongest possible evidence FOR the brief's "inherently versioned, not v1-done" premise. (2) EFFORT SPLIT — A's "medium" conflates two costs: the schema/versioning decision is LOW (a schema_version field + a tier doc), while the net-new agent skill (description->name->trigger mapping does not exist; search never reads binding.name, A5-Q8/report L59) is the medium part. (3) X11 TIMING CORRECTED — the brief implies the alias map is a Phase-1 co-requisite; Step-D renames are Axis-4/Phase-3 (report section 7), so the alias map is a Phase-3 GATE, not a Phase-1 blocker — the editable-name key is stable in Phase 1 because no rename has happened yet. So X4 does NOT have to ship the alias map to ship the catalog. (4) FROZEN-KEY STRENGTH the brief underused — the editable name is both build-invariant AND OS-invariant (only the trigger is OS-specific), so the frozen tier is immune to both the platform and build-instability problems. (5) NUANCE — even for editable bindings, one name maps to N bindings and update_custom_trigger rewrites ALL N (A1-Q9: ~9 multi-description, ~50 dup names, e.g. move_forward_one_word has two default triggers), so name->trigger is N-valued; the contract test must handle 1-or-N, and the agent cannot target one-of-N contexts (same limit as the UI). (6) COVERAGE RISK SHARPENED — A's what_breaks understates the editable-only gap: the report's "Fixed = internal/transient only" is empirically UNCONFIRMED and contradicted by X5 (the code editor registers its ENTIRE navigation set as Fixed under CodeEditorView), so a user-facing chunk of "rebind X" requests may target Fixed keys the v1 agent cannot serve. Added Option E (versioned-schema-only now, skill gated on the X11+coverage spike) which strictly dominates fallback C by keeping the ~free forward-compat insurance C drops.

### X8 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Split ownership reduces coordination cost. The watcher (D) is an Axis-1-owner implementation call that does NOT need the X2 cross-team table — it changes no schema and depends on nothing in X2. The file-format forward-compat IS the X2 cross-team architecture commitment: the keybindings-initiative tech lead / architecture owner adjudicates, with the Axis-1 (Phase-1 config/quick-wins) owner and the Axis-2/3 (engine-core) owner as binding inputs, and Product signing off on the A1-Q12 data-loss-class migration risk for the file-WRITING items. Ship the marquee win under Axis-1 ownership now; run X2 in parallel for the writers.

**Decision.** Not the binary the title poses ('layer-first co-design vs name-only throwaway scaffold'). It is three near-independent calls: (a) REJECT building Axis-3's user-layer object / ContextPredicate parser / per-binding override path in Phase 1 (zero Phase-1 user-visible payoff — per-context overrides cannot even be authored until Axis 2); (b) SHIP the schema-neutral hot-reload watcher NOW using reset-all-then-reapply, which needs no persistent applied-set and no name/context key and changes no file shape, decoupled from X2; (c) ROUTE the file-format forward-compat — the only genuinely migration-bearing artifact — to X2, which must carry the mandatory A1-Q12 duplex deserializer + absent-vs-parse-error split for the file-WRITING Phase-1 items (import/export, chord capture).

**Stakes.** Hot-reload is the single biggest configurability papercut and the marquee Phase-1 win (report L1/L7, R1). Contrary to the brief's framing, it is NOT gated on this decision or on X2: the watcher reads+applies and changes no file shape, so it springs no A1-Q12 trap and can ship while X2 deliberates. The genuine migration risk lives entirely in the file FORMAT (A1-Q12) — which the watcher never touches — and in the OTHER Phase-1 writers (import/export R7, chord capture) that DO write the file and are correctly X2-gated. Getting it wrong: Option A drags the entire Phase-2 engine refactor (layer object + ContextPredicate parser + per-binding override, plus resolving X6/X9 first) onto the critical path for ZERO Phase-1 payoff; Option C as stated needlessly bundles the marquee win behind the slow X2 cross-team table and ships an inert in-memory key; Option B freezes the flat file and, if the duplex deserializer is skipped when the schema later changes, springs the silent-reset + first-edit file-truncation trap on every user (A1-Q12).

_Constrained by:_
- CORRECTION to the brief's fact #1: no persistent applied-set exists (grep applied_names/active_custom/custom_names = 0 hits; override state is per-binding EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger>), BUT an owned applied-set is NOT required for hot-reload reset. A reset-all-then-reapply strategy needs only a trivial clear-all-custom-triggers loop over editable bindings; the brief's 'net-new regardless of which option' is overstated. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:300`
- DECISIVE MISSING FACT: the only set_custom_trigger callers are file-load (keyboard.rs:44,47) and the two UI edit paths (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592, util/bindings.rs:492), and both UI paths also write keybindings.yaml (A1-Q9). So the file is the single source of override truth -> reset-all-then-reapply is correct with no provenance tracking and no applied-set, and it encodes no key shape so it survives the Axis-2 context-scoped refactor unchanged. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:30-57 ; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-151`
- update_custom_trigger(name, Some|None) filters b.name==name over both binding vectors and takes no context/binding-id; per-context overrides cannot even be CREATED today, so round-trip is provably faithful for everything the current engine can produce and there is NO file-vs-UI capability gap. This justifies REJECTING Option A: the layer/parser/per-binding-override has zero Phase-1 user-visible payoff. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433 ; A1-Q9`
- The A1-Q12 trap (serde_yaml fails on today's flat map if a record schema lands -> read_custom_keybindings returns None -> silent reset of every keymap + first-edit truncation via fs::File::create) is sprung ONLY when a WRITER changes the file shape. The watcher reads+applies and changes no shape, so shipping it alone springs no trap. Per A1-Q12's own residual, the correct duplex/untagged deserializer is co-determined with the not-yet-fixed record shape -- so it lands WITH X2, undercutting C's 'pay it now, it's mandatory anyway' edge. — `A1-Q12 ; app/src/keyboard.rs:128-139 ; app/src/util/file.rs:132-145`
- ContextPredicate has no FromStr/Display/Deserialize anywhere in the crate, so a stored context field is inert until Axis 2 builds the parser (confirms C's typed-key context field is dead weight for one phase). The watcher path is essentially free: warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:249-264 already registers config_local_dir, where keybinding_file_path() (config_local_dir/keybindings.yaml) lives, so change events are already delivered with a 500ms debounce; only the handler branch is missing (native.rs:74-134 has no keybindings branch). — `A2-Q19 ; app/src/keyboard.rs:95-97 ; app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-139 ; app/src/warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:249-264`

**Options:**

- **A. Layer-first (co-design the user-LAYER object now)** _(very-high effort · one-way)_ — Pull Axis 3's binding-layer/precedence model into Phase 1: build a real UserLayer keyed by name+context, route hot-reload apply/reset through it, thread context through update_custom_trigger so the applied-set is the layer's own index from day one.
  - 👍 The report's cross-cutting truth (L13, L228-229, sec6) says all three goals converge on one missing primitive -- a first-class binding-layer/context-scope -- and 'building R2 once serves both Goal 1 and Goal 3.' Doing it now means the file key, override key, and applied-set key are one consistent name+context shape forever: zero throwaway, zero second migration, A1-Q12 designed away in one pass.
  - 👎 Directly contradicts the dependency spine's 'Axes 1 & 5 are Phase-1 quick wins, no schema/engine change' (end-goals L117; report sec7). Drags the heaviest Phase-2 surface onto the critical path of the low-risk win: the layer object is co-determined by the unified resolver (X6), mutate-vs-inject (X9), Unbound/tombstone semantics, and a ContextPredicate parser that does not exist (A2-Q19). Worst of all, per-context overrides cannot be authored until Axis 2 anyway (A1-Q9), so you build the entire engine refactor for ZERO Phase-1 user-visible payoff and indefinitely delay the marquee papercut fix.
- **B. Throwaway scaffold (name-only applied-set, flat file, explicitly temporary)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Ship hot-reload over today's flat name->trigger file with a name-keyed 'currently-applied custom names' set (report R1), documented as temporary scaffolding to be rewritten when Axis 2/3 land.
  - 👍 Cheapest path to the flagship win. Round-trip is provably faithful for everything the engine can produce today (A1-Q9), the watcher already delivers events (just add the native.rs branch), and the rewrite is bounded. Ships in ~a week and de-risks the rest by proving the watcher pattern.
  - 👎 The named throwaway artifact (the name-only applied-set) is actually UNNECESSARY -- reset-all needs none -- so B builds bookkeeping it doesn't need. The real costly artifact is the FROZEN FLAT FILE: the day a record schema lands you hit the A1-Q12 trap (silent reset + first-edit truncation) unless the duplex deserializer is built then under schedule pressure. So B's reversibility is not 'easy' -- a flat file in the wild is the A1-Q12 data-loss-class migration. B also leaves the file-format decision unmade, so import/export/chord-capture inherit the same unresolved schema risk.
- **C. Forward-compatible record file + typed name+context key, deferred engine (hybrid)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Ship Axis 1 hot-reload now with name-only APPLY, but (1) commit the canonical record file schema up front per X2 (context=None/platform=None) behind an untagged/back-compat deserializer, and (2) make the applied-set key a typed name + Option<context> struct. Do NOT build the layer object, parser, or per-binding override.
  - 👍 Threads the needle in spirit: B's ship-speed with neither of B's costly migrations. It is exactly X2's prescribed move -- decouple the file format from the engine refactor so the quick wins are genuinely forward-compatible -- and it never paints the user's file into a name-only corner.
  - 👎 Two distinct overcommitments. (1) The in-memory typed key is dead weight: with reset-all the key shape is never read, and the context field is inert until Axis 2 builds the parser (A2-Q19) -- so it must be spec'd as reserved and serves no Phase-1 purpose. (2) The 'pay the duplex deserializer now' edge over deferring to X2 is largely illusory: A1-Q12's residual says the correct untagged discrimination is co-determined with the not-yet-fixed record shape, so it lands WITH X2 regardless. Net: C's only real content beyond 'resolve X2' is the unnecessary key, and it gates the schema-neutral marquee win on the slow X2 cross-team table.
- **D. Decoupled watcher + reset-all reapply (no applied-set; file schema -> X2)** _(low effort · easy)_ — Ship hot-reload now by adding a keybindings.yaml branch to handle_warp_managed_paths_event (config_local_dir is already watched) that resets ALL custom triggers then reapplies the file. No persistent applied-set, no name/context key, no file-format change. REJECT building the Axis-3 layer. Route the file-format forward-compat commitment to X2, which owns the mandatory A1-Q12 duplex deserializer (co-determined with the record shape) for the file-WRITING quick wins.
  - 👍 The only set_custom_trigger callers are file-load + two UI paths that both write the file, so the file is the single source of override truth and reset-all-then-reapply is provably correct with zero bookkeeping. This is the MOST refactor-robust reset of all options: it encodes no key shape, so when Axis 2 lands context-scoped overrides it keeps working unchanged (clear-all + reapply against whatever the engine then represents). The marquee win ships in days with no X2 gate and springs no A1-Q12 trap because it changes no file shape. Per A1-Q12's residual the correct duplex deserializer can't even be built until X2 fixes the record shape, so D loses nothing by deferring the file-format call to X2 -- it just doesn't bundle it into the marquee win.
  - 👎 reset-all assumes the file stays the single source of override truth; if any future code sets a non-persisted programmatic custom_trigger (e.g. a transient mode override), reset-all silently clobbers it and the provenance problem the brief flagged returns -- so D must be paired with a rule that all override state stays file-backed. A narrow race exists if a second UI edit lands inside the 500ms debounce before its file-write, but reload is fast and idempotent against the UI's own writes. D does NOT settle X2, so import/export and chord capture still wait on X2; shipping only the read-only watcher first may feel like a partial Phase-1 win. Must confirm keybindings.yaml events reach the new branch under the existing config_local_dir registration (likely free).

**→ Recommendation: D. Decoupled watcher + reset-all reapply (no persistent applied-set; file-format forward-compat routed to X2).** The X8 binary is false AND X8 is largely a restatement of X2. Separating the schema-neutral watcher from the file-format commitment yields three clean calls. (1) Reject A: A1-Q9 and A2-Q19 prove the layer object and ContextPredicate parser have zero Phase-1 user-visible payoff (per-context overrides can't be authored until Axis 2) and A would force resolving X6/X9 first. (2) Ship the watcher now via reset-all: the grep proves the file is the single override source (only file-load + two file-writing UI callers set custom_trigger), so reset-all is correct with no applied-set and no key, and it changes no file shape so it springs no A1-Q12 trap and needs no X2. (3) The only genuinely migration-bearing artifact is the file FORMAT, which is exactly X2; and per A1-Q12's residual the correct duplex deserializer is co-determined with X2's record shape, so it lands with X2 regardless — making C's 'pay it now' advantage over deferring to X2 largely illusory and C's in-memory typed key inert dead weight. D delivers the marquee win on the fastest timeline, encodes no key shape a future refactor must rewrite, and routes the real migration concern to X2 where it is unavoidable anyway. C remains a defensible near-equivalent IF the org prefers to settle X2 before any Phase-1 file work and wants one commitment that also covers import/export — but even then it should drop the in-memory key and not gate the watcher on the X2 table.

- _Residual risk:_ Strongest surviving counter: the import/export coupling. Decoupling the WATCHER is safe, but the OTHER Phase-1 quick wins (import/export R7, chord capture) DO write the file and remain X2-gated, so the broader Phase-1 program still stalls if X2 isn't settled promptly — D removes the watcher from the X2 gate, not the file-writers, so a skeptic can argue the org should just settle X2 first (i.e. pick C) and ship everything together rather than land a read-only watcher in isolation.
