Two more: (a) reset-all silently assumes no non-persisted programmatic override ever exists; the moment one is introduced (e.g. a transient modal override) reset-all clobbers it and the provenance problem resurfaces, so reset-all must be enforced alongside a 'all override state stays file-backed' rule. (b) A1-Q12's residual warns the untagged duplex discrimination may not be unambiguous to serde_yaml depending on the record shape, so the 'duplex deserializer is cheap and mandatory-anyway' assumption underpinning both C and the X2 track is itself unproven and may force a version-field+migrate approach instead.
- _Dependencies:_ D's watcher depends on nothing in X2: config_local_dir is already registered (warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:249-264) and keybinding_file_path() lives there (keyboard.rs:95-97), so events are already delivered — only a handler branch in native.rs:74-139 plus a trivial clear-all-custom-triggers primitive are needed. Rejecting A is justified by A1-Q9 (no Phase-1 payoff), A2-Q19 (no ContextPredicate parser), and the fact A forces resolving X6 (unified resolver) and X9 (mutate-vs-inject) first. The file-format forward-compat = X2; its mandatory A1-Q12 duplex deserializer + absent-vs-parse-error split is co-determined with X2's record shape (A1-Q12 residual), so it lands WITH X2, not before. The Phase-1 items actually gated on X2 are the file-WRITERS — import/export (R7) and chord capture — NOT the watcher. Defers the engine rebuild tracked by Axis 3-Q18 / X9 and the unified resolver X6 (which Option A would force up front).
- _What would settle it:_ (1) DONE here: grep confirms set_custom_trigger has only file-load (keyboard.rs:44/47) and two file-writing UI callers (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592, util/bindings.rs:492), proving the file is the single override source -> reset-all is correct and the brief's 'applied-set required regardless' is false. (2) Confirm keybindings.yaml change events actually reach handle_warp_managed_paths_event under the existing config_local_dir registration (warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:249-264); if yes the watcher branch is a few lines and the marquee win is days of work. (3) The X2 product call: number of keybindings.yaml + Phase-1-exported files in the wild times the A1-Q12 migration risk, which governs whether import/export and chord-capture must wait for the record schema — this is X2's decisive number, NOT the watcher's. If product will not accept any future user-file migration, settle X2 before the file-writers; if hand-edited/exported YAML is vanishingly rare, the file-writers can also ship over the flat format and migrate later.
- _Red-team added option:_ D. Decoupled watcher + reset-all reapply (no persistent applied-set; file-format forward-compat routed to X2)
- _Red-team:_ Verified all 5 brief code-groundings against source. All accurate EXCEPT fact #1's claim that a hot-reload reset 'REQUIRES a new owned applied-set ... net-new regardless of which option is chosen' — overstated. Decisive missing fact: the ONLY set_custom_trigger callers are file-load (keyboard.rs:44/47) and the two UI edit paths (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592, util/bindings.rs:492), and both UI paths ALSO write keybindings.yaml (A1-Q9). So the file is the single source of override truth, which makes a reset-all-then-reapply strategy provably correct with ZERO persistent applied-set and zero key (name or name+context). That collapses the brief's framing: the applied-set KEY — which B treats as throwaway and C tries to forward-compat — does not need to exist at all. Added Option D (decoupled watcher + reset-all). Reframed X8 from a false binary into three near-independent calls and showed it is largely a restatement of X2. Showed C's distinctive value over 'B+X2' is thin: its in-memory typed key is dead weight given reset-all, and its 'pay the duplex deserializer now' edge over deferring to X2 is largely illusory because A1-Q12's own residual says the duplex/untagged deserializer is co-determined with X2's not-yet-fixed record shape (so it lands WITH X2, not before). Downgraded B's reversibility easy->costly (a frozen flat file is the A1-Q12 data-loss-class migration). Made the hidden dependency explicit: C silently gates the marquee win on the X2 cross-team table though the watcher is schema-neutral and needn't be. Confirmed deferability: the watcher reads+applies and changes no file shape, so it springs no A1-Q12 trap and can ship before X2; only the file-WRITING Phase-1 items (import/export R7, chord capture) are genuinely X2-gated. Confirmed the watcher path is essentially free: warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:249-264 already registers config_local_dir, where keybinding_file_path() (keyboard.rs:95-97) lives, so events for keybindings.yaml are already delivered — only the handler branch in native.rs:74-134 is missing.

### X10 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team (X10 is classified cross-team). Three co-signers plus an arbiter. (1) Settings-UI owner — owns ConflictMap, the chord-rekey, the R7 Conflicts-chip copy, and the truthfulness claim. (2) Keybindings-engine owner — owns exposing ContextPredicate (A5-Q2) and the universe union (A5-Q6) for v1, the overlap/satisfiability primitive for v2, the R5 both-sides model + R3b record schema for v3, and the deferred A5-Q3 fixed-binding identity project. (3) Product/Design — rules the honesty contract: exactly what 'no conflict' promises at the FLOOR (Phase 1) and at each later tier; this ruling must land before Phase 1's Conflicts chip ships. Engineering lead arbitrates the phase-cut, including the new Phase-1-floor-vs-Phase-2-v1 boundary.

**Decision.** How to sequence and scope 'truthful conflict detection' (the Axis 5 north-star, end-goals L41 'what the UI shows is the truth') given it has THREE independent truthfulness gaps — wrong binding-universe (Axis 5/A5-Q6), no context-overlap test (Axis 2/A2-Q7), and current-OS-only keystrokes (Axis 6/A6-Q6) — each gated on a different axis. The attack adds a fourth, previously-hidden dimension: the roadmap already ships an R7 'Conflicts' filter chip and R8 chord capture IN PHASE 1, so a minimal honesty ruling (what the Conflicts chip is allowed to claim) and a cheap settings-only chord-rekey are coupled to Phase 1, while the deeper universe/context/cross-platform truth is Phase 2+. The choice is therefore not binary (ship-now vs hold-all) but a four-tier contract: a Phase-1 honesty FLOOR (rekey ConflictMap to Vec<Keystroke> + scope the chip copy, no engine work), a Phase-2 v1 (union fixed/custom universe + exact structural ContextPredicate equality; honest GENERIC 'conflicts with a built-in' detection, deferring the ~469-site naming project), a v2 (Axis-2 predicate-overlap/satisfiability), and a v3 (Axis-6 both-sides platform model, gated on the R3b record schema). Each tier is strictly additive and never walked back.

**Stakes.** The architecture report names context-aware, actionable conflict detection the single highest-leverage Settings-UI change (report L9) and the Phase-2 deliverable is literally 'conflict UI tells the truth' (report L260). The brief's framing — that this north-star is gated on BOTH the Axis-2 overlap primitive AND the Axis-6 platform model — is true for the FULL claim but, if taken as all-or-nothing, needlessly back-loads the flagship win behind the deepest refactors. The attack sharpens the stakes in two directions. Downside of under-scoping: shipping a 'no conflicts' badge (or a Phase-1 'Conflicts' filter chip) that silently misses cross-platform or context-overlap collisions re-creates the exact dishonesty the initiative exists to kill (end-goals L41) and trains users to distrust the UI — and this risk is LIVE IN PHASE 1, not deferred, because R7's Conflicts chip and R8's chord capture both land then and both degrade the current map (A5-Q7). Downside of over-scoping (Option 2): the report's most-cited win slips to the end, and today's actively-wrong map (false positives AND false negatives, A5-Q6) stays in users' faces for quarters. The decision determines whether 'truthful' is a binary milestone or a tiered contract, and — newly — whether Phase 1 ships an honest or an overclaiming Conflicts chip.

_Constrained by:_
- ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize> populated only from binding.trigger with no context predicate, over the editable universe only; it flags any keystroke used by >1 editable binding regardless of predicate overlap (false positives) and never scans fixed/custom-action bindings (false negatives). Verified at the cited lines. — `A5-Q2/A5-Q6 (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-107,795-800)`
- ContextPredicate::eval takes exactly one &Context and returns bool; And/Or recurse on the same single ctx. There is NO overlap/satisfiability primitive and NO FromStr parser — only point-eval exists, not 'can two predicates ever both match?'. Verified. — `A2-Q7 (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:100-119)`
- cmdorctrl resolves to cmd(mac)/ctrl(non-mac) at parse time; Keystroke stores no platform/cmdorctrl field; OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time cfg, so the inactive platform's chord is never constructed and 'display and conflict-check of the inactive side are impossible'. Verified. This is the DATA-MODEL part of R5; A6-Q6/A6-Q7 also show the inactive side is non-uniform (key transform bracket->brace, added shift, uppercasing; cmd->ctrl lossy vs meta) so it cannot be auto-synthesized, and it couples to the Axis-2 record schema (A6-Q9). — `A6-Q6/A6-Q7 (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919,320-328)`
- Fixed bindings carry an empty lens name (name: Default::default(), keymap.rs:631-642) and live outside the conflict universe, so a fixed-binding collision is both undetected (A5-Q6) and unnameable; making conflicts context-aware requires an engine change (EditableBindingLens.context private at keymap.rs:311, CommandBinding has no scope field). CORRECTION to the brief: only the raw construction-site count is code-confirmed — 397 FixedBinding::new sites, 469 including new_per_platform (A5-Q3). The brief's '~167 user-reachable' is UNAUDITED; quantifying the user-reachable subset is exactly what decision X5 exists to produce. — `A5-Q6/A5-Q2/A5-Q3/X5 (keymap.rs:311,631-642; A5-Q3 residual)`
- CORRECTION to the brief's 'R5 is Phase-3-deep': the report labels R5 'medium' (L146) and lists it under Phase 2 as OPTIONAL (L258); only the sequencing prose (L270) lumps R5 with 'deepest refactors deferred behind cheaper prerequisites'. R6 context-aware conflicts is squarely Phase 2 (L257). So cross-platform v3 is hard-gated on the Axis-2 record schema (R3b, A6-Q9) but is not inherently the last capability — Option 2's 'slips to the very end' steelman is partly overstated. — `.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:146,257,258,270`
- NEW (brief missed): the roadmap places R8 chord capture (Phase-1 #3) AND an R7 'Conflicts' filter chip (Phase-1 #2) in PHASE 1. Per A5-Q7 the single-Keystroke ConflictMap collapses any chord to its first keystroke (trigger_to_keystroke -> keys.first()), so once R8 lands, distinct chords (K-C vs K-D) are FALSELY flagged and prefix-shadow is never surfaced. The Phase-1 Conflicts chip therefore renders demonstrably wrong conflict state — making the honesty-contract ruling a Phase-1 gate, not a Phase-2 nicety. — `report L167,L246 (Phase-1 R7/R8); A5-Q7 (app/src/util/bindings.rs:236; settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-146,1064)`
- NEW: the chord-rekey (HashMap<Keystroke,usize> -> HashMap<Vec<Keystroke>,usize>, stop routing through trigger_to_keystroke for conflicts) is an APP/settings-side change (app/util/bindings.rs + app/settings_view/keybindings.rs); the engine already stores Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>) (keymap.rs:44-49). It needs neither A5-Q2 context exposure nor A5-Q6 universe union, so it can ship in Phase 1 alongside R8. DETECTION of a fixed collision (A5-Q6 union) is also separable from NAMING it (A5-Q3 identity project): v1 can honestly show generic 'conflicts with a built-in' without the ~469-site work. — `A5-Q7 (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:44-49; app/src/util/bindings.rs:236); A5-Q6 residual`

**Options:**

- **Phase-1 honesty floor + detection-before-naming tiering (ADDED)** _(high effort · costly)_ ✅ **recommended** — Four strictly-additive tiers, re-sequenced to fix the Phase-1 exposure. FLOOR (Phase 1, ships with R7/R8): rekey ConflictMap to Vec<Keystroke> so chord capture does not corrupt conflicts, and scope the R7 'Conflicts' chip copy to 'same-key, this platform' — settings-only, NO engine work. v1 (Phase 2): union editable+fixed+custom universe (A5-Q6) and compare ContextPredicate trees for EXACT STRUCTURAL EQUALITY (kills the dominant false positives/negatives), surfacing fixed/custom collisions with GENERIC copy ('conflicts with a built-in shortcut') and DEFERRING the ~469-site A5-Q3 naming project. v2: add the Axis-2 predicate-overlap/satisfiability test. v3: add the Axis-6 both-sides platform model (gated on the R3b record schema, A6-Q9).
  - 👍 This is the brief's tiered direction made correct on the two facts it got wrong. (a) It removes the Phase-1 lie the brief never noticed: R7's Conflicts chip and R8's chords both land in Phase 1 and degrade the current map (A5-Q7), so a Phase-1 floor is mandatory regardless of how the later tiers sequence. (b) It splits DETECTION from NAMING — honest 'there is a conflict with a built-in' needs only the A5-Q6 union, not the ~469-site A5-Q3 identity project — so v1 is materially cheaper than the brief's bundled v1 and front-loads the report's single highest-leverage win (L9) earlier. The chord-rekey and chip-copy floor cost almost nothing (app-side only) yet kill the most visible Phase-1 dishonesty. Each tier strictly increases truthfulness and is never walked back.
  - 👎 More milestones (four) = more coordination and more UI-copy variants to keep honest across two teams; the honesty contract is load-bearing and easy to botch — a floor chip that says 'no conflicts' instead of 'no same-key conflicts on this platform' re-creates the dishonesty in a new shape. Exact-structural-equality v1 is only as correct as predicate-spelling canonicality: two logically-equivalent but differently-spelled predicate trees (A&B vs B&A; De Morgan variants) will mis-collapse, so v1 needs a cheap canonicalization step or it both under- and over-reports a residual class until v2. Generic-un-named v1 detection is honest but less actionable ('conflicts with SOMETHING built-in') and may frustrate users until A5-Q3 naming lands. v1 still hides genuinely-overlapping non-identical same-OS predicates until v2.
- **Tiered honesty milestone (brief's original: scoped v1 -> v2 overlap -> v3 cross-platform)** _(high effort · costly)_ — The brief's three-tier version. v1 = same-OS, full universe, exact ContextPredicate equality, chord-aware, with scoped copy — all shipped together in Phase 2. v2 adds Axis-2 overlap. v3 adds Axis-6 both-sides platform. Bundles universe-union + context-exposure + fixed-binding NAMING (A5-Q3) + chord-rekey into one Phase-2 v1.
  - 👍 Correctly identifies the three gaps as separable and front-loads most of the value in Phase 2 instead of stranding it behind R5. Killing keystroke-only false positives needs only exact structural equality (comparison, not satisfiability), and unioning the universe + chord rekey need neither overlap nor platform work — so a large fraction of today's lies die in Phase 2. Each tier strictly increases truthfulness.
  - 👎 Treats v1 as a single Phase-2 deliverable and Phase 1 as untouched — but Phase 1's R7 Conflicts chip and R8 chord capture (report L167/L246) actively touch and degrade conflicts, so the honesty ruling and chord-rekey cannot wait for Phase 2 (the gap the ADDED option fixes). BUNDLES the ~469-site A5-Q3 naming project into v1 ('plus A5-Q3 fixed-binding stable identity to NAME a collision'), inflating v1's cost when honest DETECTION needs only A5-Q6. Exact-equality under-reports overlapping non-identical predicates until v2. Three sets of UI copy is coordination overhead.
- **North-star last (single honest flip after Axis 2 + R5)** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Treat 'truthful conflicts' as one indivisible milestone that turns on only after the Axis-2 overlap primitive AND the Axis-6 both-sides platform model AND the unified universe all exist. Until then leave the conflict UI as-is or suppress it and make no truthfulness claim.
  - 👍 Avoids ever shipping a half-truth; one flip = one defensible promise with no self-caveating copy. Because the map keys on a bare current-OS Keystroke (A6-Q6) with only point-eval (A2-Q7), any earlier 'truthful' claim is provably incomplete, and a single flip forces the team to actually build the overlap primitive and platform model rather than declare partial victory.
  - 👎 Back-loads the report's most-cited win (L9) to the end. It also LEAVES THE PHASE-1 CONFLICTS CHIP SHOWING KNOWN-WRONG DATA: R7's chip and R8's chords ship in Phase 1 (L167/L246) and degrade the map (A5-Q7), so 'do nothing until the flip' means shipping a demonstrably-wrong Conflicts filter for quarters — strictly worse than today, because chords make it worse. Forecloses the near-free Phase-1 floor (chord-rekey + scoped copy) and the cheap Phase-2 universe-union. The 'R5 is the deepest deferred refactor' premise is overstated (R5 is labeled medium / Phase-2-optional, L146/L258), so the schedule argument is weaker than stated; but the option still carries the highest schedule risk if R5/R3b slip.
- **Host-platform-only, cross-platform a permanent non-goal (cheap/defer)** _(med effort · one-way)_ — Ship universe-union + exact-context-equality + chord-rekey (kills same-OS false positives and fixed/custom false negatives) and PERMANENTLY scope conflict detection to the host platform. Declare cross-platform conflict detection a non-goal; never build R5 for conflicts; no overlap engine either (exact equality only).
  - 👍 Cheapest path to a conflict UI honest about what a single-build single-OS client can actually see: the code only ever constructs the active platform's keystroke (A6-Q6) and is_binding_cross_platform forbids cmd-on-non-mac today (A6-Q7), so the host OS is the only platform with real data. If few users share configs across OSes, the cross-platform tier is speculative and R5-for-conflicts is poor ROI.
  - 👎 Directly contradicts the portability north-star ('drop in a colleague's config and it just works', end-goals L54): a mac config imported on Linux can carry a collision the Linux client never flags, and this option says that is fine forever. Entrenches the single-OS assumption into the conflict layer just as Axis 6 tries to remove it — a one-way move that costs a re-architecture AND a public-non-goal walk-back if cross-platform later becomes a goal. Exact-equality-only permanently under-reports overlapping non-identical predicates. Hurts exactly the power users/teams the portability goal targets. Also still does NOT address the Phase-1 chip exposure unless the floor is adopted.

**→ Recommendation: Phase-1 honesty floor + detection-before-naming tiering (ADDED).** The verified code refutes X10's implicit all-or-nothing framing — the three truthfulness gaps are independently unblockable (A2-Q7 exact-equality is comparison, not the satisfiability the engine lacks; A5-Q6 universe-union and A5-Q7 chord-rekey need neither overlap nor platform work) — so gating ALL conflict truth behind R5 (Option 2) needlessly back-loads the L9 flagship, and a permanent cross-platform non-goal (Option 3/host-only) is a one-way move against the L54 portability goal. That much upholds the brief's tiering direction. But the brief's own Option 1 mis-sequences and over-bundles, which the ADDED option fixes on two code-grounded points. FIRST, the decision is NOT deferrable to Phase 2: Phase 1 ships R7's 'Conflicts' filter chip and R8 chord capture (report L167/L246), and per A5-Q7 chords corrupt the single-Keystroke map (K-C/K-D collapse to false positives; prefix-shadow goes unsurfaced). So a near-free, settings-only Phase-1 floor — rekey ConflictMap to Vec<Keystroke> (the engine already has Trigger::Keystrokes, keymap.rs:44-49) and scope the chip copy — is mandatory to avoid Phase 1 shipping a chip that is demonstrably less honest than today. SECOND, honest DETECTION of a fixed/custom collision needs only the A5-Q6 union; NAMING it needs the ~469-site A5-Q3 identity project (X5-scale). Splitting them lets v1 ship honest generic detection in Phase 2 and defer the identity project, making the flagship win cheaper and earlier than the brief's bundled v1. The one non-negotiable carries over: the Product honesty ruling must fix exactly what 'no conflict' promises at each tier (floor included) BEFORE the Phase-1 chip ships — get that right and this option dominates; get it wrong and it degenerates into Option 2's failure mode in Phase 1 rather than Phase 2.

- _Residual risk:_ Exact structural ContextPredicate equality is only correct if predicates are canonically spelled. The current engine has no canonicalizer (A2-Q7: no FromStr, arbitrary 7-variant boolean trees), and A5-Q2 flags as UNAUDITED how many distinct predicate spellings exist and whether two real scopes share a spelling. So v1's exact-equality could both (a) fail to collapse a genuine false positive (two equivalent-but-differently-spelled predicates over the same keystroke) and (b) miss a genuine overlap (different spellings, overlapping satisfaction) — the latter is properly v2's job, but the former means v1 may NOT fully kill the false-positive class it is sold on without a canonicalization step. If the startup-walk audit (settle-it #1) shows the dominant false-positive source is differently-SPELLED-same-SCOPE predicates rather than genuinely-different-scope predicates, v1's value erodes and the overlap primitive (v2) becomes more urgent than the tiering implies. Secondary surviving risk: if the cross-platform-only collision rate turns out HIGH (settle-it #2, itself an R5-spike output), the v3/R5 dependency tightens and the gap between 'honest on this platform' and 'honest for shared configs' (end-goals L54) stays user-visible for longer — the tier boundary is honest but the portability promise still trails.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by X1 (one resolved-binding universe to scan vs four parallel collections) and X9 (mutate-vs-inject sets how many bindings exist to conflict-check). NEW Phase-1 coupling: the floor co-sequences with R7 (Conflicts chip) and R8 (chord capture) — the chord-rekey is settings-only (no engine dep) and the chip-copy honesty ruling must precede Phase-1 ship. v1 (Phase 2) needs A5-Q2 (expose private context predicate) + A5-Q6 (union editable+fixed+custom); it does NOT need A5-Q3 (the ~469-site fixed-binding naming project is deferred — DETECTION is separable from NAMING). v2 gated by the Axis-2 overlap/satisfiability decision atop A2-Q7's point-eval engine (and a predicate canonicalizer). v3 gated by R5 + R3b record schema (A6-Q9/A6-Q6) and X3 (platform-in-data-model); v3 VERIFICATION additionally needs X13's test-only target-OS injection seam (OperatingSystem::get() has no native override). This decision GATES R6's 'name the colliding action + one-click rebind + hard-conflict save-gate' (which also depends on the deferred A5-Q3 for naming) and is the regression-net consumer of X13.
- _What would settle it:_ Two measurements plus one product ruling, with one addition. (1) A startup walk counting, per keystroke, how many of today's flagged conflicts are FALSE POSITIVES under exact structural ContextPredicate equality vs the current keystroke-only check, AND — newly — what fraction of those false positives are differently-SPELLED-same-SCOPE predicates (which exact-equality alone misses without canonicalization) vs genuinely-different-scope (A5-Q2 flags this as currently unaudited). High false-positive rate that is mostly different-scope => v1 alone recovers most value cheaply; high rate that is mostly same-scope-different-spelling => v1 also needs a canonicalizer and v2 is more urgent. (2) An estimate (an R5-spike output, since the data does not exist yet) of how many real collisions exist ONLY on the non-host OS — low rate means v3 can safely trail. (3) A Product ruling fixing the truthfulness contract per tier INCLUDING the Phase-1 floor copy ('no same-key conflicts on this platform', not 'no conflicts'), so neither the floor chip nor v1 overclaims. If the false-positive rate is high-and-mostly-different-scope and the cross-platform-only rate is low, the ADDED tiering is clearly correct; if either the spelling-collapse share or the cross-platform-only rate is high, the v1 canonicalizer and the v3/R5 dependency respectively tighten and Option 2 gains weight.
- _Red-team added option:_ Phase-1 honesty floor + detection-before-naming tiering
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code_grounding citation against the answers doc and report; the five engine facts (A2-Q7 point-eval/no-overlap/no-FromStr; A5-Q6 editable-only universe + empty fixed-lens name; A5-Q7 single-Keystroke ConflictMap + chord collapse; A6-Q6 inactive-platform chord never constructed; A5-Q2 private context) all hold at the cited lines. The brief's core thesis (three SEPARABLE truthfulness gaps; tiering beats all-or-nothing) survives and is upheld. Four corrections HARDEN it rather than flip it: (1) HIDDEN PHASE-1 COUPLING the brief missed — the roadmap puts R8 chord capture AND an R7 'Conflicts' filter chip in PHASE 1 (report L167/L246, Phase-1 items #2/#3). Per A5-Q7 chords make the single-Keystroke ConflictMap UNSOUND (prefix-collapse false positives + prefix-shadow false negatives), and the Conflicts chip surfaces that wrong map in the UI. So the honesty-contract product ruling and the cheap chord-rekey are Phase-1-coupled; the decision is NOT cleanly deferrable to Phase 2 as the brief implies. (2) The brief BUNDLES A5-Q3 fixed-binding stable identity (~469 constructor sites / 93 files, X5-scale) into v1 'to NAME a collision' — but DETECTING a fixed collision (A5-Q6 universe union) is separable from NAMING it; v1 can honestly show 'conflicts with a built-in shortcut' un-named and defer the ~469-site identity project, making v1 materially cheaper. (3) The chord-rekey (A5-Q7) is an APP/settings-side change (app/util/bindings.rs + app/settings_view/keybindings.rs); the engine already stores Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>) (keymap.rs:44-49), so it needs neither A5-Q2 context exposure nor A5-Q6 universe and can ship in Phase 1. (4) R5 is OVERSTATED as 'Phase-3-deep' — the report labels it 'medium' (L146) and lists it as Phase-2-OPTIONAL (L258); only the sequencing prose (L270) lumps it with 'deepest refactors'. It is hard-gated on the Axis-2 record schema (A6-Q9), so cross-platform v3 trails R3b but is not inherently the last thing — this weakens Option 2's 'back-loads to the very end' steelman and lowers Option 1's v3-slip risk. Also flagged: the brief's '~167 user-reachable of 397' is UNAUDITED (that count is exactly what X5 produces; A5-Q3 only confirms 397 ::new / 469 incl. new_per_platform construction sites, not the reachable subset).

### X11 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team with a single DRI: the keymap/engine lead owns the registry primitive and is the canonical-name authority (names are warpui_core static-strs). Two required approvers ratify the contract — the settings-UI / R9-catalog owner and the AI / change-keybinding-skill owner — on (a) the registry format and (b) the unknown-name policy (warn-and-keep recommended over today's silent no-op). The CONTRACT ratification + the warn-on-unknown fix are Phase-1 deliverables owned by the engine lead; the registry BUILD + seed are deferred to land with Step-D (Phase 3) and are co-owned with whoever lands R3b's loader change (X2).

**Decision.** Split the one question into the part that must be decided now and the part that can be built later. (A) CONTRACT (decide in Phase 1, ~0 code): name a single canonical-name authority/DRI and ratify the unknown-name policy (warn-and-keep vs today's silent no-op), BEFORE the Phase-1 R9 catalog v1 ships and turns action `name` into a committed public id. (B) MECHANISM: decide whether name-drift is handled by one shared owned alias/migration registry in warpui_core consulted at all three sites (loader, R9 catalog, Step-D migration), three private per-consumer fixes, or a no-rename freeze — and WHEN to build it. The decision is to adopt the single shared owned registry but build and seed it just-in-time with Step-D (Phase 3), since that is the first deliberate rename and the only point at which a non-empty seed can exist; in the interim ship the cheap warn-on-unknown-name loader fix and make the R9 catalog the name-validation source.

**Stakes.** Action `name` becomes an implicit public API the instant the Phase-1 R9 catalog v1 emits it for the agent and a user hand-edits keybindings.yaml. Today an unknown/renamed name silently no-ops at load (no warning, dead entry left in the file — app/src/keyboard.rs only warns on an unparsable TRIGGER, never an unknown NAME; write never prunes), so any future rename or natural cross-release name drift silently orphans persisted power-user triggers and hands the agent stale ids. The PRESENT drift surface is the 393 editable inline static-str names (50 registered >1×); the 469 fixed constructor sites have NO names today (A5-Q3) and only join the drift surface after a separate id-field add. The orphan surface that the headline Step-D migration actually creates is just 3 vim-named editable bindings (A4-Q12). So the cost of choosing the MECHANISM wrong is structural and recurs every release across three consumers — but the cost of building it TOO EARLY is real too: an alias entry is append-only forever, and a Phase-1 registry would be empty with an unseedable payload. The decision that genuinely cannot wait is the cheap one: who owns names + what the loader does on an unknown name.

_Constrained by:_
- Persistence/override is name-keyed end to end: CustomKeybindings is HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>, and update_custom_trigger filters editable bindings by exact b.name==name and never touches fixed_bindings — so a renamed/unknown name matches zero bindings = silent no-op. VERIFIED. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433; app/src/keyboard.rs:169`
- On load only an unparsable TRIGGER warns (the Err branch in TryFrom); an unknown NAME never warns — set_custom_trigger(name,..) for an unknown name silently no-ops. write_custom_keybinding inserts the whole map and never prunes orphaned keys, so dead entries persist forever. read_custom_keybindings returns None on BOTH file-absent and parse-error (File::open(..).ok()? and serde Err both -> None), conflating them; the one loader chokepoint must split absent-vs-parse-error or the first in-app edit truncate-clobbers a present-but-unparseable file. No alias/migration map exists; the only 'legacy' reference is a comment refusing legacy-action support. VERIFIED. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:35-58,71-74,128-138; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:651`
- Step-D orphan surface is tiny and asymmetric: VimEnter/VimBackspace/VimDelete/VimTab forks are FixedBindings with NO name (zero orphan risk); exactly 3 vim-NAMED editable persistable bindings exist — editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_down/up and editor_view:vim_exit_insert_mode. So the original X11 premise that 'VimEnter collapses and orphans triggers' is false — only those 3 names are at risk. VERIFIED. — `A4-Q12; app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:510,517,589; app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:962`
- Fixed bindings have NO stable persistable id TODAY: as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default() (empty &str) and the only id is a volatile, non-serializable process-local BindingId; ~469 fixed constructor sites. Editable names are ~393 inline static-strs, 50 registered >1×. Consequence: the agent/catalog can only reference the 393 editable names until A5-Q3 adds a stable id to fixed bindings — so fixed-binding drift is a CONTINGENT future surface, not a present one. VERIFIED. — `A5-Q3 (keymap.rs:633,261-272); A1-Q9`
- DEFERABILITY (brief missed): report §7 sequencing puts the action-catalog v1 / agent manifest (R9) in Phase 1 over CURRENT names, the record schema R3b in Phase 2, and Step-D's ModalKeystroke rename — the only deliberate rename that orphans the 3 vim names — in Phase 3. So in Phase 1 the migration registry would be empty (no rename to migrate) and its seed cannot exist until Step-D mints the new names. Whether the loader chokepoint is even forced open in Phase 1 hinges on the UNRESOLVED A1-Q1 contradiction (end-goals lists R3b as an Axis-1 leaf; report §7 puts it in Phase 2). VERIFIED — decisive for timing. — `keybindings-architecture-report.md:244-265,270; A1-Q12 residual / A1-Q1`

**Options:**

- **Ratify-now / build-just-in-time registry (decide contract Phase 1, build at Step-D)** _(low effort · costly)_ ✅ **recommended** — Phase 1, ~0 code: (1) name the canonical-name-authority DRI (engine lead) and ratify the unknown-name policy (warn-and-keep); (2) ship the cheap warn-on-unknown-name loader fix so existing silent orphaning becomes visible; (3) make the R9 catalog v1 the name-validation source; (4) record a freeze note that the 3 vim names must not be renamed until the registry exists. LATER, co-designed with Step-D (Phase 3) and R3b's loader work (X2): build the single append-only owned alias/migration registry in warpui_core and seed it with the ACTUAL Step-D renames then. One owned contract read by loader + R9 catalog + Step-D migration — satisfies the report's 'shared infra, not three private fixes' mandate.
  - 👍 Captures every benefit of the full registry (one source of truth, ownership answered, report mandate met) while paying day-1 cost only for what Phase 1 can actually use. It refuses to bet on the unresolved A1-Q1 (does R3b land Phase 1?) and refuses to 'seed 3 renames' that don't exist yet. The genuinely time-critical move — ruling who owns names + the unknown-name policy — happens before the Phase-1 catalog turns name into a committed public id, which is exactly when that ruling is cheap and reversible. The warn-on-unknown fix converts today's silent data loss into a visible signal for the entire Phase-1→Phase-3 window without any registry.
  - 👎 A decided-but-unbuilt registry relies on PROCESS discipline: if someone renames an editable name in an unrelated Phase-1/2 refactor before the registry code exists, the orphan recurs — so the ownership ruling must carry a 'no rename without an alias entry' rule from day one even though the rewrite-on-load code lands later. Still does nothing on its own about fixed bindings lacking a stable id (A5-Q3, a separate prerequisite). The append-only public-API surface is deferred, not eliminated.
- **Shared name-alias/migration registry built NOW (original recommendation)** _(med effort · costly)_ — Stand up the versioned append-only warpui_core alias table immediately, seed v1, and wire its read at the loader chokepoint in Phase 1; consulted at loader + R9 catalog + Step-D migration; owned by engine team.
  - 👍 Only option (besides the JIT variant) that satisfies all three consumers with one mechanism and turns the implicit public API into an explicit testable one. If A1-Q1 resolves R3b-into-Phase-1, the loader is already open and co-building is efficient.
  - 👎 Premature: in Phase 1 the table is EMPTY (Step-D rename is Phase 3) and its seed cannot exist yet — 'seed the 3 Step-D renames now' is incoherent because Step-D hasn't minted the new names. 'Wire the read at the loader A1-Q12 forces open' silently assumes A1-Q1 lands R3b in Phase 1, contradicting report §7 (Phase 2). Pays the permanent append-only public-API + cross-team-coordination cost up front for a no-op read. If under-resourced it goes stale and the UI/agent lie with confidence.
- **Loader-local rename pass + warn-on-unknown (point fix)** _(low effort · easy)_ — No shared registry. Inside the loader: split absent-vs-parse-error, add a one-time rename map that rewrites keybindings.yaml, and warn when a persisted name matches zero bindings. Leave R9 catalog and the agent skill to handle drift on their own.
  - 👍 Smallest change that stops silent data loss; piggybacks on the absent-vs-parse-error split the loader needs anyway for R3b (A1-Q12). Its warn-on-unknown half is genuinely valuable in Phase 1 and is hoisted into the recommended option.
  - 👎 The catalog and change-keybinding agent still reference stale names with no shared source of truth — exactly the 'three private fixes' the report warns against. The next rename in a different consumer re-creates the bug; the actual X11 question (who owns names) is left unanswered.
- **Freeze names / no-rename policy** _(low effort · easy)_ — Forbid Step-D from renaming any editable name: keep the 3 vim names stable, route new modal non-char keys through fixed bindings (today's zero-orphan pattern), defer any registry until a rename is genuinely forced.
  - 👍 A4-Q12 proves the forked non-char keys are ALREADY fixed (zero orphan risk) and only 3 editable names exist — so 'just don't rename those 3 and keep new modal keys fixed' is a correct, cheap interim, and it is in fact adopted as the freeze-note inside the recommended option.
  - 👎 Unenforceable: nothing in code stops a future rename, and the 393 editable names drift naturally across releases. Helps the catalog/agent not at all. Punts the reckoning — when a rename is finally unavoidable there is still no migration path, so you ship the silent-orphan bug. Does not answer 'who owns names'. (CORRECTION: enacting the freeze is LOW effort — the brief's 'very-high' label conflated enactment cost with deferred-debt cost.)

**→ Recommendation: Ratify-now / build-just-in-time registry (decide contract Phase 1, build at Step-D).** The OPTION-CLASS verdict is upheld: a single shared owned registry beats three private fixes (which re-create the orphan bug in the catalog/agent — the two consumers X11 names) and beats an unenforceable freeze (which leaves 393 drifting editable names and never answers ownership). What I corrected is TIMING and one hidden bet. The decisive fact the brief omitted is report §7's sequencing: the only deliberate rename (Step-D ModalKeystroke) is Phase 3, R3b is Phase 2, and only the read-only R9 catalog v1 is Phase 1 — so a Phase-1 registry is an empty table whose seed literally cannot exist until Step-D mints the new names, and whose loader read the brief justifies via 'the chokepoint A1-Q12 forces open' actually depends on the UNRESOLVED A1-Q1 (report §7 puts R3b in Phase 2, not Phase 1). Building the registry now therefore buys a no-op read while incurring the permanent append-only public-API + cross-team cost early. The recommended option keeps the full shared-registry benefit but spends Phase 1 effort only where it pays: ruling who owns names + the unknown-name policy (the literal X11 question, ~30 min) BEFORE the Phase-1 catalog commits name as a public id, shipping the cheap warn-on-unknown loader fix (the one valuable sliver of Option B, harm-reduction for the whole Phase 1→3 window), making the catalog the validation source, and freezing the 3 vim names. The registry primitive and its real seed land WITH Step-D, co-designed with R3b's loader changes (X2). This strictly dominates 'build now': same end-state, lower day-1 cost, no bet on A1-Q1, and no incoherent empty seed.

- _Residual risk:_ A decided-but-not-yet-built registry is only as good as the process discipline behind it: natural cross-release editable-name drift can orphan a power-user's hand-edited binding between ANY two releases, independent of Step-D, and if an engineer renames an editable name in some unrelated Phase-1/2 refactor before the registry code exists, the silent orphan recurs with no rewrite path to catch it. The warn-on-unknown fix downgrades that from silent loss to a visible warning, but the user's binding is still dead until manually re-edited. So the ownership ruling MUST bind a 'no rename without a (parked) alias entry' rule from Phase 1 onward, and the team must accept that the rewrite-on-load code arriving later means the registry's authority is procedural, not enforced-in-code, during the interim. If that discipline is not held, this option degrades toward Option B's outcome.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by A1-Q1: whether R3b lands in Phase 1 or Phase 2 (an unresolved end-goals-vs-report-§7 contradiction) determines whether the loader chokepoint is open in Phase 1 — the brief's 'build now' silently assumed R3b-in-Phase-1; the report roadmap says Phase 2. Co-located with X2 / A1-Q12: when the registry IS built, its read must share the loader chokepoint that also needs the duplex/untagged deserialize + absent-vs-parse-error split, and its alias key space must match the file key. Gates Axis-4 Step-D (A4-Q12/A4-Q15): Step-D (Phase 3) must not rename any of the 3 editable vim names until the registry exists and is seeded — this is the just-in-time build trigger. Feeds Axis-5 R9 catalog (Phase 1, read-only, current names) and the agent skill; A5-Q3 (fixed bindings have no stable id) is a SIBLING prerequisite — the agent cannot reference fixed bindings at all until that 469-site id-field work lands, so near-term drift risk is editable-names-only. Related to X13: the resolution-snapshot harness is the natural regression net that catches a rename silently changing a binding's winner.
- _What would settle it:_ Three inputs, all available now: (1) Confirm from report §7 (done here) that Step-D is Phase 3, R3b is Phase 2, and the only Phase-1 name consumer is the read-only R9 catalog v1 — this confirms the registry BUILD is deferrable and that only the ownership ruling + warn-on-unknown + catalog-as-validation-source are Phase-1 work. (2) Get the A1-Q1 ruling (R3b in Phase 1 vs Phase 2) — if Phase 2, the registry's loader read co-designs with R3b later and there is zero Phase-1 loader work; if Phase 1, build the registry read alongside R3b's chokepoint changes. (3) A ~30-minute cross-team ratification fixing the canonical-name authority (engine lead) and the unknown-name policy (warn-and-keep), plus a 'no rename without a parked alias entry' rule that binds from Phase 1. Once (2) and (3) are signed, the choice resolves to: ratify now, warn now, build-and-seed at Step-D.
- _Red-team added option:_ Ratify-now / build-just-in-time registry (rule ownership + unknown-name policy + ship warn-on-unknown in Phase 1; build & seed the append-only registry at Step-D)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified all 5 code_grounding facts against source — all hold (keymap.rs:422-433 name-only filter never touching fixed_bindings; app/src/keyboard.rs:35-58/71-74/128-138 silent-unknown-name + never-prune + absent-vs-parse-error conflation; keymap.rs:651 legacy-refusal comment; A4-Q12's 3 vim-named editable bindings vs nameless fixed forks; A5-Q3 fixed-no-stable-id/469 sites; A1-Q9 393 editable/50 dup). The brief even used the corrected 469 number. Grounding is solid; the WEAKNESS is in the recommendation's urgency and one hidden bet. (1) DEFERABILITY (decisive, brief missed): report §7 sequencing puts Step-D's ModalKeystroke rename — the ONLY deliberate rename that orphans the 3 vim names — in Phase 3; R3b in Phase 2; only the read-only R9 catalog v1 is Phase 1. So in Phase 1 the migration registry is EMPTY (no rename to migrate) and its seed CANNOT exist yet (Step-D hasn't minted the new names). 'Stand up the table NOW and seed the 3 renames' is therefore premature and partly incoherent. (2) HIDDEN DEPENDENCY: 'wire its first read at the loader chokepoint A1-Q12 already forces open' silently assumes A1-Q1 resolves R3b into Phase 1 — which contradicts report §7 (R3b is Phase 2). If R3b defers, the loader is not forced open in Phase 1 and the registry has no Phase-1 home, reinforcing deferral. (3) OPTIMISM/PRICING: Option C's effort 'very-high' is wrong — enacting a name-freeze is LOW effort; its real cost is unenforceability/deferred debt, which belongs in what_breaks. Option B's warn-on-unknown sliver is actually the one genuinely valuable Phase-1 deliverable and should be hoisted out of B. (4) HAND-WAVING fix: the claim '393 editable + 469 fixed names drift' overstates the PRESENT surface — fixed bindings have NO names today (empty name + volatile BindingId, A5-Q3), so the live drift surface is only the 393 editable names; the 469 fixed sites become driftable only AFTER A5-Q3 adds an id field. Net: the OPTION choice (shared owned registry > three private fixes > freeze) is upheld; I corrected the recommendation to decide-the-contract-now / build-the-registry-just-in-time and added that as the recommended option.

### X13 — HARDENED · _cross-team_ · owner: Cross-team: the keybindings-initiative eng-lead owns it as primary (owns Phase sequencing and the 'mandatory gate' mandate, now reframed as 'cheap freeze now + just-in-time gate at the R2 boundary'). The warpui_core/platform owner is consulted but NO LONGER a hard co-sign blocker, because the attack downgraded the target-OS injection seam from a bundled prerequisite to a contingent sub-decision — they sign off only if the spike shows CI cannot capture per-OS goldens natively and a get() override is actually needed (platform/mod.rs:668-683). Ratified by the Axis 2-6 implementers, who must commit in writing to run the diff as a hard pre-merge gate once it exists (without their buy-in the just-in-time harness rots). Product is not in the loop — internal quality/test-strategy infra.

**Decision.** Whether to build, BEFORE the first resolver-reordering change (R2: resolve bindings() by (layer,recency), a Phase-2 item), a shared (context, keystroke, OS) -> winning-action resolution baseline over the real ~266 registration sites, and mandate it as the before/after regression diff for the Axis 2-6 refactors — versus letting each axis write isolated unit fixtures. Reframed by the attack into two separable sub-decisions the brief conflated: (a) CAPTURE — freeze a clean pre-refactor resolution dump (irreversible, must happen before any bindings() reorder, ~1 day); and (b) HARNESS+GATE — build the diff tooling, mandate it as a pre-merge gate, add context breadth, the keystroke-space/chord walk, and the target-OS seam (reversible, can be built just-in-time when R2/Axis-6 actually land).

**Stakes.** Five of six axes refactor the same resolver, and R2 re-resolves bindings() by (layer, recency) (keymap.rs:454-464; report:140,254) — reordering the head of the first-match iterator and able to silently flip the winner for any of 266 registrations. Today nothing would catch that: matcher_tests.rs is exactly 3 tests with no shadowing/precedence/unbind coverage and context_tests.rs is 1 test. A flipped winner on a hot keystroke (terminal copy, an editor motion) is a severe, user-facing, hard-to-localize regression that ships as a bug report, not red CI. The timing claim is real but narrower than the brief stated: the clean pre-refactor baseline can only be captured before the FIRST bindings()-reordering change (R2), which is a Phase-2 item — NOT before Phase 1. Phase-1 quick wins (R1/R7/R10, report:244,270) do not reorder bindings(), so the baseline survives all of Phase 1. Consequence: this decision is genuinely deferrable through Phase 1, the urgency is bounded, and the irreversible part (the data dump) is cheap enough (~1 day) that there is no reason to gamble the window — capture early, decide the harness later.

_Constrained by:_
- No full-keymap resolution snapshot exists; the only startup scaffolding over the real bindings is the debug-only validate_bindings() per-binding yes/no validity pass (one default cross-platform validator + four context-scoped PTY-compliance validators), which iterates bindings_for_context per registered context but never resolves a winner per (context,keystroke) and emits no diffable table. matcher_tests.rs is 251 lines / exactly 3 tests (verified); context_tests.rs is 26 lines / 1 test (verified). — `matcher.rs:181-203; matcher_tests.rs:5,89,179; context_tests.rs:4-5 (A3-Q17)`
- The 'winner' = first match in bindings() = editable LIFO (.iter().rev()) then fixed reverse-registration (.iter().rev()); push_keystroke early-returns on exact-length chord match. R2 reorders bindings() by (layer,recency), changing the head of this iterator — exactly a winner-flip, with nothing asserting it today. Verified all line refs. — `keymap.rs:441-447,454-464; matcher.rs:324-337 (esp. 329-331); report:140,254`
- 266 register_* calls (146 fixed + 120 editable, verified) plus 397 FixedBinding::new feed bindings(); 98 distinct files under app/ hold registrations and the knowledge graph is blind to app/. A baseline must boot the full app-level Matcher; a crates-only or graph-only one misses most production bindings. — `grep app/ crates/ (fixed=146, editable=120, FixedBinding::new=397, 98 app/ files); A3-Q17 answers:1238,1253-1254`
- CORRECTED necessity claim: OS affects resolution at REGISTRATION time (~14 'if OperatingSystem::get().is_mac()' branches inside binding construction in util/bindings.rs), and there is NO match-time platform resolution inside keymap/matcher (grep empty). OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time cfg_if with only the wasm branch resolving at runtime (via a OnceLock). Therefore the other-OS golden can be captured by running the characterization test natively on each CI target OS; the runtime injection seam (A6-Q14) is required ONLY for single-host cross-OS validation, not unconditionally. The brief overstated the seam as a hard prerequisite bundled into this decision. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:296-425; platform/mod.rs:668-683; wasm.rs:7-8,55; A6-Q12 answers:2357,2378`
- The baseline shape is a design (not code) choice: no canonical representative-Context list exists; Actions are Arc<dyn Action> with no stable identity/serialization (needs a name/debug projection for a diffable key); a per-trigger winner via bindings_for_context (matcher.rs:282) skips chord/prefix early-return semantics; and today's flat name-keyed schema becomes records {name,trigger,context?,platform?} in Phase-2 (R3b), so a flat-keyed dump must carry Context to survive. R3b's scope is itself an unresolved contradiction gated on A1-Q1. — `A3-Q17 residual answers:1257; A1-Q9 answers:283-300; report:140(R2),256(R3b)`

**Options:**

- **Snapshot-first complete harness** _(very-high effort · costly)_ — Before any axis refactor, build the full thing once: boot the app-level Matcher, enumerate a complete representative-Context set across all OSes, add the target-OS injection seam, do a true keystroke-space walk (chords/prefix/early-return), serialize a stable golden, and mandate the diff for every axis. The literal 'yes' of X13.
  - 👍 The only artifact that proves the global invariant 'no unrelated binding changed winner' across all 266 registrations and all OSes in one shot, and it hands Axis 6 its target-OS seam (A6-Q14) for free. Build once, fully, and every later axis inherits a true regression net with zero per-axis re-investment.
  - 👎 Boiling the ocean during Phase 1, which touches no resolver — pure speculative front-loading whose gate has nothing to protect yet and rots. No canonical context list (A3-Q17 residual), so 'complete' is unbounded and unprovable: green over 10 contexts says nothing about the 50th. Arc<dyn Action> has no stable serialization, so a naive golden is flaky (debug-string churn reviewers learn to ignore). A flat name-keyed golden is reworked when R3b lands (A1-Q9). Weeks of infra delay user-facing Phase-1 value for a net Phase-2 needs.
- **Per-axis isolated fixtures (defer / cheap)** _(low effort · costly)_ — Build no shared baseline. Each axis writes its own targeted unit fixtures. Add a shared baseline later only if pain materializes.
  - 👍 Lowest immediate cost, fully distributed, no central infra to design or maintain, no Phase-1 delay, and no bet on an unproven harness shape while the schema (flat vs R3b) is unsettled.
  - 👎 Structurally cannot prove the global invariant — a per-axis fixture only sees its own case, so the R2 (layer,recency) reorder can silently flip any of 266 winners with no test catching the cross-axis flip (keymap.rs:454-464). Starting coverage is near-zero (3 matcher tests), so regressions surface as user bug reports. Critically, it lets the clean pre-refactor baseline be destroyed by the first bindings() reorder — a one-way loss. This option is DOMINATED by the freeze-first option, which buys exactly that irreversibility insurance for ~1 day and then lets you defer the harness identically.
- **Incremental shared baseline (thin Phase-1 harness, mandate now, grow per axis)** _(med effort · easy)_ — In Phase 1 build a minimal shared characterization snapshot, project Action->name, take per-registered-trigger winners via bindings_for_context over a small representative-Context set, anchor on the validate_bindings hook, and adopt it as a mandatory pre-merge gate from Phase 1 onward. Grow context/OS/chord coverage per axis.
  - 👍 Captures the core shared-diff value while exercising the tooling and establishing the gate habit before the high-stakes R2 lands; measures flakiness early; reuses bindings_for_context and validate_bindings so marginal build cost is modest; Context-parameterized so it survives R3b.
  - 👎 Front-loads a mandatory gate during a Phase 1 that reorders nothing, so the gate guards nothing yet and risks rotting before it matters; if leads don't enforce it, it decays to the defer option. The per-trigger-winner shortcut skips chord/prefix early-return (matcher.rs:329-331), so it is blind to exactly the shadowing/ambiguity that R2/push_keystroke (Axis 3 — the riskiest consumer) changes, until extended. Pays for harness build, key-shape design, and the OS-seam question before any of them is load-bearing.
- **Decoupled freeze-first (capture now, build+mandate the diff just-in-time at the first bindings() reorder, grow per axis)** _(low effort · easy)_ ✅ **recommended** — Split the decision into its irreversible-cheap and reversible-expensive halves. NOW (Phase 1, ~1 day): boot the app-level Matcher in a test, project Action->name/debug, take per-context winners via bindings_for_context over ~8-12 hand-curated Contexts capturing MAXIMAL info (full context debug + action name + action debug + trigger + OS), and commit it as a frozen golden data artifact — pure insurance against the irreversible loss. LATER (just-in-time, at the Phase-1->Phase-2 boundary when R2 lands): build the comparison tooling, mandate the before/after diff as a hard pre-merge gate, extend to a keystroke-space/chord walk for Axis 3/4, and add the target-OS seam ONLY if CI cannot run the test natively per-OS. Grow context coverage per axis.
  - 👍 Targets the ONLY time-sensitive, irreversible thing — the baseline data — and buys it for ~1 day, neutralizing the entire urgency/irreversibility lever the brief leans on. By capturing maximal info and projecting at compare time, the frozen artifact survives the flat->R3b schema change (A1-Q9) and the X11 action renames without rework. Defers every reversible, still-undecided piece (harness shape, gate discipline, OS seam, chord walk) to when it is actually load-bearing, so it neither boils the ocean (vs option 1) nor stands up a mandatory gate over a Phase 1 that protects nothing (vs option 3) nor gambles the window (vs option 2). Dominates the defer option outright and the incremental option on speculative cost. Reuses bindings_for_context (matcher.rs:282) and the validate_bindings startup hook, so the capture is small.
  - 👎 The capture's 'low' effort is contingent on the spike confirming the full app-level Matcher (242/266 app-side registrations) can be booted in a test — if that needs real plumbing, even the freeze is medium (this risk is shared with option 3). The frozen artifact gives no live protection on its own: between the freeze and the just-in-time harness, nothing enforces the invariant — but that window is Phase 1, which reorders no resolver, so the exposure is near-zero. Requires someone to actually own building the harness at the R2 boundary; if that ownership lapses, you have a golden no one diffs. The thin per-trigger projection still under-tests chord/ambiguity until the keystroke-space walk is added for Axis 3/4.

**→ Recommendation: Decoupled freeze-first (capture now, build+mandate the diff just-in-time at the first bindings() reorder, grow per axis).** The decision's real value is the SHARED before/after diff — the only thing that can prove 'no unrelated binding changed winner', which per-axis fixtures provably cannot, and which the R2 (layer,recency) reorder makes a live risk against 266 registrations with 3 tests of coverage. So 'defer entirely' is wrong: it forecloses the only clean baseline-capture window. But the attack shows the brief conflated two things that should be split. (1) Only the baseline DATA is irreversible, and the window is all of Phase 1 (R2 is Phase-2; R1/R7/R10 reorder nothing — report:244,270), and capturing it is ~1 day via bindings_for_context (matcher.rs:282) + an Action->name/debug projection. (2) Everything else — the diff tooling, the mandatory gate, context breadth, the keystroke-space/chord walk, and the OS-injection seam — is reversible and still genuinely undecided in code (no canonical context list, Arc<dyn Action> not serializable, flat-vs-R3b in flux per A1-Q9, OS-seam necessity contingent on CI topology). Front-loading all of that (option 1) boils the ocean; standing up a mandatory gate during a Phase 1 that touches no resolver (option 3) guards nothing yet and risks rotting. The freeze-first path keeps the one property that matters by capturing maximal info now and projecting at compare time so the artifact survives R3b and X11 renames, then builds and mandates the diff just-in-time when R2 actually lands. It dominates 'defer' (same deferral of the harness, but with the irreversibility insurance bought) and beats 'incremental' on speculative cost. Net: yes, secure the shared-baseline value before the refactors — but do it as a cheap frozen capture plus a just-in-time harness, and explicitly DROP the OS-injection seam from this decision unless a spike shows CI cannot run the characterization test natively per-OS.

- _Residual risk:_ The strongest surviving counter-argument is that the freeze-first split trades live coverage for cheapness, and the cheap projection it can afford is exactly the one blind to the highest-risk change. A per-registered-trigger winner table (the only thing affordable in ~1 day) does not exercise push_keystroke's chord/prefix early-return (matcher.rs:329-331), which is precisely the semantics R2/Axis-3 rewrites. So a frozen golden can show 'every single-keystroke winner unchanged' while a chord-shadowing or short-shadows-chord ordering quietly flips — a green diff that is false comfort on the riskiest axis. Mitigation is to seed the freeze with the known short-shadows-chord and editable-beats-fixed fixtures (A3-Q17) as explicit chord cases and to commit, in writing, to extending to a keystroke-space walk before R2 merges — but that pushes real work back to the just-in-time phase, where the brief's central worry (that the gate rots without implementer buy-in) reasserts itself. A secondary residual: if the spike finds the app-level Matcher cannot be booted in a test cheaply, the 'low-effort cheap insurance' premise collapses and this option converges with the incremental one.
- _Dependencies:_ Gated by A1-Q1/A1-Q9 (R3b flat-vs-records ruling): mitigated by capturing MAXIMAL info in the frozen dump and projecting at compare time, so the artifact survives the schema change without rework — but the comparison key shape must still be chosen with R3b in mind when the harness is built. Interacts with X11 (action-name alias/migration map): the diff projects Action->name, so a Step-D rename legitimately changes the golden — the diff must consult X11's alias map to tell an intentional rename from an accidental flip. Interacts with X12 (matcher perf / trie R4): if R4 moves earlier, the keystroke-space walk added at the R2 boundary must cover trie/ambiguity semantics. NEW explicit dependency surfaced by the attack — CI topology: whether the target-OS injection seam (A6-Q14) is needed at all depends on whether CI runs the characterization test on both mac and linux runners (OS branching is at registration time, util/bindings.rs:296-425); if it does, the seam can be dropped from this decision entirely. Sequencing dependency: the irreversible capture must land before the first bindings() reorder (R2, Phase 2 — report:140); all of Phase 1 (R1/R7/R10) is safely before that line. Feeds Axis 2/3/4/5/6 acceptance: each 'no cross-axis regression' claim is gated on this diff existing by the time that axis touches the resolver.
- _What would settle it:_ A 1-3 day time-boxed spike with three measurements. (1) Boot the full app-level Matcher in a test and confirm the 242/266 app-side registrations are reachable (not just crates/) — this is the gating risk for the 'low-effort cheap freeze' premise; if it needs heavy plumbing the freeze is not cheap. (2) Over ~8-12 representative Contexts, take per-registered-trigger winners via bindings_for_context (matcher.rs:282), project Action->name/debug, serialize a sorted maximal-info table, and diff it across a trivial no-op branch to measure flakiness/signal-to-noise. Decision rule: if stable and low-noise, freeze the artifact immediately and schedule the just-in-time harness for the R2 boundary; if flaky or it needs dozens of contexts to mean anything, fall back to an in-process invariant-comparison (compare two live Matchers under a flag) instead of a committed golden. (3) Check the CI matrix: if tests already run on mac AND linux, drop the OS-injection seam from this decision; only if CI is single-OS, prototype whether get() can take a cfg(test) override cheaply (mirroring the wasm OnceLock at wasm.rs:8) or needs a deeper refactor. The non-code input that ultimately settles it: the Axis 2-6 implementers committing in writing to run the diff as a mandatory pre-merge gate before each touches the resolver.
- _Red-team added option:_ Decoupled freeze-first (capture the irreversible golden now, build+mandate the diff just-in-time at the first bindings() reorder, grow per axis)
- _Red-team:_ Re-verified every code_grounding fact against source; all core facts hold exactly (3 matcher tests / 251 lines; 146+120=266 register_ calls + 397 FixedBinding::new; 98 app/ files; bindings() LIFO-then-fixed-rev at keymap.rs:441-464; push_keystroke early-return matcher.rs:329-331; validate_bindings debug-only per-binding validity matcher.rs:181-203; OperatingSystem::get() compile-time cfg_if platform/mod.rs:668-683 with only wasm using a runtime OnceLock). Three attacks landed. (1) DEFERABILITY/URGENCY OVERSTATED: the brief implies the capture window closes at Phase-1 start; it does not. The winner-flipping change is R2 — resolve bindings() by (layer,recency) — which the report places in Phase 2 (report:140,254). Phase-1 quick wins (R1 watcher / R7 search / R10 edit-path validators, report:244,270 'no schema/engine changes') do NOT reorder bindings(), so the clean baseline survives all of Phase 1. The real gate is 'before the first bindings() reorder', not 'before any axis touches anything'. (2) OS-SEAM NECESSITY OVERSTATED: I confirmed OS branching is at REGISTRATION time (util/bindings.rs:296-425, ~14 OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() inside binding construction) and there is NO match-time platform resolution in keymap/matcher (grep empty). So the other-OS golden can be captured by running the characterization test natively on each CI target OS; the runtime injection seam (A6-Q14) is only needed for single-host cross-OS validation. The recommendation silently assumes single-host validation is required — a hidden dependency on CI topology. (3) MISSING OPTION: the only irreversible thing is the baseline DATA, and capturing it is ~1 day, not weeks. Decoupling the cheap capture from the expensive harness/gate/OS-seam dominates both 'defer' (buys the irreversibility insurance cheaply) and 'incremental' (does not front-load a mandatory gate during a Phase 1 that touches no resolver, where it would rot). Also sharpened the residual: the thin per-trigger-winner snapshot is structurally blind to the chord/prefix early-return semantics (matcher.rs:329-331) that R2/push_keystroke — i.e. Axis 3, the single riskiest consumer — actually changes; so the cheap snapshot under-protects exactly the axis it most needs to protect until extended to a keystroke-space walk.
